# Yldenfall: Game Design Bible

*Version 2.0, 2026-11-04 (the source game world; 1.0 was 2026-10-22). Owner: Claude (build team). Approver: Sean.*

This is the single source of truth for the game. Every thread that builds code, art, levels or audio works from this document. When a build decision contradicts it, either the build changes or this document is updated in the same pull request, with the reason.

---

## 1. Vision

**One line:** Nine years ago the leaves of the Ylden stopped falling. A stranger climbs the great tree to cut out the flower that will not die, so that autumn can come again: a hand-crafted, interconnected 2D action metroidvania with HD pixel art and movement that feels as good as the best of the genre.

**Pillars** (every feature must serve at least one):

1. **Movement is the joy.** Running, jumping, wall-kicking, pogoing and dashing feel crisp, forgiving and expressive. A player should enjoy moving through an empty room.
2. **The Tree is one place.** The whole world is a single climb, from roots to canopy to the Everbloom at its crown. Every zone is physically connected, and you can see places you cannot reach yet.
3. **An old soul, modern body.** Melancholy and quiet, with warm towns, Ringreaders, season seeds and keys, ranks and titles, springs to wake, and the Everbloom at the crown. Modern systems in place of stiff controls, password saves and grinding.
4. **Weight and impact.** Every hit lands with hit-stop, flash, shake, particles and sound. Bosses are spectacles with readable, fair patterns.
5. **Curiosity pays.** Every dead end hints at a secret. Secrets reward with power, lore or gold, never with nothing.

**Reference games for feel and structure:** Hollow Knight (world density, pogo, map), Celeste (jump forgiveness math), Dead Cells (combat juice), Blasphemous and Ender Lilies (HD pixel mood), Ori (traversal flow), Castlevania: Symphony of the Night (towns-and-castle RPG layer).

**Target experience:** 10 to 12 hours for the main path, about 16 hours for 100%. Desktop first (Windows, Linux, macOS) with full gamepad support; Steam Deck verified as a stretch goal.

**Names (pass 461, 2026-11-04).** The game began as a fan remake of an NES game and kept that game's names, towns and story until pass 460. Sean decided to make it an original game: the story, the world and every name are new (section 2), and the mechanics, rooms and fights stayed. `data/rename_map.json` lists every retired name with its new one, and `test_no_retired_name_is_left` fails if a retired name comes back into a game file. Pass 462 renamed the internal ids the same way (zone folders, rooms, items, abilities, people, guardians, flags and file names; the card keys are the Copper, Silver, Gold, Thorn and Glass Keys and the seeds `thaw`, `sun`, `fall` and `frost`). The table is `ids` in the same file: `GameState.migrate_ids` brings a save written before it over (save version 3; `test_an_old_save_comes_over_to_the_new_ids`), and the test also fails if a retired word is left in an id. As-built notes below that speak of "the source game" are history from the remake years: they explain why a number or rule is what it is, and nothing in them is to be copied.

---

## 2. Story and Tone

**Theme.** Grief tries to stop time, and the world rots because nothing may change. The enemy is permanence. The hero lets things end so they can begin again.

**The world.**
- **The Ylden.** A tree so large that towns live in its roots, a dwarf nation mined its trunk, and elves live in its high boughs. Its autumn leaf-fall feeds the lowland soil. Springs inside it carry water from the deep roots to the crown.
- **Hearthroot**, the hub: a stone town carved into the roots, warm lamplight, a dry fountain. Its people farm the leaf-mould. With no leaves for nine years, the fields are dead and the town is starving.
- **The Hollin**, the dwarves of the Ylden, who built the Hollin Tower and their capital, Vorrhallow. They did not invade or betray anyone. Their own king did this to them.
- **The elves of Sylvarre** in the High Boughs. Many welcomed the endless summer, because an elf fears change more than death. Some still do.
- **Stillglass**, the magenta crystal growing through Vorrhallow and making up the Evercrown. It keeps whatever it touches exactly as it is, forever: water stops flowing, a person stops aging and stops living.
- **The Everbloom**, the flower that will not die: a vast eye-flower of stillglass at the crown, with veins of light running down into the tree. It drinks the springs to stay perfect. It is not a demon but a wish that got out of hand.

**Backstory.** Nine years ago a sickness called the Withering swept Vorrhallow, and Thane Vorrund of the Hollin watched his queen die of it. He would not grieve. With his grafting blade, Severance, he cut the last blossom of that year from the Ylden's crown and set it in stillglass so it could never wither. He asked one thing of it: let nothing I love end. The Everbloom took root and granted the wish too well. Vorrhallow stopped mid-flood, his people mid-breath. The four seasons were drawn into the flower as four seeds, and the leaves stopped falling.

**The Stranger.** The hero is an elf wanderer with no fixed home. Years ago the very last leaf of the Ylden blew a thousand miles and landed at his feet, and he kept it. This spring the leaf turned to glass in his hand, so he follows the wind back to the tree.

**Arc.**
- **Act I (the Heartwood and the Hollin Tower):** Reeve Aldous of Hearthroot wants the stranger gone; strangers are bad luck in a starving town, and he pays him to go down and away. The Ringreader reads the glass leaf and sees the Heartwood Spring stopped by a dam of stillglass. Below town are the Rot Dwarves, Hollin miners the glass held for years and then let go. Gnarlmaw, a root grub woken starving when the glass cracked, gnaws the taproot. The first spring runs, and the fountain fills by a third. In the Hollin Tower the Iron Dwarves still guard the glassworks on their king's last order, and their champion Durgan rides a siege drill against you, begging to be stopped. **The turn:** a miner in Amberlee admits the Hollin did this to themselves, on their Thane's word.
- **Act II (the Haar, the High Boughs and the Storm Spires):** In the fog marsh, Lampwick lost its miners to the stillglass digs; they came back as the Drowned Dwarves, and the Lantern Widow carries their souls as lanterns. The three-headed Haar Wyrm coils on the Haar Spring. In Sylvarre the elders are split, and some want the summer to last. Snapcrown, the Devouring Bloom, is the Everbloom's first seedling in the boughs. Atop the Storm Spires, Skyrend the storm roc nests over the Sky Spring. **The turn:** the bell town of Carillon keeps the oldest record of the Withering, and the name of the one who planted the Everbloom.
- **Act III (Vorrhallow and the Evercrown):** The drowned Hollin capital is held still in the moment of its flooding. In the Hall of Still Water, Thane Vorrund, half stillglass, fights with his great-axe and royal guard. Beaten, he finally weeps, and gives up Severance, the only edge that can part the graft from the tree, and the last season seed. At the Crown Gate the stillglass stands up your Likeness: every image of you it has kept since you came in, armed. The four season seeds open the Evercrown, and you fight the Everbloom's bud, its veins, then its heart. In the last stage the Ylden itself, its springs running again, wraps a root around the flower and holds it for your blade.

**Ending.** Severance parts the Everbloom from the tree, and stillglass goes dark across the whole Ylden. For the first time in nine years the leaves fall. Hearthroot holds the Leaffall festival, and after its long autumn the tree flowers from root to crown (the post-game blossom, 4.4). The glass leaf in the stranger's hand is a leaf again.

**Tone.** Melancholy and quiet between fights, fierce in fights. Towns are warm pockets of light. Text is sparse and a little old-fashioned: Ringreaders speak of rings and grain, NPCs give cryptic hints.

**Restoration as feedback.** Each spring you restore visibly changes Hearthroot (the fountain fills by a third, NPCs return, shops stock new items) and changes the world (see section 4.4).

**The glass leaf, read (pass 463).** The first Ringreader the stranger meets reads the leaf before anything else, over his menu: "A leaf of the Ylden, turned to glass. Its grain shows me the Heartwood Spring, stopped by a dam of stillglass. Go down, wanderer. Rest here when you need to." It is said once (flag `leaf_read`, kept in the save), and from then on every Ringreader greets as usual. `test_the_first_ringreader_reads_the_glass_leaf`.

**Ringreaders.** Every town has one. They read the Ylden's growth rings and carve your mark into the wood when you rest (the save). They teach skills, raise your rank, and walk you along the Grain Road (fast travel). Their oldest rings open into the Inner Rings, the trial space (3.2).

---

## 3. World Structure

### 3.1 Overview

The world is one vertical map: **the Ylden**, **126 rooms** across 7 zones plus 2 optional areas (the plan said about 190; see below). The player climbs upward overall, and each zone sprawls horizontally. (The plan said each zone also looped back on earlier zones through shortcuts; as built, the zones meet in one chain, below.)

```
            [7 THE EVERCROWN - the Stillglass Fortress]
                         |  Threshold / Crown Gate
            [6 VORRHALLOW - drowned dwarf capital] ---- (Sunken Spring, optional:
                         |                             Glass Rise / Spring Gate)
                         |  Upper Stair / Sky Spring
            [5 CARILLON and the Canopy Spires]
                         |  Storm Ledge / Crown-Leaf
            [4 HIGH BOUGHS - Sylvarre]
                         |  Windward Climb / Haar Spring
            [3 HAAR - Lampwick and Polesend]
                         |  Fog Cut / Smelter
            [2 HOLLIN TOWER - Kettleforge] . . . (the Kettleforge lift) . . .
                         |  Tower Foot / Heartwood Spring                .
            [1 HEARTWOOD - Amberlee]                                     .
                         |  Hollow Mouth / East Gate                    .
            [0 HEARTHROOT - hub town] . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .
                 (Inner Rings, optional, through the Ringreader)
```

**How the zones meet, as built (pass 387).** The plan's diagram had the Tower meet Polesend, Sylvarre meet the Canopy Spires, and the Spires meet Vorrhallow, and said each zone looped back on earlier ones through shortcuts. The game was built otherwise: the zones meet in a single chain, each pair at exactly one doorway, and no door joins two zones that are not neighbours. Polesend and the Canopy Spires are parts of their zones (the Haar and Carillon), not zones of their own. The ways back that skip the chain are not doors: the Kettleforge lift (the Tower Foot, Kettleforge and a cage at Hearthroot's East Gate, 4.4), the Grain Road between Ringreaders (3.3), and the Ringreader's road to the Trials.

| Zones | Doorway (room / room) |
| --- | --- |
| hearthroot, heartwood | `hearthroot/east_gate` / `heartwood/entrance` |
| heartwood, tower | `heartwood/spring_of_heartwood` / `tower/foot` |
| tower, haar | `tower/smelter` / `haar/fog_cut` |
| haar, boughs | `haar/spring_of_haar` / `boughs/windward_climb` |
| boughs, spires | `boughs/crown_leaf` / `spires/storm_ledge` |
| spires, vorrhallow | `spires/sky_spring` / `vorrhallow/upper_stair` |
| vorrhallow, sunken | `vorrhallow/meteor_rise` / `sunken/spring_gate` |
| vorrhallow, evercrown | `vorrhallow/evercrown_gate` / `evercrown/threshold` |

Inside the zones, only the first two loop: a room there can be reached by more than one way. Heartwood has 8 loops (35 links among 28 rooms) and the Hollin Tower 4 (29 among 26). In Hearthroot and every zone from the Haar up, the rooms form a tree: one way into each, so the way back is the way you came, a lift, or the Grain Road. `test_the_zones_meet_as_the_bible_draws` holds the table and these counts to the rooms' doors.

**No shortcut loops from the Haar up: decided (pass 395).** Pass 387 left open whether the zones that form a tree should gain shortcut loops, as the plan wanted. They will not, for now. A loop pays when the way back is long and dangerous, and three things already keep it short: no room is more than 4 doors from a save (3 where foes hit at double, 3.3), the Grain Road carries you from any Ringreader to any other once a spring runs clean, and the zones are small (4 to 14 rooms). The rooms that touch on the map without a door were looked at, and none makes a good loop. The Polesend and Dry Cistern pair and the Drowned Nave and Drowned Market pair are already 2 doors apart. The Haar Spring sits over the Matriarch's Mire, and both are boss arenas that seal. The Upper Stair sits over the Glass Rise (11 doors apart), but a door there would let the player drop past Thane Vorrund. The cost of a dead end is the walk back to a Ringreader, so that walk is now written down and held. The table gives each zone's room farthest from a Ringreader's room, counted in doors walked (the Kettleforge lift aside). A room that makes a walk longer has to change this table.

| Zone | Farthest from a Ringreader | Doors |
| --- | --- | --- |
| hearthroot | `hearthroot/east_gate` | 3 |
| heartwood | `heartwood/dwarf_larder`, `heartwood/heartwood_rise`, `heartwood/sapwell_deeps`, `heartwood/spring_of_heartwood`, `heartwood/taproot_vault` | 6 |
| tower | `tower/foreman_hall` | 8 |
| haar | `haar/drowned_stair`, `haar/fog_cut` | 5 |
| boughs | `boughs/sunlit_bough`, `boughs/windward_climb` | 4 |
| spires | `spires/sky_spring`, `spires/storm_ledge` | 4 |
| vorrhallow | `vorrhallow/gate_hall` | 6 |
| sunken | `sunken/wellspring` | 8 |
| evercrown | `evercrown/heart` | 13 |

Two long walks are by design. The Tower's Foreman Hall is 8 doors out, but the Tower loops 4 times and the Kettleforge lift runs from its foot. The Wellspring is the end of an optional zone, and the walk back from the Drowned Warden is a victory lap. The Evercrown is the last climb, and there is no going back from the Heart. Vorrhallow's Ringreader stands in the Drowned Market only once Thane Vorrund falls, so before that its rooms count on shrines (3.3). `test_the_walk_back_to_a_ringreader_is_as_3_1_says`.


### 3.2 Zones

| # | Zone | Town | Rooms | Theme and mood | Key mechanic | Bosses |
|---|------|------|-------|----------------|--------------|--------|
| 0 | **Hearthroot** | Hearthroot | 7 (10 planned) | Stone town carved into the roots, warm lamplight, dry fountain | Hub, shops, King, Ringreader, fountain shows progress | none |
| 1 | **Heartwood** | Amberlee | 28 | Tunnels through living wood, glowing fungus, amber sap | Sap pools slow you; root-gates | Gnarlmaw |
| 2 | **Hollin Tower** | Kettleforge | 26 | Dwarven mining tower bolted into the trunk, gears, minecarts | Vertical climb; cracked stone; elevators | Durgan |
| 3 | **The Haar** | Lampwick, Polesend | 14 (34 planned) | Fog-drowned limb-marsh, lanterns, rotting boardwalks | Fog limits vision; lanterns; water levels | Gloamweaver, the Haar Wyrm |
| 4 | **High Boughs** | Sylvarre | 10 (24 planned) | Sunlit canopy, enormous leaves, wind | Wind currents (legend `>`/`<`: carry you while airborne, turned by the Sky Spring, 6.9); bounce-pods (legend `@`: land on one and it throws you about nine tiles up, 6.6) | Snapcrown |
| 5 | **Storm Spires** | Carillon | 9 (26 planned) | Sky islands, storms, ruined elf shrines | Lightning conduits; air dash routes | Skyrend |
| 6 | **Vorrhallow** | Vorrhallow | 14 (28 planned) | Flooded dwarf capital, stillglass growing through masonry | Time-stop puzzles; illusory walls | Thane Vorrund, the Likeness |
| 7 | **The Evercrown** | none | 8 (14 planned) | Inside the Everbloom: organic stone, alien light, gravity wrong | Gauntlet using every ability | The Everbloom |
| opt | **Sunken Spring** | none | 4 (8 planned) | Under Vorrhallow, first source of the springs | Underwater swimming (Torrent-drained rooms) | Optional: The Drowned Warden |
| opt | **Inner Rings** | Hearthroot | 6 | Abstract inner rings, monochrome | Pure platforming challenges | none |

**As built (pass 323).** The Rooms column was the plan's until pass 323, and the overview promised about 190 rooms. The game has 126 room files (the gym aside). The Heartwood, the Tower and the Trials were built as planned. The zones after them came out at a third to two thirds of the plan, in rooms of about the same size (1,200 to 1,900 tiles on average, the Evercrown and the Sunken Spring the largest). Each still holds its towns, its guardians and its ability gates (4.1, 4.2), so what fell short was the filler between them, not a beat of the critical path. Where a zone fell short, the column says so, for example "14 (34 planned)". `test_the_bible_counts_each_zones_rooms` holds each count and the total to `levels/`. A later pass that adds rooms changes the count with them.

**Guardians and prizes.** Every guardian, its arena, its lore and its prize are in section 8 and `BossArena.BOSSES`. (Until pass 461 a ledger here split the zones and guardians into those kept from the source game and those the remake added. The game is all its own now, so the ledger went with the old names.)

**Inner Rings (pass 113, the first two of six; the third in pass 155, the fourth in 160, the fifth in 162, the sixth and last in 168):** `levels/trials/`, the inner rings, is off the map and out of the completion count (`World.OFF_MAP`). The Ringreader in the Hall of Rings gains a "Inner Rings" row (cleared/built). It is dry until a spring flows, and its list shows each built trial as Sealed (with the springs it needs), Open, or your best time. Trials open two by two, one pair per spring restored (`Trials.LIST`); a trial whose room isn't built isn't offered. Choosing one carries you to its start (`P`), and the clock runs from that moment: the trial's name, the clock and your best (or "First walk") sit top center on the HUD. In a trial a hazard costs time, never health: it sets you back on your last footing with a hurt flash. The bell at the far end (`prop: x, y, trial_goal`) rings when touched. It shows your time, keeps your best (`GameState.data.trials`, trial id -> seconds), and the first clear rings a Grain Shard out of it (bible 10). After 1.2 s the ring carries you back to where you stood by the Ringreader. The pause menu gains "Leave the trial", which stops the clock and returns you there uncleared. Look: pale grey masonry with no moss or warmth, a void behind with great faint broken ring rings and pale slabs hanging nearer, pale flecks in the air; the bell hangs in a stone frame with a slow glow. Sound (pass 116): music `trials` (D lydian at 92 over a drone that never changes chord, a dry tick on every eighth like the trial's clock, a soft pulse to run to, and a bell chanting a four-bar ring above), ambience `amb_trials` (a hollow void hum that breathes, thin air, far chimes of the ring rings), `trial_begin` as the ring carries you in (a shimmer rising a fifth), `trial_bell` at the end (a struck bronze bell with inharmonic partials ringing long), and `trial_record` on a new best (three bright rising notes). Built: **Ring of Walls** (three pillars over spikes, thorned on their far sides and with thorn bulbs in the gaps; climb each with the Climbing Glove from the nub at its foot) and **Ring of Edges** (1- and 2-wide pillars over a spiked floor, four tiles apart and at changing heights, then four slats under a low ceiling that allows only short hops, then rising pillars to the bell). Both are proved clearable with the glove alone by `test_every_ringreader_trial_can_be_cleared_with_the_glove`. Planned: Thorns and Winds (two springs), Stillness and Falling (three); built since: Thorns (155), Winds (160) and Stillness (162). The cosmetics the bible pairs with trials (cloak dyes) came in pass 164: see **Ring dyes** in 10. **The Ring of Thorns (pass 155), the third:** opened by the second spring, and built for the Glove and the Delver's Pick (`Trials.LIST` gains `needs`, the abilities a course is built for; the reachability test checks each trial with exactly those). A pogo course 96 tiles long: a 20-tile corridor of spikes from the start ledge (pogo along it), a pit under a chain of five thorn bulbs four tiles apart (the bulbs are the fast way; fall in and you pogo the spikes to the far wall and climb it), then two bulbs rising to a slab that hangs from the east wall over more spikes, with the bell on it. Nothing climbs to the slab, so the last two bulbs are the only way up; without the Delver's Pick the bell can't be reached. A simple pogo-playing hand (walk right, jump off edges, steer over the next bulb, strike down while falling) clears it in the real game in about 12 s. **The Ring of Winds (pass 160), the fourth:** opened with Thorns by the second spring, and built for the Glove and the Featherstep Boots. It teaches the High Boughs' wind currents (6.9) a zone ahead, over spikes. The course is 100 tiles long:

1. Jump from the start ledge into a current that carries you over a 25-tile spike pit to a pillar.
2. Climb the wall that rises beside the pillar.
3. Leap off the wall's top into a high current that drops you on a second pillar.
4. Make a jump of five rows, too high without the Boots, up to a hanging plank.
5. Step into the last current, and hold Down to let go over the bell's slab. Ride it on and it throws you onto spikes.

The inner rings has no weather, so its winds never turn with the Sky Spring (`WindCurrent.dir`, and `Reach.wind_dir` for `trials/` rooms), and pale flecks ride them instead of leaves. The checker clears it with the Glove and Boots, before and after the Sky Spring. It cannot clear it without the Boots, or with the winds taken out even with the Stride. In the real game, the first current carries a jump from the ledge to the pillar. The Ringreader now also keeps a trial sealed until you hold what it is built for (`Trials.missing`, `Trials.ready`): the springs can flow before the Featherstep Boots are found, and then the row says "Its footholds want the Featherstep Boots."

**The Ring of Stillness (pass 162), the fifth:** opened by the third spring, and built for the Glove and the Stillstone. Time-locked water (`f`, 4.5) is the whole course, and the waits between turns of the glass (2.5 s still, 8 s to run back) are part of the ring: be still. `levels/trials/stillness.room` (72 x 56):

1. From the start, still the fall at the end of the hall and climb its four footholds to a ledge.
2. A curtain of water hangs from the void over a spiked pit. Still it, jump onto its footholds, climb three of them, and jump to the far ledge. Flowing, it beats you down onto the spikes.
3. Climb a long fall of seven footholds. A dry niche halfway lets you step out and wait for the glass to run back. A quick climber can skip it.
4. Reach the bell at the end of the top ledge.

The checker reaches the bell with the Glove and the Stillstone, and the niche on the way. Without the glass, it can't reach even the first ledge, even with the Boots, the Stride and the Delver's Pick. In the real game, short hops climb the first fall within one turn of the glass. **Stilled water is never safe footing:** a foothold of frozen water melts when time runs on, so standing on one no longer sets the place a hazard puts you back (`Player._on_stilled_water`). Until pass 162 a fall onto spikes after the curtain thawed could set you back into the flowing water, which carried you down again. Planned: the Ring of Falling (three springs).

**The Ring of Falling (pass 168), the sixth and last:** opened by the third spring with Stillness, and built for the Glove alone: the one trial about letting go. `levels/trials/falling.room` (40 x 90) is a descent, start at the top, bell at the bottom:
1. **The steps:** five three-tile ledges fall away under a 38-tile spike floor, each four or five rows below the last and three or four columns over, east, west, east. Walk off and steer: there is no climbing back.
2. **The well:** off the last ledge, a gap in the spike floor drops you into a well crossed by three planks. Drop through each (Down + Jump).
3. **The needle:** walk west off the well's floor into a nine-tile shaft 28 rows deep. Thorn bulbs stand in it every four rows, left of center, then right, so only a fall held to the middle column comes through clean. The bell stands on a three-tile slab at the bottom, with spikes either side.

The checker falls to the bell with the Glove. In the real game, a bot that only steers its falls and drops through the planks rings the bell in about 7.2 s with no set-back. The ring time is 10 s, so a clean run has to fall without stopping on each ledge. The dye is Plummet Umber. All six of the trials bible 3.1 plans are now built.

**Vorrhallow begins (pass 63):** `levels/vorrhallow/`, below Vorrhallow's upper gate at the end of Carillon (the Sky Key opens it; a shaft past it drops into the zone). The Upper Stair (60,-11, 40x45) is the dwarf kings' old trade stair: nine landings switch back down a drowned colonnade to the Upper Gate-Hall (59,-10), with broken pillars, a shrine and the west way choked with stone and crystal for now. Look: squat dark dwarf masonry with a teal tide line on every block where the flood stood, veins of magenta stillglass forcing through the joints of exposed faces, teal waterweed for a cap; behind, a sunken great hall of colossal pillars and arches with crystal clusters glowing on the far walls and dark water below, and nearer, broken colonnades run through with crystal spears. Decor: crystal shards, drowned dwarf helms, fallen masonry, crystal stalactites, seeping drips, wall crystals and dead braziers. Crystal dust hangs in the air. Music `vorrhallow` (D phrygian at 70: a dirge of deep hall pads, an octave bass, a low tolling bell and far-off crystal chimes) and ambience `amb_vorrhallow` (a still water drone, drops into pools, a trickle, a faint crystal hum). Pass 64 opened the Gate-Hall's west way onto the Flooded Hall (57,-10, 60x23), the old hall of audience: broken pillars, a crystal-spiked pit, a gallery with a chest, and two Crystal Dwarves. Pass 65 opened its west wall onto the Sunken Stair (56,-10, 40x45), five landings down a drowned colonnade over a crystal-spiked trench, and below it the Seer's Crypt (55,-9), the masons' tomb, where the Seer's Lens lies on a dais. Both rooms hide a secret behind an illusory wall up a run of hidden planks, so the Lens pays off at once on the way back. In pass 66 the way on became the main-route illusory wall: the crypt's hidden chamber hides its west door to the Drowned Nave (53,-9, 80x23), where the king's guard knelt. It has broken hanging pillars, two galleries, a spiked pit, two Shade Knights and a Crystal Dwarf, and a Grain Shard up hidden planks under the vault. In pass 67 the Stillstone came to its west end: it stands on a plinth by the stopped water-clock with a shrine beside it, and the west door opens on the Waterfall Stair (52,-10, 40x45), two shafts of falling water that only a frozen moment lets you climb, to a gallery with a Life Fruit and a chest. In pass 69 the gallery's east door opened on the Drowned Market (53,-10, 80x23), where the lower city sold its ore: Stillglass Slugs creep its floor, a fall of time-locked water pours into a spiked pit across the hall (frozen, its lowest foothold lies flush with the floor, so the glass is the bridge), and past it a Crystal Dwarf guards the stair down to the king's halls. In pass 70 that stair's last stone cracked: a doorway at the market's east end leads down the Thane's Stair (55,-8, 40x45), where the king's fall pours down the stair-well. The upper flight's only way down is through the water, and the only way back up is a frozen climb with a dry niche halfway to wait out the glass. At its foot, by a shrine, the Thane's Kennels (56,-7, 80x23) hold three Clockwork Hounds among their stalls, a spiked drain, and the kennel-master's loft up hidden planks with a chest. Their east end opens on Thane Vorrund's Throne Hall (58,-7, 60x23, pass 71), a bare hall with his dais at the east end. Since pass 241 the hall is titled **Hall of Still Water**, the source game's name for the castle where the dwarf king kept the Severance and the Frost Seed; the Thane's Stair's sign says it goes down to the Hall of Still Water, and the kennels' sign calls the throne its heart. In pass 169 a plank lid over the kennels' old spiked drain became a shaft down to Alpha's Den (57,-6), where the Clockwork Alpha waits (section 8).

**Carillon as built (pass 54):** `levels/spires/`, entered by the Crown-Leaf's east door. Storm Ledge (a fallen causeway span over thorns: pogo it or stride it), Sky Steps (one-way island shelves stacked up a tall room, with storm crystals bristling on the cliffs so nothing can be climbed around them; an elven veil before the exit ledge makes the Stride the way out, pass 227) **The Sky Steps' gap at every game speed (pass 473):** the exit had only been crossed by a scripted intent at full speed. `test_the_sky_steps_gap_is_crossed_with_the_buttons_at_every_game_speed` holds Right, presses Jump at the island's edge and Dash at the jump's top, all for real, at 100, 90, 80 and 70% game speed. The hero lands on the exit ledge each time, and without the Stride the same presses never do. The veil, not the gap's width, is the gate: a dash under half its length (0.07 of 0.15 s) still crosses, so no game speed can close the way out and the Broken Shrine (a shrine, a Life Fruit, and the fallen bridge to Carillon town). Look: pale ashlar shrine stone with gold inlay, a violet storm sky with lightning and drifting islands, rain driven on the wind. Music `spires` (E dorian at 116, thunder toms) and ambience `amb_spires` (gusting wind, rain, rolling thunder).

**Carillon town (pass 57):** the Broken Shrine's east door opens on the Fallen Bridge (52,-11), the great elf bridge broken into floating spans a stride apart, with hornets in the wind and a beetle on the old footing below (a fall walks you back to the near pier; storm crystals on the far pier stop a climb round). Past it, Carillon (54,-11), the bell town on the last great island: a Ringreader, Calder the Armorer (Knight's Plate), Scribe Ysolde (the Kindle scroll), Grocer Wenna, Bell-Ringer Tamsin on the bell loft gallery (the Carillon chart), the Mayor (who names Skyrend) and the old Bellkeeper (who points at the bramble-choked bell cellar, where a Potion Shard waits for Kindle). Music `spires_town` (G mixolydian at 92, a carillon over warm pads). The Spires beyond, with Skyrend and the Stormcall spell, come next.

**The Spires begin (pass 58):** a passage through the town's bell tower (from the bell-loft gallery) leads to the Spire Foot (56,-11), the old lift-house, where the Stormcall scroll lies on the conductor's lectern and one dead conduit stands before a conduit gate. Past it the Spire Stair (57,-12, 40x45) climbs old landings to a silver hatch that opens only when both its conduits wake (one on the floor, one on a landing), and above it to a chest on the top landing below the roost. The Storm Harpy came in pass 59. In pass 60 the top landing's east door opens on the Roost (58,-12), Skyrend's arena (8.6). In pass 62 the Roost's east door, sealed with the arena until the roc falls, opens on the Sky Spring (60,-12): the spring on a dais, and past it Vorrhallow's upper gate (legend `g`), shrine stone with a gold lightning glyph that only the Sky Key opens. The way down to Vorrhallow beyond it comes with that zone.
**The Sunken Spring begins (pass 86):** `levels/sunken/`, the optional zone under Vorrhallow. The way in is the last stone of the Glass Rise: three cracked stones in the floor at its foot (the Delver's Pick breaks them) over a shaft that is still full of water, so it is the zone's first swim (bible 6.8). The shaft drops into the Spring Gate (60,-6, 40x45), a drowned cistern of pale limestone with two Spring Eels, a chest and a potion shard on the bottom, and a dome of trapped air whose shelf holds a shrine. Let go of the controls in the shaft and the water floats you back up into the Rise. Its east door, on the bottom, opens on the Drowned Aqueduct (61,-5, 80x23), the dwarves' old culvert up to their city: the water stands at row 9 under four broken arch tops you can climb out onto, two walls hang down into the water so you swim under them, three eels hunt it, a Grain Shard sits on an arch and a dry gallery at the east end holds a chest. Under the water at its east end the Source Hall (63,-5) is a black pool beside a dry shore with a Shade Knight, a Life Fruit on a shelf, and a sign: the Source lies deeper still, and the way to the Drowned Warden (8.10) opens in a later pass. Look: limestone laid down by the spring in wavy strata like the rims of terraced pools, pocked, with a blue-green mineral stain; behind, a flooded cavern of stepped pools under slanting light, and nearer the aqueduct's arches on their piers with weed swaying up from the bottom. Decor: rimstone dams, spring pearls, waterweed, limestone straws, seeps of the spring's light and flowstone. Specks of light rise through the water. Music `sunken` (E dorian at 64: tidal pads, a pluck rippling through each chord like light on water seen from below, a far bell) and ambience `amb_sunken` (a deep pressure hum, a current washing through stone, bursts of bubbles).

**Lampwick (pass 145):** the first of the Haar's two towns, and the first town of the source game's eight that the remake had left out. `levels/haar/lampwick.room` (25,-8, 80x23), west of the Feather Hollow: a shelf at the Hollow's west wall leads in, so the town is reached with the Lantern Key, through the room where the Featherstep Boots wait. Lampwick is the lamp town on the boardwalks, built before the fog and never left. Its miners went down the drowned stair to dig stillglass stone for the dwarf king's gold and came back as the Drowned Dwarves of the mire (bible 7). There it has:
- a Ringreader (save, skills and a Grain Road stop);
- Keymaster Odo (Copper 150 g and Silver 400 g keys, ointment and elixirs; bible 10.2 names Lampwick as a key master's town);
- Fogwalker Bram on a plank loft, selling the Haar chart (500 g);
- Marta, a miner's widow, and Lamplighter Nell, who both have new lines once the Tree blooms (4.4).

The fog lies over the town at 0.6 (0.88 out in the marsh). It is lit by four lamp posts (prop `lamp`, `TownLamp`: a lantern on an iron arm whose flame clears an 84 px pocket of fog), so the street is a string of warm pockets with dark between. Its houses are boardwalk houses (`HouseFront` in the `haar` style: reed-grey planks, a damp dark frame) whose windows burn from the first visit, unlike Amberlee's, which wait for their people. Music `lampwick` is the Haar's B minor waltz come in out of the cold: D major in 3/4 at 84, the same music-box pluck on two and three but dry and near, warm pads, a walking bass and a lamp-bell on every other downbeat. The bed stays the Haar's. **Polesend**, the second Haar town, came in pass 148 (below).

**Polesend (pass 148):** the Haar's second town and the last of the source game's eight to be built. `levels/haar/polesend.room` (33,-6, 80x23) lies under the Scorched Hall and the Dry Cistern. A shaft in the Scorched Hall's floor, beside the sign that always said "The Polesend road runs on east under black water", drops down a ladder onto the old barge road. East of the landing the road is drowned: a 20-tile dip of black water over spikes, with a row of drowned boardwalks (legend `b`) that rise when the Haar Spring flows (4.4), as on the Fog Cut and the Lampwick Causeway. Before the spring, a skilled player can pogo along the spikes with the Delver's Pick and climb the far side with the Glove; after it the town is a walk. The ladder climbs back out. Past the water is the stilt town where the marsh-folk stayed and fished:
- a Ringreader (save, skills, a Grain Road stop);
- Scribe Orla, who sells the Kindle scroll (1,200 g), as 10.2 planned, a zone before Carillon's scribe sells it too;
- Eelwife Brannagh's stores (ointment, elixir, Copper Keys);
- Old Teague the Poler, who poled the dwarf king's ore barges down this road, and has post-game lines.

Like Lampwick, it has boardwalk houses lit from the start, three lamp posts, and fog at 0.6. Music `polesend` is a poler's song: E dorian in 3/4 at 72, a low plucked line over an open-fifth drone that never moves, a soft drum stroke on each downbeat like a pole set in the mud, and a lead that dips and rises like a barge. With Polesend, seven of the source game's eight towns stand with a Ringreader and shops: Hearthroot, Amberlee, Kettleforge, Lampwick, Polesend, Sylvarre and Carillon. The eighth, Vorrhallow, is the drowned city itself; its steward and mason come back once Thane Vorrund falls. **Vorrhallow wakes (pass 159):** in the source game, Vorrhallow is the last town, with a Ringreader and a key shop before the final climb, and 10.2 names it a key master's town. Once Thane Vorrund falls, the Drowned Market is a town again. Its slugs and crystal dwarf are gone (room header `calm: boss:thane_vorrund`: once the flag is set the room spawns no foes). A Ringreader stands there, the last before the Evercrown, and the Grain Road can reach him. Keymaster Sigrun, a dwarf, keeps "Sigrun's Locks": Copper, Silver, Gold and Thorn keys, Barkbalm and Amber Draughts, but never the Glass, which still lies on the stall-plank above. With Vorrhallow, all eight of the source game's towns stand with a Ringreader and a shop. Since pass 179 the east end of the boardwalk (door 2) opens on the Matriarch's Mire, where the Marsh Hag Matriarch waits (section 8).

### 3.3 Room and map rules

- **Room size:** multiples of one screen (640x360 px internal = 40x22.5 tiles of 16 px). Rooms are 1x1 up to 4x3 screens. **As built (pass 331):** every room on the map is at least one screen (40x23 tiles) and no more than 4x3 screens (160x68). Most are whole screens (40x23, 80x23, 40x45, 40x68, 120x23, 80x45), but 14 are a screen and a half wide (60 tiles: arenas and halls such as the Crown-Leaf, the Throne Hall and the Evercrown's last rooms, and the Roost, 60x29) and the Heart Gate is 56; the camera stops at a room's edges, so a part screen only means the camera travels less. The Inner Rings are off the map and keep their own shapes (96x20, 72x56, 40x90 and others): two are shorter than a screen, and the inner rings's void fills the view below them. `test_every_room_keeps_the_bibles_size` holds the bounds.
- **Camera:** room-bounded; camera locks to room edges; transitions are seamless scrolls (no fade) within a zone and a brief fade between zones. The scroll is a screen push: the old view slides out the way the player went while the new room slides in (0.34 s). Inner doorways (Up to enter) fade, since there is no direction to scroll. Coming up a shaft you arrive inside the floor door of the room above, still rising; fall back before you clear it and that door takes you back down (pass 146), instead of letting you drop out of the room. Through any door, the arrival is the new room's first safe spot (pass 151): a hazard met before the first landing, such as spikes under a drop, sets you back there, never at the last room's footing. Every floor and ceiling door ends on footing (pass 299): down one, you fall onto tiles with no hazard beside them, which the set-back keeps; up one, a steer to either side lands you beside the floor door instead of dropping back in, even a steer begun a quarter second after you arrive (pass 304: a player's time to see the new room and react; the Old Flue, the tightest, fails at 0.35 s). The rise (`World.RISE_SPEED`, 430 px/s) has room to spare: the Alpha Den, the tightest ledge, still lands at 370 and fails at 340. Played with no abilities, so neither the Glove nor the Boots is needed for it. A gate under a ceiling door (the flue damper, bound fog) shuts only the way up; you can't come down to it while it is shut. **Doors by the buttons (pass 411):** every door test drove a scripted intent, which lasts one step, but a real Up is held for many steps. `PlayerInput` counts a held Up as one press, so a hero who arrives through an inner door with Up still down stays in the new room. `test_the_doors_answer_the_real_controller` plays the doors with the real controller. Holding Right walks through a side door and holding Left walks back. Standing in an inner doorway does nothing, and Up goes through. Up still held does not go back out, and letting go and pressing again does. **A door gives back the air moves (pass 448):** every door hands the hero their double jump and air dash back, as landing does (`Player.teleport`, which each arrival goes through). A climb up a shaft that spends both on the way comes up through the next room's floor door with both again, so a shaft's rooms are each climbed fresh. A side door's arrival stands at the door's sill, so there it changes little. `test_a_door_gives_back_the_air_moves` rises through a ceiling door with both spent, and in the room above the real Jump double jumps and the real Dash air dashes. **In real time (pass 477):** the fade and the scroll were tweens on game time, so at 70% Game speed (14) a door took 1.4 times as long, and walking out of an arena in a guardian's slow motion after its death blow (30%, 8) nearly three times. Both now ignore the time scale (`World._fade`, `World._scroll`), as the music does (13.3, pass 476). `test_a_door_fades_in_real_time_at_every_game_speed` takes the East Gate's door at 100, 70 and 30%, and each fade ends within four frames of its 0.3 s (18 frames).
- **Save points:** each town has a Ringreader (save, respec, rank-up). Plus 2 to 3 **Meditation Shrines** per zone (save only). No room is more than 4 rooms from a save or a shortcut back to one. **As built (pass 331):** the distance rule decided where shrines went, not the count, so the count follows each zone's size and its towns: Heartwood 4, Hollin Tower 5, The Haar 2 (with the Ringreaders of Lampwick and Polesend), High Boughs 2, Storm Spires 2 (with Carillon's Ringreader), Vorrhallow 5, Sunken Spring 2 (four rooms), The Evercrown 3, and Hearthroot none (its Ringreader). `test_every_zone_has_the_shrines_the_bible_counts` holds these to `levels/`. Held by `test_no_room_is_far_from_a_rest` since pass 195, counting doors walked to the nearest Meditation Shrine or Ringreader; where foes hit at double (Vorrhallow, the Sunken Spring and the Evercrown, 5.5) the limit is 3. The test found one room over the limit: the Bell Loft at the top of the Tower, a dead end five doors from the nearest rest (the Mine Vault's shrine, by the Crown Lookout, the Rafters, the Mine Shaft and the Rail Gallery, or Kettleforge's Ringreader by the Mine Mouth), with Gear Bats roosting the whole way. The Crown Lookout now has a shrine of its own, by the sign where the dwarves watched for the Haar, so the climb to the tower's crown ends somewhere to rest.
- **Fast travel:** unlocked after the first spring: Ringreader-to-Ringreader travel through the "Grain Road" menu. Speaking to a Ringreader marks that Ringreader as a stop; the Ringreader's menu lists every other stop you have met, and choosing one fades you to a step beside that Ringreader. Before any spring flows the row is shown but dry, so players learn it exists. **Walked with the buttons (pass 451):** `test_the_grain_road_is_walked_with_the_buttons` stands at Kettleforge's Ringreader with the Heartwood Spring flowing and presses for real: Up opens his menu, Down walks to the Grain Road, Jump opens it and Jump on Amberlee carries you there. Play goes on unpaused with the menu shut, the hero stands on the floor a step from Amberlee's Ringreader (the Jump that chose the stop is not taken as a jump), and the real Right walks him on at once.
- **Map:** auto-maps visited rooms. A cartographer in six of the eight towns sells each zone's outline (empty rooms shown dimmed). Map pins (custom markers) available from the start: with the map open, Jump drops a pin where you stand or lifts the one there, up to 12. Cartographers so far: Pell in the Hearthroot market (Hearthroot 40 g, Heartwood 250 g), Surveyor Brin in Kettleforge (Tower 400 g), and Skywatcher Orin in Sylvarre (Haar 500 g, High Boughs 650 g), Fogwalker Bram in Lampwick (Haar 500 g, pass 145), and the bell-ringer in Carillon (Carillon 700 g). **Sounder Hulda (pass 261):** Vorrhallow and the Sunken Spring under it had no chart, the two zones with the most hidden rooms. Hulda, a dwarf who swam the drowned halls by memory for forty years, stands in the Drowned Market once Thane Vorrund is at rest and the water is down. She sells Vorrhallow (750 g) and the Sunken Spring (800 g). Every zone the map draws now has a chart for sale, all but the Evercrown, the Everbloom no one has drawn. `test_every_zone_on_the_map_has_a_chart_for_sale`. Doors, gates and items get icons once seen. Built: shrines, key doors still shut (pass 127, see 4.5) and Treasure Sense's star (pass 100). **Every rest on the map (pass 403):** the map and the minimap marked each Meditation Shrine but no Ringreader, though a town's Ringreader rests and saves as a shrine does. A player looking for the nearest rest saw none in Hearthroot, Amberlee or any town. Now a Ringreader's cell is listed with the shrines (`World.build_catalog`) and drawn with the same mark, which the legend now calls "Rest", so every place to rest and save shows. `test_the_map_marks_every_rest`. The charts' prices here are the shops' own (`data/shops.json`), held by `test_the_bibles_chart_prices_are_the_shops` (pass 347), which also counts the towns with a cartographer.
- **The builder's notes (pass 355):** `levels/README.md` is how a room is written: its legend, doors, map placement and the layout rules of thumb. Two of its claims had gone unchecked. It still said rooms were whole screens, though pass 331 found fourteen a screen and a half wide and the Heart Gate 56 tiles; it now says what this section does. Its safe jumps for layouts (a standing jump clears 4 tiles up with 2 across, 3 up with 4 across, or 2 up with 5 across, with 3 tiles of headroom, so a platform reachable from the floor goes 4 rows above it) had never been made. `test_the_readmes_safe_jumps_are_real` reads them from the README and makes each one from a standstill with the real controller, under a ceiling at the README's headroom. **The legend, held both ways (pass 363):** the README's legend is the key to every room's grid, and nothing held it either. The Everbloom's light veins (`x`, the Veins, 8) had stood in a room since pass 82 with no legend row, so a builder reading the README could not have known the character was taken. The README now has the row. `test_the_readme_legend_matches_the_rooms` checks both ways: every character any room's grid uses has a legend row, and every legend row is a character the loader places.
- **Landmarks:** every zone has at least 3 visually unique landmark rooms (a giant statue, a waterfall, a crashed airship-dwarf barge) so players can navigate by memory. The Tower's are the great winch in Winch Hall (still until Kettleforge is freed, then turning), the Kettleforge forge, and the Smelter's hanging crucible pouring molten ore. **Every zone's three (pass 371):** the line held for the Tower alone, and nothing checked it. Each zone's three, by room and what marks it: Hearthroot, the Fountain Plaza's fountain, the Reeve's Hall with the King on his throne, and the East Gate's lift; Heartwood, the Taproot Vault's gnawed taproot, the Heartwood Spring, and the town of Amberlee; The Haar, the Haar Spring, the boardwalk town of Lampwick, and the Sunken Chapel where Gloamweaver waits; High Boughs, the Crown-Leaf (Snapcrown), the Thorn Yard (the Bark Knight Captain) and the town of Sylvarre; Carillon, the Sky Spring, the Roost (Skyrend) and the town of Carillon; Vorrhallow, the Hall of Still Water's water clock, the Crown Gate, and the Alpha's Den; the Evercrown, the Heart of the Bloom, the Throne of the Evercrown and the Veins, each a guardian's own arena. The Sunken Spring had one, the Wellspring's arena, and two rooms of plain flooded stone. It now has two set pieces, drawn in code like the rest: in Spring Gate a dwarf king carved in stone, taller than a house, stands drowned at the foot of the shaft with his hammer grounded, weed swaying from his crown and a bead of air slipping from his lips (`drowned_statue`); in the Drowned Aqueduct the dwarves' channel ends snapped off under the ceiling and still pours the spring into the flood in a white fall (`aqueduct_spill`). These are the giant statue and the waterfall this line always named. `test_every_zone_has_its_three_landmarks` holds each room to its zone and its mark, and holds this paragraph to the rooms' names.

---

## 4. Progression

### 4.1 Abilities (the gates)

Abilities are permanent and listed in acquisition order. Each one opens new routes in every earlier zone.

| # | Ability | Root | What it does | Gate it opens | Found in |
|---|---------|---------------|--------------|---------------|----------|
| A0 | **Wanderer's Kit** (start) | (original hero) | Run, jump, variable jump, slash, up-slash, Sapflask flask | none | Start |
| A1 | **Climbing Glove** | Glove | Wall slide and wall jump | Tall shafts, narrow chimneys | Heartwood, mid-zone shrine |
| A2 | **Delver's Pick** | Delver's Pick | Down-strike in the air: **pogo** off enemies, spikes and thorn bulbs; breaks cracked stone | Cracked walls and floors, thorn-bulb chains, spike corridors | Hollin Tower, after a mini-boss |
| A3 | **Featherstep Boots** | Featherstep Boots | **Double jump** (feathers burst), refreshed by pogo and wall touch | Wide gaps, high ledges; on the critical path, the Elven Stride hung in the Windloft's open air (pass 232) | The Haar, Lampwick side: on a perch in Feather Hollow (27,-8), west of the Lampwick Causeway |
| A4 | **Torrent** (spell) | Torrent | Water bolt: puts out flame barriers, fills basins (floating platforms rise) | Fire seals, dry basins | The Haar, Polesend side: a drowned scribe's scroll on the Marsh Road (32,-7), east of the Sunken Chapel |
| A5 | **Elven Stride** | (new, elven) | **Air dash** with brief invulnerability; passes through mist curtains | Mist curtains, long horizontal gaps, wind walls | High Boughs: the Windloft shrine (46,-10), up Sylvarre's north bough; a warden's cache behind an elven veil in the North Bough is the first stride gate (pass 216, see below); since pass 388 a veil also bars the Windloft's east door, so Snapcrown's Crown-Leaf and the way on to Carillon need it (8) |
| A6 | **Stormcall** (spell) | Stormcall | Bolt that arcs to conductors; powers elevators and doors | Dead conduits | Carillon: the Spire Foot (56,-11), through the bell tower |
| A7 | **Seer's Lens** | Seer's Lens | Passive: illusory walls shimmer; hidden platforms faintly visible | Illusory walls (secrets and one main route in Vorrhallow) | Vorrhallow, early |
| A8 | **Stillstone** | Stillstone | Stops time for 2.5 s (cooldown 8 s): freezes falling water, crushers, platforms, projectiles | Time puzzles, waterfall stairs | Vorrhallow, mid-zone |

**Stillstone as built (pass 67):** its own button, Stillstone (T or E; left stick click). No MP. Time stops for 2.5 s, and the glass runs back for 8 s from when it was turned. The world drains to a cold blue while it holds. Enemy projectiles (feathers, gears, globs, seeds, rubble, spikes, shockwaves, storm bolts, since pass 120 the Marsh Hag's mire hand, mark or risen, and since pass 228 Gnarlmaw's sap, the Haar Wyrm's mist breath and a Bog Puffcap's spores) hang where they are and harm no one until time runs again, and time-locked water freezes into footholds. You and the enemies themselves go on, except the Clockwork Hounds (pass 70), which are clockwork and stop dead: it is a tool for puzzles and for slipping through a volley, not a pause button. Its last three quarter-seconds tick, and time running again rushes (pass 230; see the sound notes in 13.3). One rule holds it (pass 228): anything a foe looses that can strike you stops with the glass, whether it flies, falls, rolls or hangs in the air, and only the foes' own bodies go on. Until then the sap Gnarlmaw shakes down, the Haar Wyrm's mist breath and a Puffcap's spores kept dripping, rolling and stinging in stopped time. `test_the_stillstone_holds_every_loosed_hazard` checks the three and reads every script under `scripts/bosses/` and `scripts/enemies/` for any other hazard that ignores the glass.

**The Stillstone (deviation, pass 337):** the source game's hourglass stopped foes for a fixed time. The Stillstone stops what foes loose and the time-locked water, and only clockwork stops dead: the Clockwork Hounds and their Alpha. Every other foe goes on. A glass that froze every foe would be a pause button on a 2.5 s timer, turned every 8 s, and it would empty every fight in Vorrhallow and the Evercrown, where it is found and where the hardest foes are. Holding what foes throw keeps its oldest use, a safe moment in a fight, while the fight itself goes on. Freezing the water makes it the key to the drowned city's climbs, and its time stays fixed. `test_the_stillstone_stops_clockwork_and_lets_other_foes_go_on` stops time over a Rot Dwarf and a hound: the dwarf walks on, the hound does not.
| A9 | **Elf, Ruby, Hollin and Demon Rings** | Rings | Story keys: the four rings open the gate of the Evercrown | The Crown Gate | Gnarlmaw, Durgan, the Haar Wyrm, Thane Vorrund |
| A10 | **Severance** | Severance | The only blade that can hurt the Everbloom's core | Final boss | Thane Vorrund gives it up (Vorrhallow), as in the source game: he swallowed it (pass 217; it lay on an Evercrown altar before) |

Supporting spells (optional, combat-focused, found or bought): **Kindle** (arcing fireball, burns bramble walls for secrets), **Wisplight** (homing spirit orb; built pass 95: 22 MP, 14 damage. It leaves the hand drifting up and forward and hangs 0.15 s, pulsing, then locks on the nearest foe within 240 px, a foe behind counting as twice as far, and flies at 230 px/s, turning at most 6 rad/s, so a quick foe can slip it; walls stop it; 2,500 g at the Carillon scriptorium), **Wither** (screen-wide, very expensive, late secret; built pass 132: 40 MP of a base 60, so one cast from a full flask. A ring of black light rolls out from you to 300 px over 0.45 s, through walls, and every foe it passes takes one blow of 36 that no shield faces; bosses take half, 18. It tolls a deep knell (`death_wave`). The scroll (legend `{`) lies in the Thane's Kennels in Vorrhallow, beside the kennel-master's chest on the loft that only hidden planks reach. It is last on the Next Spell cycle. Deep Wither, the Arcana node after Time Thief (pass 134, 3 points), halves its cost: 20 MP, 17 with Thrift).

**Kindle as built (pass 57):** sold by Carillon's scribe for 1,200 g (the Polesend shop comes with Polesend). 20 MP; the ball is thrown forward and up (170 px/s across, 150 up, falling at 520 px/s²), so it lands about seven tiles ahead and arcs over a low foe's head at point-blank range. It bursts on a wall, floor or foe, scorching every foe within 20 px for 18 and lighting every bramble in reach. It lights the fog as it flies. The Next Spell button (G or O; right stick click) cycles the spells you own, and the HUD's magic-bar gem shows the equipped spell's color. The save keeps the choice.

**The spells' power (deviation, pass 329):** each spell is a tool with its own job, not a rung on one ladder of power. Kindle strikes for 18 against Torrent's 12, Stormcall for 16, Wither for 36 (18 on a guardian) and Wisplight for 14. Torrent and Stormcall open gates (basins, fire seals, conduits), Stormcall chains between foes, Kindle arcs over cover and burns brambles, and Wisplight homes on a foe you cannot reach. Their price is their MP, and the shared 0.35 s cast cooldown keeps a stronger spell from replacing the blade. Wither hits hardest, and Wisplight's homing is its strength.

**The source game's spent tools, kept for good (deviation, pass 353):** in the source game, the Featherstep Boots and the Delver's Pick were used up. The Boots let you fly for a time that was shorter the higher your rank, and then they were gone. Each Delver's Pick broke one blocked passage and was spent. The remake makes both permanent abilities (A2, A3), because a metroidvania's gates are crossed again and again. Every cracked stone stays a gate after the first one breaks, and the backtracking (section 4: "Each one opens new routes in every earlier zone") would strand you if the tool were gone. A timed flight would also outrun every gap the reach checker measures, and the flight shrinking with rank would turn the game's reward for rank into a loss. What each tool does is the genre's form of the source game's. The Boots lift you once more in midair (a short flight that comes back on every landing, wall and pogo), and the Delver's Pick breaks every cracked stone and adds the pogo. Neither is sold, and neither is spent: `GameState.fresh().abilities` keeps both as yes-or-no, and no item in `data/items.json` is named for either.

**The Barkbalm lasts as long at every rank (deviation, pass 361):** in the source game, the Barkbalm's protection, like the Featherstep Boots' flight, ran out sooner the higher your title, so a rank earned made a bought Barkbalm worth less. Speedrunners note that "higher titles from experience tend to be a net negative in this game" (the Speed Demos Archive knowledge base, *Yldenfall: Game Mechanics and Glitches*). The remake keeps what the Barkbalm does, harm turned away for a time, and gives it the same 10 s at every rank (`Player.OINTMENT_TIME`), for the reason pass 353 gave the Boots: rank is the game's reward, and should never take something away. Whether it is rubbed on from the pause menu or goes straight on from a full pouch, it lasts the same. **The Barkbalm by the buttons (pass 441):** it had only ever been put on by a call, never from the pause menu with the buttons, nor against a real blow. `test_the_ointment_is_rubbed_on_with_the_buttons` runs the whole game: Pause, Down to Use Barkbalm and Jump rub it on and close the menu with the full ten seconds left; a Rot Dwarf beside the hero swings through it and nothing lands, and once it wears off his next blow lands.

**The Featherstep Boots' gate on the critical path (pass 232):** the table gives the Boots "wide gaps, high ledges", but the reach checker found they opened no room at all: with every earlier ability and no Boots, the same rooms were reached as with them, so the Boots only reached two secrets (the Feather Hollow perch and the Sky Steps purse). A core movement ability was optional. Now the Elven Stride hangs in the Windloft's open air (`boughs/windloft`, 24,11), its centre 136 px over the floor and at least 15 tiles from the entry ledge and the east wall, where its old plinth and the one-way plank above it are gone. A plain jump lifts the head 112 px, the second jump 160 px, so the Boots come before the Stride, and the Stride before Carillon (pass 227). The sign says so: "They hung the Stride where only a second step in the air can reach it." Proved with the real controller, since the checker can't prove "out of reach" (`test_the_stride_in_the_windloft_needs_the_featherstep_boots`): 13 timings each straight up, from a run, off the entry ledge, and wall-jumping off the east wall near the vault with the Glove never reach it without the Boots; with them, a jump and a second jump under it do (9 of 13). The checker agrees (`BOOT_GATES`); it now lets a jump touch a pickup up to 8 rows over the cell you stand in with the Boots, 6 without (`Reach.TOUCH_RISE`, `BOOT_TOUCH_RISE`), both short of the real reach. Climbing back out to the North Bough is the Glove's wall climb up the ledge's face.

**The Stride's gate on the critical path (pass 227):** the Sky Steps were meant to be climbed with the Stride, "each gap past any jump", and the reach checker kept Carillon shut without it (`STRIDE_ROOMS`). But the checker's jumps fall short of real ones (pass 216), and the real controller crossed the last leap, from the top island to the exit ledge, with a plain running jump: 270 of 300 jump and second-jump timings with the Featherstep Boots made it, and one with no boots at all. So Carillon, Stormcall and everything past them opened without the Stride. An elven veil (`|`, 4.5) now stands in the column before the ledge (x33, rows 4 to 9, from the ceiling to the ledge's top), so only a dash passes. `test_the_sky_steps_exit_needs_the_stride` plays it: 27 jump and second-jump timings with the boots never stand on the ledge, and a jump and a dash with the Stride do. The sign at the foot still says what to do: "An elf would dash the last of it, in the air." Screenshot: `/mnt/project-files/pass-227/sky_steps_veil.png`.

**Each secret holds against the abilities found before it (pass 211):** the Featherstep Boots always come before the Elven Stride (the Haar before the High Boughs), so a Stride secret must be out of reach of a double jump too. Until pass 211 the North Bough's warden cache (a Life Fruit on a one-tile plank over the gap) sat five tiles from the bough's ledge at a five-tile rise, so the Featherstep Boots took it and the Stride gated no pickup at all. It now hangs one row lower and one tile further west, and the west shelf under it ends a tile sooner: from the ledge it is seven tiles across at a four-row rise, and from the shelf six across at a five-row rise. Each is one tile past a double jump and well inside a double jump with an air dash. The Sky Steps' purse on the climb's first island is a double jump up from the island below, so it is listed with the Featherstep Boots' secrets, which is what it always was; the Stride still gates the Sky Steps' upper islands. `test_elven_stride_crosses_the_gap_to_the_warden_cache` now starts with the Featherstep Boots. **Correction (pass 216):** a real jump disproved this. The reach checker's envelopes are kept *under* what the player can do, so that a route it passes is comfortable in play. That makes them right for proving a place can be reached, but they can't prove a place is out of reach. A scripted run in the actual room took the Life Fruit with a single jump, no boots at all. The gap was never a gate. The fruit now sits in a sealed alcove under the North Bough's ceiling, behind an elven veil (below). That is a gate in the physics itself, so it holds whatever a jump can do. Any future secret that is meant to be out of reach should be sealed by a gate tile, never just set at a distance. **The boots secrets checked (pass 219):** the same doubt applied to the two Featherstep Boots secrets, so pass 219 ran real jumps at them. The Sky Steps' purse failed: its island stood four rows (64 px) over the island below it, and a plain running jump off that island's edge landed on it. The island now stands five rows up (80 px). That is past the 72 px apex of any single jump, a ceiling in the physics rather than an estimate of distance, and the thorn bulbs lining the wall beside it leave nothing to climb. `test_the_sky_steps_purse_needs_the_featherstep_boots` runs the jump both ways: without the boots it falls short, and with them it lands. The purse moved from 6,20 to 6,19 (`MOVED_PICKUPS`). Feather Hollow's Life Fruit was thought to hold on the same bound, its perch plank six rows (96 px) over the nearest plank. **Correction (pass 235):** a real jump disproved that too. The apex bounds the feet, but a pickup is touched by the head, 40 px above them. Running off the lower plank's west end, a plain jump lifted the head into the fruit in six of fourteen timings. The perch now stands a row higher (plank at row 7, fruit at 18,6, `MOVED_PICKUPS`). The fruit's cell begins 128 px over the lower plank, past the head's 116 px reach, and the Boots' second jump lands on the perch: their feet rise 124 px. `test_the_feather_hollow_perch_needs_the_featherstep_boots` proves it with the real controller. Without the Boots, none of fourteen running jumps off the plank takes the fruit, nor do jumps from the floor under it or leaps off the west wall with the Glove. With the Boots, a jump and a second jump off the plank take it. The reach checker's Boots envelope now allows a seven-row rise across two tiles (`Reach.BOOT_JUMP`), the same 12 px margin under the real feet rise that the plain jump keeps. So for a pickup, the bound to beat is the head's reach, not the apex. A height past the apex, with no wall to climb, is the one distance that is a real gate: across, the checker's short jumps always undersell a real one.

### 4.2 Critical path

1. Hearthroot: talk to the King, get 1,500 gold, buy a weapon. The Ringreader teaches saving. Enter the roots.
2. Heartwood: Climbing Glove. Boss **Gnarlmaw**. Restore the **Heartwood Spring** (first third of the fountain). Get the **Thaw Seed**.
3. Hollin Tower: Delver's Pick. Boss **Durgan**. Elevator shortcut back to Hearthroot. Get the **Sun Seed** (from Durgan, whose defeat frees the dwarves of Kettleforge).
4. The Haar: Featherstep Boots (Lampwick side), Torrent (Polesend side, in either order). Bosses **Gloamweaver** (optional-feeling but required for the lantern key) and **the Haar Wyrm**. Restore the **Haar Spring** (second third). Get the **Fall Seed**.
5. High Boughs: Elven Stride. Boss **Snapcrown**.
6. Storm Spires and Spires: Stormcall. Boss **Skyrend**. Restore the **Sky Spring** (fountain full; Hearthroot celebrates). Skyrend drops the **Sky Key** that opens Vorrhallow's upper gate.
7. Vorrhallow: Seer's Lens, Stillstone. Boss **Thane Vorrund** gives the **Frost Seed** and the **Severance**, the sword he swallowed (pass 217). Boss **the Likeness** guards the Crown Gate.
8. The Evercrown: gauntlet, **The Everbloom**. Ending.

*Ring sources, final:* Thaw Seed (Gnarlmaw), Sun Seed (Durgan), Fall Seed (the Haar Wyrm), Frost Seed (Thane Vorrund).

### 4.3 Sequence breaks (deliberate)

Expert players can skip ahead, and the game must never soft-lock if they do:
- Pogo chains in the Haar let skilled players reach Sylvarre before Featherstep Boots.
- A wall-jump route skips most of the Hollin Tower after Durgan is beaten: the Old Flue, a three-screen chimney with no ledges above the Tower Foot, then the Rafters under the tower's crown, straight to the top of the Mine Shaft. It skips the lift, Kettleforge, the Mine Mouth and the shaft climb. An iron damper (legend `Q`) seals the flue until the winch turns.
- ~~Stillstone can be reached before Seer's Lens with precise dashes.~~ Changed in pass 66-67: the Lens's main-route illusory wall leads to the Stillstone, so it now always comes second. A dash skip can come back with the lower city.
All story triggers check items, not route order.

### 4.4 World-state changes

| Trigger | Change |
|---------|--------|
| Heartwood Spring restored | Sap pools in Heartwood harden into walkable amber; new route to a secret; Amberlee NPCs return; Hearthroot fountain 1/3 |
| Durgan defeated | Kettleforge dwarves freed (Kettleforge town behind the lift: Ringreader, smith, elder, miners; its own theme); the Kettleforge lift runs again, stopping at the town, the Tower Foot and a cage at the East Gate of Hearthroot down the outside of the trunk (the elevator shortcut home; press Up in a cage to pick a stop. The lift by the buttons (pass 419): `test_the_lift_answers_the_real_controller` presses Up in the East Gate cage for real, where it hangs still while Durgan holds Kettleforge, then once she falls opens the stops, Down moves to the Tower Foot and Jump rides there); dwarf smith opens (weapon upgrades) and his forge burns again (a hearth, bellows and an anvil that rings and throws sparks); the great winch in Winch Hall turns again and hauls open the Old Flue's damper in the Tower Foot (the wall-jump skip route of 4.3); the Kettleforge Deep Workings open east of the town (Mine Mouth, Mine Shaft, Rail Gallery, Mine Vault, Smelter, and two side rooms: the Sorting Floor, a loop from the Mine Mouth up into the Mine Vault, and the Slag Heap under the Smelter), the ore road toward the Haar, which leaves the Smelter's east end for the Fog Cut, the Drowned Stair and Lantern Landing, a shrine where the boardwalk to Lampwick is out, with the Sunken Chapel, Gloamweaver's lair, past its east door |
| Gloamweaver defeated | Her lanterns go dark and she gives up the Lantern Key, which opens the way to the Haar Spring and the Haar Wyrm. The key's light parts the bound fog (a pale wall of unmoving mist, legend `Y`, that tells you it wants a lantern) sealing the shaft above Lantern Landing; it parts for good and the key is kept. Beyond it the Veiled Rise climbs to the Lampwick Causeway, a spiked boardwalk dip on the road west to Lampwick, and past it Feather Hollow, where the Featherstep Boots wait on a plank below a perch whose Life Fruit only a second jump reaches. Her chapel's east door opens on the Marsh Road toward Polesend, where the Torrent scroll lies before a fire seal; the Scorched Hall waits behind it, the Dry Cistern past that, and past the cistern the Haar Spring, the Haar Wyrm's lair |
| The Haar Wyrm defeated | Fall Seed; the Haar Spring's tanks stay full, and the spring on its shelf can be cleared; the arena's east door opens on the Windward Climb, where bounce-pods throw you up out of the Haar into the High Boughs (the Sunlit Bough, then Leaf Landing, a shrine on the elves' leaf road) |
| Haar Spring restored | Fog lifts by half in the Haar (vision radius grows); drowned boardwalks resurface (legend `b`: one-way planks over the spiked dips of the Fog Cut, the Lampwick Causeway and the Polesend road (pass 148), nothing before); fountain 2/3. **A spring by the buttons (pass 425):** every spring test set a scripted Up, which lasts one step. `test_a_spring_answers_the_real_controller` presses the real Up at the Haar Spring: while the Haar Wyrm lives it does nothing; pressed in the air and held to the landing it does nothing either; one tap on the ground restores it, a third of the fountain fills, and it is announced once with the chime; Up again announces nothing more |
| Sky Spring restored | The storm breaks: Carillon's rain thins to a drizzle (Motes.SETTLES). Hearthroot festival: the fountain runs full, a piper plays in the plaza and Old Wena rejoices (pass 62). The High Boughs' wind currents turn and blow the other way (pass 92), carrying you into the Thicket Road's sealed wind niche. The final shop tier comes in on the sky road (pass 93, 10.2): the Dwarvish Broad Sword in Hearthroot and a Life Fruit in Carillon |
| Bark Knight Captain defeated (optional, pass 178) | By Sylvarre's east door, Squire Ithil had warned of the Thorn Yard's Captain and that fire was the one thing his bark feared; he now speaks of the yard won back (`sylvarre_squire`, then `sylvarre_squire_glad`) |
| Thane Vorrund defeated | Vorrhallow drains; Sunken Spring entrance opens. **The water, as built (pass 259):** until then Vorrhallow's drowning was only in what people said: no hall had water in it. Now the Flooded Hall and the Drowned Nave stand in still water (`tide: 19`, `Tide`): a tile deep across the floor and filling the spike pits, translucent so the spikes still show, with a pale breathing surface. Wading splashes (the steps sound `wade`, their own slosh since pass 262; `mud` until then). It is not deep enough to swim, and it neither slows you nor hides a hazard. Pass 272 holds all three: in the Flooded Hall's water the run reaches full speed, a jump rises its full height, you never swim, and the spike pit under the surface still hurts (`test_wading_neither_slows_nor_floats_nor_hides_the_spikes`). Once he is at rest both halls are dry, with a teal tide mark where the surface stood and silt on the floor. The Upper Gate-Hall stays dry, since its sign already says the water stood there once. The Flooded Hall's sign has a line for after. `test_vorrhallow_drains_when_thane_vorrund_is_at_rest`. Screenshots: `/mnt/project-files/pass-259/flooded_hall.png`, `flooded_hall_drained.png` |
| Clockwork Alpha defeated (optional, pass 178) | Once Thane Vorrund's fall wakes the Drowned Market, Houndkeeper Brand, who wound the king's hounds for forty years, tells of the Alpha still pacing its den under the kennels; once it is wound down he sleeps easy and blesses the Life Fruit it kept. A room's person can now name several conditions, space-separated, all of which must hold (`Room.flags_hold`: `boss:thane_vorrund !boss:clockwork_alpha`) |
| Marsh Hag Matriarch defeated (optional, pass 179) | In Polesend, Reedcutter Siobhe stands by the east door and warns of the Matriarch in the mire past it, and of how her brother learned to break a hex; once the Matriarch sinks for good she can cut reeds in the east mire again |
| Everbloom defeated | Post-game: the Tree blooms (built pass 141, below). ~~Palette shift to spring colors~~ waits for the art polish phase. ~~All enemies respawn as "remnants"~~: foes already return when you re-enter a room, and they are the Tree's own creatures, so they stay as they are for the 100% cleanup |

**Every guardian's hall answers its fall (pass 267):** most arenas' signs already read differently once their guardian was beaten. The Heartwood's chewing stops, the Spires' nest is empty, and the Hound's chain has run down. Seven still spoke of theirs as alive:
- the Sunken Chapel's lanterns "still burn" after Gloamweaver's go dark, and the landing above it still said something sings there;
- the crown-leaf still grew "nothing but the Bloom";
- the Thorn Yard's knight still kept the watch;
- the Roost still told you to wake the conduits and call the storm down;
- the Wellspring's Warden still "has never let anyone near it";
- the Gate of the Evercrown and the Throne Hall did not change;
- the Foreman's doorway still spoke of its golem at work.

Each now has a `[boss:<id>]` line that reads once its guardian is beaten (Old Cormac's word on the chapel is in the past tense too). `test_every_guardians_hall_answers_its_fall` holds every sign in an arena outside the Evercrown to a word for after, and reads the doorways before and after. The Evercrown's halls are left as they are, since the ending follows them.

**The spring keepers (pass 249):** in the source game a man stood before each of the three springs, and the spring woke with him: an Amber Draught for the Heartwood Spring, a sleeper woken with water at the spring in the sky. The remake's springs stood empty, cleared with a press of Up and no one to say what they were. Each now has its keeper, standing beside it (`heartwood_keeper`, `haar_keeper`, `sky_keeper` in `data/npcs.json`, each with a `_glad` self once the spring flows):
- **Odran**, a dwarf of the Heartwood, at the Heartwood Spring from the start. His father swore it took an Amber Draught to wake a spring (the source game's riddle); it takes someone to clear the spout. Once it flows he names the two springs higher up and the keepers waiting at them.
- **Ilka**, of Lampwick, at the Haar Spring once the Haar Wyrm is beaten (no one stands in the fight), who watched the coil from the cistern stair. Once it flows, the fog thins, and Ilka warns that the sky's keeper is a sleeper.
- **Arel**, an elf, at the Sky Spring, who slept while the roc sat on it (the source game's dozing keeper), and once it flows points down through the clouds to Vorrhallow.
Each has a word once the Tree blooms. Deviation: the keepers don't wake the springs themselves, and no spring asks for an Amber Draught. Clearing a spring stays the player's act (a press of Up), so the critical path asks for no gold. `test_every_spring_has_its_keeper` holds where each keeper stands, and when.

**The Tree blooms (pass 141):** a save that has seen the ending (flag `cleared`) carries on into a changed world.
- **Blossom:** a second particle layer drifts pink petals down through every living zone of the Tree: Hearthroot, the Heartwood, the Tower, the Haar, the High Boughs and Carillon (`Motes.BLOSSOM`, `World.blossom`). There are 40 at a time, each two pixels long, turning end over end as it sways.
- **The stillglass's air settles:** Vorrhallow's crystal dust thins to 35% and the Evercrown's cyan spores to 20% (`Motes.SETTLES`, now keyed by any flag).
- **People:** sixteen people (everyone a cleared save can meet who talks rather than trades) say something new once the Tree blooms, through a `lines_cleared` list in `data/npcs.json` that takes over from their other lines (`Npc.lines`). The King marvels at the flowering, Old Wena fishes petals out of the fountain, the dwarves of Kettleforge mourn Vorrund as a king, Vorrhallow's mason chips grey crystal out of dry walls, and Hrothgar's digger points you to the smith with your Stillglass Shards.
- The ending still returns you to the title. Continue picks up the cleared save where you last rested.

### 4.5 Gate visual language

Every gate has a consistent, readable look so players remember it:
- **Cracked stone:** pale crack lines, small pebbles fall when you are near (Delver's Pick).
- **Thorn bulbs:** red glowing pods on spike vines (pogo targets).
- **Fog (the Haar):** a grey-green murk over every Haar room hides all but a pocket of sight around you (about 7 tiles), the lanterns hung through the zone (about 4 tiles each) and the glow of a shrine. The fog drifts and bands like dithered pixel fog. Restoring the Haar Spring thins it by half and widens your sight by half. A room can set its own density with `fog:`. Lights that matter to play (a boss's lanterns, a Marsh Hag's wisp and her mark, spells, abilities) are lit before the decor's lanterns, so a room crowded with lanterns can never leave a foe dark (pass 120).
- **Mist curtains:** shimmering vertical fog with a faint elven rune (Elven Stride). Built in pass 216 as the **elven veil** (legend `|`, `StrideVeil`), because the name "mist curtain" had gone to the Haar's bound fog (`Y`, the Lantern Key). The veil is a column of silver-green strands over a soft teal body, with a pale elven rune that breathes. It sits on its own collision layer (`Player.LAYER_VEIL`), which the player's mask drops while dashing, and keeps dropped until the player is clear of it (`Player.passes_veils`), so a dash that ends inside lets you out. Walking or jumping into it, it is a wall. Ground and air dashes both pass it, and it flares with a rush and two glassy notes (`veil`). Without the Stride, touching it says "An elven veil. Only a warden's stride passes it." Only the player is stopped, so foes are kept out of veiled spaces. The reach checker treats it as solid until the Stride is found. Tests: `test_an_elven_veil_lets_only_a_dash_through` and `test_the_warden_cache_needs_the_stride`.
- **Fire seals:** red runic flames (Torrent). They block like a wall, burn for 10 on touch and light the fog around them. One Torrent bolt douses every seal in the room for good, in a hiss of steam (legend `Z`, a column of three under a stone arch).
- **Dry basins:** cracked mud basins with a water-line stain (Torrent). A Torrent bolt cast across the tank (from its rim is enough) fills it over a second, drowning anything left in it, and a raft floats up to the stain and stays there as a one-way platform for good. The first is the Dry Cistern (mist 34,-7) east of the Scorched Hall, whose shelf over the tank's middle only the raft reaches; the Haar Wyrm's basins work the same way (legend `u` for the mud bed, a row of `=` for the stain).
