- **Bramble walls:** a knot of dry thorn-vine, crossed vines with pale thorns (Kindle, legend `w`). Blades only snag in it. A fireball sets it alight and the flames creep tile to tile through the whole knot, which burns to ash in about a second and stays burnt. Kindle is optional, so bramble only ever hides secrets, never the way on.
- **Dead conduits:** copper coils with a dim blue spark (Stormcall). A Stormcall bolt that passes within 72 px of one (not behind it) leaps to it in an arc and wakes it for good: the coil glows and lights the fog. When every conduit in a room is awake, the room's conduit gates (elf-silver shutters with a lightning glyph, legend `k`; a conduit is legend `c`) slide up for good (flag `conduits:<room>`). Stormcall as built: 18 MP, a fast bolt (380 px/s) that strikes one foe for 16.
- **Illusory walls:** a 1-pixel seam visible only with Seer's Lens. As built (pass 65): any fake wall (`%`, or a `secret:` cover) gets a thin violet seam round its edge that breathes and sweeps with light once the Lens is carried. Hidden planks (legend `i`) are one-way planks that are always solid but drawn only through the Lens, at about 40% opacity; a leap of faith finds them without it, so they guard secrets and never the only way on.
- **Time-locked water:** waterfalls with clock-glyph droplets (Stillstone). As built (pass 67, legend `f`, a block of cells): flowing, it bears down at 260 px/s on anything in it, so you can't jump or climb up through it, though you can walk out. While time is stopped it stands still, a pale frozen column with footholds every three rows up from its bottom (one-way ledges), a stair you climb before time runs on.
- **Key doors:** Copper, Silver, Gold, Thorn and Glass doors, each with a mark of its own. As built (pass 117): every key door is three tiles tall (legend `J`, made another kind by a `lock: x, y, kind` header line) and wears its mark: the Copper is oak with iron bands and a gold acorn, the Silver red lacquer with gold bands and a pale Ylden leaf, the Gold blackened iron with the Reeve's gold crown, the Thorn elf-silver with a blue thorn, and the Glass violet with green bands and a gold stillglass shard whose glint winks. **Marks of their own (pass 471):** until then the doors wore the card suits (spade, heart, club, diamond, a jester's star) of the game Yldenfall began as; each now bears a mark of this world, of a shape of its own (`KeyDoor.DOORS`, `KeyDoor.draw_mark`; `test_the_key_doors_wear_marks_of_their_own`). Screenshot: `/mnt/project-files/pass-471/doors.png`. Walk into one holding its own key and it opens for good and spends that key; any other card's key does nothing. The map's status line lists the keys you hold, and since pass 127 the map marks every key door you have seen and not yet opened: a little slab in its card's color (the Sky Gate in its shrine stone) with a gold band, standing where the door stands, until the door opens (legend "Locked door"). A key bought zones later has somewhere to go. Card doors guard vaults, never the way on: a roofed pocket behind the door with a chest worth more than the key (header `chest: x, y, gold`). Built: the Copper door in the Deep Roots, the Silver vault in Feather Hollow (900 g), the King vault in the North Bough (1,800 g), the Thorn vault on the Spire Stair (3,200 g) and the Glass vault on the Glass Rise (5,000 g). The keys are sold by the grocers on the road to each one (Silver at Amberlee, King at Sylvarre, Thorn at Carillon, see 10.1). The one Glass Key is never sold: it lies on the stall-plank of the Drowned Market (`prop: x, y, glass_key`). Sounds (pass 122): walking into a door without its key rattles the latch, with two dull knocks against the bolt (`door_locked`), once per touch; the key turning clicks through the wards, draws the bolt back with a thunk and rings a small bright chime (`key_turn`). The Sky Gate uses the same two. **A card door by the buttons (pass 417):** every door test had walked by a scripted intent, so `test_a_card_door_answers_the_real_controller` holds Right for real. A Silver door stops the hero holding only a Copper Key and rattles once however long he leans on it; stepping back and leaning again rattles again; and the Silver Key opens it on the touch, so the held Right walks straight through and only the Silver Key is spent. **The King's gold to the Copper door, by the buttons (pass 459):** the source game's first steps in Hearthroot, played one after another with the real controller in `test_the_kings_gold_buys_the_key_that_opens_the_door`. Up at the King gives his purse with a clink and opens his words, and Jump turns the pages and puts them away. Right walks to the Merchant, and Up opens his shop. Down finds the Copper Key, Jump buys it with the King's gold, and Dash leaves. Then Right held into the Copper door turns the key with no rattle and walks through, and the key is spent.
- **Root-gates (Heartwood):** a woven pillar of living roots beaded with sap. No ability opens it: find and strike the room's Root Heart, a pulsing amber bulb, and every gate in the room draws back into the ceiling for good. A puzzle of finding, not of power, so the zone has gates before the Climbing Glove.

**The gates' numbers, held (pass 339).** The numbers above are the code's: the fog's sight of about 7 tiles and a lantern's 4 (`Fog.VISION`, `Fog.LANTERN_RADIUS`), the Haar Spring halving the fog and widening sight by half, a fire seal's burn of 10 (`FireSeal.BURN`), a basin filling over a second, a conduit's reach of 72 px (`ThunderBolt.ARC`), time-locked water's push of 260 px/s and its footholds every three rows (`Waterfall.PUSH`, `Waterfall.STEP`), and a card door three tiles tall. So are the Haar's fog of 0.88, Lampwick's four lamp posts and Polesend's three, each town's fog of 0.6 and a lamp's 84 px pocket (3.2). Until pass 339 no test read any of them; `test_every_gates_number_is_written_in_bible_4_5` holds each one to its constant or its room.

## 5. Player and Combat

### 5.1 Stats

| Stat | Start | Max | Raised by |
|------|-------|-----|-----------|
| Health (HP) | 5 masks of 20 HP (100) | 14 masks (280) | Life Fruit: 2 fruits = +1 mask (18 fruits: 13 in the rooms, one at each of the 4 grocers, 1 from the Clockwork Alpha) |
| Magic (MP) | 60 | 220 | Mana Dew: each +10 (16 dews: 9 in the rooms, one at each of the 4 grocers, 3 from Gloamweaver, the Bark Knight Captain and the Marsh Hag Matriarch) |
| Sapflask flask | 2 charges, heals 40 HP | 6 charges, each still 40 HP | Potion Shards (3 shards = +1 charge; 9 shards) and Deep Pockets (+1). Pass 321: this row had said 5 charges healing 70 HP, though nothing ever raised the heal and the shards and Deep Pockets make 6. Pass 243: The Heartwood and the Tower had grown 18 shards, enough for 8 charges; nine (three in the Heartwood, six in the Tower) became chests of gold at the same cells, so a save that took one finds the chest already open |
| Attack | Weapon base | | Weapons, smith upgrades, skill tree |
| Defense | Armor base | | Armor, shields |

HP is shown as masks (chunks), so damage is readable at a glance, while the math uses HP for fine tuning.

**The Sapflask (deviation, pass 321):** in the source game a potion was one item, bought at a shop and drunk whole. The Sapflask is a flask of charges, each a fixed 40 HP that nothing raises, which a Ringreader's rest refills. A full heal on demand would make every fight up to the last mask safe and make the masks themselves meaningless; a fixed sip that takes 0.7 s and is lost to a blow keeps a drink a choice made in a lull, and the flask's growth (Potion Shards, Deep Pockets) is a reward for exploring that a stack of bought potions would not be. A full restore is kept where it belongs: the Ringreader's rest and the Amber Draught.

### 5.2 Weapons and armor (Yldenfall tiers)

| Slot | Tier 1 | Tier 2 | Tier 3 | Tier 4 |
|------|--------|--------|--------|--------|
| Blade | Short Sword (start, fast, short: slash 8, heavy 14, reach 0; called the Hand Dagger before pass 184) | Wayfarer's Sword (400 g; slash 11, heavy 19, reach 8) | Dwarvish Broad Sword (2,800 g, two-handed, slow, huge arc; built pass 93 as the Giant Blade, renamed pass 186: slash 17, heavy 32, reach 16, arc 20) | Severance (Thane Vorrund's, given up when he falls, as in the source game (pass 217); built pass 80: slash 18, heavy 30, reach 12) |
| Armor | Leather (200 g) | Riveted Coat (1,100 g) | Knight's Plate (4,200 g) | Elven Plate (secret: the Drowned Warden, 60% less damage) |
| Shield | Buckler (150 g; wards 20% of sorcery, pass 225) | Kite Shield (900 g; wards 30%) | Warding Shield (3,600 g, reflects small projectiles on parry; wards 40%) | (none: the Elven Helm has its own slot since pass 185) |
| Helmet | Elven Helm (secret: +1 mask, 10% less damage, wards 20% more sorcery) | | | |

**The source game's gear, and what changed (pass 193).** The source game sold twelve pieces of gear: four weapons, four armors and four shields. All twelve are in the remake. Where one changed, this table says how and why. `test_the_originals_twelve_pieces_of_gear_are_all_here` keeps it true: each original name must be an item, or a row here naming the item that took its place, and each id in `GameState.RENAMED_ITEMS` must have a row.

| Original | In the remake | Changed in | Why |
|----------|---------------|------------|-----|
| Hand Dagger | Short Sword (`short_sword`, from `hand_dagger`) | pass 184 | Sean: every swing of it is a slash, so it reads as a short sword. The dagger idea became the thrown Rune Dagger. |
| Giant Blade | Dwarvish Broad Sword (`dwarvish_broad_sword`, from `giant_blade`), two-handed | pass 186 | Sean: a name with a maker, and two hands with no shield, so its weight is a real trade. |
| Elven Helm | Elven Helm, in its own Helmet slot | pass 185 | Sean: worn as a shield, it cost you the Warding Shield's parry. Now it adds a mask and 10% less damage on top of any shield. |
| (none) | Rune Dagger, a thrown tool beside the blade | pass 184 | Sean's idea: a ranged answer before the spells. It is not a weapon tier and takes no temper. |

**Shields ward sorcery (pass 225).** The source game's manual gives a shield one job: it resists enemy magic (Small 50%, Large 75%, Magic 90%, and the Elven Helm "intercepts almost all"). The remake's shields guarded and parried but did nothing against a hex you didn't block. Now a worn shield also wards a foe's sorcery, guarding or not: Small 20%, Large 30%, Magic 40%, and the Elven Helm adds 20%, to at most 60% (`ward` in `data/items.json`, `Player.magic_ward`, `GameState.MAX_WARD`). The shares are kept under the source game's so that guarding, a parry and the damage sheet (5.5) still matter. Sorcery is the group `Player.MAGIC`: the Marsh Hags' and the Matriarch's hex (`MireGrasp`), the Likeness's bolts, Thane Vorrund's crystal shards and the Everbloom core's beam. Blows, bites, breath, storm and thrown things are not sorcery. A shield on the back of a two-hander wards nothing; the helmet still does. Nor does a shield taken off for the Rune Dagger (pass 313): the source game had no thrown dagger, and its shield, once bought, was always on the arm and always resisting magic. In the remake the off hand holds a shield or the dagger (Sean's rule, below), so taking the dagger gives up the shield's ward along with its guard and parry, and only the helmet's ward stays. That is a choice the source game never asked for, kept honest by the Equipment page's Ward figure. Test: `test_a_shield_wards_a_foes_sorcery`.

The other nine keep their names. Eight of them (Wayfarer's Sword, Severance, Tanned Jerkin, Riveted Coat, Knight's Plate, Buckler, Kite Shield, Warding Shield) keep their place as well. The ninth, the Elven Plate, does not (deviation, pass 377): it is won from the Drowned Warden in the Sunken Spring, a zone and a guardian of new (8.10; the ledger in 3.2). The source game replaced gear as you bought it; the remake keeps it (pass 186, above).

**The source game's relics, the Acorn Charm and the Briar Wand (pass 201).** The source game hid two more finds that sold nowhere, and until pass 201 the remake had neither. Both are now found once and kept, laid by `prop:` lines and counted as treasures (`Room.FOUND_PROPS`); their numbers live in `combat_tuning.gd` (Relics).

| Relic | Where (original) | Where (remake) | Effect |
|-------|------------------|----------------|--------|
| Acorn Charm | the end of the Hollin Tower, in a corner of its guardian's room, easy to miss | the far corner of the Matriarch's Mire (`haar/hag_mire`, 37,16), the Haar's optional guardian's room | every blade hit lands a tenth harder (`pendant_mult` 1.1, through `Player.blade_mult`) |
| Briar Wand | Sylvarre, up a ladder on the right of the town | Sylvarre's high plank (73,7), up a new ladder at the town's east end | Torrent bolts strike half again as hard (`rod_torrent_mult` 1.5: 12 to 18, as the source game's 6 to 9) |

Deviation: the source game's Acorn Charm was meant to raise attack, but a slip in its code made it lower it, so players learned to leave it behind. The remake gives it the effect it was meant to have, kept small (10%) so the damage sheet (5.5) still holds without it. The Rune Dagger's sting is not a blade hit and takes no Acorn Charm. Held by `test_the_pendant_and_the_briar_wand` and `test_the_pendant_and_the_rod_can_be_reached`.

Each blade has its own swing speed and reach, so a weapon change is a play-style change, not just a number. The dwarf smith in Kettleforge upgrades each blade twice (+15% each) for gold plus Stillglass Shards. (Built: Hrothgar's Kettleforge Forge sells Temper Blade I for 700 g and II for 1,600 g, per blade, so a new blade starts untempered.)

**Stillglass Shards (built pass 140):**
- Splinters of the Everbloom, flung down the Tree when it struck the crown: a black rock heel with three magenta crystal spurs and a cyan light beating in the crack between them. Legend `(`.
- Temper Blade II costs one besides its gold (`"shards": 1` on the shop entry); Temper I is gold alone. Without one the forge shows "Needs shard", and the temper's line says how many you carry. A shard is spent when the smith folds it in.
- Five lie in the world, one more than there are blades, so one can go to a blade you later put down: on the Crown Lookout above the potion shard (Tower, near the smith), above the Scorched Hall's platform (the Haar), in the Sunlit Bough's hidden pocket (High Boughs), in the Broken Shrine's hidden pocket (Carillon), and at the end of a Glass Rise shelf (Vorrhallow). They count as treasures for completion and Treasure Sense.
- Carried in `GameState.data.meteor_shards`.

**Dwarvish Broad Sword (built pass 93 as the Giant Blade; renamed and made two-handed in pass 186):**
- **Two-handed (Sean, pass 186):** while you hold it, your shield stays on your back. Block does nothing, so there is no guard, no parry, no Riposte and no Warding Shield reflect (`"two_handed": true`; `refresh_stats` clears `has_shield`). The shield's layer is never drawn, since the block pose never plays. To pay for that, it hits harder than it did: slash 17 and heavy 32 (was 15 and 28), about 13 damage per second of swinging against the Wayfarer's Sword's 11. **Changed by the buttons mid-guard (pass 445):** the swap had only ever been scripted. `test_the_equipment_page_changes_hands_mid_guard` holds Block the whole time while the real controller walks the pause menu to the Equipment page and back. The Broad Sword put in hand drops the guard though Block is still held, and the Short Sword back in hand raises it again at once, as a held Block always guards, with no fresh press and so no parry window.
- No room or fight needs a parry. Every parryable attack can also be dodged or outranged, and reachability never uses one. You can also swap back to a one-handed blade from the Equipment page at any time.
- 16 px more reach than the Short Sword.
- Its side slash sweeps 20 px taller, so it covers you from head to heel and catches fliers and crawlers a plainer blade misses (`arc` in `data/items.json`).
- Every swing takes 30% longer to recover (`swing` 1.3). Commit to a cut and space your foes. A player who dislikes the slow swing keeps the Wayfarer's Sword and can swap back (Equipment, below).
- It is drawn two pixels broader, with a larger smear.
- Hearthroot Arms sells it for 2,800 g once the Sky Spring is restored (10.2). Its id is `dwarvish_broad_sword`; a save naming `giant_blade` is carried over through `RENAMED_ITEMS`, tempers and all.

**Equipment: gear is kept (Sean, pass 186):**
- Every blade, armor, shield and helmet you buy, find or win goes into `GameState.data.gear` and is put on at once (`GameState.obtain_gear`). Nothing is thrown away. (The source game replaced the slot; this changes it so a weapon is a choice, not only an upgrade.)
- The pause menu's **Equipment** page lists your gear by slot (Blades, Armor, Shields, Helmets), lowest tier first, marking what you wear. Choose an item to put it on (`GameState.wear`). Choose armor, a shield or a helmet you already wear to take it off; you always hold a blade. Tempers stay with each blade.
- **Numbers and the cloak (pass 189):**
  - Each blade's detail on the Equipment page, and on a blade shop's shelf, gives its numbers as you would swing it, with tempers in (`Menus.blade_numbers`), e.g. "Cuts 17, heavy 32, reach +16, 30% slower". Before this, the only way to learn what "slow" or "hits harder" meant was to buy the blade and try it.
  - The page ends with a **Cloak** section: Warden Green, every dye bought or won, and the Crown Gold once it is yours (`Menus.cloak_dyes`). Choose one to put it on anywhere (`Menus.choose_equipment`), as gear is. Ilse's shelf still sells the dyes and puts them on too.
  - Every detail fits the menu's three-line box, which a test checks with all gear owned. Two item texts were cut to fit: the Broad Sword's, and the Warding Shield's, which had run to four lines under a two-hander since pass 186.
- **What you wear, added up (pass 229):** armor and the helmet stack by what each lets through (the Elven Plate under the Elven Helm stops 64%, not 70%), and the ward (5.2, pass 225) is the shield's plus the helmet's, capped at 60% and lost to a two-hander. None of it showed anywhere, so the page's footer now gives the totals as `refresh_stats` works them out, e.g. "Armor 64%   Ward 60%" (`Menus.worn_totals`), and the subtitle says what each means ("Armor stops blows; a ward turns sorcery"). A shield slung for a two-hander says it gives no guard or ward. Test: `test_the_equipment_page_adds_up_what_you_wear`. Screenshot `--wear=A,B` owns and wears gear for it.
- A shop shows only what you have as Owned. A lower tier you never bought can still be bought.
- Saves from before pass 186 keep what they wore, plus the Short Sword.
- The worn armor still decides the secret ending (the Elven Plate, 11) and the hero's body set; the blade in hand still decides whether the Everbloom's heart can be cut (the Severance).
- With the paper-doll setup, each slot is its own layer, so a swap changes the look at once.

**The Inventory page (pass 205):** the Equipment page shows only what you wear. A found ability, spell, relic, ring or key was known only by the banner at its pickup, so a player who missed the Acorn Charm's line had no way to learn it was in the pack. The pause menu gains **Inventory**, under Equipment. It is read only, and lists what you have under headers, in a fixed order: Abilities (Climbing Glove, Delver's Pick, Featherstep Boots, Elven Stride, Seer's Lens, Stillstone, Rune Dagger), Spells (the readied one marked Ready), Relics (Acorn Charm, Briar Wand), Rings with how many of the four you hold, Keys with their counts, and the Potion, Ring and Stillglass Shards you carry toward their next whole. Each row's line says what the thing does: the pickup's banner line, or for the Delver's Pick the Foreman's, and for Kindle and Wisplight their scroll's. Nothing you lack is shown, so the page spoils no find, and a new game shows "Nothing yet". `Menus.inventory_rows`. Test: `test_the_inventory_page_lists_what_you_have_found`.

**Supplies and charts on the Inventory page (pass 269):** the page listed abilities, spells, relics, rings, keys and shards, but nothing you carry to use, and no charts. An Amber Draught showed nowhere at all once bought. Only the pause menu's "Use Barkbalm" row counted Barkbalms. Charts showed only as dimmed rooms on the map, so nothing told you which zones were still uncharted. Now a **Supplies** group lists Barkbalms and the Amber Draught with how many you carry and what each does. A **Charts, n of 8** group lists each chart you hold, and a greyed line counts those not yet charted ("The towns' cartographers sell the rest."). `test_the_inventory_lists_supplies_and_charts`. Screenshot: `/mnt/project-files/pass-269/inventory.png`.

**Short Sword (renamed pass 184):** the starting blade was called the Hand Dagger, but every swing of it is a slash, so Sean renamed it. Its id is `short_sword`; a save that still names `hand_dagger` (equipped, or in its tempers) is carried over by `GameState.sanitize` through `RENAMED_ITEMS`, tempers and all.

**Rune Dagger (built pass 184):** an elven throwing dagger bound by a rune, a tool beside the blade rather than a blade (Sean's idea, pass 184). It answers the one gap in the kit before the spells: a way to reach a hornet or a turret without spending magic.
- **Where:** on the floor of the Heartwood's Hornet Gallery, by the entrance sign (legend `}`, `levels/heartwood/hornet_gallery.room` at 8,21). The room teaches it: three hornets overhead and a Sap Spitter across the thorns. Since the dagger goes straight into a shield's hand here (the off hand, below), the room must be clearable with it alone, so each foe is in a throw's 160 px from ground with no thorns: straight up for a hornet, along the floor for the spitter. Two hornets hung 161 to 162 px up, a throw that only grazed them; pass 315 lowered each by a tile (to 8 and 11 rows down). `test_the_hornet_gallery_can_be_cleared_with_the_dagger` throws at each foe from its nearest safe spot.
- **The off hand (Sean, 2026-10-31):** the dagger is flicked from the left hand, the shield's arm, so the off hand holds a shield or the Rune Dagger, never both. The Equipment page's Shields section is now **Off Hand**: **Rune Dagger** first once found, which takes the shield off, then each shield owned. With a shield worn, Throw does nothing; with the hand free, there is no guard, parry or ward from a shield. Found while a shield is worn, the dagger goes straight into the hand (the shield to your back, as the banner says) so the Hornet Gallery that teaches it plays at once. It is a build choice: no boss needs a parry (5.2), and none needs the dagger. `GameState.offhand_free`, `Menus.OFFHAND_DAGGER`. Tests: `test_the_off_hand_holds_a_shield_or_the_rune_dagger`, `test_the_hand_dagger_is_the_short_sword_and_the_rune_dagger_is_found`. **Changed while the dagger flies (pass 447):** a player can throw, pause before it lands and strap a shield on. `test_the_off_hand_changes_while_the_dagger_flies` does it through the real controller: the dagger in the air finishes its flight and comes home alone, the shield then guards and Throw does nothing, and the dagger put back in the off hand from the Equipment page throws again.
- **Button:** Throw, on B or ; on the keyboard and the right bumper (RB) on a pad. Dash keeps the B button and the right trigger; the bumper it gave up was its third binding. Hold Up to throw it straight up.
- **Flight:** 420 px/s, point first, for 160 px (about ten tiles). It strikes the first foe it meets for 6, scaled by Keen Edge like the blade (`melee_mult`), with a 0.03 s hit-stop. Walls and gates stop it; it passes up through one-way planks. It lights the fog as it flies.
- **Return:** when its flight ends, on a foe, a wall or at the end of its reach, it breaks into motes of rune light and reforms at your hip 0.4 s later with a soft chime (`dagger_return`). One flies at a time. It costs no MP and is never lost; left in another room, it comes home all the same.
- **Not while** dashing, drinking, guarding, swinging, reeling from a hit or on a ladder. A press in the last 0.15 s before you can throw is held and goes the first frame you can, as Attack is (pass 192, `CombatTuning.throw_buffer`).
- **A sting, not a blow (pass 188):** it hurts what it hits, but what a blow does to a guard stays the blade's (and fire's). In bark (a Bark Knight, the Captain) it lands the bark's quarter and splits no plate; it stings a hexing Marsh Hag or the Matriarch without breaking the spell; and the bound Heart turns it even with the Severance in hand. Elsewhere it is an ordinary hit from where it flew: The Likeness's false double still bursts, a Lantern Widow's lantern still breaks, armor still halves a hit on the front. `Enemy.take_throw` sets `Enemy.thrown` while the hit lands, and each guard reads it in `_modify_damage`.
- **Gates nothing.** Every route it could help with already has another answer, so reachability does not change and it is never required. It is not temperable (it is not in the blade slot), so the five Stillglass Shards still cover the four blades.
- **Drawing:** code-drawn, in flight and as a pickup. The hero sheet is drawn empty-handed for a paper-doll setup (Sean, pass 184), so the blade and the dagger are separate layers over the body, never baked into it.
- Built in `scripts/combat/rune_dagger.gd` (`RuneDagger`) and `Player._try_throw`. Tests: `test_combat.gd` (flight, hit, one at a time, no MP, return, Up, walls and reach) and `test_systems.gd` (the rename, save migration, bindings, the pickup).

**Elven Helm (built pass 118; its own slot since pass 185):**
- A helmet, in the equipment's fourth slot (`equipment.helmet`), found and never sold. It lies in a hidden pocket at the Everbloom's Threshold (`evercrown/threshold`), behind fake wall west of the shrine (`secret: 1, 17, 6, 19`), so the Seer's Lens's seam shows the way in. It is laid by `prop: x, y, elven_helm`.
- Touch it and it goes straight on your head, like the Severance into your hand. Your health fills to the new top. Your shield stays in hand.
- It is worth one more mask (+20 HP, `hp` in `data/items.json`) and takes 10% off every hit (`defense` 0.1). It does not guard or parry. The mask comes from the gear, not the base stat, so a save keeps it through `refresh_stats`.
- **Stacking with armor:** the helmet and the armor each cut what the other lets through, so the cuts multiply rather than add: damage taken is `(1 - armor) x (1 - helmet)`. Under the Elven Plate (60%) it lets 0.4 x 0.9 = 36% through (64% less damage); under Leather (15%), 76.5%. With no armor it is 10%. Damage taken is still at least 1.
- **Why its own slot (Sean, pass 185):** in the shield's slot it replaced the shield, so players with the Warding Shield had to choose between the shield's parries and the helmet's mask. As a helmet it adds to every build.
- **Old saves:** a save that wore it as a shield (`equipment.shield == "elven_helm"`) wears it as a helmet, and its hand gets the Warding Shield: the helmet had guarded and turned shots exactly like the Warding Shield, and while it was worn every shield showed as owned in the shops, so nothing better could have been bought (`GameState.sanitize`).
- **Drawing (paper-doll):** the hero's sheets are drawn bare-headed. The helmet is its own layer: Sean's pixel helmet (his Autosprite pixel conversion of the right-side reference, `armor/battle-helmet/pixel-right.png`, kept at its own pixel grid in `art/elven_helm/right.png`), shrunk by `tools/art/helmetgen.py` to 16 sheet px wide (about the bare head's width at the 2x density: Sean found 19 too big), outlined and written to `assets/sprites/elven_helm.png`. `PlayerVisual._draw_helmet` sets it on the crown of the head, mirrored with the facing. It replaced the pass-185 code-drawn helmet, which Sean found too round and shiny. `autosprite_import.head_point` finds the crown in each frame's pixels on import (the cloak masked out, a blade or fist opened away, a crown that jumps over both neighbours dropped back), so every frame of `player.json` and of the five body sets carries a `head` point (manifest flag `"head": true`).

**Warding Shield (built pass 91):**
- It guards 70%, like the Kite Shield.
- A parry also catches small shots no other shield can parry: Sap Spitter globs and Glass Slug globs (group `small_projectile`). It catches Snapcrown's seeds too, which any shield can parry.
- The caught shot flies back as a mote of shield light, glowing with the colour of what it caught (`ReflectedShot`). It goes at 340 px/s toward the nearest foe within 260 px on the side you face, or straight ahead, and strikes the first foe it meets for 16.
- Feathers, gears and shadow bolts already turn back on any shield's parry, and still do.
- A caught sap glob leaves no puddle.

You start with no shield: Block and parry come with the Buckler, the first purchase most players make in Hearthroot. Gear values live in `data/items.json`:

| Item | Values |
|------|--------|
| Short Sword | 8 light / 14 heavy |
| Wayfarer's Sword | 11 light / 19 heavy, +8 px reach |
| Tanned Jerkin | 15% less damage taken (minimum 1) |
| Buckler | Guard halves damage (5.3) |
| Riveted Coat | 30% less damage taken |
| Kite Shield | Guard blocks 70% of a hit |
| Amber Draught | Carry one; a fatal hit uses it and revives you in place at full health and full magic (pass 345) |

**The Amber Draught (pass 345):** the Amber Draught revived you at full health but left your magic where it was, so a mage who died empty rose empty. Now a drink fills both. What stays new: the Amber Draught is drunk by the fatal hit itself, and you carry only one, so it is a second life and never a heal you sip mid-fight (the flask is that, 5.1). Held by `test_elixir_revives_once_and_is_limited_to_one`. **In a real fight (pass 433):** that test lands its fatal blow with a call on a hero standing still. `test_the_elixir_saves_a_real_fight` walks a hero at 5 health into the Rot Dwarf with Right held on the real controller, so his swing is the fatal blow. The hit drinks the Amber Draught: the hero rises at full health and full magic, and the toast reads "The Amber Draught burns. You rise again." Its doubled grace carries the hero, Right still held, through the dwarf and past him untouched. With no Amber Draught, the same swing met the same way fells the hero.

### 5.3 Combat moves

- **Slash:** 3-hit ground combo, 2-hit air combo. Each hit pushes the player back 4 px (knockback against enemy on hit; `slash_recoil` 120 px/s, raised from 90 in pass 105, which only gave 2.3 px). A side blow that a foe's guard turns (a Rot Dwarf's shield, a Shade Knight's) pushes you back harder, at 200 px/s (`turned_blade_push`, pass 442), so a turned blade reads as a clang, not a cut. **A guardian's guard, too (pass 444):** four guardians turn blows with guards of their own (Thane Vorrund warded on his throne, Durgan's open cockpit from the side, the Everbloom's core, and the Evil Heart to any blade but the Severance), and each played its thud but pushed back only as a landed cut does, so a turned blade on a guardian read as a hit. Each now pushes the blade back as every other guard does (`Enemy.turn_blade`). `test_a_guardians_guard_turns_the_blade_back` slashes each for real: the blow does nothing, and the hero is pushed back at 200 px/s. A hit stops a foe's thinking for 0.18 s (`Enemy.HITSTUN`); the heavy finisher drives it back 1.8x as hard and holds it 1.8x as long (0.32 s), so a full combo buys a breath.
- **Up-slash:** hits above. In midair, an up-slash that lands on a foe holds you up: a fall turns into a 150 px/s rise (`up_slash_pop`, about 7 px), once per swing, never adding to a jump (built pass 105; it applies to every foe, not only launcher types, so juggling a flier slows your fall without letting you climb).
- **Down-strike (Delver's Pick):** see pogo in 6.6.
- **Thrust (Blade skill, pass 149):** hold Attack past 0.4 s and let go for a charged lunge (see 9).
- **Parry (shield):** tap Block within 0.12 s of a hit: no damage, 140 ms hit-stop, enemy staggered. Holding Block reduces damage by the shield's percent but drains MP.
- **Guard break (pass 242):** a guard held with less MP than a blow costs used to fail in silence: the shield stayed up in the guard pose, at guard speed, and the blow landed whole with only the hurt yelp, so it read as a guard that did nothing. Now it breaks: a dry crack of splitting wood over a dropping knock (`guard_break`, never the held guard's `guard`), a burst of guard dust and a shake. The blow still lands whole, and the shield stays down until Block is let go and raised again (`Player.guard_broken`), so you never hold up a guard you can't pay for. `test_a_guard_with_no_mp_breaks_and_drops`.
- **Spells:** one equipped spell on the Spell button, cycled with the Next Spell button (the shoulders are taken by Throw and Block, so it is the right stick click on a pad; changed in pass 57). MP regenerates slowly and on melee hits.
- **Damage taken:** 0.8 s invulnerability, 0.15 s hit-stop, knockback, screen flash. The flash is a red glow at the screen's edge that fades in about 0.4 s; it never covers the middle, and the Flashes option scales it (built pass 98). Every hit yelps (`hurt`). A blow that costs a whole mask (20 HP) or more after armor adds a low body thud and a crunch of grit under it (`hurt_heavy`, pass 198). Since passes 194 and 196 the deep zones' foes and guardians land such blows through par armor while the Heartwood's do not, so you hear the difference a zone makes, and whether your armor keeps up.
- **Low health (built pass 98):** at one mask (20 HP) or less, a muffled heartbeat sounds every 0.85 s and the screen's edge pulses faintly red in time with it. It stops the moment you heal above a mask. **On the beat (pass 484):** the edge's pulse ran on a clock of the HUD's own, started when the HUD was made, while the heartbeat counts from the moment you fall to one mask, so the two were out of step by chance: in the test's case the edge was at its dimmest just as each beat sounded. The pulse now reads the heartbeat itself (`Player.heartbeat_phase`), brightest in the frame the beat sounds and dimmest halfway to the next, at every Game speed. `test_the_red_edge_swells_on_the_heartbeat_at_every_game_speed` listens for each beat at 100 and 70% and finds the edge at its brightest on every one.

**Starting combat values** (in `scripts/player/combat_tuning.gd`). Every row is held to this table by `test_the_starting_combat_values_match_bible_5_3` (pass 202; until then about a third of the rows were only read back from the tuning by the tests that played them), and each has a play test. Since pass 330 `test_every_combat_number_is_written_in_bible_5_3` also reads this section: every number in the tuning must be written here, and each spell's row must name its own cost, damage and speed from `scripts/combat/spells.gd`. Until then only Torrent had a row, and Stormcall's, Kindle's, Wisplight's and Wither's numbers were held by nothing but tests that read them back from the code: Since pass 346 `test_every_combat_number_is_written_in_its_own_row` holds each number to its own row (or move) and its exact form: the looser reading rounded any number under 100 to a whole one, so the five hit-stops, Executioner's 1.35 and the 1.2 s before you rise passed on any "0" or "1" though 5.3 never wrote them. The Hit-stop and Executioner rows, and the wait in the Wither row, are new. Since pass 347 that strict reading (`TestSuite.written_exactly_in`) is the only one: the looser helper is gone, and the tests that still used it (all of 5.3 and 6 at once, the spells' rows, 6.14's rows) read strictly and still pass.

| Value | Setting |
|-------|---------|
| Light hit / heavy third hit | 8 / 14 damage (Short Sword) |
| Swing reach (pass 362) | a slash cuts a box 36 px long and 26 px tall before you, from inside the body to 34 px past its front edge; an up-slash a box 34 px wide and 36 px tall, reaching 32 px over your head; a Thrust a point 52 px long and 13 px tall from the body's front edge. A blade's reach (5.2) lengthens a slash and a Thrust by that many px, and its arc heightens a slash by that many and a Thrust by half. The down-strike is 6.6's 28x24 |
| Combo window, cooldowns | 0.40 s to chain; 0.20 s, 0.20 s, 0.38 s after hits 1 to 3 |
| Attack buffer | 0.15 s |
| Throw buffer (pass 192) | 0.15 s: a Throw pressed while you can't throw yet (a swing or a dash still out, a drink, the Rune Dagger still reforming) goes the first frame you can |
| Hit-stop (6.7) | 50 ms on a hit, 90 ms on a heavy hit, 80 ms on a kill, 150 ms when you are hit, 350 ms when you fall |
| Parry window | 0.12 s from the Block press; 140 ms hit-stop, enemy staggered 1 s, +6 MP |
| Guard (Buckler) | 50% less damage, 8 MP per blocked hit, 40% walk speed; with less than 8 MP the guard breaks (pass 242) |
| Knockback when hit | 160 px/s back, 220 px/s up, 0.18 s control lock |
| Low health | 20 HP or less: heartbeat every 0.85 s, red edge pulse |
| MP | 60 max, +2/s, +4 per melee hit on an enemy |
| Sapflask | 2 charges, +40 HP, 0.7 s to drink, cancelled (charge kept) if hit |
| Torrent | 15 MP, 12 damage, 300 px/s |
| Stormcall | 18 MP, 16 damage, 380 px/s |
| Kindle | 20 MP, 18 damage in the burst, thrown at 170 px/s across and 150 px/s up |
| Wisplight | 22 MP, 14 damage, homing; with Wisp Swarm (9) three orbs of 10 for one cast's MP |
| Wither | 40 MP, 36 damage to every foe the ring passes; a guardian takes 18 |
| Spell reach (pass 370) | how far each carries over open ground, cast standing: a Torrent bolt flies 1.2 s, about 355 px (22 tiles); a Stormcall bolt only 0.7 s, about 260 px (16 tiles), the price of its speed, and leaps 72 px to a dead conduit; Kindle bursts where it lands, about 113 px ahead on flat floor, striking all within 20 px (or in the air after 1.6 s); a Wisplight orb lives 2.2 s, flies at 230 px/s and locks on the nearest foe within 240 px; Wither's ring rolls out to 300 px over 0.45 s; the Rune Dagger (5.2) flies 160 px at 420 px/s and comes home |
| War Glove (rare drop, 7) | blade hits x1.5 for 15 s |
| Thrust (skill, 9) | hold Attack 0.4 s, let go: heavy hit x1.5, a 320 px/s lunge for 0.16 s on the floor, 0.35 s cooldown |
| Executioner (skill, 9) | melee blows on a guardian in its opening x1.35 |
| Gold coins | Magnet after 0.4 s within 100 px |
| Relics (pass 201, 5.2) | the Acorn Charm: blade hits x1.1; the Briar Wand: Torrent x1.5 |
| Death (5.4) | 60% of the purse dropped where you fell (on the last safe ground if that was not the room's own floor); you rise at the last rest 1.2 s after you fall |
| Spikes and thorns (pass 282) | 20 damage (one mask) before armor; you are set back on the last safe ground |
| Casting (pass 282) | 0.35 s between casts; a press made sooner casts nothing and is not saved |
| Bread / great loaf (pass 282, 7) | +15 HP / +40 HP (the loaf: a slain Silt Adder's). A slain Bog Puffcap always leaves bread (pass 289) |
| Drops (pass 290, 7) | each kill rolls once: bread 8%, a mana orb 10% (+12 MP), poison 7% (Tower foes on, `poison_drops`; skull-marked, sickens you), a rare item 2.5% (the War Glove 60%, else an Barkbalm). A foe with a sure drop (the Silt Adder, the Puffcap) leaves it instead of rolling. Taking any drop shows a toast that says what it gave (pass 301: the mana orb was the only one that showed nothing, so its +12 MP was nowhere a player could read it) |

**How far a swing reaches (pass 362):** every foe is placed and tuned against the reach of a swing, but no line of 5.3 gave it. The boxes were three constants in the player's code (`Player.SLASH_SIZE`, `UP_SLASH_SIZE`, `THRUST_LENGTH`), and the blades' reach in 5.2 named only the two later swords. Only one test touched any of it, a relative one: the Broad Sword's box grows by its reach and arc. The Swing reach row now gives every box, and `test_every_swing_reaches_as_far_as_5_3_says` holds each blade's reach and arc to 5.2. It swings each blade for real and measures the hit box against the row, then strikes a training dummy standing at the edge of the Short Sword's reach and misses one standing just past it.

**Every combat button answers on its frame (pass 386):** pass 384 timed the moves through the real controller (6.7, Input), but the fight's own buttons were still only ever scripted. `test_every_combat_button_answers_on_its_frame` presses each through `Input` and finds it answered in the first physics step that sees the press: Block raises the guard and opens the 0.12 s parry window, Spell casts (the MP is spent and the bolt is out), Next Spell turns to the next spell, Sapflask starts the drink, the Stillstone stops time, and Throw lets the Rune Dagger fly. The Thrust is a release, not a press: Attack held past its 0.4 s charge lunges in the very step the button comes up, since a held button is read as held or not rather than as just pressed. **The Rune Dagger, by the buttons (pass 410):** that test found Throw answered, but the dagger's aim, its buffer and Sean's off-hand rule were only ever scripted. `test_the_rune_dagger_answers_the_real_controller` plays them through `Input`. Throw hurls it the way you face, and with Up held it goes straight up. Pressed in the last 0.15 s of a swing, it waits and goes as the swing ends. With a shield on the arm, Throw does nothing, since the off hand holds one or the other. **An ordinary foe parried with the buttons (pass 418):** pass 412 parried Thane Vorrund by the buttons, but the parry window against an ordinary foe and the held guard were only ever scripted. `test_an_ordinary_foe_is_parried_through_the_real_controller` presses Block for real against the Rot Dwarf's swing: seen 7 steps (0.12 s) before the blade lands, it parries, the dwarf reels, no damage lands and the parry pays its 6 MP; seen 8 steps before, the whole blow lands. Block held from long before guards instead: no reel, the blow halved from 20 to 10, and MP paid. **Being hit with the buttons (pass 426):** `test_being_hit_matches_the_bible` takes the blow standing still, but a player is usually holding a direction and often drinking. `test_being_hit_answers_the_real_controller` presses Heal for real, holds Right toward the Rot Dwarf and lets his swing land: the blow spoils the drink and keeps the flask's charge, it knocks you back against the held Right for the whole 0.18 s control lock, and once the lock is over the same held Right, never pressed again, steers you while the i-frames still run. **A Thrust is not let go by a pause or a hold (pass 434):** a player often opens the pause menu, takes a door or reads a sign with a Thrust's charge full. The Attack let go meanwhile was seen as a release once play went on, and the hero lunged out of the menu, the door's scroll or the dialog. A hold now spends the charge, and an Attack let go while the game was paused does not release it. Held through the pause, the charge is kept and the player's own release lunges. `test_a_thrust_is_not_let_go_by_a_pause_or_a_hold` lets go in the pause menu and through a hold, and neither lunges, then holds Attack through a pause and lets go in play, which lunges. **Spells by the buttons (pass 442):** pass 386 saw Spell answered on its frame, but no spell cast through `Input` had met a foe. Cast at a Rot Dwarf across the room, the Torrent met his shield, and the shield's push, meant for a blade at arm's length, shoved the caster back from 200 px away (a Stormcall bolt, a Kindle burst or a thrown Rune Dagger did the same). A turned slash, meanwhile, was pushed only by its ordinary recoil, which wrote over the push. Now only the blade's own blow is pushed back when a guard turns it (`Player.blade_turned`), and harder than a cut that lands. `test_spells_answer_the_real_controller_at_a_real_foe` presses Spell for real at the dwarf: the bolt meets his shield with its thud and the hero stays put, and cast as his axe goes up it strikes for its 12. Next Spell readies Stormcall and Spell casts it; a press inside the 0.35 s cooldown casts nothing, then or after; with Block held Spell casts nothing, and lowered the next press casts. A slash his shield turns pushes the hero back at 200 px/s. **The Thrust at a shield, by the buttons (pass 450):** pass 434 let the Thrust go with the real Attack, but no real Thrust had met a guard. `test_the_thrust_answers_the_real_controller_at_a_shield` holds Attack for real five tiles from a Rot Dwarf (the first cut falls short) and lets go. Into his shield the Thrust is turned like a cut: no harm, the guard's thud and no cut, the lunge stopped and the hero pushed back at 200 px/s. Into his back it bites for the heavy hit times 1.5, is heard as a cut with no thud, and the lunge stops at him rather than carrying the hero into his body.

**The guard, held (pass 394):** pass 386 timed Block's press, the parry window opening, but the guard itself is read from Block held (`block_held`), and no test had held it through the real controller. `test_the_guard_stands_while_block_is_held` does: the guard stands past the parry window for as long as Block is down, drops in the step it is let go, and a broken guard (pass 242) stays down while Block is still held, is mended by letting go, and rises again on the next press.

**The combo's windows, pressed (pass 402):** the two windows that decide whether a second swing chains were held only with a scripted intent: the attack buffer (0.15 s, `attack_buffer`) and the combo window (0.40 s, `combo_window`). `test_the_combo_windows_hold_through_the_real_controller` swings once through `Input` and presses Attack again a set number of steps later. A press in the last 9 steps (0.15 s) of the first hit's 12-step (0.20 s) cooldown is held, and every such press swings on the same step, the one the cooldown ends. A press one step before the buffer opens is dropped. A press 23 steps after the swing chains into the second hit; one at 24 steps (0.40 s) starts the combo over.

**Combat at every game speed (pass 472):** pass 470 found that the dash's timer cut its stride at 70% game speed, and combat's timers had never run at any speed but 100%. Game speed (14) is an assist, so slowing the game must never make a window tighter to hit. `test_combat_windows_only_widen_as_the_game_slows` presses the real buttons at 100, 90, 80 and 70%. Counted in frames on screen, the parry window (8, 8, 9 and 11 frames) and a slash's cutting time (6, 6, 7 and 8) only grow as the game slows. Each stays within one step of its game time (0.12 s and 0.10 s). The Thrust's lunge carries at least its 51 px at every speed, and no more than one step past it. All held: no bug. **Thrown and cast at every game speed (pass 480):** the Rune Dagger and the spells had been thrown and cast at full speed only. `test_thrown_and_cast_reach_holds_at_every_game_speed` presses Throw and Spell for real at 100 and 70% across an empty hall. Each carries as far either way, within a step: the dagger 154 and 157 px, Torrent 355 and 357, Stormcall 260 and 262, Kindle 116 and 121. The dagger comes home in 25 frames on screen at full speed and 35 at 70%, never sooner. All held: no bug.

### 5.4 Death and recovery

On death you return to the last Ringreader or shrine. You drop **60% of carried gold** as a glowing purse where you died. Reach it to recover it; die first and it is gone. XP and items are never lost. (Deviation, pass 297: in the source game you also start again at the last Ringreader's House you visited and keep every item, but "his experience will be the lowest point for that rank and the amount of money he has when he recovers will also be determined by his rank" (the source game's manual). So a death undid a rank's progress and took a share of gold no skill could win back. The remake keeps the restart and the items, never takes experience, and makes the gold a purse you can walk back to: the loss still stings, but it can be undone by playing well.) The purse is part of the save (`data.purse`: its room, where it lies and the gold in it, as `value`), so quitting and loading leaves it waiting. Until pass 250 loading asked for the gold under another name (`gold`) and threw every purse away, so a death followed by a load lost the 60% for good; a save that names it `gold` is still read. `test_a_dropped_purse_survives_a_save_and_load`. **Ground you can come back to (pass 293):** the purse is left where you fell only when you fell on the room's own floor. Killed in the air, on footing that can go (6.13) or by a hazard, you leave it on the last safe ground you stood on (`Player.safe_position`). It had lain wherever the blow found you. Killed in the Bloom's gap or Gnarlmaw's pit, it lay in the pit, and the next time the room loaded the floor was whole again around it: the gold was buried in the tiles for good. `test_a_death_in_a_torn_pit_leaves_the_purse_beside_it`. **A purse on floor that tears (pass 300)** moves with the set-back spot to the nearer tile beside the new pit. Left on the leaf from a death earlier in the fight, it had hung over the thorns, and taking it back cost a mask. `test_a_purse_on_torn_leaf_moves_beside_the_gap`. **Raised at the Ringreader, by the buttons (pass 449):** rest with a Ringreader by the real Up and Jump, walk by the real Right into the spikes with one heart left, and you fall with 60 of your 100 gold in a purse on the ground short of them; you rise at the Ringreader's side (not the room's start), made whole, and the real Right walks you back to take the 60 again. `test_a_death_raises_you_at_the_ringreader_you_rested_with`. **The rise, by the buttons (pass 456):** a player falls holding a direction and often keeps holding it. You rise made whole with 1.0 s of i-frames, and whatever you were doing (a swim, a ladder, a swing, a drink) is over. `test_the_rise_answers_the_real_controller` falls with the real Right held: it moves nothing while you lie dead, and once you rise the same held Right, never pressed again, walks you on at a run. A Jump pressed in the first step after the rise jumps. Spikes touched at once do no harm for that 1.0 s, and then they do.

### 5.5 Damage balance sheet (pass 194)

The rubric (15.3) asks for a damage balance sheet within targets. Until pass 194 there was none, and drawing it up showed the curve upside down: a Rot Dwarf in the Heartwood swung for 20, a Shade Knight in Vorrhallow thrust for 18, and by then Knight's Plate took 45% off. Against the kit you carry there, the deep zones' worst blows cost half a mask where the Heartwood's cost nearly one, while your health had doubled.

**Threat.** Every blow a foe or a foe's shot lands is multiplied by its zone's threat (`CombatTuning.ZONE_THREAT`, applied in `Player.receive_attack` through `Room.threat`). Since pass 196 a boss's arena takes its zone's threat too (below). A world hazard such as a fire seal stays at 1. Hearthroot, the gym and the Inner Rings are off the sheet and stay at 1.

| Zone | Threat | Par kit (the critical path's shops and finds by then) | Par slash | Hardest blow through par armor | Slashes to fell its foes |
|------|--------|------|-----------|------|------|
| Heartwood | 1.0 | Wayfarer's Sword, Leather | 11 | 17 (Rot Dwarf swing, a Hornet's touch) | 2 to 4 |
| Tower | 1.0 | Wayfarer's Sword, Leather | 11 | 20 (Iron Dwarf slam) | 2 to 6 |
| Haar | 1.4 | Wayfarer's Sword +1 temper, Riveted Coat | 13 | 18 (Drowned Dwarf slam, Lantern Ghost lunge) | 2 to 4 |
| High Boughs | 1.5 | Wayfarer's Sword +1, Riveted Coat | 13 | 21 (a Hornet's touch) | 2 to 5 |
| Carillon | 1.6 | Wayfarer's Sword +2, Riveted Coat | 14 | 22 (a Hornet's touch) | 2 to 4 |
| Vorrhallow | 2.0 | Wayfarer's Sword +2, Knight's Plate (the Severance past Thane Vorrund, since pass 218) | 14 (18) | 20 (Shade Knight thrust) | 3 to 4 |
| Sunken Spring | 2.0 | Severance, Knight's Plate (it lies past Thane Vorrund; pass 218) | 18 | 20 | 2 to 4 |
| The Evercrown | 2.0 | Severance, Knight's Plate (foes corrupted, 1.5x health) | 18 | 26 (Iron Dwarf slam) | 2 to 6 |

**Targets**, held by `test_the_damage_balance_sheet_stays_within_its_targets`, which reads every room's foes, their `*DAMAGE` and `*CONTACT` constants and their shots:
- Each zone's hardest ordinary blow costs 0.75 to 1.4 masks (15 to 28 HP) through its par armor, and no foe anywhere hits harder than that.
- Every foe falls in 2 to 7 slashes of the par blade (heavy finishers, spells and skills only shorten it).
- Threat never falls along the critical path.

So a hit costs about a mask from the first root to the Everbloom, and what grows is your health (5 masks to 14): you get sturdier as you go, but no zone's foes go soft. Players ahead of par (the Broad Sword in Vorrhallow, the Elven Plate and Helmet) feel it as power, and players behind it (Leather in Vorrhallow: 31) feel the push to visit the smith and the armorer.

**The shield on the sheet (pass 226).** Since pass 225 a shield wards a foe's sorcery (5.2), but the sheet still weighed every blow through armor alone. The par kit now names a shield: the Buckler in the Heartwood and the Tower (Hearthroot, 150 g), the Kite Shield from the Haar (Amberlee, 900 g), the Warding Shield from Vorrhallow (Carillon, 3,600 g). A sorcerous blow (a script that joins `Player.MAGIC`) is weighed as the game takes it: threat, then the ward, then the armor. That showed the Marsh Hag Matriarch's hex, her whole threat, falling to 8 through the Kite Shield and Riveted Coat, under the guardians' floor of 10 (0.5 masks). The hex (`MireGrasp.DAMAGE`, the hags' too) is 16 now, where it was 12: 11 through the par kit, and 15 with no shield. The other sorcery stays as it was: The Likeness's bolts and Thane Vorrund's shards were never their hardest blows, and the core's beam still costs 10 through the Warding Shield and Knight's Plate.

**The guardians (pass 196).** Pass 194 left the arenas at 1, meaning to tune each boss on its own. Drawing up their sheet showed that this made the deep guardians softer than the corridors leading to them. Through Knight's Plate, Thane Vorrund's worst blow cost 12 and the Everbloom's cleave 11, while a Shade Knight in Vorrhallow's halls landed 20. Pass 196 lets each arena take its zone's threat (`Room.threat` has no boss exception, and `Player.receive_attack` scales a boss's blows and shots like any foe's). Hardest blow through the zone's par armor, after threat:

| Zone | Guardians (hardest blow) |
|------|--------------------------|
| Heartwood | Gnarlmaw 17 |
| Tower | Durgan 20, the Foreman 19 |
| Haar | Gloamweaver 20, the Haar Wyrm 15, the Marsh Hag Matriarch 11 (her hex, through the Kite Shield's ward) |
| High Boughs | Snapcrown 25, the Bark Knight Captain 21 |
| Carillon | Skyrend 25 |
| Vorrhallow | Thane Vorrund 24, the Clockwork Alpha 22, the Likeness 22 |
| Sunken Spring | the Drowned Warden 24 |
| The Evercrown | the Everbloom's avatar 22, its heart 15, its veins 10 (the core's beam, through the Warding Shield's ward) |

`test_the_guardians_hit_as_hard_as_their_zone` holds every guardian's hardest blow to 0.5 to 1.5 masks through par armor, and each zone's guardians to at least 0.75 masks. That way no boss is softer than the foes that lead to it. The Matriarch is the lowest: her threat is the hex, not her blows. The Haar Wyrm's rising flood (`HydraFlood`, 8) wears you down as a hazard does and is not scaled. A boss rush runs in the same arenas, so it takes the same threat, against the endgame kit a 100% save carries.

**The guardians' health (pass 204).** The rubric asks for balanced boss health (15.3), but nothing measured it. In slashes of the zone's par blade (5.5):

| Zone | Guardians (par slashes) |
|------|--------------------------|
| Tower | Durgan 24 (33 until pass 212), the Foreman 20 |
| Haar | the Haar Wyrm 24, Gloamweaver 20, the Marsh Hag Matriarch 18 |
| High Boughs | Snapcrown 20, the Bark Knight Captain 19 |
| Carillon | Skyrend 23 |
| Vorrhallow | Thane Vorrund 23, the Clockwork Alpha 19, the Likeness 18 (Severance slashes; 320 HP since pass 218, was 240) |
| Sunken Spring | the Drowned Warden 38 (Severance slashes; 680 HP since pass 218, was 520; optional, the hardest fight) |
| The Evercrown | the Everbloom: its avatar 20, then its heart 17 (37 in all) |

`test_the_guardians_last_as_long_as_their_place` holds every guardian to 15 to 40 par slashes. Since pass 324 it also holds this table's counts to the code, and every guardian's health the bible names beside its class ("`DrownedWarden`, 680 HP") to its `MAX_HP`. The Warden's room note still said 520 HP from before pass 218, and nothing had caught it. A mini-boss (the Foreman, the Matriarch, the Bark Captain, the Clockwork Alpha) is no longer than 25 and never outlasts its zone's own guardian. The Everbloom, avatar and heart together, is the longest fight on the critical path; only the optional Warden runs longer. No guardian outlasts the longest guardian of every later zone by more than two slashes (pass 212), so the fights grow, or at least hold, as the game goes on. Durgan had 360 HP, 33 par slashes: the second guardian ran longer than any required fight before the finale, and since her hull lets only a quarter of a side blow through, her real fight ran longer still. She has 260 now (24), level with the Haar Wyrm, the longest later guardian, and still above the Tower's Foreman. Her phases are fractions of her health, so they come at the same points in the fight. Gnarlmaw's 600 is spread over a plated body (a quarter damage) and a soft tail, and the Everbloom's veins are a climb, not a health bar, so neither is on the sheet. Heavy finishers, spells, the Acorn Charm and skills only shorten a fight. Slashes are a proxy for the rubric's 2.5 to 5 minutes: most of a fight is spent reading tells and waiting for openings, not swinging.

**Past Thane Vorrund (pass 218).** Since pass 217 Thane Vorrund gives up the Severance, so everything past his throne is fought with it (slash 18, against the Wayfarer's Sword +2's 14): the Glass Rise, the Crown Gate and its mirror, and the Sunken Spring below the Rise. The sheet still assumed the Wayfarer's Sword there. The Likeness fell in 14 Severance slashes, under the sheet's floor of 15, and the optional Warden in 29, no longer the hardest fight. Each gets back its slash count: The Likeness 240 to 320 HP (18) and the Drowned Warden 520 to 680 (38). Their phases are fractions of their health, so they come at the same points. The sheet's par for the Sunken Spring is the Severance, and the Likeness is measured against it (`PAST_VORRUND_KIT`, `PAST_VORRUND_BOSSES` in `test_combat.gd`). The Rise's foes still take 2 to 7 of its slashes. `test_the_sheet_past_thane_vorrund_holds_the_severance` holds the kit to the game: Vorrund's reward is that blade, and the Rise, the Crown Gate and the Sunken Spring lie past his throne.

**A guardian's brood answers to the sheet (pass 258):** a foe a guardian calls into its fight is held to the same rows as a room foe: 2 to 7 slashes of its zone's par blade, and no blow past 1.4 masks through par armor. The Matriarch's daughters (Marsh Hags) and the Alpha's pack (Clockwork Hounds) also stand in rooms, so the sheet already held them. Snapcrown's Mothlings (8.5) stand nowhere else, and with 10 HP they fell to one slash of the tempered Wayfarer's Sword (13). They have 20 now, two slashes. `test_a_guardians_brood_answers_to_the_sheet` finds every Enemy a guardian's script makes and requires it to be a room foe on the sheet, a guardian's shot on its row, or listed in `SUMMONED` with its zone and checked there.

**A brood that dies with its guardian pays nothing (pass 266):** the Bloom, the Matriarch and the Clockwork Alpha took their Mothlings, daughter and hounds down with them by striking them as a blow would. So each one sprayed gold, rolled a drop, gave experience, counted as a kill for Wanderer's Grace, and stacked a kill's hitstop and shake onto the guardian's own death. None of it was the player's doing. Now they `perish` (`Enemy.perish`): they dissolve and cry out like any foe, but pay nothing, count as no kill, and leave the guardian's death its own weight. A brood the player strikes down still pays as written. `test_a_brood_that_dies_with_its_guardian_pays_nothing` also reads every guardian's script for a brood killed any other way.

---

## 6. Movement and Game-Feel Spec

All numbers are at the internal resolution (1 unit = 1 px at 640x360). Tile = 16 px. Player collision box = 12x40 px (sprite about 48x56). These are starting values for the movement prototype, tuned there and then frozen.

Every number in 6.1 to 6.6 and 6.8 to 6.12 is held to this text by a test that reads it from `MovementTuning` (`test_movement.gd`), and each one also has a test of what it does in play. The last left out were pinned in pass 208: the bounce-pod's launch (6.6), the glide's fall speed and brake (6.10), and the pogo's 45 ms hit-stop, which had been a bare number in the player's code and is now `pogo_hitstop`. Until pass 200 the run, the brakes, the turn, the jump cut and the fall limits were only read back from the tuning by their play tests, so a changed number would have moved its test along with it. Those pins still hold each number to a literal copied from here, so a number changed in the tuning and in its test together would have left this section saying the old one. Since pass 328, `test_every_movement_number_is_written_in_bible_6` reads this section itself: every number `MovementTuning` exports (62 of them) must be written somewhere in it, in a form the bible uses (165, 1,800, 0.10 s, 45 ms, 40%, or a derived one such as gravity to the whole px). A small number such as 2 is found almost anywhere, so the check catches the distinctive values, which are most of them.

### 6.1 Ground movement

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| Max run speed | 165 px/s (about 10 tiles/s) |
| Ground acceleration | 1,800 px/s² (reaches max in about 0.09 s) |
| Ground deceleration | 2,600 px/s² |
| Turnaround boost | deceleration x 1.6 when input opposes velocity |
| Analog walk (pass 183, Sean) | a stick walks into a run the farther it leans. Its lean (`PlayerInput.tilt`, 0 to 1 past the 0.35 deadzone) sets the pace, `MovementTuning.speed_fraction`: up to 0.45 it walks at 40% of a run (66 px/s); from there the pace climbs evenly to a full run at 0.9. Keys and the d-pad are always a full run, so every jump, dash and reach is the same at full tilt. The pace applies on the ground and in the air, so a light lean also places a short hop. Under 70% of a run the hero shows the `walk` pose: the sheet's `walk` clip once Sean's Autosprite walk is imported (`art/autosprite/manifest.json`), and until then the run clip slowed to the pace. Footsteps slow with it (up to 1.6x the run's 0.26 s) |
| Stick angle (pass 368) | a stick counts as Up or Down only 35 degrees or more from level (`MovementTuning.stick_vertical_angle`), within 55 degrees of straight up or down. A thumb pushing sideways drifts, and past the 0.35 deadzone the drift had read as Down or Up: a running jump on a one-way platform dropped through it, a fall became a fast fall, an air slash with the Delver's Pick a pogo, a slash an up-slash, and a run past a door walked in. Keys and the d-pad are digital and always count as pressed, diagonals too. A stick held in a drift and swung on to vertical presses Up as it starts to count, so a door still opens; Up held through a door or a dialog is not pressed again. `test_a_sideways_stick_drifting_down_is_not_down` drives the real input map and the real controller |
| Slope handling | snap to slopes up to 45°, no speed loss going up. Built in pass 115: 45° slope tiles (legend `/` rising east, `\` rising west) in every zone's tileset. You run up and down them at full run speed along the slope without leaving the ground (4 px floor snap), stand still on one without creeping, and jump from one as from flat ground. A slope's face is floor, never a wall: the Climbing Glove doesn't catch on it and the ledge clip leaves it alone. The box rests on its downhill corner, so the sprite is drawn down onto the slope (up to 6 px). Walkers treat a ramp as the end of their patrol. In the gym's hill, Leaf Landing's hump, the king's dais, the Sky Spring's dais and the Ore Vault's mound |

### 6.2 Jump

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| Full jump height | 72 px (4.5 tiles) |
| Time to apex | 0.36 s |
| Gravity (rising) | 1,111 px/s² (derived: 2h/t²) |
| Jump velocity | 400 px/s (derived: 2h/t) |
| Gravity (falling) | rising x 1.6 |
| Jump cut (release early) | vertical velocity x 0.45 |
| Apex hang | gravity x 0.5 while holding jump and \|vy\| < 40 px/s |
| Max fall speed | 520 px/s; 620 px/s while holding down |
| Air acceleration | 1,400 px/s²; air deceleration 900 px/s² |
| Coyote time | 0.10 s, off any footing: a solid floor, a one-way platform, a ledge that vanishes from under you (a Bark Knight's lance), a ledge into a wind current or drift pocket, and the water (pass 111) |
| Jump buffer | 0.12 s (works on ground, wall and double jump; a double jump pressed late in an air dash fires the frame the dash ends) |
| Corner correction | nudge up to 6 px horizontally on head bump; 4 px vertically on ledge clip, including while wall-sliding with the Climbing Glove (the clip steps you onto the ledge instead of catching you in a slide). Both limits are inclusive (pass 107). The ledge clip covers one-way platforms too: having no side to bump, their lip is found from above, and a drop-through is never lifted back (pass 111) Head correction (pass 139): moving across in the air, level or falling (a dash, a jump past its peak), a head that clips the top edge of an opening by up to 4 px (`head_correction_px`) is nudged down into it instead of stopping dead on the lip. A wall met below the head is left alone. |
| Minimum jump height | about 18 px (tap) |

### 6.3 Wall slide and wall jump (Climbing Glove)

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| Wall slide speed | max 90 px/s (falls slowly with dust particles, and the glove rasps: `wall_slide` on the catch and every 0.24 s after, pass 238) |
| Wall stick | 0.08 s before pushing away detaches |
| Wall jump velocity | 220 px/s outward, 380 px/s up (pushing away from the wall) |
| Climb jump velocity | 120 px/s outward, 380 px/s up (neutral or toward the wall; lock 0.06 s) |
| Input lock after wall jump | 0.10 s, then control blends back over the next 0.10 s (the blend starts when the lock ends; until pass 107 the two ran together, so there was no blend) |
| Same-wall climb | allowed (neutral wall jump goes up and slightly out, Hollow Knight style) |
| Wall coyote | 0.08 s after leaving a wall |

### 6.4 Double jump (Featherstep Boots)

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| Height | 48 px (3 tiles), velocity 326 px/s |
| Count | 1, refreshed by ground, wall touch, pogo, and stride seeds (the dash-reset orbs, pass 97: legend `r`, a glowing elf-seed floating in open air. Touched in the air with a double jump or dash spent, it bursts and gives both back, then grows again after 2.5 s. With nothing spent it stays whole. Seeds only help and never gate a route. In the gym over the dash gap, and three in the Sky Steps) |
| Feel | feather burst, small upward squash, wing whoosh |

### 6.5 Air dash (Elven Stride)

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| Speed and duration | 380 px/s for 0.15 s (about 57 px) |
| Gravity during dash | none; exits with vy = 0 |
| Invulnerability | first 0.10 s |
| Charges | 1 in air (2 with skill node), refreshed like double jump |
| Ground dash | yes, same values, 0.25 s cooldown. It hugs the floor (pass 121): a dash begun on the ground snaps to the floor from 10 px (`ground_dash_snap`, against the run's 4 px), so it rides a slope down as it already rode one up, instead of sailing off the ramp into the air. A ledge of a whole tile or more still drops away beneath it, and an air dash never snaps. Since pass 153 a thrust's lunge (9) hugs the floor the same way, and so does any slide on the floor still faster than a run (the end of a lunge or a dash), so a lunge that runs out halfway down a ramp doesn't hop off it |
| Dash buffer | 0.10 s (pass 146), like the jump's: a dash pressed when it can't go yet (on the cooldown, or in the air with the dash spent) is held and goes the first frame it can, at the cooldown's end, on the landing, or when a kill with Wanderer's Grace or a stride seed gives the dash back. Pressed earlier than that, it's dropped. Without the Elven Stride a press isn't held, so picking it up never fires a stale dash |
| Through a door (pass 443) | a dash taken through a side door goes on in the next room for the time it had left, so it carries as far as on open ground, then ends at a run on its cooldown (`Player.arrive_through_door`). Through any other door it ends as a dash ends. Until pass 443 the door cut the dash short with no cooldown, and the hero skidded into the room at 380 px/s for 8 frames without the dash, free to dash again at once. `test_a_dash_through_a_door_goes_on_in_the_next_room` |
| The dash at every game speed (pass 470) | Game speed (14) slows the game by shortening each physics step. The dash's last step had moved a whole step at a run, so at 70% it stopped 0.01 s early and fell 4 px short of its stride. The last step is now part dash, part run, so the dash covers its 57 px at every speed, and at most one run step beyond (`test_the_dash_keeps_its_stride_at_every_game_speed`) |
| Afterimages | 3 ghost frames, elf-green tint |

### 6.6 Pogo (Delver's Pick down-strike)

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| Trigger | attack + down while airborne |
| Hitbox | 28x24 px below the feet, active 0.14 s |
| Bounce | sets vy = -380 px/s (more than a jump cut, less than a full jump); holding jump gives -420 |
| Refreshes | double jump and dash |
| Valid targets | enemies, projectiles marked pogo-able, thorn bulbs, spikes, lanterns, cracked-stone tops |
| Hit-stop on pogo | 45 ms |
| Swing timing (pass 256) | a slash or up-slash cuts for 0.10 s (`slash_active`) and the down-strike for 0.14 s. An up-slash or down-strike then recovers for 0.28 s (`attack_cooldown`) before the next swing, however fast Attack is pressed. A press in its last 0.15 s is held (`attack_buffer`, 5.3) and swings the frame it ends. A side combo's recoveries are combat numbers (5.3, `combo_cooldowns` 0.20, 0.20 and 0.38 for the heavy finisher). A blade's swing speed scales all of them (5.2). These two had been in `MovementTuning` since the prototype but in no table and no test |
| Bounce-pod (High Boughs) | landing on a pod from above sets vy = -600 px/s (about 9 tiles of rise in play); release doesn't cut it; refreshes double jump and dash. The pod isn't solid, so walking into it does nothing, and a hop onto it launches **At every game speed (pass 481):** the pod catches the feet by their step through its top, and a step is shorter at a slower game speed, so the catch had been seen only at full speed. `test_a_bounce_pod_throws_as_high_at_every_game_speed` hops on it with the real Jump at 100, 90, 80 and 70%: it catches every time and throws 147, 149, 150 and 152 px, within 6 px of each other, so the ledges it opens open at every speed. All held: no bug. |

### 6.7 Juice checklist (all required before the movement milestone closes)

- **Squash and stretch:** a move sets the sprite to a shape (across x up) and it eases back at 16 per second (`SQUASH_RECOVERY`, 1 - e^(-16t): over half of the way in 3 frames, nearly all in 0.2 s). Jump 0.85x1.2; double jump 0.8x1.25; wall jump 1.15x0.9, stretched across the way you leap; land 1.25x0.8 at full fall speed (520 px/s), less for a slower fall, scaled by the fall speed; dash 1.3x0.8; pogo 0.8x1.2; bounce-pod 0.75x1.25; Feather Fall's catch 1.2x0.88; a swim stroke 1.1x0.9; a heavy swing 1.2x0.9; a thrust 1.3x0.85; a dagger throw 1.06x0.97; a cast 1.1x0.95; a parry 1.15x0.9 (`PlayerVisual.SQUASH`). **Written and held (pass 360):** this line had said "jump 0.85x1.2 for 3 frames" and "wall jump stretch toward direction", and named no other move. The shapes were bare numbers in the player's code, and no test held one. In fact a squash had never lasted 3 frames: it eased back from the frame it was set. `test_every_squash_is_written_and_felt` holds each shape to this line and jumps, double jumps, wall jumps, dashes and lands for real.
- **Dust:** on run start, turnaround, land, wall slide (every 0.06 s, the overshoot carried so the beat holds at 60 fps), wall jump, and a dash: a kick of dust thrown back off the heel on the ground, a pale puff of air in midair (pass 104). The dash also leaves a trail of afterimages. **What the feet kick up takes the ground (pass 264):** the run, jump, landing and floor-dash puffs were the same pale dust everywhere, even in the Haar's marsh, in amber sap and in Vorrhallow's standing water, though the steps there already sounded of mud, sap and water. They now take the ground underfoot (`Room.surface_at`, `Dust.GROUND_TINT`). Marsh mud flings dark brown clods and sap flings amber gobbets, and both fall back rather than drift. Standing water throws spray up from its surface (not from the floor under it), three drops more than the dust and higher, falling back in. Stone, moss, plank and iron keep the pale dust. `test_what_the_feet_kick_up_takes_the_ground_s_look`. Screenshot: `/mnt/project-files/pass-264/splash_24.png`.
- **Hit-stop:** 50 ms normal hit, 90 ms heavy, 80 ms when a foe falls, 150 ms when you are hit, 350 ms when you fall, 140 ms parry, 200 ms boss stagger (each phase break, pass 98), 350 ms boss kill (with slow-mo ramp): `Feel.boss_kill(seconds)` freezes 350 ms, then runs at 0.3x easing back up to full speed (quadratic) over the boss's slow-motion time, 1 to 1.5 s, and the screen flashes white at full strength, 1.0 (`BOSS_KILL_FLASH`, pass 356). Every boss uses it (pass 104; until then the kills held only 120 to 200 ms and the slow motion ended in a step). The kill, hurt and fall hit-stops were bare numbers in the code until pass 210; they now live in `CombatTuning` (`kill_hitstop`, `hurt_hitstop`, `death_hitstop`), held to this line by `test_the_starting_combat_values_match_bible_5_3` and in play by `test_a_kill_and_a_fall_hold_their_hit_stops`. A boss's own blows and the spells' hits keep their own.
- **Hurt and recoil (pass 344):** two numbers in the movement tuning act in a fight, and 5.3 tells them: a hit leaves 0.8 s of invulnerability (`hurt_invuln`, doubled by the mercy assist, 14), and a slash that lands pushes you back at 120 px/s (`slash_recoil`). Until pass 344 section 6 never named either, and its check (`test_every_movement_number_is_written_in_bible_6`) passed only because the land squash's "0.8" and an unrelated "120" stood elsewhere in the section. `test_every_movement_number_is_written_where_it_belongs` now holds each tuning number to its own subsection.
- **Screen shake:** trauma model (shake = trauma²), decays 1.5/s. Light hit 0.15, heavy 0.3, boss slam 0.6. Full shake moves the view at most 10 px across and 7 px up or down (`Feel.MAX_SHAKE_OFFSET`), with no roll. Option to reduce or turn it off.
- **Flash:** enemy flashes white for 2 frames on hit (pass 161: the flash and the tell pulses wash the foe's own silhouette white through `Enemy.wash` and `shaders/wash.gdshader`; until then fourteen code-drawn foes and bosses laid a translucent white box or disc over themselves, which read as a glitch); player flashes red and blinks during invulnerability, and the screen's edge flashes red (pass 98, 5.3).
- **Camera:** look-ahead 48 px in facing direction (eased over 0.4 s); vertical look-down when holding down for 0.4 s, and look-up (pass 133) when holding Up standing still for 0.4 s, 72 px each way (on a ladder Up climbs instead, and the climb lead takes over); lerp smoothing with deadzone 16x24 px: the camera aims at a point 28 px above the feet (`FOCUS_OFFSET`, about the chest) and closes the gap at a rate of 10 per second (`FOLLOW_SPEED`, 1 - e^(-10t), so about 63% of it in 0.1 s). On a ladder (pass 130) the camera leads 64 px the way you climb, eased over 0.5 s, and drops the sideways look-ahead; hanging still keeps the lead, and stepping off eases it back.
- **Wide camera (pass 94):** a room with `camera: wide` in its header (big arenas, sky rooms) eases the framing out over about 0.6 s to 0.8 scale, showing 25% more each way (800x450 world pixels). It never frames past the room's edges: a room too small for the full pull-out gets as much as fits. Leaving for a normal room snaps back at once. The world still renders on whole render pixels, the HUD and PixelFont are unscaled, and the weather motes tile across the wider view.
- **The camera's numbers (pass 352):** pass 344 held every movement tuning number to its own subsection, but the camera's and the shake's were held only by tests that copied them as literals (look-ahead, deadzone, look-up, the climb lead), so a number changed in the code and its test together would have left this list saying the old one. The look-down's 72 px had no test at all, and the follow rate, the focus point and the shake's reach were in no text. Every number in `GameCamera` and `Feel` now has its line here (the render density's in 12.1), held by `test_every_camera_number_is_written_on_its_line`. An unused `MAX_SHAKE_ROLL` (0) was removed.
- **Rumble:** on gamepads, light for hits, strong for damage and boss slams. A landing from 7 tiles or more thuds with a light rumble and a little shake (pass 97); an ordinary jump's landing doesn't shake.
- **Sound:** layered footsteps per surface; distinct jump, double jump, wall jump, dash, pogo and land sounds. **Heard through the buttons (pass 438):** pass 422 heard the fight through the real controller, but these six were only ever heard under a scripted intent. `test_the_moves_are_heard_through_the_buttons` presses each through `Input`: Jump, Jump again in the air, Jump off a wall with Left held, Dash, and Down with Attack over spikes are each heard once and alone, in the step of the move, and a landing is heard once with only the surface's step layered under it.
- **Particles:** spark burst on hit (pass 98: `HitSpark`, a white core, an opening ring and five rays thrown along the swing, with a hot orange rim so it reads over the foe's white flash; it sits where the blade meets the foe, holds through the hit-stop, lasts 0.14 s, and the heavy finisher's is 1.6x), gold coins that bounce and magnet to the player after 0.4 s.
