- **Input:** every action responds on the same frame it is pressed. 60 fps physics, fixed timestep; rendering uncapped with interpolation. **Timed through the real controller (pass 384):** every move test had scripted the player's intent, so the path from a button through `Input` and `PlayerInput.poll` to the move was never timed, and this was the one line of the checklist no test held. `test_every_move_answers_on_the_frame_it_is_pressed` presses each move through `Input` itself and finds it answered in the first physics step that sees the press: a jump, a dash, a slash and an up-slash on the floor; a double jump, an air dash and a down-strike in the air; a wall jump off a slide; a stroke in deep water. **Held and let go, too (pass 392):** that test timed presses only; the moves that read a button held or released (the jump cut, a tap's short hop, the apex hang and the fast fall) were still scripted. `test_every_held_button_answers_on_its_frame` holds and lets go of Jump and Down through `Input`: the release cuts the rise to 0.45 in the step it is made, a tap hops about 18 px, Jump held over the top hangs at half gravity, and Down held falls at 620 px/s and drops back to 520 the step it is let go. **Timing windows, too (pass 400):** coyote time and the jump buffer were held to the frame only with a scripted intent. `test_the_timing_windows_hold_through_the_real_controller` runs off a ledge holding Right and presses Jump through `Input`, counting each window from the step the game first sees the press (a button pressed in a test is seen a step after it goes down): the 6th frame off the edge (0.10 s) still jumps and the 7th falls. Dropped onto a floor, a Jump pressed 7 frames (0.117 s) or 1 frame before landing jumps on touching down, and one pressed 8 frames (0.133 s) before is dropped. **Down and Up held, too (pass 408):** the moves that read Down or Up held together with another button were still scripted. `test_drops_ladders_and_pogo_through_the_real_controller` plays them with `Input` alone. On a one-way floor, Down alone stays put and Down held with Jump drops through without rising. At a ladder, Up held takes hold and climbs at ladder speed, letting go hangs, Down held climbs down, and Jump lets go. Over spikes, Down held with Attack pogoes off them unhurt. **Running, sliding and gliding, too (pass 416):** the moves that read a direction or Jump held over many frames were only ever scripted, so `test_running_sliding_and_gliding_through_the_real_controller` holds them for real. Right held runs at 165 px/s within 7 frames, Left held turns at 1.6 times the brake, and letting go stops within 5 frames. Right held into a wall slides down it at 90 px/s, and Left held sticks about 5 frames (0.08 s) before letting go. Jump held after the double jump glides at 60 px/s with Feather Fall, and letting go falls. **Swimming, too (pass 424):** a stroke was pressed for real since pass 384, but the rest of the water was only scripted, so `test_swimming_through_the_real_controller` holds it for real. With nothing held the hero floats up to the surface; Down held sinks at 120 px/s and no faster, and letting go floats back up at 50 px/s; Right held swims across at 0.7 of a run; and Right and Jump held at the surface climb out onto the bank. **A press the player means is kept (pass 432):** passes 429 to 431 drop the presses of the hero's first step back after waking, after a pause, and after a dialog, because a screen had already read them. Pass 431 dropped them after any freeze or death, which swallowed a press no screen had read. That is a Jump pressed in the very frame a door's scroll or a respawn let the hero go, the moment a player is waiting to act. The drop is now asked for by the screen that read the press: the dialog box sets `drop_next_presses` as it closes. A door's hold and dying keep the player's next press. `test_a_jump_pressed_as_a_hold_ends_is_kept` ends each hold and presses Jump in the same frame, and the hero jumps both times. "Uncapped" means the game sets no frame cap of its own (`max_fps` 0): drawing follows the display's vsync, which is on, and physics interpolation smooths it between the 60 steps (`physics_ticks_per_second`). **A press made with the Up that reads a sign is dropped (pass 440):** Jump, Dash, Attack and Throw wait in a buffer for their moment, and Up and another button pressed in one frame at a sign put the other press in its buffer before the sign froze the hero. Put away, the sign let it go: the hero leapt, dashed or swung on their own. A hold (a sign, a door, a fade) and a pause (a shop, a menu) now forget every buffered press, as a set-back has since pass 296 (`Player.forget_buffers`). `test_a_press_made_with_the_up_that_reads_a_sign_is_dropped` presses Up with each of Jump, Attack and Dash for real at a tablet: the sign opens, and put away, the hero stands still, and a fresh press afterwards is the hero's. Up with Jump at a shopkeeper opens the shop (a pause), and Up with Jump or Attack at an inner door takes it (a hold): neither press fires once the shop is left or the door is through. **Timing windows at every game speed (pass 478):** pass 400 held both windows at full speed only. `test_the_timing_windows_only_widen_as_the_game_slows` runs off the ledge and lands from a fall with the real buttons at 100, 90, 80 and 70%. Counted in frames on screen, coyote time (6, 6, 7 and 8 frames) and the jump buffer (7, 7, 8 and 10) only grow as the game slows, each within a frame of its game time (0.10 s and 0.12 s). A probe also found a jump pressed during hitstop is kept and taken as the freeze ends, and held on it rises as high as a jump pressed outside it, and that a full jump (69 to 70 px) and a double jump (45 to 46 px) keep their heights at every speed. All held: no bug.

### 6.8 Swimming (the Sunken Spring)

Deep water (legend `a`) is a volume, not a surface: while the middle of the body is in it the player swims. Numbers live in `movement_tuning.gd` (Swimming group).

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| Float | the body eases toward rising at 50 px/s, so it comes to rest with its middle at the surface and the head out |
| Sink | holding Down eases it toward sinking at 120 px/s |
| Drag | 520 px/s² toward the float or sink speed |
| Stroke | a jump press under water sets vy = -230 px/s (weaker than a jump); no limit on strokes |
| Jump out | with the head above the surface a jump press is a full ground jump (and swimming refreshes the double jump and air dash). The water counts as footing for coyote time, so the frames the bob lifts the body clear still jump out at once (pass 111) |
| Across | 0.7x run speed |
| Attacks, dash | work as on land; the dash ignores the water: it runs at the land's 380 px/s for its 0.15 s and holds its line, neither floated nor slowed. The one land move the water takes is the Thrust's charge (9): it is a lunge along a floor, and a swimmer has none, so holding Attack under water swings and never glints. Held since pass 376 with the double jump and air dash the water hands back: `test_swimming_keeps_the_lands_moves` |
| Breath | none: swimming is about routes and foes, not a timer |
| Feel | a "dive" splash and spray when the body crosses the surface fast; a soft "swim" whoosh per stroke; the water is drawn over the bodies in it, translucent, with a rippling waterline, drifting caustics and bubbles |

The reachability checker treats a spot with the feet in water as a standing spot: it swims to the water cells beside, above and below, and from a spot with the head out (no water two rows up) it jumps out a little short of a floor jump (rise 3, 3 across).

### 6.9 Wind currents (the High Boughs)

Bands of wind blow across the open sky between Sylvarre's boughs (legend `>` blows east, `<` west). A current only carries you while you are airborne, and only while the middle of your body is in it. Numbers live in `movement_tuning.gd` (Wind group).

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| Push | 200 px/s added to your horizontal target speed (with it you outrun a run, against it you hang nearly still) |
| Bear up | falling, your fall eases to level at 2,400 px/s², so you sail the band; a jump rises through it as normal |
| Ground | none: standing on a floor or platform inside a band, you aren't moved |
| Drop out | hold Down to fall out of it |
| Against a wall | a current pressing you into a wall stops bearing you up, and you slide down the face (pass 224). It had held you hanging still: once the Sky Spring turned the Thicket Road's band, a jump into it rode to the wind-niche with the head 16 px up against the lintel and stopped there, the opening just below, until you thought to hold Down. `test_the_wind_niche_needs_the_spring_of_sky` plays the real controller: 42 jump, second-jump and air-dash timings with the Featherstep Boots and the Stride never get in against the wind, and one jump with it turned does |
| Reversal | once the Sky Spring is restored (`spring:sky`), every current in the High Boughs blows the other way (pass 92). The Inner Rings' currents never turn (pass 160) |
| Look | a faint wash of the sky, streaks racing downwind and torn leaves tumbling with them |

The reachability checker rides a current downwind (turned after `sky_spring`) from wherever a jump or fall enters it, adding every spot it can stand on or drop to with Down, and at the band's end it lands or falls on. The Thicket Road's sealed niche (its chest) faces the far side of a current, so only the turned wind carries you into it.

### 6.10 Feather Fall (skill, pass 101)

The Wanderer skill Feather Fall [2] (after Second Stride, needs the Featherstep Boots) turns a held Jump into a glide once the double jump is spent. The numbers live in `movement_tuning.gd` (Feather Fall group).

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| When | airborne, falling, double jump spent, Jump held, Down not held, not on a wall |
| Glide fall speed | 60 px/s (a normal fall reaches 520) |
| Settling | a fast fall brakes to glide speed at 1,500 px/s²; a slow one falls into it at the normal fall gravity |
| Air control | unchanged (1,400 / 900 px/s², run speed 165), so a glide carries about 2.75 px across for every 1 px down |
| Leaving it | release Jump, or hold Down to drop at full speed; landing, a wall, a dash, water, wind or an updraft end it |
| Feel | a wide squash and a soft whoosh (`glide`) as it opens; the cloak-spread apex pose held; a feather shed every 0.14 s |

The glide replaces gravity while it lasts. It doesn't refresh any air move, so it never opens a route a double jump and a dash couldn't reach. It makes long drops readable and gentle, and makes crossing wide gaps easier for players who bought it.

### 6.11 Ladders (pass 124)

The source game was a game of ladders: every climb in the Ylden went up one. The remake's wall jump, double jump and pods do most of that work now, but ladders are still the plain way up between floors, where no ability is needed. Legend `"`: a column of ladder cells. The top cell is also a plank (a one-way platform), so you can stand on a ladder's top. Numbers live in `movement_tuning.gd` (Ladders group).

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| Take hold | Up with the body on a ladder (on the ground or in the air), or Down standing on its top. Not mid-dash, mid-swing, swimming or drinking. Taking hold refreshes the double jump and the air dash |
| Climb speed | 90 px/s up or down (Up, Down); no gravity, so let go of both and you hang |
| Centering | the body slides onto the middle of the rungs at 240 px/s |
| Top | climbing past the top steps you onto its plank; Down there climbs back on |
| Bottom | climbing down onto a floor lets go; climbing off the bottom of a ladder that hangs in the air lets you fall |
| Jump | leaves with a full ground jump, moving the way you hold (run speed) |
| Dash | a stride leaves the ladder and dashes as usual. Only a dash that goes lets go (pass 240): before the Elven Stride, or on the ground dash's cooldown, a dash press keeps you on the rungs instead of dropping you off them; a press on the cooldown is held (6.5) and goes when it ends |
| Regrab | after you jump or are knocked off, the same ladder can't be grabbed for 0.25 s |
| Hits | any damage knocks you off the rungs |
| Attacks | none while climbing (as in the source game): jump off to fight |
| Feel | the wall pose's reach, cycling only while you climb; a hollow knock and a creak of the rungs (`ladder`) about every 8 px |
| Camera | leads 64 px up or down the way you climb (6.7, pass 130), so a long shaft shows where you are going |
| Look | two wooden rails and two rungs per tile, drawn behind the tiles so the top plank caps them |

**Held to the table (pass 280):** the take-hold row's rules and the attacks row had no test of their own until pass 280. `test_a_ladder_refreshes_air_moves_and_holds_no_attacks` checks three things. Catching a hanging ladder from the air with the double jump and the air dash spent gives both back. Up during a swing does not take hold until the swing ends. On the rungs, a swing, a Rune Dagger throw or a spell pressed there does nothing (no swing, no dagger, no mana spent, no bolt), and you stay on. Leaving out the refresh or the swing guard makes it fail.

The reachability checker treats a spot with the feet on a ladder as a standing spot: it climbs to the ladder cells above and below and over the top onto the plank, and jumps from a ladder as from the floor.

### 6.12 Sap (pass 248)

Amber sap in the Heartwood' pools, and the puddles a Sap Spitter's globs leave for 4 s, hold your feet. The numbers live in `MovementTuning` (`sap_speed`, `sap_jump`); until pass 248 they were constants in the player's code, outside this section and its tests.

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| In the sap | the point 6 px above the feet lies in a sap tile (`Room.is_sap_at`) |
| Run | 50% of the pace (82 px/s at a full run) |
| Ground jump | leaves at 72% of its speed, 288 px/s: about 37 px of rise, a little over two tiles |
| What it doesn't hold | the double jump, the wall jump and the dash, at full strength: the way out. A dash in the sap goes at 380 px/s (`test_sap_never_holds_a_dash_and_lets_go_at_its_edge`) |
| Leaving | the run comes back the frame you step clear |
| Feel | the feet sink into the drawn sap, and each step squelches (13.3) |
| Heartwood Spring | once it is restored the pools harden into amber you walk on (4.4) |

**Where ladders go (pass 125):** where the source game put them, between floors that already connect, so a ladder is the plain way up and never a key. They go in shafts you otherwise climb by a zigzag of planks, and never in a shaft that is an ability gate (the Fungus Shaft is the Glove's). `test_ladders_never_open_a_gated_room` takes every ladder out and checks that, before the Glove and with it, the ladders open no room the bare rooms keep shut. Placed so far:
- the Hollow Mouth: floor to the high plank under the way to the Mossy Overhang;
- the Tower Foot: the floor up the right wall to the ledge at the Winch Hall door, beside the plank zigzag;
- the Mine Shaft: the bottom of the shaft up the right side, through the upper floor beside the miners' doors.

### 6.13 Set back (pass 288)

A spike or a thorn (5.3: one mask) puts you back on the last safe ground. That is where you last stood on the floor with no hazard within 20 px either side or just below your feet.

**It must be the room's own ground.** Footing that can go is never kept as the safe spot. That covers:
- a cracked block you break;
- a Bark Knight's rooted lance, or the Captain's;
- a crystal shard;
- a gate or raft that opens or comes;
- a foothold of stilled water, which melts (pass 162).

Until pass 288, only the water was left out. Stand on a cracked block over spikes, then hop and pogo it to pieces: you fell onto the spikes and were set back into the air where the block had been, so you fell onto them again. That cost four masks in three seconds, and the loop never ended. Now only a floor of the room's tiles counts (`Player._on_fleeting_ground`). The set-back lands you on the floor you stood on before the block, for one mask.

`test_a_spike_never_sets_you_back_onto_footing_that_is_gone`.

**Nor the room's own ground once a guardian tears it away (pass 292).** The Bloom's phase-2 bite tears a four-tile chunk out of the leaf where it lands, with thorns below (8.5), and it lands where you stand. Your safe spot was that very tile, so the thorns set you back over the gap and you fell onto them again: five masks, and death, from one bite. Two things now keep it off. When a floor is torn (`Room.tear_floor`, the Bloom's leaf and Gnarlmaw's pit), a safe spot over it moves to the nearer solid tile beside the pit. And a tile that is gone no longer counts as footing for the one frame its collision lingers (`Player._on_fleeting_ground`), so you can't take the spot back as you drop. The thorns cost one mask and set you on the leaf beside the gap. `test_a_torn_floor_never_sets_you_back_over_the_gap` (both sides of the root). **A ledge's corner is footing (pass 295):** the gone-tile check read the cell under the floor contact, and on a ledge's corner the contact lies on the line between two cells. The empty cell to its right was read, so standing on that corner never kept a safe spot: 150 of 63,792 floor frames in a walk through five zones' rooms. Both cells beside the line are read now, and the floor is gone only when both are empty. `test_a_ledge_corner_is_footing_for_the_set_back`. **A set-back is a fresh start (pass 296):** a dash pressed as you fell onto the spikes was still buffered when they set you back, and the set-back refills your air dashes, so it fired on arrival and carried you 64 px off the safe spot with no press of your own, often back toward the spikes. A set-back (`Player.set_back`, also the fall rescue under a room) now drops every buffered press (jump, dash, swing, throw) and the ledge's and wall's grace. You land facing the way you were, still. `test_a_set_back_drops_the_presses_buffered_on_the_way_down`.

(Deviation: spikes and their set-back come from the genre, not from the source game, where harm comes from foes and a blow knocks you back. The Delver's Pick's pogo (A2) is built around them, so the remake keeps them, and the set-back is the genre's rule for them.)

**Blightcap is no blow (pass 312).** Since pass 306 a poison tick takes up to 4 health, and passes 309 and 310 gave it a green number and a glug. It still never touches movement: no knockback, no hurt flash or invulnerability, no lost dash, slide or climb. A poisoned hero and a well one, given the same inputs, move frame for frame alike through a dash, a jump, a wall slide and a ladder climb with a tick landing in each (`test_a_poison_tick_never_touches_movement`). A hop over a flask before a door (pass 307) clears it from 22 to 58 px short at a run with a short hop and 22 to 94 px with a full jump; at a walk the window is 16 to 40 px with a full jump.

**A throw is drawn, not done (pass 320).** Sean's Rune Dagger flick (13.5, 2026-10-30) plays a low ready crouch over the legs' poses, and since his off hand (5.2) the dagger is a whole build's ranged tool, thrown all through a fight. The flick is drawn only: a throw never stops, slows or nudges the hero, and never costs a jump or a wall slide. `test_a_dagger_throw_never_touches_movement` runs a hero who throws on the run (frame 6), at the top of a jump (50) and in a wall slide (100) beside one who never throws, and their paths must match frame for frame. A throw that so much as added 20 px/s of fall parts them by frame 54.


### 6.14 Fields outside the tuning (pass 336)

Five things change how you move without living in `movement_tuning.gd`: the fields a zone or a guardian lays over you, and two numbers the world keeps. Each was written only where its zone or guardian is described, and the tests read each one back from its own constant, so a changed number moved its test with it. They are gathered here, and `test_every_field_number_is_written_in_bible_6_14` holds each row to its constant.

| Field | Setting | Constant |
|-------|---------|----------|
| Wrong gravity (the Evercrown, 8.9) | you keep 40% of your weight, gravity and top fall speed alike | `GravityDrift.PULL` |
| The falling crown-leaf (Snapcrown, 8.5) | you weigh 85% while it falls | `LeafFall.WEIGHT` |
| Skyrend's updraft (8.6) | lifts you to 240 px/s, reached at 1,100 px/s² | `Updraft.LIFT`, `Player.UPDRAFT_ACCEL` |
| Rising through a floor door (3.3) | you arrive in the room above rising at 430 px/s | `World.RISE_SPEED` |
| A big drop (6.7) | a landing from 112 px (7 tiles) or more thuds | `Player.BIG_DROP` |

---

## 7. Enemies

About 4 to 5 enemy families per zone plus variants (recolors with new behavior). Every foe has a name of its own.

| Zone | Enemies |
|------|---------|
| Heartwood | Blinkhop (one-eyed hopper: it crouches for 0.4 s, then hops at you), Hornet (swooper: it buzzes in place for 0.45 s, then swoops at where you were), Rot Dwarf (shield-bearing walker: he winds up his axe for 0.5 s, then swings), Grub Lump (crawls over floors, walls and ceilings; from a ceiling it shivers for 0.45 s, then drops on you), Sap Spitter (turret; it swells for 0.55 s, then lobs sap globs that leave a slowing puddle for 4 s) |
| Hollin Tower | Iron Dwarf (plated walker: raises a hammer for 0.65 s, slams, and sends a shockwave along the floor that a hop clears; his plate halves blows except from above or while the hammer is stuck in the floor for about 1 s), Minecart Rider (a dwarf hunched in an ore cart: trundles the floor; with you level and ahead he hauls the brake for 0.6 s, wheels shrieking, then charges the length of the floor. Jump him. At a wall or the floor's end the cart stops dead and tips over for about 1 s, the rider dazed and open; otherwise the iron tub turns most blows except from above), Gear Bat (clockwork flier: roosts under ceilings, wakes when you pass, hangs off to one side of you, whirs the gear on its back for 0.5 s and flings it; the gear flies out, slows and returns to the bat; parry it and it flies home and staggers the bat), Silt Adder (pass 273, the source game's serpent that floats up and down over the ladders: a sea-green hooded serpent the length of a man, legend `)`. It rises and sinks 40 px either way of its home along its own column, one swing every 3.2 s of game time, so a climb past it is timed to its swing. Level with it, its head within 24 px of your chest and you in front within seven tiles, it draws back and flares its hood with a hiss (`silt_adder_hiss`) for 0.5 s, following your height along its column at up to 60 px/s (slower than a climb, never past its swing), then strikes straight out along that level at 260 px/s for 0.4 s (`silt_adder_strike`) and bites for 16, and glides back to its column at 120 px/s, waiting 2 s before it hoods again. So a climb past it is timed to its strike (pass 275; until then its coil froze it in place and every climber holding Up was already above the bite): holding Up straight past is met nearly every time, while baiting it (climb into its level, drop back as it hoods, climb again once it has glided off the ladder) gets past every time (`test_a_climb_past_a_silt_adder_is_timed_to_its_strike`). Since pass 274 the bite is a blow, as the Spring Eel's, the Hound's and the Mantis's are: a parry takes no harm and sets it reeling for 1 s. Its floating body only scrapes for 8, and only a wall ends a strike early (its long body grazing the floor once did, so a Silt Adder never reached a hero on the ground; `test_the_silt_adders_bite_can_be_parried`). 22 HP. Slain, it always leaves the largest bread there is, as the source game's did (pass 281): a great loaf, twice a bread's length, that heals 40 against a bread's 15 (`Drop.LOAF_HEAL`, through `Enemy.sure_drop`, which a foe leaves instead of rolling for a drop; `test_a_slain_silt_adder_leaves_a_great_loaf`). So a climb that meets the serpent and wins is paid back in health. Both Silt Adders float over shafts whose floors open onto the room below, so until pass 283 a third of loaves fell out of the room: 30 of 72 tried in the Tower Foot and 18 of 72 in the Mine Shaft, through the gap or out a side door. Now no drop leaves its room (`Drop._keep_in_room`): one that pops past a side edge stops at it, and one that falls through a gap in the floor comes to rest on the floor at the gap's nearer edge (`test_a_silt_adders_loaf_lands_where_you_can_reach_it`, every kill point along the serpent's swing and strike, popping left, straight or right). One floats by the ladder of the Mine Shaft and one by the Tower Foot's. `test_the_silt_adder_floats_its_column_and_strikes_level`), Rock Golem (mini-boss: the Foreman, a golem in a foreman's lamp-helm with the Delver's Pick wedged in its back. 220 HP; its rock halves blows except from above or while stunned. The rock by the buttons (pass 420): `test_the_foremans_rock_answers_the_real_controller` presses Attack for real at its side, which the rock halves, then holds Down and presses Attack for real as the hero falls onto its helm, and the pogo lands in full and bounces him back up. Pound: fists up 0.75 s, a slam with a shockwave each way. Throw: a boulder overhead 0.6 s, then lobbed at you. Charge: scrapes 0.85 s, then barrels across the hall; hitting a wall stuns it 1.8 s and shakes rocks from the ceiling, each marking its spot 0.7 s before it falls. Below half: tells 25% shorter, faster charge, the pound also drops rocks. Reward: the Delver's Pick (no skill point)) |
| The Haar | Lantern Ghost (a hooded wisp of a drowned dwarf that drifts through wood and stone; out in the fog it is all but invisible and shows only near light. Close in, its eyes flare for 0.55 s, bright even in the murk, then it lunges and fades back), Bog Puffcap (a squat marsh mushroom rooted in the mud. Come within about five tiles and its cap swells for 0.6 s while its gills glow, a light that shows through the fog; then it bursts into a spore cloud 40 px across that drifts up, stings for 8 on contact and again every 0.7 s, and can't be parried. It thins away in 2.4 s. Spent, its cap sags open for 1.1 s and takes half again the damage, so the answer is to back off and then strike. As in the source game, where the Puffcap always drops health-restoring bread, a slain one always leaves a loaf of bread, +15 (pass 289, `Enemy.sure_drop`)), Drowned Dwarf (built in pass 108: a Lampwick miner the marsh took, risen with the anchor he drowned on, his skin gone blue-white and his beard matted with weed. He lies sunk in the mire with only his helm's crest and a trickle of bubbles showing, and there he can't be hit and does no harm. Come within about five tiles, level with him, and he hauls himself out over 0.7 s, taking half again the damage while half out of the mud. Then he shambles after you at 26 px/s. Close in, he heaves the anchor overhead, water pouring off it, for 0.6 s (pose, white pulse, a low groan), and slams it down in front of him for 18. The slam can be parried. It buries the anchor in the mud for 0.8 s, and every blow lands at half again. Lose him (out of sight for 3 s) and he sinks back into the mire to wait. 46 HP, contact 10 once risen. Legend `p`; in Marsh Road, Lampwick Causeway, Drowned Stair, Veiled Rise and Feather Hollow), Marsh Hag (built in pass 119: a bent crone of the drowned villages in a shawl of dry reeds, moss for hair, leaning on a crooked willow staff with a green marsh-wisp caught in its crook. The wisp is a light, so she shows through the fog from far off. She spots you from about 15 tiles and shuffles nearer at 22 px/s until you are within 11. Come within four tiles and she backs away from you at 34 px/s, still facing you, for up to 1.2 s; then she stands and hexes anyway. Her hex: she draws herself up and raises the staff, the wisp flares (pose, white pulse, a cackle over welling bubbles), and a ring of bubbling green bog light marks the floor or plank under your feet, all for 0.6 s. Then a hand of black mud bursts up out of the mark for 16 (12 until pass 226), which can't be parried: step off the mark. Strike her mid-hex and the spell breaks: the mark fizzles, the staff clatters into the mud and she gropes for it for 1.0 s, taking every blow (the breaking one too) at half again. So the answer is to close in, through the fog, toward her light. She hexes every 1.6 s. 30 HP, contact 6. Legend `?`; in the Fog Cut, across the dip, and on a plank over the Lampwick Causeway's dip), Marsh Hag Matriarch (mini-boss, pass 179: the hags' mother in a mire east of Polesend; see section 8) |
| High Boughs | Leaf Mantis (a mantis the green of the leaves, folded on a bough like one more leaf cluster and harmless to touch. Come within about seven tiles and level with it and it rears and spreads its scythes for 0.5 s, then lunges along the bough for 0.25 s and slashes for 16; it stops at the bough's end. The slash can be parried. A lunge leaves it overreached for 0.8 s, taking half again the damage; then it stalks you and unfolds again, or folds back into a leaf once it has lost you for 2 s. Striking a folded one springs it open); Bark Knight (pass 110: a knight the Tree grew in living bark, with a lance of green bough. He paces the branch roads at 22 px/s. Come level with him within about five tiles in front and he braces, lance back and eyes lit, for 0.6 s, then thrusts it out to about four tiles for 16; the thrust can be parried. Then he roots for 1.1 s with the lance left out stiff as a bough, a one-way ledge you can stand on, drop through (Down + Jump, as any one-way) or pogo off to get over him. His bark is a guard of three plates: each blow on it deals a quarter, rounded up, and knocks off a plate; bare heartwood he takes full damage. Rooted, the bark doesn't guard him, but one plate grows back as he pulls free. Kindle catches living bark (pass 177): a fireball's burst burns every plate off at once and lands in full on the heartwood. 54 HP; legend `!`); Seed Bomber (pass 114: a pod-bellied canopy bird with leaf wings and a husk of hard seeds for a belly. Within about 15 tiles it keeps five tiles over your head, out of reach of a plain slash. Right above you it squeezes the husk, wings up, belly swelling and glowing, seeds rattling, for 0.5 s, then drops a seed that stings for 10 if it lands on you. On a branch or plank the seed sputters for 0.6 s, seams blinking faster and its reach showing, then bursts in thorns 28 px around for 12, unparryable. Strike the seed (or parry it as it falls) and it flies back up into the bird, bursting there for 18 and knocking it out of the sky: it lies dazed on its back for 1.4 s taking blows at half again, then climbs back up. You can pogo off a seed, too. 28 HP, touch 8; legend `;`, in Thicket Road and North Bough), Bark Knight Captain (mini-boss, pass 171: the first knight the Tree grew, in the Thorn Yard east of Sylvarre; see section 8); Mothling (pass 257: Snapcrown's brood, loosed three at a time from its petals and found nowhere else, as in the source game; a violet imp with petal wings that screeches 0.4 s before it dives, 8.5) |
| Storm Spires | Storm Harpy (a winged hunter, storm-grey and violet with white hair and gold talons. Within about 16 tiles she hangs 4 or 5 tiles to one side of you and 5 above, out of reach of a plain slash. Her tell: she rears and throws her wings up with a screech for 0.55 s, then flings a fan of three feathers at you (8 each, 200 px/s, 15° apart). They can be parried, and a parried feather flies back and strikes her for 12. After a volley she sags about two tiles for 0.8 s, winded and in reach of a jump, then waits 1.4 s before the next. HP 30), Thunder Beetle (a beetle as big as a dog, storm-violet with a gold stripe, trundling the shrine stones at 28 px/s and turning at ledges. Its shell turns 70% of a side blow but none from above, so pogo it. Come within about four tiles and its wing cases lift and crackle with static for 0.6 s, then it discharges a ring of lightning 46 px around itself for 14 that can't be parried and reaches above the shell too: get off and get clear. Spent, its cases hang open for 1.1 s and every blow lands in full; it can't charge again for 1.4 s), Spire Sentinel (built in pass 123: a knight of pale shrine stone the elves set to guard their stairs, helmed and crested, with a halberd and the Sky Key's gold lightning cut into its breastplate. It is stone while you watch it: as long as you face it, it never moves and every blow clinks off. Turn your back within about 13 tiles and its eyes light and it grinds after you along the floor at 64 px/s, and the moment you turn round it is stone again. So climbing away from one is the danger, not fighting it. In reach (about 2.5 tiles) it raises the halberd overhead while the glyph blazes, 0.6 s (pose, white pulse, a rising hum), and slams it down in front for 16. The slam can be parried, which staggers it; a slam that lands or misses buries the blade for 1.2 s. Either way the stone cracks open with bright seams and every blow lands in full. 40 HP, contact 8. Legend `]`; at the Spire Foot and at the bottom of the Spire Stair) |
| Vorrhallow | Crystal Dwarf (glass-fused; built in pass 64: magenta crystal has grown out of his back like a shell and swallowed his right arm. He trudges toward you; with you ahead and within about seven tiles he raises the crystal fist, blazing, for 0.6 s, then drives it into the floor, 16 to anything right in front, and six crystal spikes erupt along the floor toward you one after another, 20 px apart and 0.09 s apart, each glowing on the floor for 0.18 s before it stabs up 26 px for 12, unparryable: jump or dash them. Each spike cracks up through the floor with a dry snap and a glassy ping as it rises (`crystal_stab`, pass 246; its glowing mark is silent), so the chain ticks along the floor toward you; the same spikes serve Thane Vorrund, the Everbloom's avatar and the Evil Heart, and two that rise on one frame sound once. His fist stays rooted for 0.9 s, and then every blow lands. The rest of the time his crystal back turns any blow from behind, and only a pogo from above gets past it. 48 HP), Shade Knight (parry-heavy; built in pass 66: the drowned ghost of a king's guard in a dwarf great helm, a tower shield on his arm, mist where his knees should be. The shield turns every blow from the front, and two blows on it in a row bring a quick riposte, 0.36 s tell, a cut for 14. He advances behind it to about four tiles, then draws his blade back, a cold light gathering at its point, for 0.55 s and thrusts, sliding forward, for 18. Then he raises it, 0.38 s, and cuts down for 14. Both blows can be parried, and a parry leaves him reeling, shield wide. If the pair finishes he stands winded for 0.7 s, shield low. Blows from behind or from above always land. 56 HP), Clockwork Hound (built in pass 70, the clockwork enemy: one of Thane Vorrund's brass hunting dogs, still running on the spring the king last wound. It trots after you; within about seven tiles it crouches and its key spins up, ratcheting, eyes blazing, for 0.45 s, then pounces in an arc aimed to land on you and bites for 14. Its brass plating turns half of every blow. Parry the bite and it tumbles onto its back for 1.3 s, belly bare, and every blow lands in full. It is clockwork, so the Stillstone stops it dead, mid-leap or not; it is the one enemy time stops. 44 HP), Glass Slug (built in pass 69, the Stillstone's enemy: a great grey slug under a lumpy shell of stillglass-rock, molten in its cracks. It creeps along the floor and turns at ledges. With you within about ten tiles its shell swells and its cracks blaze for 0.6 s, then it heaves three gobbets of molten rock in high arcs, one at you, one short, one long, 12 each, unparryable; where one lands on a floor it leaves embers that burn for 8 over 1.4 s. The Stillstone holds globs and embers still and harmless, so you can walk out from under a volley. The shell turns pogos except in the 0.7 s it sags after a heave; its soft sides take every blow. 40 HP), Clockwork Alpha (mini-boss, pass 169: the hounds' brass sire in a den under the kennels; see section 8) |
| The Evercrown | Remixes of every family, corrupted with stillglass. As built (pass 161): besides the Vorrhallow guard, the Threshold holds Gear Bats, a Storm Harpy and a Rot Dwarf, the Heart Stair a Leaf Mantis, an Iron Dwarf and a Thunder Beetle, the Drift Well a Lantern Ghost, and the Heart Gate a Bog Puffcap and a Seed Bomber: a family from every zone, a dozen or more in all (`test_evercrown_holds_a_remix_of_every_zones_foes`) |
| Not yet built (pass 123) | **Planned**: none; every planned foe is built (the Seed Bomber in pass 114, the Marsh Hag in pass 119, the Spire Sentinel in pass 123). **Cut**: The Haar Wisp (the Lantern Ghost is the Haar's wisp), the Wind Imp (the Storm Harpy already holds Carillon's air) and the Sky Ray (the High Boughs' wind currents make the open sky the challenge there). |
| Sunken Spring | Spring Eel (built in pass 86, the swimming enemy: a long pale eel gone blind in the dark water, with a lamp of the spring's light grown on its brow. It never leaves the water; it weaves back and forth near where it lives. Within about seven tiles it draws back into an S, its lamp blazing, for 0.5 s, then darts straight at where you were at 300 px/s and bites for 14. The dart stops dead at the water's edge, so the bank is safe. Spent, it hangs slack for 0.8 s and takes every blow at half again. 30 HP. Legend `l`, a water cell with the eel in it) |

**Every foe's numbers, as built (pass 338).** The zone table tells each foe's fight in words, and until pass 338 it gave a wind-up for 21 of the 25 foes and a health for 12. These are the scripts' own numbers, before the zone's threat (5.5) scales a blow: health, the touch of its body, its first wind-up and its main blow. `test_every_foes_numbers_are_written_in_bible_7` holds each row to its script, and every wind-up, health and touch the zone table names to the same script.

**What a foe pays (pass 378).** The table gave what each foe costs you and not what it pays. Its purse and its experience lived only in the scripts (`gold_min`, `gold_max`, `xp`), though the gold sheet (10) and the ranks (9) are built on them: 9 said only that "starting enemies give 5 XP (Rot Dwarf 12)". The Gold and XP columns are the scripts' own. A foe's gold is then multiplied by its zone's threat (5.5) as it dies, so the gold sheet's ranges are these times the threat; its experience is not, so a Blinkhop pays 5 XP in the Heartwood and in the Evercrown alike. Corruption (8.9's the Evercrown remixes) doubles both. The test holds both columns to the scripts, and a real kill to them: a slain foe adds its XP and sprays its gold times its room's threat.

| Foe | HP | Touch | Wind-up (s) | Blow | Gold | XP |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Blinkhop | 24 | 20 | 0.4 | 20 (its hop) | 3 to 6 | 5 |
| Hornet | 16 | 20 | 0.45 | 20 (its swoop) | 2 to 4 | 5 |
| Rot Dwarf | 40 | 20 | 0.5 | 20 (its swing) | 5 to 9 | 12 |
| Grub Lump | 18 | 14 | 0.45 | 16 (its drop) | 2 to 4 | 5 |
| Sap Spitter | 20 | 12 | 0.55 | 12 (a sap glob) | 2 to 5 | 6 |
| Iron Dwarf | 64 | 12 | 0.65 | 24 (the slam) | 7 to 12 | 18 |
| Minecart Rider | 56 | 10 | 0.6 | 22 (the charge) | 8 to 14 | 20 |
| Gear Bat | 22 | 12 | 0.5 | 14 (the gear) | 4 to 7 | 9 |
| Silt Adder | 22 | 8 | 0.5 | 16 (the bite) | 3 to 5 | 6 |
| Lantern Ghost | 18 | 10 | 0.55 | 18 (the lunge) | 3 to 6 | 7 |
| Bog Puffcap | 26 | 10 | 0.6 | 8 (the spores, each 0.7 s) | 3 to 7 | 8 |
| Drowned Dwarf | 46 | 10 | 0.6 | 18 (the anchor) | 7 to 11 | 18 |
| Marsh Hag | 30 | 6 | 0.6 | 16 (the mud hand) | 8 to 12 | 18 |
| Leaf Mantis | 30 | 10 | 0.5 | 16 (the slash) | 4 to 8 | 9 |
| Bark Knight | 54 | 10 | 0.6 | 16 (the thrust) | 8 to 12 | 20 |
| Seed Bomber | 28 | 8 | 0.5 | 10 (a seed) | 6 to 10 | 14 |
| Mothling | 20 | 8 | 0.4 | 8 (the dive) | 1 to 2 | 3 |
| Storm Harpy | 30 | 12 | 0.55 | 8 (each feather) | 6 to 12 | 16 |
| Thunder Beetle | 44 | 12 | 0.6 | 14 (the ring) | 6 to 12 | 14 |
| Spire Sentinel | 40 | 8 | 0.6 | 16 (the slam) | 10 to 16 | 20 |
| Crystal Dwarf | 48 | 14 | 0.6 | 16 (the fist) | 9 to 15 | 24 |
| Shade Knight | 56 | 12 | 0.55 | 18 (the thrust) | 10 to 16 | 28 |
| Clockwork Hound | 44 | 10 | 0.45 | 14 (the bite) | 8 to 14 | 22 |
| Glass Slug | 40 | 10 | 0.6 | 12 (a gobbet) | 8 to 12 | 20 |
| Spring Eel | 30 | 8 | 0.5 | 14 (the bite) | 6 to 10 | 16 |

Every enemy has: a readable telegraph (minimum 0.35 s wind-up with a pose, a flash and a sound, section 13.3; every named wind-up of every foe and guardian, and every phase-quickened tell, is held to it as a whole by `test_every_wind_up_in_the_game_is_fair` since pass 234. The one shorter warning, a crystal spike's 0.18 s floor glow, only marks where a spike will rise after its caster's full tell). **Every tell flashes the foe white (pass 354):** the flash in a tell is the 6.7 wash, the foe's own silhouette pulsing white. Five foes lit only a part: the Clockwork Hound its eyes, the Shade Knight his blade's point, the Crystal Dwarf his fist, the Glass Slug its cracks and the Spring Eel its lamp. Their tells never flashed the way the other twenty did, so a player who had learned the pulse read them late. Each keeps its own glow and now pulses white with it. A Stillglass Seed is a crystal shard with no body, so its flicker is its flash. `test_every_foes_tell_flashes_it_white` sets each foe into each tell and checks that it draws white (`Enemy.drawn_white`), a hurt reaction, a death animation with a dissolve, and drops (gold, MP orbs, rare items). Besides gold, about 1 kill in 12 leaves **bread** (heals 15, the source game's staple) and 1 in 10 a **mana orb** (+12 MP); both wait on the floor for 10 s. (Deviation, pass 297: the source game's manual names only "food and money" from foes, with the rare items below. The mana orb is the remake's: magic here pays for every spell you cast (5.2), and the orb keeps a fight's magic coming between Ringreaders as bread keeps its health.) From the Hollin Tower on, some enemies (Iron Dwarf, Gear Bat) leave Yldenfall's **Blightcap** about 1 kill in 14: a violet skull flask that is a hazard to step around while collecting. Walking over it poisons you for 8 s, costing 2 health a second but never the last point; your health masks and sprite turn green. Bread, resting at a shrine or Ringreader, or dying cures it, and an Barkbalm keeps it off. (Deviation, pass 305: the manual's Blightcap is one bite: "The hero's power will decrease by a fixed amount." The remake spreads it over 8 s, 16 health at most in the Tower and never the last point, so it reads as a sickness that bread can still settle, not a fine taken before you see it. The manual's Barkbalm protects the hero "from any enemy attack", and poison is no attack; that it keeps poison off too is the remake's.) **Blightcap bites as hard as the room's foes (pass 306):** poison is a foe's leaving, so each tick is scaled by the room's threat (5.5) as a blow is: 2 a second in the Tower, 3 in the Haar, the High Boughs and Carillon, 4 in Vorrhallow, the Sunken Spring and the Evercrown, where every foe drops it. A fixed 16 health was under 6% of a late hero's 280 while every blow beside it doubled, a flask not worth stepping around. It still never takes the last point, and an Barkbalm rubbed on mid-poison stops the rest of it (`test_poison_bites_as_hard_as_the_rooms_foes`).

**Rare drops (pass 147):** "rare items" had never been built: no foe left anything but gold, bread, mana and poison. Now about 1 kill in 40, from any foe anywhere (`Drop.rare_chance` 0.025), leaves one of the source game's rare items. Both wait on the floor like bread, and both are drawn in code:
- **War Glove** (60% of rare drops). This is the source game's Glove, renamed so it can't be mistaken for the Climbing Glove. It looks like a red leather fighting glove with a gold-studded cuff. Walking over it puts it on: for 15 s every blade hit, the pogo and a Riposte included, lands 1.5x as hard (`war_glove_mult` and `war_glove_time` in `combat_tuning.gd`, through `Player.blade_mult()`). Red sparks rise off the sword hand while it lasts. A red fist and a draining bar sit on the HUD beside the Barkbalm's row, and the bar blinks in its last 3 s. Another glove starts the time over, and dying takes it off. It turns a lucky drop into a short push: clear the room while it burns.
- **Barkbalm** (40%). It goes in your pouch like a bought one (up to 3). With the pouch full and none on, it goes straight on, as the source game's did; with one already on, it is wasted.

**A fall out of the room (pass 291).** A foe knocked through a gap in its room's floor died to nothing: it fell on below the room for good, and its gold, drop and experience were lost with it. Fifteen walkers in fourteen rooms can be driven out this way, by blows alone: Blinkhops and Rot Dwarves in the Heartwood and the Tower, and a Marsh Hag in Fog Cut. Now a foe (never a guardian) that falls more than 24 px below its room's floor line dies as it goes (`Enemy._fall_out_of_room`, `FALL_MARGIN`). It counts as a kill, and its coins and drop come to rest at the gap's edge (`Drop.keep_in_room`, passes 283 and 285). `test_a_foe_knocked_out_of_its_room_falls_to_its_death` drives every walking foe in every room both ways and holds both. **A fall is a kill, not a blow (pass 298):** it pays, cries out and refills Wanderer's Grace like any kill. But it comes below the screen a moment after the blow that sent it, so the kill's 0.08 s freeze and its shake there only hitched the game for nothing you could see. A fall (`Enemy.fell`) now dies without them. `test_a_foe_that_falls_out_pays_without_a_kills_freeze`.

**The dissolve (pass 126):** until pass 126 a foe simply vanished in a puff of debris. Now its body stays where it fell for 0.45 s (`Enemy.DISSOLVE`). It flashes white, then breaks up pixel by pixel (one world pixel, two art pixels, at a time), the top a little first, each mote glowing pale gold as it goes (`shaders/dissolve.gdshader`). Then it is gone (freed, or hidden until it respawns). The dying body is harmless and can't be struck or touched, and the gold, drops and death sound come at once as before. Every foe that dies through `Enemy.die` uses it. Bosses keep their own deaths and go at once when they reach `Enemy.die`.

---

## 8. Bosses

Each boss has: a pre-fight arena reveal, 2 or 3 phases, an ability it tests, a "stagger" window, a cinematic kill (slow-mo, white flash, shatter), and a reward. Health bars show phase markers. **The boss bar, held (pass 364):** a guardian's bar runs 320 px along the bottom of the screen under its name. Red is its health, and a pale trail behind the red catches up a blow at half the bar a second (`GameHud.BOSS_TRAIL_SPEED`), so a heavy blow reads as a chunk falling away. A gold tick stands at each mark where a phase begins (its `PHASES`, the table below). The Likeness's bar follows the real one through its third phase (8.8). No test had ever looked at the bar. `test_every_guardians_bar_shows_its_phase_marks` raises it in every guardian's arena and checks four things: the name, the fill against the guardian's health, a tick at each phase mark inside the bar, and the trail. It then splits the Likeness and checks the bar stays on the real one. **The kill's white flash (pass 356):** only Gnarlmaw's kill had ever flashed the screen white; the other fifteen froze and slowed, but their screens stayed as they were. `Feel.boss_kill`, which every guardian calls as it falls, now throws the full white flash over the screen itself (`Feel.BOSS_KILL_FLASH`, scaled by the flashes option, 14), so no guardian can be written without it, and `test_every_guardian_falls_within_the_bibles_dying_window` checks the flash for all sixteen. **The openings, as built (pass 163):** each boss names its stagger window as `punishable()`, which Executioner and Bloodletting read (9). The windows are: **The reveal, held (pass 372):** every fight opens on its reveal. As the last door seals the guardian shows itself (Gnarlmaw bursts from the far wall and roars, Snapcrown rises out of the leaf, Skyrend drops from the storm, the Warden wades up out of the Wellspring, the rest turn and rear) under its name card, its name and epithet, with a sound of its own and a shake. The reveal runs from 1.2 s (the Likeness) to 2.4 s (Gnarlmaw: 0.5 s out of the wall, 1.3 s of roar, 0.6 s back), most of them 1.4 to 1.6 s (each script's `INTRO_TIME`). It is a moment to look: the guardian strikes no one and no blow lands on it until it is over. Nothing had held any of this. `test_every_guardian_opens_with_its_reveal` starts every arena, listens for the card (once, with the script's name and epithet), times the reveal against this range, strikes whatever a blade could reach every third of a second through it (the guardian, or its segments where those are its only hitbox), and checks the hero at the door is untouched.

| Boss | Opening |
| --- | --- |
| Gnarlmaw | his plates cracked by a stagger |
| Durgan | the drill stuck in a wall or the floor |
| Gloamweaver | fallen from the vault |
| The Haar Wyrm | a head lying where it bit |
| Snapcrown | the jaws hanging open |
| Skyrend | grounded by its own lightning |
| The Foreman | stunned against a wall (rock never reels from a parry: none of its blows can be parried, pass 276) |
| The Bark Knight Captain | rooted after a thrust, planted after a vault, or reeling from a parry |
| The Marsh Hag Matriarch | groping for her staff after a broken hex, or reeling from a parry |
| The Clockwork Alpha | flipped by a parry, jammed in a wall, or stopped by the Stillstone |
| Thane Vorrund | reeling from a parry, or fallen |
| The Likeness | reeling from a parry, or spent after a blow of its own (the cut from behind, or a free cut, heavy, cast or dash) |
| The Drowned Warden | staggered, or exposed after a slam |
| The Everbloom, phase 1 | reeling from a parry, or spent after its burst (the pull's end) |
| The Everbloom, phase 3 | bound by the root |

Since pass 276 `test_every_opening_the_table_names_a_parry_for_opens_on_a_parry` holds the table to the fights: each guardian whose row names a parry is sent reeling in its own arena and must stand open, and the Foreman must not. Its row had said "or reeling from a parry" since pass 163, but its rock never reels (`Foreman.stagger` does nothing), so Executioner and Bloodletting had only the wall stun there all along.

**An opening without a shield (pass 314).** Sean's off hand (5.2) makes the Rune Dagger and a shield a choice, and the two-handed Broad Sword already put the shield on your back. The Likeness and the Everbloom's first phase opened only on a parry, so a hero with the dagger in hand never saw Executioner or Bloodletting (9) work in either fight, against "so the skill has a window in every fight". Each now also opens when spent after its own move: The Likeness for the 0.6 s after the cut from behind (`recover`) or any free blow, cast or dash (`f_recover`), and the Everbloom for the 0.7 s after its pull ends in the burst (`burst`; its plate is bare then too, as when it reels). A decoy has no opening. Every row that names a parry must name another way in: `test_every_guardian_opens_without_a_shield`.

The core (phase 2) has none: no blade reaches it, only the veins.

**What a win says (pass 220).** A guardian's win shows a banner: the prize's name and a line about it. The Likeness and the Everbloom's avatar, veins and heart each had a line of their own written in `BossArena.BOSSES`, but no prize, so the banner took the plain branch and showed only "+1 skill point": the mirror's and the Everbloom's story lines were never seen. The reverse held for the five bosses whose banner tells their prize (the Lantern and Sky Keys, the Elven Plate, the Severance): each gives a skill point and never said so. Now a boss with a line shows it, and a skill point is always told, in the banner for a boss with no line or in a toast under it for one with. Every line fits the banner's width. `test_every_guardians_win_says_what_it_gave` wins every arena (all but the Everbloom's last, whose win starts the ending) and listens. **The Reward lines (pass 332):** the sections below still left out the skill point that Thane Vorrund, Skyrend, the Likeness and the Everbloom's three forms each give, and the Foreman's prize was told only in passing in 7. Every guardian now has a Reward line naming its prize and its skill point, or "no skill point" for the Foreman and the three mini-bosses. `test_every_guardians_reward_line_names_its_prize` holds each line to `BossArena.BOSSES`. **Read in real time (pass 482):** the banner's 3.2 s and a toast's 2.2 s counted down on game time, so at 70% Game speed a guardian's banner stayed 4.6 s, and the slow motion of a boss's death blow (8) stretched the very banner it was showing. Words are read in real time, like the music and the door fades (passes 476, 477): the HUD counts both down with `Feel.unscaled`, while the hurt flash and the gold roll-up stay on game time. `test_a_guardians_banner_reads_in_real_time_at_every_game_speed` times both at full speed, at 70% and in slow motion.

**A won fight stays won (pass 239).** A guardian dies in a dying window of 1.2 to 2.5 s (the slow-mo, flash and shatter) before its arena opens. Those are game seconds; to the eye the window is longer, since the kill's 350 ms freeze and its slow motion (6.7) stretch it. The Foreman falls quickest and the Everbloom's heart slowest (each script's `DYING_TIME`; Gnarlmaw, who has none, takes about 2.1 s, shattering segment by segment). Since pass 348 `test_every_guardian_falls_within_the_bibles_dying_window` fells every guardian in its own arena, times the fall, and holds this range to the shortest and longest. Spikes, shards, the flood and any blow already swung stayed live in that window, so a hero at low health could land the killing blow and still die. If the respawn came before the arena opened, it took them back to their last rest with the boss already down but the arena never opened: no flag, no prize, the fight to do again. Now `BossArena.falling()` is true from the killing blow until the doors open, and the hero takes no harm in that time. `test_a_guardians_win_cant_be_lost_while_it_falls` has Thane Vorrund's blows land before the killing blow, none after it, and the flag and the Severance kept.

**The Rune Dagger against bosses (pass 188):** it reaches every boss that a blade does, for 6 a throw, and is meant as a way to keep up pressure from a distance, not a way around a fight's lesson. So it splits none of the Bark Knight Captain's plates, breaks none of the Matriarch's hexes and never cuts the Heart (5.2, the Rune Dagger). The two-handed Dwarvish Broad Sword (pass 186) cannot parry, and no boss needs a parry: every opening that a parry gives also comes some other way, or only feeds Executioner and Bloodletting. The Elven Helm's slot and kept gear change nothing in a fight, apart from letting you swap blades at a pause. **Held since pass 316:** with Sean's off hand the dagger is a whole build's ranged tool, so `test_the_rune_dagger_reaches_every_guardian` fights each guardian of the Boss Rush order in its own arena and throws until one throw stings it, from a step to either side, from under it when it is high, and from the ground beside it when it stands below you. The Drowned Warden, waist-deep in the Wellspring, is reached only that last way: a throw from the ledges or the planks flies over its head, so the islands are where a dagger fights it. The veins and the bound Heart are left out, as above.

**A guardian fought with the buttons (pass 404).** Every boss test scripted the hero's intent or called the boss's own hit, so no fight had ever been driven by the buttons a player presses. `test_a_guardian_falls_to_the_real_controller` fights Gnarlmaw with `Input` alone. Holding Right walks in, wakes it, and seals both doors. Holding Left into the sealed way in goes nowhere. When a stuck lunge leaves the tail by the wall, Attack pressed beside it lands the last blow. Then it shatters, the doors open, the Thaw Seed is won, and Right held walks out through the opened door to the Heartwood Spring.

**A fight lost beside its own shrine starts over (pass 452).** A death returns you to your last rest (5.4), and the room you rose in was only built afresh when it was not the room you died in. The Likeness's Crown Gate has a Meditation Shrine inside her arena, so a player who rested there and fell to her rose beside the shrine still sealed in, with the Likeness up, her wounds kept, and the fight's music still lifted: a fight that could be worn down one death at a time and a rise into her swing. Now a death while a guardian's fight goes on in the room you rest in builds that room afresh, as any other rise does: no guardian, the way in open, the zone's music as written, and walking in wakes her whole. `test_a_fight_lost_beside_its_own_shrine_starts_over` rests at the shrine by the real Up, walks in by the real Right, wounds her past her marks and falls, then walks in again.

**No rest while a guardian stands (pass 455).** The same shrine could be used during the fight: Up at it with the Likeness up made the hero whole, refilled his magic and his flask and saved, as many times as he liked. A shrine now refuses while any guardian's fight is going on (`BossArena.any_fighting`), as it does in the boss rush: it plays `menu_refuse`, says "The shrine is silent while the guardian stands." and mends and saves nothing. Before the fight, and once the doors open again, it rests as any shrine does. `test_no_rest_while_a_guardian_stands` presses the real Up at it before, during and after.

**A guardian parried with the buttons (pass 412).** Every boss parry test called `stagger()` itself. So a parry as a player makes it had never been played against a guardian: Block pressed on the real controller in the moments before a blow, the blow caught in the 0.12 s parry window, and the guardian thrown reeling. `test_thane_vorrund_is_parried_through_the_real_controller` checks this against Thane Vorrund's chop. Block pressed three frames before the axe falls parries it: he reels open, the parry rings, and the chop does no harm. Block pressed at the start of his 0.6 s wind-up is past the window by the time the blow lands, so it does not parry, and the raised shield guards half the chop.

**A guardian's blow jumped with the buttons (pass 428).** The parry was still the only boss defence played through `Input`. A jump over a low blow, the oldest dodge there is, never was. Thane Vorrund's combo is a tall chop that reaches 54 px and then a low, wide sweep that reaches 64 px at 22 px high. `test_thane_vorrunds_sweep_is_jumped_through_the_real_controller` stands 62 px from him. Standing still, the chop falls short and the sweep finds you. Jump pressed for real six frames before the sweep carries you over it, and nothing lands. Jump pressed once the axe is already sweeping is too late, and the sweep lands in full.

**A guardian holds still in hitstop (pass 474).** The Snapcrown's head, the Haar Wyrm's heads at rest and the Gloamweaver's lanterns sway gently while they wait, and that sway is where their hurtboxes are. It was timed by the wall clock, so through hitstop, when every blow freezes the whole game, the Snapcrown's head and the lanterns kept drifting under a blade that was frozen mid-cut (up to 3.4 px), and under a slower Game speed (14) they swayed at full speed while the rest of the fight slowed. Each foe now keeps its own game time (`Enemy.game_time`), which stops in hitstop and slows with the game, and the sways read it. Only the drawing's shimmer still reads the clock. `test_a_guardian_stands_still_through_hitstop` freezes each of the three for 0.4 s of real time and finds nothing moved, and the Snapcrown's clock runs 0.7 s in a second at 70%.

**A rest at every guardian's door (pass 396).** A lost fight sends you back to your last Ringreader or shrine (5.4), and the walk from there to the arena is paid on every retry. No line of 8 said how long it may be, and three guardians were 3 doors from the nearest rest. Skyrend was past Carillon's Ringreader, the Spire Foot and the Spire Stair. The Haar Wyrm was past Polesend's Ringreader, the Scorched Hall and the Dry Cistern. The Drowned Warden was past the Spring Gate's shrine, the Drowned Aqueduct and the Source Hall. Each now has a Meditation Shrine in the room before its arena: on the Spire Stair's top ledge by the roost door, on the Dry Cistern's east floor by the spring, and on the Source Hall's dry shelf at the water's edge, under the warning sign and well back from its Shade Knight. Every guardian's arena is now at most one door from a rest, counted in doors walked to the nearest shrine or Ringreader room. The exceptions are the Everbloom's veins and heart (2 and 3 doors), the last two halls of the final fight past the throne's shrine, which are left as one climb. `test_every_guardian_is_a_door_from_a_rest`.

**Every fight meets the ability it tests (pass 388).** Each of the source game's guardians names the ability its fight tests, but a fight can only test what the map makes you hold when you reach it. Six name one: Gnarlmaw the Climbing Glove's wall jumps, Durgan the Delver's Pick's pogo, the Haar Wyrm the Torrent, Snapcrown the Elven Stride's air dash, Skyrend Stormcall, and Thane Vorrund the Stillstone. With what the critical path (4.2) gives before each, five arenas were already out of reach without that ability. Snapcrown's was not: the Stride hangs in the Windloft's open air, and you could walk under it and on through the east door to the Crown-Leaf. An elven veil now stands before that door, so a dash is the only way through, and the Crown-Leaf, the Storm Ledge and the Sky Steps join the Stride's rooms. `test_every_guardian_is_met_with_the_ability_it_tests` (test_reachability) checks all six arenas against the reach checker, out of reach without the ability and in reach with it. `test_the_windloft_veil_keeps_the_bloom_for_the_stride` walks the real controller into the veil, and dashes it through to the Crown-Leaf. Gloamweaver tests reading by light and the Likeness all movement, so neither names a single ability. The Everbloom's section names none, and the Proving Scar on the way to him asks for every ability (`test_the_proving_scar_asks_for_every_ability`).

**Every guardian's numbers, as built (pass 340).** Each guardian below is told in words. Until pass 340 Gnarlmaw's section gave none of his three wind-ups, his health or the mark where his second phase starts, and Snapcrown's gave no mark for its third. No test read a guardian's phase marks or wind-ups from the bible. This table gives each guardian's health, the health marks where its phases begin (its `PHASES`) and every named wind-up in its script, before a phase quickens them. `test_every_guardians_numbers_are_written_in_bible_8` holds each row to its script, and each "Phase N (below ...)" line in a guardian's own section to the same mark.

**What a guardian pays (pass 380).** As with the foes (7, pass 378), the table gave what a fight costs and not what it pays: each guardian's purse and experience lived only in its script (`gold_min`, `gold_max`, `xp`; Gnarlmaw's `GOLD` and `XP`). The Gold and XP columns are the scripts' own, before the arena's threat scales the purse (10.1). Gnarlmaw's purse is a set 120, the others a range. The source game's nine guardians give 4,090 XP between them, the Everbloom's three parts 1,900 of it, and new five give 1,100 more: 5,190 in all, short of Ylden's Own's 5,500 (9), so the last titles are the foes' to give. A boss rush pays none of it (pass 260). `test_every_guardians_numbers_are_written_in_bible_8` holds both columns to the scripts, these totals, and 10.1's purse range (60 to 500 g) to the smallest and largest purse.

| Guardian | HP | Phases at | Wind-ups (s) | Gold | XP |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| Durgan | 260 | 65%, 30% | 0.7, 0.8 | 120 to 160 | 300 |
| Gloamweaver | 260 | 66%, 30% | 0.55, 0.6, 0.65 | 70 to 90 | 160 |
| The Haar Wyrm | 300 | 60%, 30% | 0.6, 0.7 | 80 to 100 | 200 |
| Snapcrown | 260 | 50%, 25% | 0.5, 0.55, 0.6, 0.7 | 120 to 160 | 220 |
| Skyrend | 320 | 50%, 25% | 0.6, 0.65, 0.8 | 150 to 200 | 260 |
| Thane Vorrund | 320 | 50%, 20% | 0.4, 0.45, 0.5, 0.6 | 120 to 160 | 400 |
| The Likeness | 320 | 50%, 20% | 0.4, 0.45, 0.5 | 140 to 180 | 450 |
| The Everbloom's avatar | 360 | 50% | 0.5, 0.55, 0.6 | 120 to 160 | 400 |
| The Everbloom's veins | 10 a vein | 50% | 0.6 | 150 to 200 | 500 |
