### 8.1 Gnarlmaw, the Root-Gnawer (Heartwood)
- **Concept:** A colossal armored grub that has been chewing the Tree's taproot dry. Its segments are plated in bark; its face is a wheel of mandibles.
- **Arena:** a cylindrical root chamber with wooden ledges on both walls.
- **Tests:** wall jumping (just earned).
- **Body:** 600 HP, spread over a chain of 26 bark-plated segments.
- **Phase 1:** bursts out of a wall and lunges across the room; you wall-jump over it. Its tail segments are the weak point. Before each lunge the wall he will burst from glows and cracks for 0.9 s.
- **Phase 2 (below 60%):** burrows into the ceiling and rains sap clumps that harden into temporary platforms. A column of falling dust marks the spot for 0.8 s before he plunges or dangles his tail there.
- **Phase 3 (below 30%):** tears the floor away; the fight continues on the wall ledges only.
- **Reward:** Thaw Seed, 1 skill point, restores the Heartwood Spring.
- **Reveal:** on entry the walls shake, the banner names him, and he bursts out of the wall across from you, roars with his mandibles spinning, and pulls back in before the first lunge. Nothing he does in the reveal hurts.
- **Stagger:** while he is stuck in a wall or dangling from the ceiling, tail hits build poise damage. At 48 he staggers for 2 s: plates crack open and every segment takes full damage, not just the tail.

### 8.2 Durgan, the Hammer of Kettleforge (Hollin Tower)
- **Concept:** A dwarf champion fused with a mining engine, piloting a walking siege drill: a rust-red riveted hull on two piston legs, a bronze drill, two exhaust stacks and a glass cockpit with the dwarf inside. The dwarf inside is still conscious and begging to be stopped.
- **Arena:** the Kettleforge Lift (tower 19,-4), a one-screen lift platform in an elevator shaft, reached from the Kettleforge Gate. From phase 2 the lift rises: girders and cable marks stream down the shaft wall behind the fight. (Built as one screen with the rise drawn behind it, rather than a scrolling arena, so the fight stays readable.)
- **Tests:** pogo. The hull lets only a quarter of a side blow through; down-strikes on the stacks and cockpit land in full and bounce you (Delver's Pick), so the fight is won from above. Ramming a wall or diving into the floor leaves the drill stuck (1.5 s / 1.1 s), open to any blow.
- **Phase 1:** drill charges (spins up 0.8 s, then rams across the lift; dodge onto the side ledges or jump it) and stomps (rears up 0.7 s, then slams a shockwave out each way).
- **Phase 2 (below 65%):** the lift rises; debris falls every 2.4 s, one rock over you, each marked 0.7 s before it drops (dodge it). Every third attack the drill runs to a wall, climbs it, marks your spot on the floor (0.7 s) and dives at it. Tells are 20% shorter, never under the minimum.
- **Phase 3 (below 30%):** the cockpit bursts open on the Everbloom-crystal harness that binds the dwarf. Side blows glance off completely; strikes from above hit the harness for 1.5x.
- **Reward:** Sun Seed and a skill point; frees Kettleforge. Later: unlocks the dwarf smith and the elevator to Hearthroot (section 4.4).

### 8.3 Gloamweaver, the Lantern Widow (The Haar)
- **Concept:** A spider-wraith that carries the souls of the drowned as lanterns on her legs. She is invisible except where her lanterns glow.
- **Arena:** the Sunken Chapel (mist 30,-7, 60x23), east of Lantern Landing: a drowned nave under fog of 0.92, with two side ledges and a high central ledge.
- **Tests:** reading by light, and keeping your blade ready in the dark. (Until pass 236 this line said double jump, but the Featherstep Boots lie past the fog her Lantern Key parts; at her chapel the player holds the Glove and the Delver's Pick.)
- **Body:** 260 HP. Her four lanterns are separate targets on her legs, and each is a light in the fog (70 px). A lantern breaks in one blow and costs her 18 HP. Each broken lantern frees a soul that clings to her as pale fire, so she shows a little more (8% plus 16% a lantern). Her tells always show at least 80%, so they stay readable in the murk.
- **Phase 1:** she stalks in the dark. Close up she sweeps her front legs (0.55 s tell); farther off she rears and pounces at where you stand (0.65 s tell).
- **Phase 2 (below 66%):** she climbs to the vault and hangs there. Web: a thread flickers toward you (0.6 s), then a strand shoots along it (360 px). If it catches you it reels you toward her for 0.9 s; a slash cuts the strand, and so does the Elven Stride's dash once you have it. (Pass 236: until then only a dash broke free, and the Stride lies a zone past her, so a player caught at her chapel had no answer. `test_a_slash_cuts_ripasheikus_web` holds it with only the Glove and the Delver's Pick.) Drop: she shivers (0.6 s), marking a shadow below, then falls on you and lies stunned for 1.3 s, taking 1.5x damage.
- **Phase 3 (below 30%, or all lanterns broken):** she comes down for good. She is fully visible, fast, and desperate, with tells a quarter shorter (never under the minimum).
- **Reward:** Lantern Key (opens the path to the Haar Wyrm), Mana Dew, a skill point.

### 8.4 the Haar Wyrm, the Haar Wyrm (The Haar)
- **Concept:** A three-headed serpent made of fog and bone, coiled around the Haar Spring. Only the head that is solid at the moment can be hurt.
- **Arena:** the Haar Spring (`haar/spring_of_haar`, east of the Dry Cistern): the spring on a shelf in the middle with the hydra's coil wrapped around it, three dry tanks across the floor (dry basins, 4.5), floating debris between them and steps up to the shelf. The water level changes across phases. The tanks are part of the fight: dry again on every try, full for good once he is beaten.
- **Tests:** Torrent (filling the tanks raises rafts, the high ground) and air control.
- **Phase 1:** the three heads take turns being solid (about 3.4 s each); the solid head hangs low over its tank and follows you along its lane. It rears back (0.6 s, its eyes flash and a ring marks where it will strike), bites down at you, and lies where it bit for about a second, open to blows; a parried bite stuns it longer. A mist head breathes: mist wells at its jaws and pools on the floor below it (0.7 s), then a low bank of fog rolls along the floor toward you (12 damage, can't be parried): jump it or stand on the tank walls. Blows on a mist head pass through.
- **Phase 2 (below 60%):** the three heads turn to mist and keep breathing; eyes glint in the coil. The fourth head comes out only once all three tanks are full: Torrent each one and its raft floats up. While a tank is dry it shimmers (its stain glows, water beads under it), and each tank that fills douses the head above it: the head sags, its eye goes dark and it stops breathing, so the player sees each tank count (playtest; the toast names Torrent and repeats while tanks stay dry). It hangs high under the shelf and bites no lower than the rafts and walls. Phase 1 can't be cut short past phase 2.
- **Phase 3 (below 30%):** the fourth head sinks, the coil bursts and the spring floods the arena to the tanks' rims; feet under the water take 8 damage a second. Fight on the rafts, the tank walls and the debris. The three heads take turns again, a quarter faster (never under `Enemy.MIN_TELEGRAPH`), and the fog rolls along the water's surface.
- **Reward:** Fall Seed and a skill point. With him gone the spring can be cleared (press Up at it), which restores the Haar Spring.
- **Sound:** his own theme (`haar_wyrm`), a roar at the start and at each phase change, a growl-and-clack bite, a throaty mist breath, and a rushing flood.
- **Look:** code-drawn at 2x for now (the body and necks in bone-ringed green scale, the heads in whole pixels; mist heads are see-through fog in the same shape). An HD sheet can come in the art polish pass.

### 8.5 Snapcrown, the Devouring Bloom (High Boughs, Sylvarre)
- **Concept:** A carnivorous flower the size of a house, rooted in a leaf, with a turtle-shell bulb that snaps shut. Snapcrown "chomps" (paku-paku).
- **Arena:** the Crown-Leaf (47,-10), east of the Windloft: a giant leaf over open sky, the Bloom rooted in its middle, bounce-pods at both ends and high perches above them. The way in from the Windloft is through an elven veil (pass 388), so the Bloom is always met with the Stride it tests.
- **Tests:** air dash through its vine lashes and over the gaps it bites in the leaf.
- **Body:** only the head can be struck. The shell turns 70% of a blow; after a bite the jaws hang open on the leaf for 1.3 s (the weak point) and every blow lands in full. 260 HP.
- **Phase 1:**
  - Bite (within about seven tiles): the head rears back, jaws parting, for 0.55 s, then lunges down where you stand for 24. Parried, the jaws hang open 0.9 s longer.
  - Vine whip (farther off): a vine coils at the root for 0.6 s, then lashes 170 px along the leaf on your side for 16, too long to parry. Jump it, or stride through it on the dash's invulnerability.
  - Seed spit (alternating with the whip): the bulb swells for 0.5 s and spits three seeds in arcs, 12 each, parryable.
- **Phase 2 (below half):** tells a fifth shorter (never under 0.35 s). Every other bite tears a four-tile chunk out of the leaf where it lands, up to three gaps with thorns below, never next to the root or the walls. Their thorns set you back beside the gap, never over it (6.13, pass 292). You stride or bounce across, and strike the open jaws from the gap's edge.
- **The brood (pass 257):** in the source game, Snapcrown looses Mothlings three at a time until those are destroyed. The remake's Bloom had no brood, so the fight lost the one thing the source game's was known for. From phase 2, when it would whip or spit from afar and none of its brood is left, it broods instead (the first far move below half, then no sooner than three moves after the last). The petals flare wide and spin for 0.7 s (a fifth shorter like every phase-2 tell, never under 0.35 s), with a rustling hum (`bloom_brood`, since pass 262), then three **Mothlings** fly up out of them. A Mothling (`Mothling`, code-drawn) is a violet imp the size of a hand with pink petal wings: 20 HP (10 until pass 258, when the sheet found it fell to one slash of the High Boughs' par blade), harmless for its first 0.5 s while it clears the petals, then it hangs above and to one side of you, each of the three in its own place. It screeches with its wings flared for 0.4 s, dives at where you were (250 px/s for 0.45 s, 8 on touch) and climbs back on the other side. Struck down, a Mothling never returns. While any flies the Bloom only whips and spits; once all three are down it may brood again. They die with the Bloom, paying nothing (pass 266, 5.5). Test: `test_the_bloom_looses_its_liliths_three_at_a_time`. Screenshots: `/mnt/project-files/pass-257/brood_260.png`, `brood_320.png`.
- **Phase 3 (below a quarter):** it uproots and chases you along a falling leaf (short chase sequence).
  - **As built (pass 90), below a quarter:**
    - When its head next draws back, it spends 1.2 s wrenching its root out of the leaf (the tell). The crown-leaf, cut loose, starts to fall (`LeafFall`): wind and torn leaves rush up past it, and the player weighs 85% while it falls, so jumps hang longer.
    - The Bloom scuttles after you on its roots at 80 px/s, slower than your run. It keeps 48 px from the walls and stops 40 px short of you.
    - Its bites lunge: the whole plant hops up to 80 px in behind its jaws. Every other bite still tears the leaf.
    - Get past it by running under its head or striding through it, and strike the jaws where they land.
    - When it dies the wind dies away.
  - **Deviation:** the chase stays inside the Crown-Leaf arena rather than scrolling along a separate falling leaf. The whole leaf is falling, which keeps the fight in one room and its doors sealed.
- **Look:** code-drawn at 2x (a green stem, two great leaves at the root, a shelled bulb with gold-rimmed jaws, white teeth and a red maw, and a ring of pink petals). Its own theme, `snapcrown`.
- **Reward:** skill point, large gold drop, and the way east from the crown-leaf toward Carillon (the Crown-Leaf's east door, sealed with the rest of the arena until the Bloom falls; built in pass 54). (Changed in pass 50 from "the Sylvarre gate opens": the town is the zone's save and shop stop, so it opens to you on arrival. Pass 52 put the arena past the Windloft, so the Bloom guards the way onward instead.)

### 8.6 Skyrend, the Storm Roc (High Boughs, Carillon)
- **Concept:** A titanic thunderbird nesting on the highest spire, its feathers made of rain clouds.
- **Arena:** three floating islands; the camera pulls out wide.
- **Tests:** Stormcall (charge the conduits on each island to ground the bird) and everything in the air.
- **Phase 1:** dive bombs and feather volleys; ground it by powering conduits.
- **Phase 2:** a storm; lightning hits the tallest point, so stay low or use it.
- **Phase 3:** aerial duel: you ride its updraft and pogo its head.
- **Reward:** Sky Key and a skill point; restores the Sky Spring.
- **As built (pass 60):** the Roost (58,-12, 60x29 since pass 94), east off the top landing of the Spire Stair. Three stone islands float over a cloud-stone floor, each with a dead conduit on top, with wooden landings stepping up to them. 320 HP. In the air it is mostly cloud and blows land at 30%; it touches for 14 as it flies.
  - Feather volley: it hangs still and throws its wings wide for 0.6 s (screech), then flings a fan of five storm feathers (8 each). A parried feather flies home and hits it for 12. A player perched high on the islands always gets the volley rather than a dive.
  - Dive bomb: it rises, screaming, and fixes on you for 0.65 s, then stoops through where you stood and on past it for 22, unparryable. Stride through it or get out of the line. It climbs back into the sky after.
  - Grounding: wake all three conduits with Stormcall (the bolt leaps to a dead conduit within 72 px, so cast from the island or from beneath it) and lightning arcs from each to the bird. It falls to the floor and lies stunned for 4.5 s, taking blows in full. Then it shakes the charge off, the conduits go dead and it climbs again, so every grounding means waking all three again. The roost's conduits leave no world flags.
  - Code-drawn at 2x, drawn half again larger than its body box (a storm-blue body with a pale chest, a gold hooked beak, a crest, and wings of chained cloud billows with flight feathers and rain trailing under them). Its own theme, `skyrend` (E minor at 150). Reward: the Sky Key and a skill point.
  - Phase 2 (pass 61, below half): the storm breaks. Every 3.2 s a bolt marks the tallest point for 0.6 s (a thread of light from the sky and a glow where it lands), then strikes that column for 18, unparryable. The tallest point is you, if you stand three tiles or more above the floor; otherwise it is one of the dead conduits, and the bolt wakes it. So the storm will wake conduits for a player who stays low, and punishes one who climbs. In return, a woken conduit now drains after 6 s unless all three are awake together, so the storm alone is too slow: you still Stormcall at least one yourself, in time. The volley throws seven feathers.
  - Phase 3 (pass 89, below a quarter): the duel. Every other move is the gale. It flies to whichever of the two open lanes between the islands is nearer you, beating its wings for 0.8 s while dust stirs on the floor where the wind will rise. Then it hovers there for 3.5 s over an updraft, a column of rising wind from the floor to a little above its head. The wind carries you up at 240 px/s and gives back your air moves. Hold Down to drop out of it onto the bird's head. While it holds the gale, blows from above (the pogo) land in full and it doesn't touch you, and the storm waits. Side blows are still turned by its cloud. Grounding it with the conduits still works.
  - The wide camera (pass 94, 6.7): the Roost has six rows of open sky above the fight and the camera pulls out wide, so all three islands, the bird and the storm are in frame at once. The roc keeps to the 16 rows above the floor. (The Sky Spring is restored in pass 62.)

### 8.7 Thane Vorrund, the Unweeping (Vorrhallow)
- **Concept:** The dwarf king who bargained with the Everbloom. Tragic, regal, half crystal. He fights with a great-axe and royal guards he summons.
- **Arena:** a flooded throne room; the water clock on the wall drains during the fight.
- **Tests:** Stillstone (freeze his crystal shards in the air and use them as platforms) and parrying.
- **Phase 1:** honorable duel; heavy axe combos with clear parry windows.
- **Phase 2:** summons crystal guards; shards fly; time-stop them into stepping stones to reach his crown.
- **Phase 3:** the Everbloom takes control; his body cracks; frantic, faster, corrupted patterns.
- **Reward:** Frost Seed, the Severance and a skill point; the dwarves of Vorrhallow are freed; Vorrhallow drains.
- **The grafting blade (pass 217, retold in pass 461):** only Severance, the blade Thane Vorrund used to cut the Everbloom from the Ylden, can part it from the tree. Beating him puts it straight into your hand (`BossArena.BOSSES.thane_vorrund.blade`), with the banner "Severance": "Thane Vorrund weeps at last, and gives up his grafting blade. With it, the Frost Seed." **He weeps (pass 479):** section 2 has him beaten, weeping at last and giving up the blade, and until pass 479 the game showed only the giving: the banner named the blade and the seed, and the Kennels' sign to his throne said after the fight "The king waits for no one now." No one in the game saw him weep; only Kettleforge's elder said so at second hand, after the end. The banner now says it, and so does the sign ("The king wept there at last, for his queen."). `test_every_turn_of_the_story_is_told_in_the_game` holds each beat of section 2 to the words the game speaks and to the room where they are spoken. The Heart Gate's altar is bare, with a sign: "An empty altar, cut to hold a blade. Thane Vorrund took his grafting knife down into Vorrhallow." A save past Vorrund that never took the sword from the altar gets it in hand (`GameState.sanitize`). Tests: `test_thane_vorrund_defeat_gives_the_frost_seed_and_the_severance`, `test_a_save_past_thane_vorrund_holds_the_severance`.
- **As built (pass 71, phases 1 and 2):** `scripts/bosses/thane_vorrund.gd`, code-drawn at 2x (a broad dwarf in a flood-rotted robe and a gold-edged mantle, a beard to his belt, the back of him grown over with magenta crystal, crystal grown up through his crown, a great-axe whose blade is half crystal), in the Throne Hall of the Hall of Still Water (`vorrhallow/throne_hall`, 58,-7, 60x23; titled Hall of Still Water since pass 241) off the Thane's Kennels, the dais at its east end six tiles high. 320 HP. Reward: the Frost Seed and a skill point, and since pass 217 the Severance (below).
  - Phase 1, the duel: he walks you down. Close in, he raises the axe overhead, its edge glinting, for 0.6 s and chops for 20, then draws it back low for 0.4 s and sweeps for 16. Both can be parried, and a parry leaves him reeling for 1.2 s, open to every blow. Otherwise his crystal half turns half of every blow. Keep away and he crouches 0.6 s and leaps after you, bringing the axe down for 22 (unparryable) with a shockwave rolling each way.
  - Phase 2, the throne (below half): he leaps onto the dais, plants the axe and wards himself in crystal: blows from the side do nothing, and crystal thorns along the dais burn anyone who climbs up beside him (10). Every 3.2 s his crown blazes for 0.5 s and four crystal shards (`scripts/bosses/crystal_shard.gd`) glow in the floor for 0.4 s, nearest the dais first, a quarter-second apart, then shoot up to the vault at 140 px/s, 12 to anything they pass through. Rising together they make a moving stair, highest by the throne. Turn the Stillstone and they hold where they are with flat tops to stand on; when time runs again a held shard cracks and falls. Climb them and strike down on his crown: he is thrown from the dais, open to every blow for 1.8 s, and duels again (tells at 0.8x) for 7 s before he climbs back.
  - Phase 3 (pass 72, below a fifth): the Everbloom takes him. He roars and cracks open for 1.4 s, untouchable, crystal light blazing through the cracks, and comes down off the throne for good. Frantic now, he walks you down at 1.8x, his tells at 0.65x (never under the minimum), and every chop and sweep ends with the axe raised again (0.45 s at full, shorter frantic) and driven into the floor: six crystal spikes burst out in a line toward you, 22 px and 0.08 s apart (jump them). Cracked, he turns no blow, and a parry still throws him reeling.
  - The household guard (pass 268): the concept above has him summon royal guards in phase 2, but until pass 268 the throne called only shards. Now the first wave of shards from the throne also calls a **Shade Knight** of his household guard (7, the drowned ghost of one of his own guard) up out of the flood. It rises with a splash at whichever end of the hall floor is farther from you: by the west wall, or just past the shards' reach. While it stands he calls no other. Once it falls, the second wave after calls the next, and no more than two come in a fight (`KingGrieve.GUARDS`, `GUARD_EVERY`). So the climb up the held shards has a parry fight on the floor below it, which is the fight's other test. His guard will not serve the Everbloom: when the Everbloom takes him (phase 3), and when he dies, it goes down with him (`Enemy.perish`, paying nothing, 5.5). `test_thane_vorrund_calls_his_household_guard_from_the_throne`. Screenshot: `/mnt/project-files/pass-268/guard_780.png`.
  - The water-clock (pass 72, room prop `water_clock`): a tall bronze-framed glass column of flood-water by the west wall. Full before the fight, it drains with his health and stands empty once he is beaten.
  - His theme, `vorrund` (pass 72): the Vorrhallow dirge risen into a march, D phrygian at 112, war drums, a tolling bell, crystal stabs on the offbeats.
  - After him (pass 73): a door behind the throne, barred by the fight's iron until he is beaten, leads up the Glass Rise (60,-9, 40x68), eleven switchback shelves of crystal-choked shaft with his guard still on them (Crystal Dwarves, Shade Knights, a hound), to the Crown Gate (61,-9): a shrine, and the gate of the Evercrown, black stillglass-iron with four ring sockets that glow for the rings you hold. It does not open yet: The Likeness (8.8) waits before it. The Sunken Spring's way down is marked, sealed, at the foot of the Rise (optional, M7). As built (pass 86): three cracked stones in the Rise's floor, under the sign; the Delver's Pick breaks them, and the flooded shaft below drops into the Sunken Spring.
  - Vorrhallow drains (pass 73, a first step): once he is at rest the drowned dwarves wake. His steward Hrafna stands by the empty throne and a mason in the Drowned Market, both saying the water is going down. The time-locked water stays where it is: the water-clocks still hold their hour, so the Stillstone routes are unchanged. Since pass 259 the Flooded Hall and the Drowned Nave stand in water until he is at rest and keep a tide mark after (4.4).

### 8.8 the Likeness, the Mirror (Vorrhallow, Crown Gate)
- **Concept:** A shadow copy of the player, made from every fight the Everbloom has watched. Uses your abilities and your equipped spell.
- **Arena:** a mirrored hall with a reflective floor; the player's reflection is the boss.
- **Tests:** mastery of all movement.
- **Phase 1:** mirrors your inputs with a delay.
- **Phase 2:** acts on its own, using your highest-level skill tree branch against you.
- **Phase 3:** splits into two; one is real (only the real one shows in the floor reflection).
- **Reward:** a skill point, and the Crown Gate opens.
- **As built (phase 1, pass 74):** the gate hall (`vorrhallow/evercrown_gate`) is laid out symmetrically, with a landing at
  each end, and a faint seam of light marks the mirror line down its middle. The shadow (`scripts/bosses/likeness.gd`)
  stands where you were 0.6 s ago, mirrored across that line, in your own pose, drawn as your sprite cut out of the dark
  with a violet rim. Each swing you make comes back at you 0.6 s later from its side, its blade glinting for 0.4 s first,
  so you can only reach each other where you meet at the line, and a blow you land is answered. Step away from the echo
  or parry it: a parried echo leaves it reeling and out of the mirror for 1.2 s. If you hold still for 2.2 s it sinks
  into the floor and rises right behind you, blade raised for 0.45 s, then cuts. Until phases 2 and 3 are built it has
  150 HP, and beating it gives a skill point. The gate does not open yet, because the Evercrown beyond it is not built.
- **As built (phase 2, pass 75):** HP is now 240 (320 since pass 218, 5.5), and below half the glass bursts. The shadow is untouchable for 1 s,
  then steps out with real physics and fights with the skill tree branch you have the most nodes in (Blade on a tie or
  an empty tree):
  - **Blade:** two cuts and a lunging heavy blow (tells 0.4/0.4/0.5 s).
  - **Arcana:** it keeps about 150 px away and casts shadow bolts (0.5 s tell); parry one and it flies back into the
    shadow.
  - **Wanderer:** it air-dashes through you (0.45 s tell), and once, below a quarter health, it stops to drink a
    draught (+12%) that a blow spills.
  - A parried blow staggers it for 1.2 s. It has its own theme, `likeness` (E minor, reversed pads, and a lead echoed a
    beat late from the other side).
- **As built (phase 3, pass 76):** below a fifth it splits in two (`SPLIT_TIME` 0.8 s, untouchable). The false
  double appears on your far side, out of the walls, and half the time the two trade places as they part. Both fight as
  in phase 2. Only the real one shows a faint upside-down reflection in the floor under its feet, and the boss bar
  follows it. Strike the false one and it swells for `BURST_TELL` (0.45 s), then bursts for 10 damage (unparryable)
  around itself, and the real one loses nothing. The real one splits again 3.5 s later. Beat the real one and the false
  one goes with it.

### 8.9 The Everbloom (the Evercrown)
- **Concept:** Not a demon king but a parasite: a vast eye-flower of stillglass rooted into the Tree's crown, feeding through veins of light.
- **Arena:** the heart of the Everbloom; three arenas in sequence.
- **Phase 1 (The Throne):** a humanoid avatar in armor duels you; standard boss.
- **Phase 2 (The Veins):** the avatar shatters; you climb a vertical chamber cutting light-veins while the core fires beams (uses wall jump, dash, pogo, Stillstone).
- **Phase 3 (The Core):** only the Severance can hurt the core; each strike restores a spring's light and the Tree fights alongside you (roots lash the core).
- **Reward:** a skill point for each of its three forms (the avatar, the veins and the heart), then the ending; credits climb up the Tree from Hearthroot to the crown.
- **The way in, as built (pass 77):** the gate of the Evercrown stands against the east wall of the gate hall, and the hall's
  door 2 is behind it. While shut, it bars the way from floor to vault. Once all four rings are held and
  `boss:likeness` is set, its leaves swing back on the Everbloom's cyan light and you walk through into `levels/evercrown/`:
  - **Threshold** (62,-9): a landing and a shrine, flesh pillars grown up from the floor and down from the vault, a
    spiked trench, and a Shade Knight and a Crystal Dwarf.
  - **Heart Stair** (63,-10, 40x45): switchback shelves of stillglass flesh with a Glass Slug, Crystal Dwarf, Shade
    Knights and a Clockwork Hound. At the top, the heart is not open yet.
  - the Evercrown has its own tiles (`paint_meteor`: rounded cells of black-violet scar tissue with blood-red seams and cyan
    pores), backdrops (ribbed walls, light veins, a red glow below), decor (polyps, veins, tendrils, wall eyes),
    cyan spore motes, a theme (B locrian over a heartbeat, detuned pads) and an ambience (breathing drone, heartbeat,
    glassy whines).
  - **Corrupted foes, as built (pass 78):** every foe spawned in an Evercrown room is `corrupted` (a room header
    `corrupted:` overrides it either way). Corruption is one remix applied to every family rather than new
    species: x1.5 health, double gold and experience, poison drops, a violet cast and stillglass grown out of
    the back with red veins and a cyan eye that beat with the Everbloom's heart. When it dies it leaves a
    **Stillglass Seed**: a crystal that hangs where it died and swells for 0.7 s, then bursts in a ring (30 px,
    10 damage, unparryable). One slash or a pogo cracks it harmlessly, so pressing forward is safer than
    backing off, and seeds give a corrupted fight a rhythm of pogo chains. The Stillstone holds seeds.
  - **Wrong gravity, as built (pass 79):** pockets of `GravityDrift` (legend `v`) where the player keeps 40% of
    their weight (`Player.gravity_scale`, gravity and top fall speed alike). A plain jump rises about 11 tiles and
    a fall drifts down. Leave the pocket mid-flight and the true pull takes hold. They read as violet columns
    with cyan motes rising through them. Only the player is affected. The movement numbers in section 6 are
    unchanged; the pocket scales them. The reachability checker allows a 9-tile rise from inside a pocket when
    the column is drift up to the landing.
  - **Drift Well** (64,-11, 40x45), beyond the Heart Stair's upper door: three drift pockets carry you up
    nine-tile ledges a jump could never reach, past a slug, a hound, a Crystal Dwarf, a hornet in the second
    pocket and a Shade Knight. A shrine is at the top. The heart beyond is not open yet.
  - **The Severance and the Heart Gate, as built (pass 80):** the Drift Well's top platform runs to an upper
    door into the **Heart Gate** (65,-11, 56x23). A hall with a Shade Knight, a Crystal Dwarf and a hound leads
    past a drift pocket to the flesh. The pocket lifts you to an altar where the **Severance** lay (legend
    `&`; tier 4 blade: slash 18, heavy 30, reach 12) until pass 217; it is now Thane Vorrund's (8.7), and the altar is bare. The way on is
    closed by a **heart seal** (legend `+`, `HeartSeal`): a wall of living flesh with a seam of cyan light and a
    watching eye. Every other blade glances off it, and one cut with the Severance tears it open for good.
    Beyond it, the heart (the Everbloom's arenas) is next.
  - **The Proving Scar, as built (pass 170):** the zone table promised the Evercrown a "gauntlet using every ability",
    and 4.2 puts it between the Severance and the Everbloom, but until pass 170 the Heart Gate opened straight
    onto the throne, and the climb to it asked for nothing past the Glove, the Stillstone and the drift. Now the
    Heart Gate's door 2 opens on **The Proving Scar** (`evercrown/proving_scar`, 67,-12, 80x45), a scar through the
    stillglass that walks every earlier road once more, one gate after another:
    1. A **fire seal** that only Torrent puts out.
    2. A **chimney** 23 tiles tall with no ledge in it, climbed only with the Climbing Glove.
    3. Two **steps** six tiles high whose faces are lined with thorn bulbs, so no glove can climb them. Only the
       Featherstep Boots' second jump reaches the top.
    4. A **gap** nine tiles wide over a pit, crossed only with the Elven Stride's dash. A Storm Harpy hangs in the
       pit, and thorns line the far wall so you can't climb out of the pit onto the far side.
    5. A **conduit gate** that only Stormcall wakes, guarded by a Shade Knight.
    6. A **plug of cracked stone** that only the Delver's Pick breaks, over a shaft down to a hound.
    7. A shaft of **time-locked water**, climbed only while the Stillstone holds it.
    8. An **illusory wall** at the top, which the Seer's Lens shows.

    Past these, the last hall holds a shrine before the throne's door. Its foes are corrupted like every the Evercrown
    foe. The checker crosses the Scar with every ability and fails without any one of them
    (`test_the_proving_scar_asks_for_every_ability`). The throne and the arenas past it moved two screens east
    on the map (throne 69,-11, veins 71,-13, heart 72,-13), and the Evercrown now has 8 of its 14 rooms.
  - **Phase 1, as built (pass 81):** the Heart Gate's door 2, past the flesh (and since pass 170 the Proving Scar), opens on the **Throne of the Evercrown**
    (`evercrown/throne`, 69,-11 since pass 170, 60x23, `boss: everbloom_avatar`), a long hall with two hanging planks and the throne's
    dais at the east end. The **Avatar on the Throne** (`EvilAvatar`, 360 HP, code-drawn at 1.3x) is a knight of
    black stillglass with a horned crown, a slit of light for eyes and a crystal greatsword. It stalks you
    and chooses from four attacks:
    - **Cleave** (close, tell 0.55 s): a wide overhead arc, 20 damage, parryable.
    - **Lunge** (middle, tell 0.5 s): a crouch, then a drive across the floor, 18 damage, parryable.
    - **Pull** (tell 0.6 s, a raised glowing palm): the Everbloom's wrong gravity drags you toward it for 1.2 s,
      then it bursts in a 52 px ring, 14 damage, unparryable. Run or dash against it.
    - **Rain** (far, tell 0.6 s): crystal spikes erupt in a line toward you, each marking its spot.

    It uses pull at least every fourth attack. Its plate halves blows to its front; its back, its crown and a
    parried guard (1.2 s reeling) are bare. Below half health its tells shrink to 0.75x (never under 0.35 s), its
    cleave comes back for a second swing and its pull is stronger. Beaten, the avatar shatters ("The Avatar
    Shatters", 1 skill point) and the light flees up the veins. Phase 2 (The Veins) is next. Theme: `everbloom`
    (B locrian at 120, a quickened double-kick heartbeat, a bass that drops a tritone, a cold lead).
  - **Phase 2, as built (pass 82):** the throne's door 2, high on its east wall, opens on **The Veins**
    (`evercrown/veins`, 71,-13 since pass 170, 40x68, `boss: everbloom_veins`), a tall shaft of stillglass flesh climbed on zigzag one-way
    ledges three rows apart. At its vault hangs the **core** (`EvilCore`), a crystal eye-flower that watches you.
    No blade reaches it. It feeds through six **light veins** (legend `x`, `LightVein`) grown above the ledges up
    the shaft; one cut (or a pogo, which also bounces you) severs a vein, and its health bar is the veins still
    lit (10 each). While you climb it fires **core beams** (`CoreBeam`) at you: the line flickers for its tell
    (0.6 s) and then burns for 0.35 s, 16 damage, unparryable. The core's `zap` marks the line; since pass 244 the lance sears as it fires (`core_beam`: a whoomph of light, a falling whine, a low thrum), so the dodge has a beat to the ear. Until then every other tell-then-strike hazard in the game sounded its strike, and the beam, the final fight's main pressure, was silent (`test_a_cores_beam_sears_as_it_fires`). Below half (three veins left) the tell is 0.45 s,
    volleys come every 1.6 s instead of 2.2 s, and every other volley is a pair of beams fanned to either side of
    you (stand still between them). The Stillstone holds the core and its beams. With the last vein cut the core
    gutters ("The Veins Go Dark", 1 skill point); the veins stay dark for good (`boss:everbloom_veins`). Deviation:
    the bible asked for wall jump and dash here too; the as-built shaft is a ledge climb so the beams are the
    pressure, and wall jumps are allowed but not required. Phase 3 (The Core) is next.
  - **Phase 3, as built (pass 83):** a door high on the Veins' east wall, reached from a ledge under the vault,
    opens on **The Heart of the Bloom** (`evercrown/heart`, 72,-13 since pass 170, 60x23, `boss: everbloom`), a long chamber with two
    low ledges. The **heart** (`EvilHeart`, 300 HP, `PHASES [0.66, 0.33]`) hangs far above the floor, a crystal
    eye-flower with petals all around it, out of any blade's reach. It drifts after you and attacks every
    2.0 / 1.7 / 1.4 s (by phase):
    - **Beam** (a `CoreBeam`; the line flickers for its 0.6 s tell, 0.45 s in phase 3).
    - **Seeds**: three Stillglass Seeds around you that swell for 0.7 s and burst. Crack them or step clear.
    - **Wave** (phase 2 on): crystal gathers under it for its tell, then spikes run out along the floor both ways.

    **The Tree fights alongside you:** every 7 / 6 / 5 s (the first after 4 s) the floor under the heart cracks and
    glows green for 0.8 s, and a **root** (`TreeRoot`) whips up, binds it and drags it to the floor for 2.4 s. Only
    then can it be hurt, and only by a swing of the Severance (other blades and magic glance off). Each third
    of its life cut away frees one of the springs' stolen lights (`HeartLight`, three sockets in the vault): the
    socket fills with blue light and pours a shaft down, the player regains a quarter of their health, and the
    roots come quicker. In phase 3 the heart bursts as the root lets go (56 px, 14 damage, unparryable), flashing
    for the last 0.5 s it is held, so step back before the release. The Stillstone holds the heart and its roots:
    stopping time while it is bound is time to cut. Beaten, it dies for good ("The Heart of the Bloom Dies",
    `boss:everbloom`, 1 skill point) and every light goes home. Deviation: the bible had each strike restore a
    spring; as built it is each third, so the restoration lands as a beat rather than a trickle. The ending and
    credits are next.
  - **The ending, as built (pass 84):** four seconds after the heart dies (the banner shows first; since pass 247
    the four seconds stop while the game is paused, where the ending had come on under the pause menu and shut it, and
    the wait belongs to the arena, so a world torn down in them never ends the game; `test_the_ending_waits_out_a_pause_and_dies_with_its_world`) the game
    marks the save `cleared` (and counts `clears`), freezes the world and plays the **EndingScreen** to its own
    theme (`ending`: the Hearthroot lydian come home at 76 bpm, a rising arpeggio for the climb):
    1. **Epilogue**: The Ylden against the night, the dead stillglass at its crown. Its cyan light drains
       away as motes, then the three springs in the trunk light one by one, under four cards ("The heart of the
       stillglass is dead." ... "And in Hearthroot, the fountain fills.").
    2. **Credits**: the view climbs the Tree from the houses of Hearthroot at its roots, past each zone's name
       (Hearthroot, the Heartwood, the Tower, the Haar, the High Boughs, Carillon, Vorrhallow, the Crown), the springs running
       clear in the trunk, branches and leaves thickening, the sky warming from night to dawn. It ends on the
       crown in leaf where the Everbloom was, with the sun coming up. The credits rise over the trunk.
    3. **Thanks**: "Thank you for playing", the completion card (section 11, pass 106), your rank and time played; a press returns to the title. **The time played is time in play (pass 437):** the flask's details (14) and this card show the time played, and until pass 437 it counted every second the game was open after a journey began, paused or not: a pause menu left open overnight showed as hours played. It now stops while the game is paused (a menu, the map, a sign being read, the flask shelf). `test_the_time_played_stops_while_the_game_is_paused` opens the pause menu with the real Pause and reads a sign, three seconds each, and neither adds to the time; three seconds of play add three. **Real time at every game speed (pass 475):** the clock counted game time, so at 70% Game speed (14) three seconds of play showed as 2.1, a ten-hour journey as seven on the flask and this card, and a boss's slow motion all but stopped it. It now turns each frame back into real time (`Feel.unscaled`; in hitstop, the real frame). `test_the_time_played_is_real_time_at_every_game_speed` plays three seconds at 100, 90, 80 and 70% and a second of slow motion, and each counts in full. The Inner Rings' and the boss rush's clocks still count game time on purpose: a run is timed by the game it plays, at any speed.

    Jump or attack turns an epilogue card early and holds the climb at 4x; pause skips to the thanks card,
    which ignores presses for its first second. The save is written with the cleared flag (save room and
    spot unchanged), so Continue resumes at your last shrine.
  - **The secret ending, as built (pass 88):** the save counts every death of the run (`deaths`, World adds one
    each time the player falls; a new game starts at 0). If the heart dies with `deaths` at 0 and the Elven Plate
    worn (`GameState.secret_ending()`), the save is also marked `cleared_secret` and the epilogue runs three more
    cards: "Far under Vorrhallow, the Sunken Spring wakes." (a fourth spring lights under the roots, ringing),
    "Its water climbs the whole Tree, root to crown." (a column of spring light rises up the trunk) and "No one
    saw you fall. The Tree remembers that." (the crown flowers in gold and white). Every thanks card now shows
    your deaths; the secret one adds "The Source runs again." and "A flawless climb, in the Warden's Battle
    Suit." Shoot it with `--ending=SECONDS[,PART] --secret`.
  - **The farewell (pass 167):** The game ends with the hero thanked by Hearthroot and leaving it, a wanderer
    again. The remake's epilogue had stopped at the fountain. Two last cards now close every ending, after the
    secret one's too: "In Hearthroot, the King waits by the full fountain to thank you." and "But the wanderer is
    already on the east road, walking on." While they show, the hero (the player sheet's own run cycle, drawn as
    a dark shape against the dawn, `EndingScreen.FAREWELL`, `wanderer_x`) walks out of the foot of the Tree and
    off the east edge before the climb begins. The plain epilogue is now six cards (24 s), the secret one nine.

### 8.10 Optional boss
- **The Drowned Warden (Sunken Spring):** an ancient guardian of the spring source; the hardest fight in the game; drops the Elven Plate.
  - **As built (pass 87):** the Source Hall's east wall opens on **the Wellspring** (64,-5, 60x45): a deep pool from row 20 down, an entry ledge and a far ledge with steps down to the water, two islands level with the surface and a plank over the middle. The Warden (`DrownedWarden`, 680 HP (520 until pass 218), boss id `drowned_warden`, music `warden`: E minor at 126, a rolling bass under a galloping beat and a fast ripple) is a colossus of pale limestone and green bronze with a dwarf-king's helm, lamp eyes and a trident, standing waist-deep; it rises out of the deep to begin and wades the pool between the islands (they hide its lower half).
    - **Thrust** (within about 12 tiles): the trident draws back, aimed at you and tracking for most of the 0.6 s tell, tines glowing, then drives 150 px along that line for 18. Parry it and it reels for 1.4 s, open.
    - **Wave** (farther, and every third attack in phase 1): arms raised and the water churning for 0.7 s, then a `TidalWave` runs across the surface at 230 px/s, 40 px tall and 24 px deep, 16 unparryable. Jump it, or dive under it (swimming, 6.8). In phase 3 a second wave follows from the far side.
    - **Geyser** (phase 2+, every third attack): it sinks out of sight (untouchable) and stalks you under the water, a trail of bubbles the only sign; the water boils at your spot for 0.6 s, then it bursts up through it in a 44 px column of water, 20 unparryable, and hangs streaming for 1.3 s, open to every blow.
    - **Undertow** (phase 3, every fourth attack): its chest lamp opens (0.6 s) and the current drags you toward it at 85 px/s for 1.4 s; then the trident goes up (0.45 s) and it slams around itself for 22, parryable.
    - Its stone halves blows from the front; from above (the plank is there for a pogo), from behind, reeling or hanging open it takes them all. At two-thirds and one-third it roars (1 s, untouchable) into the next phase; tells shrink to 0.88x and 0.75x, never under `Enemy.MIN_TELEGRAPH`. Contact hurts for 14.
    - **Reward:** the **Elven Plate** (armor tier 4, 60% less damage) goes straight on (BossArena reward key `armor`), with a skill point; every armor in the shops then shows as owned. It crumbles and sinks back into the spring.

Mini-bosses (about one per zone): The Foreman (rock golem), Marsh Hag Matriarch, Bark Knight Captain, Clockwork Alpha. All four are built (the Matriarch last, in pass 179).

**The Bark Knight Captain (built pass 171).** `scripts/bosses/bark_captain.gd`, code-drawn at 3x: the first knight the Ylden grew, half again as tall as his Bark Knights, with five plates of bark over pale heartwood, a crown of living leaves and a bough lance six tiles long. He keeps the Thorn Yard (`boughs/thorn_yard`, 46,-8, 40x23), through a new east gate in Sylvarre (door 3), on the `miniboss` theme. 240 HP. His moves, each with a tell of at least the minimum: His bough is footing like his knights' lances (pass 176, `test_the_captains_bough_holds_your_weight_until_he_pulls_free`): you land and stand on it, Down + Jump drops through it, the Delver's Pick pogoes off it, and when he pulls it from under you the usual coyote time still lets you jump before you fall to the floor. Kindle burns his whole bark coat off at once, as it does his knights' (pass 177); a plate still grows back each time he pulls free, so the Kindle spell bought in Polesend is the Thorn Yard's answer to his guard, at 20 MP a cast.

- **Thrust** (0.65 s tell, braced with the lance back): when you are level and within about seven tiles he drives the lance out to 110 px for 18, parryable. Then he roots for 1.3 s and the lance stays out as a one-way bough you can stand on or pogo off.
- **Vault** (phase 2, 0.7 s tell): when you keep your distance or stand off his level, he pole-vaults high and comes down lance-first where you stood, for 20. He hangs planted in the floor for 1.4 s.

His bark works like his knights' (section 7): while he wears it, every blow chips one of five plates and lands a quarter. Bare, or open, he takes blows in full, and a blow while he is open chips nothing. Each time he pulls free of a root or a planted lance, one plate grows back, so a slow fight never strips him. He is open while rooted, while planted, and while reeling from a parried thrust (1.4 s). Below half his tells are 20% shorter. Reward: a Mana Dew (no skill point). He is not in the Boss Rush.

**The Marsh Hag Matriarch (built pass 179).** `scripts/bosses/hag_matriarch.gd`, code-drawn at 3x: the oldest of the Haar's hags and mother of all of them, grown huge and bent under a bell of dry reeds and a heap of moss, a great marsh-wisp in the crook of her willow staff. She keeps the Matriarch's Mire (`haar/hag_mire`, 35,-6, 40x23, fog 0.5), through a new east door at the end of Polesend's boardwalk (door 2), on the `miniboss` theme. Two plank shelves at either end and a high one in the middle give height to dodge from. 230 HP, contact 10. She fights as her daughters do, from a distance and through the fog, and her wisp (a light, brighter while she casts) shows where she is. Her moves, each with a tell of at least the minimum:

- **Hex** (her staple, 0.8 s tell, staff raised, a cackle over welling bubbles): rings of bog light mark the floor under you and to either side, 40 px apart (three marks; five below half). Then a mud hand bursts out of every mark (`MireGrasp`, 16 since pass 226, unparryable): step between them, or onto a plank. Strike her mid-hex and the whole spell breaks: every mark fizzles, the breaking blow lands in full, and she gropes for her staff for 1.4 s, open.
- **Sink** (0.5 s tell, bubbles and a gurgle as she settles): stay within five tiles of her for half a second and she sinks into the bog, untouchable for 0.6 s, and rises (0.4 s) at the far end of the mire from you. She drifts away from you as she walks, so you must chase her through the fog.
- **Call** (below half, every third move while no daughter walks; 0.7 s tell): she calls a Marsh Hag up out of the bog at the far side, never more than one at a time. The daughter sinks with her when she dies.

Her reed shawl halves every blow unless she is open: groping for her staff, or reeling from a parry (1.2 s; a parry also fizzles any marks). Below half her tells are 20% shorter. Her voice is her own (`matriarch_cackle`, `bog_sink`, `bog_rise`) over her daughters' hex sounds. Reward: a Mana Dew (no skill point). She is not in the Boss Rush. Tests: `test_boss.gd` (the Matriarch's tests, and `test_a_blow_mid_hex_breaks_the_spell_and_opens_her`).

**The Clockwork Alpha (built pass 169).** `scripts/bosses/clockwork_alpha.gd`, code-drawn at 3x: a great brass hound with a crown collar and a winding key in its back, the first of Thane Vorrund's Clockwork Hounds. It waits in Alpha's Den (`vorrhallow/alpha_den`, 57,-6, 40x23), under a plank-lidded shaft in the Thane's Kennels' floor, on its own `miniboss` theme. 260 HP; its plating halves every blow unless it is open. Its moves, each with a tell of at least the minimum:

- **Pounce** (0.6 s tell, a crouch and a ratchet): a leap at you with a bite of 18. It skids 0.55 s on landing.
- **Charge** (0.7 s tell, the key spinning): it runs at 300 px/s (340 in phase 2) for up to 2.6 s, with a body blow of 20. If it reaches a wall it jams there for 1.6 s, gears spilling.
- **Howl** (phase 2 only, 0.8 s tell): it winds a Clockwork Hound pup out of a grate at either end of the den, never more than 2 at once. Its pups run down when it dies.

It is open when a parry flips it (1.5 s on its back), when it is jammed in a wall, or while the Stillstone is turned: clockwork stops dead in stopped time, in the air or not. Below half its tells are 25% shorter. Reward: a Life Fruit (no skill point; a mini-boss gives none). It is not in the Boss Rush, which keeps to the zone bosses and the Foreman.

---

## 9. Skill Tree

The **Ringreader** in each town teaches skills. Points come from:
- **Ranks:** XP from kills raises your title through 16 ranks named for a tree's growth (Seedling, Sprout, Sapling, Thornling, Barkskin, Wayfarer, Ranger, Bough-Guard, Leafblade, Heartwood, Rootwarden, Stormbough, Oakheart, Crownward, Elder, Ylden's Own). Each rank after Seedling = 1 point (15 total).
- **Bosses:** 1 point each from the 12 guardians whose reward names one (12 total): Gnarlmaw, Durgan, Gloamweaver, the Haar Wyrm, Snapcrown, Skyrend, Thane Vorrund, the Likeness, the Drowned Warden, and the Everbloom's three forms (avatar, veins and heart). The Foreman and the three mini-bosses give their prize instead (8).
- **Grain Shards:** hidden secrets; 3 shards = 1 point (15 shards, 5 points, plus one from each of the six Inner Rings: 7 points in all). Pass 243 turned the two spare shards the rooms had grown (17, so two never made a ring) into chests, in the Slag Heap and on the Marsh Road.

That is **34 points** available against a tree planned at **68** in all (49 as built, over 27 nodes), so players must choose. (Pass 333: this line said 54, the first draft's total. The list below, three branches of twelve, has cost 68 since that draft, and nothing summed it. `test_the_bible_prices_the_skill_tree_as_built` now adds up the list's brackets, holds each built node's bracket to `data/skills.json`, and holds the built total and node count to the code.) **Counted since pass 253:** this line said 29, from the 9 main bosses planned. But the guardians built since then each give a point, so the game has handed out 34 since the Everbloom's forms were built. Nothing checked it. `Completion.skill_points` now counts every source (ranks from `GameState.RANK_XP`, the guardians' rewards in `BossArena.BOSSES`, and the rooms' Grain Shards plus one for each Ringreader Trial, three to a point). The Journey page shows "Skill points" earned against that whole. Earned is the points held plus what the learned skills cost, so learning and unlearning never move it. Test: `test_the_journey_counts_every_skill_point_the_game_gives` pins it at 34. Respec at any Ringreader for gold (200 g, then 400 g, 600 g and so on, capped at 2,000 g).

**Learned and unlearned with the buttons (pass 453).** The tree's rules were held by calls (`Skills.learn`, `Skills.respec`), but no skill had been bought through the Ringreader's menu with the buttons. `test_a_skill_is_learned_and_unlearned_with_the_buttons` presses for real at a Ringreader: Up opens his menu, Down and Jump open Learn skills, the cursor waits on Keen Edge past the Blade branch's name, and Jump learns it for its point, the blade cutting harder at once; Jump on it again spends nothing; Dash steps back out to play. Up, Down twice and Jump on Unlearn skills then forgets it for its gold, and the point comes back.

**The Blade skills, by the buttons (pass 458).** Fourth Strike, Rising Cut and Riposte had been proved with scripted intents only. `test_the_blade_skills_answer_the_real_controller` presses for real: four taps of Attack, each a tap after the light cooldown, run the learned ground combo to a fourth hit that alone is heavy, every cut with Keen Edge's damage; Up with Attack throws a Blinkhop more than 30 px into the air; and Block pressed as a Rot Dwarf's blade comes down parries it, and the Riposte answers once with a heavy cut, heard.

**Bloodletting, by the buttons (pass 464).** Its test had cut a foe staggered by a call. `test_bloodletting_answers_the_real_controller` makes the opening the way a player does: a real Block parries a Rot Dwarf's blade. Real Right closes in, and two real Attack taps while he reels each heal 2 HP with the `bloodlet` sound. Once he is back on his feet, the same tap still cuts but heals nothing.

**Executioner on a guardian, by the buttons (pass 466).** Its test had cut a Rot Dwarf that was put in the boss group and staggered by a call. `test_executioner_cuts_into_a_parried_guardian_by_the_buttons` plays it against Thane Vorrund twice, with the Blade branch learned up to the Thrust. Each time, a real Block parries his chop, real Right steps in, and one real Attack tap cuts into his reeling. Without Executioner the cut lands plain. With it, the same cut lands for 35% more and plays the `execute` sound.

Rank XP thresholds (in `GameState.RANK_XP`): 0, 40, 100, 180, 300, 460, 660, 900, 1,200, 1,560, 2,000, 2,500, 3,100, 3,800, 4,600, 5,500. Starting enemies give 5 XP (Rot Dwarf 12), so the Heartwood take a careful player to about Thornling. Ranks rise the moment you pass a threshold and give the point right away; the Ringreader is where you spend it. As in Yldenfall, the title itself is the Ringreader's to give: the next time you visit one after ranking up, the Ringreader names you by your new title ("From this day you shall be called ADEPT") to a short fanfare while the music dips, then opens the usual menu. Higher ranks hear grander words. `GameState.data.named_rank` remembers the last title named.

**The titles' climb (pass 369):** no title costs less than the one before it (the steps run 40, 60, 80, 120, 160, 200, 240, 300, 360, 440, 500, 600, 700, 800 and 900), and there is no last leap, so the final point is not a grind. Reeve Aldous gives his 1,500 g once a journey, whatever you carry, so a coin picked up on the walk to his hall never costs you the purse (`Npc.KING_GOLD`, the flag `king_gold`). `test_the_titles_climb_as_the_originals_do` holds the curve to this shape and the Reeve's purse to a hero who already holds gold.

Three branches, 12 nodes each. Costs in brackets. Nodes deeper in a branch need the one before.

**What a skill waits on (pass 237).** A branch deepens in order, not by what you have found, so a skill can be learned before the ability or spell it works through. Pick Mastery can be learned before the Foreman, Time Thief before the Stillstone, and Feather Fall before the Featherstep Boots. Only Second Stride, Feather Fall, Wisp Swarm and Deep Wither said so, and only in their own words. Now each of the nine names what it uses (`uses` in `data/skills.json`: the Delver's Pick, Torrent, Wisplight, Stormcall, the Stillstone, Wither, the Stride twice, and the Featherstep Boots). Until that is found, its line in the tree ends "Does nothing until you find the Featherstep Boots." (`Skills.waits_on`, `Menus.skill_rows`). Nothing stops the choice, since a point spent ahead still pays off later, but the tree no longer spends a scarce point without saying so. `test_the_skill_tree_says_what_a_skill_waits_on` checks every "uses" against the real abilities. It also catches a skill whose text names an ability without one, and keeps every line within the menu's three.

Nodes are built tier by tier as the acts that need them arrive. In the game now: Blade 1-4, Arcana 1-3 plus Wisp Swarm (after Torrent Pierce, 2 points: three orbs fanned 0.5 rad apart, 10 damage each, for one cast's MP; pass 95) and Wanderer 1-4 (`data/skills.json`). Rising Cut lifts any walking foe of 40 HP or less (`Enemy.LAUNCH_MAX_HP`) and leaves it helpless for 0.5 s; rooted foes (Sap Spitter, Bog Puffcap), fliers and bosses stay put. Torrent Pierce strikes each foe in the bolt's path once. Second Stride does nothing until you own the Elven Stride.

**The Act III tier (pass 100).** Ten more nodes take the built tree from 15 points to 36, past the 29 points the game hands out (38 with Feather Fall, pass 101), so a full build needs choices:
- **Blade**, after Pick Mastery: Riposte [2], Keener Edge [2], Bloodletting [2].
  - Riposte: a parry answers with a free heavy cut at the foe that swung (`Player._riposte`).
  - Keener Edge stacks with Keen Edge, for +20% melee damage in all.
  - Bloodletting heals 2 HP per hit on a staggered foe.
- **Arcana**, after Wisp Swarm: Thrift [2], Forked Storm [2], Time Thief [2].
  - Thrift makes spells cost 15% less.
  - With Forked Storm, a Stormcall bolt that strikes a foe leaps to the 2 nearest other foes within 110 px, one after another, for 75% damage each. The arcs fall from above, so shields don't stop them.
  - Time Thief makes the Stillstone hold 3.5 s instead of 2.5 s (`TimeStop.start(extra)`, `TimeStop.total`).
- **Deep Wither (pass 134)**, the Arcana branch's capstone, after Time Thief [3]: the Wither spell costs half its MP (`Player.RING_OF_DEATH_MULT`, applied after Thrift by `Player.spell_cost`), so 20 MP, or 17 with Thrift. The scroll alone already unlocks Wither, so the node's "unlocks the spell" half is dropped; its menu line says it needs the scroll. The built tree now costs 41 points against the 34 the game gives (counted as 29 until pass 253).
- **Wanderer**, after Second Stride: Feather Fall [2] (pass 101, see 6.10), Treasure Sense [2], Resilience [2], Last Stand [2], Second Wind [3].
  - Treasure Sense puts an orange star on every room the map shows (seen or charted) that still holds an item or chest. The World's catalog lists each room's treasures.
  - Resilience adds one mask (`GameState.RESILIENCE_HP`).
  - Last Stand: below 20% health, you take 25% less damage.
  - Second Wind: once between rests (a shrine, a Ringreader or a respawn), a killing blow leaves you at 1 HP.
- **Wanderer's Grace (pass 142)**, the Wanderer branch's capstone, after Second Wind [3]:
  - A dash lets harm pass through you for 0.2 s instead of 0.1 s (`Player.GRACE_INVULN`, `Player.dash_invuln`).
  - Any foe's death gives back the air dashes you have spent (`Enemy.die` calls `Player.on_kill`), with a puff of feathers and a catch of wind. So a kill in midair can be dashed out of, and a run of kills across a gap can carry you over it.
  - The built tree now costs 44 points (Wanderer 19 over 10 nodes) against the 34 the game gives (counted as 29 until pass 253).
- **Thrust (pass 149)**, the Blade branch's eighth node, after Bloodletting [2]: a thrust, as a charged move.
  - The press still swings at once, so the charge builds through the first cut. Held past `thrust_charge` (0.4 s), the blade rings and a spark of light runs off its point. Let go and you thrust.
  - The thrust deals the heavy hit times `thrust_mult` (1.5) through `blade_mult()`, so Keen Edge and the War Glove count. Its point is a long, low box, 52 px past the body (a cut reaches about 34) and half a cut's height. It strikes every foe in it once and drives them back like a heavy hit.
  - On the floor it lunges at 320 px/s for 0.16 s, about 50 px. The lunge stops at the first foe it strikes and recoils like a cut, so it never carries you into a body. Off a ledge it ends, and you step off at no more than a run (pass 153; until then the lunge's full 320 px/s threw you about 67 px out past the lip, four times a run's step). Down a ramp it hugs the floor like a ground dash (10 px snap, 6.5) and rides the slope to the end. In the air the thrust strikes where you are and carries you nowhere, so it never crosses a gap the Elven Stride gates.
  - A blow, a dash, a guard, a drink, a ladder or the water spends the charge. The Likeness echoes a thrust as a cut.
  - The built tree now costs 46 points (Blade 13 over 8 nodes) against the 34 the game gives (counted as 29 until pass 253).
- **Executioner (pass 163)**, the Blade branch's ninth node, after Thrust [3]: melee blows on a boss in its opening land for 35% more (`executioner_mult` 1.35, in `combat_tuning.gd`). A common foe gets nothing more, nor does a boss on its guard.
  - Each boss names its opening (`Enemy.punishable()`; for a boss of pieces, `BossSegment.punishable()` asks the boss), so the skill has a window in every fight (8). Bloodletting heals on the same openings. Until now it read only a parry stagger, which most bosses never take.
  - The built tree now costs 49 points (Blade 16 over 9 nodes) against the 34 the game gives (counted as 29 until pass 253).

Deviations, and why:
- The build order skipped Thrust (built in pass 149), Wildfire and the rest. They are bigger mechanics and still come later in their branches.
- Light Step is dropped: a dash's own invulnerability already carries you over spikes, so the node did nothing.
- Pathfinder is dropped: shrines already refill potions.
- Treasure Sense takes Light Step's place in the build order, at 2 points.
- **Every number on a skill's page is the code's (pass 322).** Each built skill's in-game page (`data/skills.json`) and its line in this list name the number the code gives: the skill is learned alone and its effect read off the hero (`test_every_skill_page_names_the_number_it_gives`). Nothing held them before, so retuning Bloodletting or Quick Draught would have left both promising the old figure. Executioner's line here said "staggered bosses"; it pays on any opening (pass 314 gave every guardian one without a shield), as its page says.

**Blade (melee)**
1. Keen Edge: +10% melee damage [1]
2. Fourth Strike: ground combo gains a 4th heavy hit [2]
3. Rising Cut: up-slash launches small enemies [1]
4. Pick Mastery: pogo deals +50% damage [1]
5. Riposte: parry triggers an automatic counter-slash [2]
6. Thrust: hold attack 0.4 s for a charged lunge (a thrust) [2]
7. Keener Edge: +10% melee damage [2]
8. Bloodletting: hits on staggered enemies heal 2 HP [2]
9. Whirlwind: air spin attack [2]
10. Heavy Hand: heavy hits stagger elites faster [2]
11. Executioner: +35% damage to a boss in its opening (staggered, or any opening section 8 names) [3]
12. Dragon Heart: charged lunge releases a shockwave [3]

**Arcana (spells)**
1. Clear Mind: +20 max MP [1]
2. Siphon: melee hits restore +50% more MP [1]
3. Torrent Pierce: water bolts pierce [1]
4. Forked Storm: Stormcall jumps to 2 more targets [2]
5. Wildfire: Kindle leaves a burning patch [2]
6. Wisp Swarm: Wisplight fires 3 orbs [2]
7. Thrift: spells cost 15% less [2]
8. Mana Shield: blocking costs MP instead of HP when shield breaks [2]
9. Overflow: full MP doubles spell damage for the next cast [2]
10. Time Thief: Stillstone lasts 1 s longer [2]
11. Elemental Mark: spells mark enemies; melee hits on marked enemies deal bonus damage [3]
12. Deep Wither: unlocks the Wither spell if the scroll is owned; halves its cost [3]

**Wanderer (movement and survival)**
1. Deep Pockets: +1 Sapflask charge [1]
2. Gold Sense: coins magnet from farther away; +10% gold [1]
3. Quick Draught: drinking a potion is 40% faster [1]
4. Second Stride: 2 air dashes [2]
5. Feather Fall: hold jump to glide slowly after double jump [2]
6. Light Step: no spike damage while dashing [1]
7. Treasure Sense: map shows rooms with unfound secrets [2]
8. Resilience: +1 mask [2]
9. Last Stand: below 20% HP, take 25% less damage [2]
10. Pathfinder: shrines restore potion charges [2]
11. Second Wind: once per rest, survive a lethal hit with 1 HP [3]
12. Wanderer's Grace: dash invulnerability +0.1 s and dash refreshes on kill [3]

## 10. Shops and Economy

### 10.1 Currency

**Gold** only. Enemies drop gold scaled by zone; chests and secrets give lumps.

**As built (the gold sheet, pass 197).** Until pass 197 a table here promised drops of 35 to 70 g a foe in Vorrhallow. In the code no foe paid more than 16 anywhere, so "scaled by zone" was not true: the chests carry the economy. Pass 197 makes it true by the same measure as the damage. A foe's gold is multiplied by its zone's threat (5.5, `Enemy.die` through `Room.threat`), so a Vorrhallow foe pays double for hitting double. An Evercrown foe pays double again for its corruption. A boss's purse (60 to 500 g) takes its arena's threat as well, as its blows do (pass 196). The numbers, from the rooms:

| Zone | Foe drop (after threat) | Chests (count, range) | Chest gold | One pass of foes |
|------|------|------|------|------|
| Hearthroot | none | 3: 60 to 1,987 | 2,167 g | none |
| Heartwood | 2 to 9 g | 12: 90 to 200 | 1,500 g | about 240 g |
| Hollin Tower | 2 to 14 g | 20: 160 to 300 | 4,510 g | about 430 g |
| The Haar | 4 to 17 g | 9: 180 to 900 | 3,080 g | about 260 g |
| High Boughs | 3 to 18 g | 7: 260 to 1,800 | 4,040 g | about 160 g |
| Carillon | 3 to 26 g | 5: 380 to 3,200 | 5,220 g | about 190 g |
| Vorrhallow | 16 to 32 g | 6: 520 to 5,000 | 8,640 g | about 540 g |
| Sunken Spring | 12 to 32 g | 2: 120 and 160 | 280 g | about 120 g |
| The Evercrown | 8 to 64 g (corrupted, x2) | none | none | about 1,080 g |

**The sheet kept to the rooms (pass 341).** The table was drawn in pass 197 and the rooms kept growing after it. By pass 341 the Heartwood held 12 chests where it said 9, the Tower 20 where it said 13 and the Haar 9 where it said 8: 1,970 g the sheet didn't know. The par purse after Knight's Plate had grown from about 3,700 g to 4,830 g. Nothing failed, since the par test only asks that the kit stays affordable. `test_the_gold_sheet_counts_the_rooms` now holds every row (the foes' drop range, the chests' count, range and gold, and one pass of foes to within 10 g) and the purse left after Knight's Plate to the rooms.

**Par without grinding**, held by `test_the_par_kit_is_affordable_without_grinding`. Start with the King's 1,500 g, open half the chests and fell each foe once. That is enough to buy the damage sheet's par kit (5.5) at the shops where it stands: the Wayfarer's Sword and Leather in Hearthroot, Riveted Coat in Amberlee after the Heartwood, Temper I in Kettleforge after the Tower, and Temper II and Knight's Plate after Carillon. About 4,800 g is left after Knight's Plate. Potions are a flask refilled at every rest, not bought, so the deeper damage costs no gold. Barkbalms (300 g) and the Amber Draught (1,500 g) keep their prices.

The goal: buy one key upgrade per zone without grinding, and buy everything only if you explore.

**Gold stays in the room (pass 285).** Pass 283 kept every food and item drop inside its room, but coins were left out. A coin falls until something stops it, so gold from a foe slain over a floor gap fell through and was lost unless the hero stood within the magnet's 100 px. Struck from a ladder with a Rune Dagger or a spell, the Tower's two Silt Adders lost 18 and 13 of 72 test coins down their shafts. Coins now keep to the room by the same rule as drops (`Drop.keep_in_room`): a coin stops at a side edge, and one that falls through a gap rests on the floor at the gap's nearer edge. `test_a_silt_adders_gold_lands_where_you_can_reach_it`.

**No drop rests at a side door (pass 307).** A drop or coin that reached a side edge stopped 8 px in, inside the door's cell, and one could also come to rest on the tile just inside. A door is three tiles tall, so a jump over a flask there meets the wall above the door and drops you on it: once pass 306 made poison bite 4 a second in the deep zones, a flask by a door was a toll on the way out unless you waited out its 10 s. Where the two tiles inside a side edge are open, `Drop.keep_in_room` now stops drops and coins on the second of them (`Drop.DOOR_CLEAR`, 40 px in). A hop from anywhere 20 to 44 px short of the flask clears it and lands short of the door (`test_a_drop_never_rests_in_a_doorway`); at one tile in, no hop of any timing did. Gold is drawn in by the magnet as before.

**No drop rests on spikes (pass 308).** Pass 300 moved a purse off the Bloom's torn leaf, but not the other drops. A Mothling struck down over a tear dropped its bread, mana or War Glove into the thorns, and bread lying on the leaf when it tore fell in after it. Taking it cost a blow, so the 2.5% War Glove was a trap. A drop that comes to rest on spikes, a torn pit's or a level's, now moves to the nearest floor beside them, at most four tiles up the pit's side (`Drop.SPIKE_STEP_RISE`). `test_a_drop_never_rests_in_the_thorns` tears the leaf under bread and drops a War Glove into the pit: both rest on the leaf and are taken without a blow. Gold needs no rule: the magnet draws coins out of a pit.

### 10.2 Shops

| Shop | Location | Sells |
|------|----------|-------|
| Weapon smith | Hearthroot, later Kettleforge dwarf smith | Blades, smith upgrades |
| Armorer | Hearthroot, Amberlee, Carillon | Armor, shields |
| Item shop | every town | Barkbalm (10 s invulnerability, 300 g; carry up to 3, rubbed on from the pause menu), Amber Draught (auto-revive, max 1, 1,500 g), Mana Dew (one per shop, 500 to 1,200 g), Life Fruit (one per shop, 800 to 2,000 g). As built, every one of the source game's eight towns has a keeper who sells an Barkbalm or an Amber Draught and one who sells Copper Keys, the source game's tool shop and key shop (pass 209, `test_every_town_keeps_the_originals_shops`). The Mana Dew and Life Fruit stand in the larger towns' shops only (Hearthroot, Amberlee, Sylvarre, Carillon). The planned Blightcap cure (80 g) was dropped: poison lasts only 8 s (16 health in the Tower, 32 at most in the deepest zones), and bread, the Kettleforge hospital and any rest at a shrine or Ringreader already clear it for free |
| Key master | Hearthroot, Lampwick, Vorrhallow | Copper (150 g), Silver (400 g), Gold (900 g), Thorn (1,800 g) keys; the Glass key is found. As built (pass 117), the grocers are the key masters: every grocer sells Copper Keys, Amberlee's the Silver, Sylvarre's the King and Carillon's the Thorn, each a zone before its door. Lampwick's Keymaster Odo (pass 145) is the one key master by trade: Copper and Silver Keys, the Silver for the Feather Hollow's vault next door, with ointment and elixirs. **One key per door (pass 245):** each card has exactly one door in the world, yet every grocer cuts Copper Keys, so a second key of a card was gold for nothing. A shop stops selling a card's key once the keys you hold open every door of it still shut (`Shop.doors_shut`, read from the rooms by `World.world_locks`): the row reads "Enough" and says "Enough for every Copper door left", or "Its door stands open" once it is opened. `test_a_key_shop_sells_no_key_without_a_door` |
| Cartographer | Hearthroot, Kettleforge, Lampwick, Sylvarre, Carillon, Vorrhallow | each zone's chart (40 to 800 g; one for every zone the map draws, the Evercrown aside), map pins. As built (pass 277): Amberlee and Polesend keep none, since Hearthroot sells the Heartwood's chart and Lampwick and Sylvarre the Haar's |
| Spell scrolls | Polesend, Carillon | Kindle (1,200 g), Wisplight (2,500 g). As built: Polesend's Scribe Orla (pass 148) and Carillon's Scribe Ysolde both sell Kindle |
| Ringreader | every town | save, skill tree, respec, rank-up |

Carillon as built (pass 57): Calder sells Knight's Plate (tier 3, 45% less damage, 4,200 g); the Warding Shield (3,600 g, parry reflects small projectiles) joined his stock in pass 91 (see 5.2). The scribe sells Kindle, and Wisplight (2,500 g) since pass 95. The grocer sells Amber Draught, Barkbalm, Copper keys and a Mana Dew (1,200 g, once), and the bell-ringer the Carillon chart (700 g).

**Kettleforge's hospital and larder (pass 175):** in the source game, Kettleforge kept a hospital, a meat store and an item shop, and the remake's Kettleforge had only the smith and the surveyor, so it was the one town without an item shop. Now Provisioner Dagny keeps the Kettleforge Larder (an Amber Draught 1,500 g, the Silver Key 400 g; her greeting offers the source game's dried meat), and Physician Halvar keeps the hospital, the town's other house. Kettleforge does not charge the one who silenced the drill, so his treatment is free: health and magic made whole and poison drawn out (`Npc.treat`, role `physician`). It neither saves nor refills the Sapflask flask, which stay the Ringreader's rest, so the shrine keeps its place; with nothing to mend he only talks. (Deviation: the source game's hospital charged gold for treatment, 500 in Kettleforge. A paid heal next to a free Ringreader rest would never be bought, so the remake makes it free and keeps the building and its role.)

**The hospital, by the buttons (pass 457):** Right walks the hero over a Tower foe's poison flask and on to Halvar, the poison biting on the way. One real Up mends health, magic and poison to the mending sound and opens his words, which hold the world, so nothing ticks while he talks. Jump turns his pages, the Jump that closes the last one does not leap, no tick follows the hero out, and he pays nothing (`test_the_hospital_cures_poison_through_the_buttons`).

**Polesend's tavern (pass 265):** the source game's Polesend kept a bar, where the bar maid gave useful word and a man at the back told stranger things. The remake's Polesend had a Ringreader, a scribe, a grocer, a reedcutter and a ferryman, but no bar. Now the house by its Ringreader is **the Black Water**, under a tavern sign ("Ale, eel stew, and whatever the barges heard"). **Barmaid Brigid** stands at its door and **Old Cormac**, forty years an ore-barge poler, beside her; both are talkers drawn by `castgen.py`. What they say is true of the world, and the test holds it to the rooms:
- Brigid puts the Acorn Charm (5.2) in the far corner of the Matriarch's mire past the east door, and the Haar Wyrm round the Haar Spring past the Dry Cistern.
- Cormac names the messenger's boots at Feather Hollow by Lampwick, and warns of the Sunken Chapel.

Once the Tree blooms they have other words. (Deviation: no ale is sold, and the source game's Polesend hospital, 1,000 g, stays Kettleforge's alone, for the reason given above.) `test_polesend_keeps_the_originals_tavern`. Screenshot: `/mnt/project-files/pass-265/tavern.png`.

**The final tier (pass 93):** a shop entry can name a GameState flag (`requires`), and it stays off the shelf until that flag is set (`Shop.shelf`). With the Sky Spring restored (`spring:sky`), trade returns on the sky road: Hearthroot Arms stocks the Dwarvish Broad Sword (2,800 g, 5.2; the Giant Blade until pass 186) and Carillon's grocer a Life Fruit (2,000 g, once), the top of the Life Fruit range. (Deviation: the bible paired the Broad Sword with no shop or moment. It arrives here because the sky road is what the Sky Spring restores, and because by then the player can afford it.)

**Gold sinks:** respecs, Barkbalms and Amber Draughts, keys for optional doors (each guards a reward), smith upgrades, cosmetics (cloak dyes, 6 total, purely visual). **As built (pass 136):** Ilse the Dyer keeps Ilse's Dyeworks in Hearthroot's Market Row. She sells six cloak dyes: Ember Red 300 g, Tide Blue 500, Dusk Violet 800, Ash Grey 1,200, Saffron 1,800 and Night 2,500. A new dye goes straight on. One you own, or the Warden Green you start in, is worn again for nothing ("Wear"; the one on shows "Worn"), here or, since pass 189, from the Equipment page anywhere (5.2). The save keeps `dyes` and `dye`. The player sheet's cloak is its only green, so `shaders/cloak_dye.gdshader` recolors every green texel to the dye and keeps its shading (`PlayerVisual.dye_look`). The bible's "Ylden's Own" glow is the seventh dye: see 11. **Ring dyes (pass 164):** the Inner Rings' cosmetics. Each trial has a ring time (`Trials.LIST` `grain`): Walls 12 s, Edges 14, Thorns 15, Winds 14 and Stillness 32. Ring its bell within that time and the trial dyes your cloak a color of its own, with a toast: Grain Slate (Walls), Ledge Coral (Edges), Thorn Rose (Thorns), Wind Jade (Winds), Still Frost (Stillness) and Plummet Umber (Falling, pass 168, a8703a). `GameState.earn_dye` owns it, puts it on, and sets the flag `dye:<id>`, which puts it on Ilse's shelf (price 0, `requires`) to wear again. The Ringreader's list names each open trial's ring time and its dye, or says the dye is yours. The ring times are set from each course's length and its waits: the Thorns pogo hand clears in about 12 s, and Stillness needs about 29 s with both waits at the niche. They ask for a clean run, not a perfect one.

**A shop by the buttons (pass 413):** every shop test called `Shop.buy` or the menu's choose function itself, so shopping the way a player does had never been played. `test_a_shop_answers_the_real_controller` visits the Hearthroot arms dealer with the real controller. Up at him opens his shop and pauses the game. Jump on the Wayfarer's Sword buys it: 400 gold is paid, the sword is in hand, the coin clinks, and the shop stays open. Down moves to armor he can't be paid for, and Jump there buys nothing and plays `menu_refuse`. Dash leaves, and the game runs on.

## 11. Secrets

Target: **at least 3 secrets per zone room-cluster**, about 70 total.

**Secrets per zone, as built (pass 379).** The target above was the plan's, and nothing counted it. Counted the way the completion card counts (below): a hidden room is a room with a `secret:` cover, and a treasure is any item or chest the map's catalog lists for a room (`World.catalog`, the `treasures` Treasure Sense marks). Some of those treasures sit in plain sight; the rest lie behind cracked stone, fake walls, card doors and hard climbs. The world holds **12 hidden rooms and 128 treasures, 140 in all**: twice the plan's "about 70". Every zone but the Evercrown holds at least three. The Evercrown holds two by design, its hidden pocket in the Threshold with the Elven Helm in it: it is the last climb, and it keeps no side treasure in its rooms (pass 135). The Sunken Spring hides no room, but its four rooms hold five finds. The Inner Rings are off the map and hold one Grain Shard each, rung from the bell (3.2). `test_every_zone_holds_the_secrets_the_bible_counts` holds every row to the rooms.

**A hidden room, found with the buttons (pass 465).** Walking into a `secret:` cover fades it to a quarter, chimes (`secret`) and keeps the find (`secret:<room>`). Walking out brings the wall back. The chime marks a find, but it rang every time the room was entered, as if the room were found again. Now it rings once in a journey, and a hidden room already found fades in silence. `test_a_hidden_room_is_found_with_the_buttons` walks through a cover with the real Right, out with Left, in again, and in once more after leaving the room.
