| Zone | Rooms | Hidden rooms | Treasures | In all |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Hearthroot | 7 | 2 | 4 | 6 |
| Heartwood | 28 | 2 | 25 | 27 |
| Hollin Tower | 26 | 2 | 31 | 33 |
| The Haar | 14 | 1 | 21 | 22 |
| High Boughs | 10 | 1 | 16 | 17 |
| Storm Spires | 9 | 1 | 10 | 11 |
| Vorrhallow | 14 | 2 | 15 | 17 |
| Sunken Spring | 4 | 0 | 5 | 5 |
| The Evercrown | 8 | 1 | 1 | 2 |

**Hidden rooms in every zone (pass 131):** the Haar, the High Boughs and Carillon, 29 rooms between them, had hidden no room at all; their secrets were chests in plain sight. Each now hides one behind a fake wall at floor level, where a curious player walks into the stone: in the Veiled Rise, the hollow corner block west of the Drowned Dwarf (500 g); in the Sunlit Bough, the hollow foot of the west buttress (600 g); and in the Broken Shrine, a cell in the cliff east of the shrine (700 g). The Seer's Lens shows each seam on the way back. `test_every_large_zone_hides_a_room` holds every zone of five rooms or more to at least one hidden room.

**Cracked-stone caches (pass 135):** after the Tower, only one room in Vorrhallow asked for the Delver's Pick again, so it stopped mattering once its own zone was done, and Hearthroot never asked for it at all. Four caches now sit behind cracked stone, each with its prize in plain sight through the gap: in the West Gate, beside where a new game starts, a Life Fruit in a niche in the town wall (the first thing the game shows you that you can't have yet); in the Scorched Hall (the Haar), a Mana Dew beside the shelf's Life Fruit; in the North Bough (the High Boughs), a Mana Dew in a niche on the west wall; and in the Spire Foot (Carillon), a Grain Shard hollowed into the west block. That brings Mana Dew to the table's 9, Life Fruit to 13 and cracked stone to 13 rooms. `test_every_large_zone_hides_something_behind_cracked_stone` keeps every zone of five rooms or more (but the Evercrown, which holds no side treasure) asking for the Delver's Pick, and the reachability checker keeps the West Gate's fruit shut until the Delver's Pick is found.

**Cracked stone by the buttons (pass 427):** every cracked-stone test broke it with a scripted intent that lasts one step. `test_cracked_stone_answers_the_real_controller` uses the real buttons. Attack held with no Delver's Pick leaves a cracked wall whole and silent, and gaining the Delver's Pick with Attack still held breaks nothing, because a held button is not pressed again. A fresh tap breaks it, and the break is heard. On a cracked floor, Down held alone lands on it and leaves it whole; Down held with a tap of Attack on the way down breaks it, and you fall through.

| Type | Count | How it's hidden | Reward |
|------|-------|-----------------|--------|
| Breakable walls (Delver's Pick) | 14 (18 planned) | Cracked stone, sometimes faint | Gold, Life Fruit |
| Illusory walls (Seer's Lens) | 14 rooms (10 planned), 12 of them hiding a room | Seam visible only with Lens | Grain Shards, lore |
| Key doors | 5 (12 planned): one of each card | Visible, need a card key | Gold vaults (4.5), never the way on |
| Life Fruit | 18 (13 in the rooms) | Mix, plus grocers and a mini-boss | +HP |
| Mana Dew | 16 (9 in the rooms) | Mix, plus grocers and three guardians | +MP |
| Potion Shards | 9 | Mix | +flask. Held with the rooms' Life Fruit (13), Mana Dew (9), Grain Shards (15) and Stillglass Shards (5) by `test_the_rooms_hold_the_finds_the_bible_counts` since pass 243 |
| Grain Shards | 15 | Mostly hard platforming | Skill points |
| Stillglass Shards | 5 (pass 140; was 10) | Hard rooms, hidden pockets | The smith's second temper (5.2). Each blade takes one, so five (one per blade and one spare) replace the planned ten, and none drop from foes: a shard is a find, not a grind |
| Inner Rings | 6 | Unlock as springs are restored | Cosmetics, a Grain Shard each |
| Lore tablets | 142 (12 planned) | Every sign is a carved tablet, in every zone | Story, map hints |

**The table, as built (pass 349).** Like the zone table (3.2) until pass 323, four Count cells were still the plan's, and nothing read them. The rooms hold 14 cracked-stone walls, not 18; the Seer's Lens's seam shows in 14 rooms, not 10 (12 of them hide a room, the Journey's hidden rooms); there are 5 card doors, one Copper, Silver, Gold, Thorn and Glass each, not 12, and they guard gold vaults, never equipment, spells or the way on (4.5); and every sign is a lore tablet, 142 of them, where the plan had 12 in the dwarf ruins. `test_the_secrets_table_counts_the_rooms` counts each from `levels/` and holds the table to it.

**The signs remember (pass 251):** a sign was carved once and never changed, so the world told you of things long after they had happened. The Hearthroot fountain stayed "dry for a season" with all three springs flowing. Amber Falls said "when the spring runs again" after it had. Five bosses still "waited" or "drilled" after they were beaten, and the Spire Foot's gate stayed "cold" once it stood open. Now several sign lines may stand on one cell, and they make one tablet. A line may open with world flags in brackets, the same as a person's fourth field (`[spring:heartwood]`, `[boss:thane_vorrund]`, `[!x]`). When the tablet is read, it shows the first line whose flags all hold, and a plain line holds always, so the plain line goes last as the old text. The tablet picks its line when you read it, so a spring cleared in its own room changes the sign there at once. 16 rooms have a line for afterwards: the plaza fountain (part full, then full), Amber Falls, the Heartwood Spring's spout, the Root Chamber's gate, the Scorched Hall, the Lampwick Causeway, the Haar Spring and the Sky Spring (each for the boss beaten, then the spring cleared), the Hag Mire, the Kettleforge Lift's gate, the Spire Foot's gate, the Spire Stair, Alpha's Den, the Thane's Kennels, the Source Hall and the Windloft. Tests: `test_a_tablet_reads_what_the_world_has_become`, and `test_every_world_change_rewrites_its_sign`, which checks that every bracketed flag names a real boss, spring or room of conduits, that every cell ends on a plain line, and that each of these signs has its line.

**Easter eggs:** a hidden room in Hearthroot rendered in authentic 8-bit NES palette and resolution, with the source game's music style; the Ringreader carves your mark: resting with him shows a 16-letter mark cut into the wood, made from your progress (`GameState.grain()`, "Rested. Your mark in the wood: ..."); it is never typed back in. Until pass 401 only the ring itself was tested; nothing held that the Ringreader says it. A rest that saved quietly, as a shrine's does, would have lost the mark. Now `test_the_ringreader_gives_a_grain_for_the_journey` rests with a Ringreader and hears "Rested. Your ring is: " and the saved journey's ring. It also rests at a shrine, which saves without one, so the ring stays the Ringreader's. **By the buttons (pass 409):** the King's 1,500 gold and the Ringreader's rest were held only by scripted intents and direct calls. `test_the_king_and_the_ringreader_answer_the_real_controller` meets both with the real controller. Up at the King takes his purse once. Up at the Ringreader opens his menu, and Jump on "Rest and save" closes it, rests, saves with the chime and recites the ring. A secret ending if you reach the Everbloom with no deaths and the Elven Plate.

**Old Hearthroot (pass 152):** the hidden room is built. In the Hall of Rings, the stone at the east end is a fake wall over a small chamber with a door (`secret:` cover, so it counts as a hidden room). Up at the door goes to Old Hearthroot (`hearthroot/old_hearthroot`, a room of its own on the map above the temple): the town as a cartridge drew it, with a fountain, a stair of square blocks, two tablets of new text and a chest of 1987 gold, the source game's year. A room header `retro: true` turns on `RetroFilter`, a layer under the HUD that cuts the world into blocks of 2 world pixels (240 x 180 in a 4:3 frame with black bars, close to the NES's 256 x 240) and matches each block to the nearest of the NES palette's 55 colours (`shaders/retro.gdshader`; since pass 155 each block is exactly 2 x 2 world pixels: the world view draws at `GameCamera.DETAIL` with a pixel of margin, and the filter takes its scale from that, where it had divided the view's height by 360 and drawn blocks of 4.02 render pixels that drifted across the world's pixels). The HUD stays sharp, so the game's own controls still read. The music is `old_hearthroot`, a new tune in F major at 132 on the 2A03's four voices only: a 12.5% pulse lead, a 25% pulse playing the chords as fast arpeggios, the stepped 4-bit triangle walking the bass, and the noise channel for drums, all dry. It is not the source game's town theme (16: no remixes of NES tracks). There is no ambience in the room.

**Completion screen:** shows % with category ticks; 100% unlocks a boss rush and an "Ylden's Own" title glow on the cloak. As built (pass 106), it is the ending's thanks card: "Thank you for playing", then one percent "of the world" in large type, and four rows counted up over 1.2 s as the card fades in. The rows are rooms explored, hidden rooms (rooms with a `secret:` cover whose flag `secret:<room>` is set), treasures (the catalog's item and chest ids collected; since pass 187 this includes the finds a `prop:` line lays, the Elven Helm and the Glass Key, which had been left out of the percent, the Journey and Treasure Sense although they lie in rooms like any other find. `Room.FOUND_PROPS` lists them, and the catalog gives each the id the room gives its pickup) and bosses beaten. The percent is their average, rounded down, so 100% means every row is whole. A whole row turns gold. Below come rank, time and deaths, plus the clear count from the second clear on (`Completion.tally`, `EndingScreen.completion_rows`). The boss rush is built (pass 156, `BossRush`). The Ringreader of the Hall of Rings offers it under the Inner Rings. It stays sealed until the `lord` flag is set, and the row then shows the best time. It runs all 13 guardians in the order of the critical path, with the Drowned Warden before the Evercrown. Each fight is in the boss's own arena, entered by door 1, the way you first came in, and starts the usual way as you walk in. You begin whole, with full magic and potions. Between fights a toast counts you on, and the ring mends a third of your health and all your magic. The clock runs from the first arena to the last blow, and the HUD shows the count, the clock and the best time. Nothing is won again: no rings, keys, flags or gold, no doors open, and the Everbloom's fall plays no ending. **Pass 260:** that held only for what the arena hands out. A guardian's real death still sprayed its gold, rolled a drop and gave its experience every rush (Gnarlmaw paying on his own, the rest through `Enemy.die`), and so did any foe it called in (Mothlings, daughters, hounds): a rush won some 3,000 experience and a purse each time. The rush test ended each fight by the arena's signal, so it never saw a real death. Now nothing that dies in a rush leaves gold, a drop or experience. `test_a_boss_rush_kill_wins_no_gold_or_experience` kills the first guardian and a foe beside it for real, and requires every script that pays on its own to ask the rush first. A blow that would kill you, with no Amber Draught to save you, ends the rush ("The Rush Ends") and carries you back to the Ringreader whole. Winning it keeps the best time in `data.trials["boss_rush"]` and carries you back too. "Leave the rush" in the pause menu stops it. Sound (pass 157): the arenas wait under the trials' theme (`trials`, the inner rings carrying you on) in place of their zone's music, and each boss brings its own track when it rises. When a boss falls the theme returns, and the bronze bell of the trials rings soft with a warm swell rising a fourth as the ring mends you (`rush_mend`). The win rings the bell three times up the major chord, the last left ringing (`rush_won`), with the record sting on a new best. A fall tolls the bell low and muffled over a sinking drone (`rush_fall`), and the music stops. Edges (pass 158): no one stands in an arena during a rush (Thane Vorrund's steward waits outside it), and a shrine on the way (the Crown Gate's) allows no rest, so it neither refills nor saves. Once a rush is won or lost the clock stops, and a second fall does nothing. A move that finds the world mid-fade waits for it. The moves between arenas pause with the pause menu, and they do nothing once the world is gone (quit mid-rush). A new or loaded game always starts with no trial or rush under way. **Entered and left by the buttons (pass 460):** every rush test began it with `BossRush.start` and ended it with a call. `test_the_boss_rush_is_entered_and_left_with_the_buttons` plays the whole game in the temple with every guardian down, using only real input. Up at the Ringreader, Down to Boss Rush and Jump begin the rush whole, to the trials' sound, and the ring carries the hero into the Root Chamber. Right walks him in, and the doors seal under the guardian's music. Pause, Down to "Leave the rush" and Jump bring him back beside the Ringreader. The clock is stopped, the game runs, no arena is still fighting, the guardian's music is gone and no flag has changed. The cloak's glow is (pass 136): a clear at 100% sets the `lord` flag (`Completion.award`), and the Crown Gold appears on Ilse's shelf, free. A pale gold cloak with a band of light running down the cloth.

**The Journey page (pass 172):** the percent was only ever shown on the ending's card, yet 100% gates the Crown Gold and the boss rush, and the mini-bosses (section 8) now count among the guardians. So the pause menu has a **Journey** row, between Controls and Quit. It opens a read-only page with the same `Completion.tally` the ending uses:

**Life Fruits and Mana Dew on the Journey page (pass 180):** the planned 14 fruits and 9 dews counted only the finds in the rooms; the grocers' shelves (one of each per town shop, 10.2) and the mini-bosses' rewards had raised the real totals to 18 and 16 without the stat table above noticing, so the true ceiling is 14 masks and 220 MP (240 with Clear Mind). `Completion.stat_items` counts every source (the rooms' `L` and `W` cells, the shops' stock, the guardians' rewards) and reads a save's share from its health and magic, which nothing else raises. The Journey page shows Life Fruits and Mana Dew under the guardians, so a player hunting the last one knows how many remain. They stay outside the percent, which already counts the ones in the rooms as treasures. Test: `test_the_journey_counts_every_life_fruit_and_mana_dew` pins the totals to this table.

- the percent "of the world";
- rooms explored, hidden rooms, treasures and guardians beaten, each as found / total;
- under "Guardians found", every guardian whose arena you have entered, in the order of the climb (`Completion.GUARDIANS`), marked Beaten or Waits;
- a last line counting those not yet found.

An arena you have not entered is never named, so the page spoils nothing. The world catalog records each room's `boss:` header so the page can tell which rooms are arenas.

---

## 12. Art Style Guide (HD Pixel Art)

### 12.1 Resolution and scale

- **World resolution:** 640x360 world pixels. All gameplay numbers (movement, hitboxes, rooms, tiles) are in world pixels.
- **Render density 2x (changed 2026-10-23):** the world renders at 1280x720, two render pixels per world pixel
  (`GameCamera.DETAIL`). Sean's reference for the hero is high-detail pixel art about 200 px tall; at one pixel per
  world pixel a 48 px hero loses the face and armor detail. Character sheets marked `"density": 2` are drawn at double
  detail in the same world size. Older 1x art still draws correctly (each pixel becomes 2x2) and moves to 2x over the
  polish passes: hero first, then enemies and bosses, then NPCs, tiles and decor. Done so far (2026-10-23): the
  hero, the Blinkhop, Hornet and Rot Dwarf (re-imported free from their clips), Gnarlmaw, the Iron Dwarf, the Foreman,
  Durgan, Gloamweaver and the zone tiles (the small Gear Bat stays code-drawn at 2x).
  The art loop (2026-11-05) carries on foe by foe to `docs/art/enemy-style-guide.md`, starting with the Shade Knight
  (PixelLab, the guide's reference foe). Batch 1 (art pass 2) remade the Sap Spitter, Grub Lump, Gear Bat (its
  gear still drawn in code), Minecart Rider and Silt Adder in PixelLab.
- **Smooth camera with crisp pixels:** render the world into a SubViewport of 642x362 world pixels (1 px margin) at
  render density, upscale with a sharp-bilinear shader (`shaders/sharp_pixels.gdshader`: nearest at integer scales,
  a one-pixel edge blend at 1.5x for 1080p), then offset the image by the camera's remainder below one render pixel.
  UI renders at native resolution on top, so text can be sharp.
- **Tile size:** 16x16 world px, drawn from 32x32 atlas tiles at 2x density (`TilesetFactory.DENSITY`; tile layers are scaled by one half, so cell coordinates are unchanged). Large props are free-form.
- **Decor and backgrounds** are painted at 2x too: decor cells are 16x32 world px from 32x64 atlas cells (`"density": 2` in the zone's decor JSON), and the 640x480 parallax layers are 1280x960 images scaled to world size.
- **Character size:** in world pixels, player sprite about 48x56 px on a 64x64 canvas, counting the blade and cloak (the
  body itself is about 20x46, matching the 12x40 hitbox). At 2x density the hero sheet is 128x128 per cell, about 96 px
  tall. Standard enemies 32 to 64 px. Bosses 160 to 400 px tall (multi-part, rigged).

### 12.2 Palette

- One **master palette of 64 colors** (stored in `art/palette/master.gpl` and as a Godot resource). Every asset is quantized to it,
  except the HD (2x density) characters made in Autosprite: each of those sheets keeps its **own palette of 56
  colors** cut from its reference clip (plus the master outline and the neutral stone ramp). Quantizing them to the
  master ramps turned the hooded hero's leather red and flattened his armor detail, which is what Sean asked for
  (2026-10-23). They stay limited-palette pixel art (a test caps each sheet at 96 colors).
- Each zone uses a **sub-palette of 24 to 32** of those colors, plus 4 accent colors.
- No pure black (#000) or pure white (#fff). Darkest is a deep blue-violet; lightest is a warm cream.
- **Hero look (changed 2026-10-23):** a tall, slender hooded elf ranger after Sean's own reference
  (`/mnt/project-files/autosprite/sean-halfling-reference.png`): charcoal hood over brown hair, pointed ears, layered
  brown leather armor with brass buckles, a dark green cloak with gold trim that trails and shows movement, leather
  boots. Sean rejected the earlier green tunic and blond hair because it read as Link from Zelda; avoid that combination.
  The brown leather sits close to the Heartwood's brown bark, so the hero gets a thin cool rim light on his top and back
  edges (manifest `"rim"` in `art/autosprite/manifest.json`, applied at import) to stand off it.
- **Zone moods:** Hearthroot warm amber and stone grey; Heartwood brown, amber, fungus cyan; Hollin Tower iron, rust, lamp orange; Haar grey-green, bone, lantern yellow; High Boughs leaf green, sky blue, cloud white; Carillon storm violet, gold; Vorrhallow flooded teal, crystal magenta; the Evercrown black-violet, alien cyan, blood red.

### 12.3 Rendering rules

- **Outlines:** 1 px selective outlines (darker shade of the local color, not black). Characters get a full outline for readability; backgrounds get none.
- **Light source:** top-left key light by default; zones may override with in-scene lights.
- **Shading:** 3 to 5 tones per material; hue-shift shadows toward blue/violet and highlights toward yellow.
- **Dithering:** only in backgrounds and skies; never on characters.
- **Anti-aliasing:** manual, sparing, on large curves only.
- **Readability layers:** foreground silhouette (darkest, blurred-free), gameplay layer (highest contrast), midground (desaturated 30%), far background (desaturated 60%, lighter). Gameplay layer must pass a grayscale contrast check against the background.
- **Lighting:** Godot 2D lights with normal maps on tiles and characters (normal maps generated by code from height maps). Glow on emissive pixels (fungus, lanterns, crystals) via the WorldEnvironment glow pass, kept subtle.

### 12.4 Animation

- Base rate 12 fps for most animation; key actions (attack, dash) use held frames and smear frames for snap.
- Every attack has anticipation (1 to 3 frames), action (1 to 2 frames with a smear), and recovery (2 to 4 frames).
  Exception: the player's own swings skip anticipation and open on the strike frame, because the hitbox is live on
  the press (section 5) and a wind-up would read as input lag. The recovery frames carry the weight instead. Enemies
  always telegraph (section 7).
- The scarf and hair are secondary motion driven by code (verlet chain), not hand-animated, so they react to real movement.

### 12.5 Parallax

4 to 6 layers per zone: sky or deep background, far silhouette, mid background, gameplay tiles, foreground decor, foreground silhouettes. Plus particle layers (spores, mist, rain, leaves).

---

## 13. Asset Production Plan

### 13.1 Tools and when to use each

| Method | Best for | Why |
|--------|----------|-----|
| **Autosprite** (API or MCP, about 500 credits) | Character animations: player, enemies, bosses, NPCs | Generating consistent multi-frame character animation is the hardest, most expensive thing to do by code. Spend credits only here. |
| **PixelLab** (API via `tools/art/pixellab.py`, or its MCP server in `.mcp.json`; token as a cloud network secret for api.pixellab.ai) | The hero and his gear (13.5, Sean 2026-10-30), and a second source for other generated art: characters with 4 or 8 directions, text and skeleton animation, sidescroller tilesets, map objects, UI, inpaint and pixel clean-up | Connected 2026-10-28 so art is not blocked on Autosprite credits. Sean asked for the hero and gear in PixelLab with quality first (5,000 generations a month). Log every call in `art/pixellab-ledger.csv`. |
| **Code-generated pixel art** (Python + Pillow/NumPy scripts in `tools/art/`) | Tilesets, autotile variants, props, UI, icons, fonts layout, VFX sprites, normal maps, palettes, recolors | Deterministic, free, reproducible, easy to iterate in the loop. |
| **Blender** (MCP) | Large background paintings, boss parts that rotate, sky gradients, 3D-to-pixel renders of complex shapes (gears, Tree trunk, stillglass) | Render low-res with flat shading, then quantize to the palette and clean up by code. |
| **Other art-app MCPs** (Aseprite, Krita, Photoshop, as available) | Cleanup passes, palette remaps, sprite sheet packing, hand-fixing artifacts in Autosprite output | Precision edits. |
| **Godot shaders and particles** | Hit flashes, dissolves, water, fog, wind sway, glow, screen effects | Runtime effects cost no art budget. |
| **Sean's free music library** | Music | Sean supplies; Claude curates, loops and masters. |
| **Code-synthesized SFX** (sfxr-style generator plus layering) and free CC0 SFX packs | Sound effects | Fast, free, tunable. |

### 13.2 Autosprite credit budget

**Real costs** (2026-10-23): a side-view animation is **5 credits** on the turbo tier (a 2 s clip) or **10 credits**
on the pro tier (a 4 s clip); a weapon attack move is 5 (one engine only), a pose 3, a portrait 1, a new character 1
(turbo) or 3 (pro or pixel art), and **uploading our own image as a character is free**. Re-cutting a finished clip at
another size or frame count (`regenerate_single_spritesheet`) is **free**, so every clip is re-cut at its full rate
(about 24 frames per second, 256 px) and the import picks the frames.

**Tier: chosen by result, never by price.** Sean asked for HD quality over the cheapest option (2026-10-23). For
humanoids, **pro**: on the same run clip pro stayed in clean side profile with more readable limbs, while turbo drifted
to a three-quarter view. For simple non-humanoid creatures, **turbo in reference mode**: the pro Blinkhop clip invented a
second creature and morphed into a legged, front-facing design, while turbo reference kept the exact base on every
frame (it is also the mode Autosprite calls the most consistent).

**Style lock:** each character starts from its code-drawn sprite (tools/art/spritegen.py, castgen.py) blown up 10x and
uploaded free. That soft blow-up animated poorly (the test idle turned three-quarter and barely moved), so the base is
first redrawn once as an **HD side-view pose** (`generate_pose`, 3 credits, prompt names the outfit and "strict side
profile facing right"), and that pose is uploaded free as the character the clips are made from. The player's base
is Autosprite character `cmvkgu36o002f1g8yyrxrwqbp`, the hooded ranger (12.2); the first player set, on the
Link-like design Sean rejected, is retired. When `generate_pose` fails on a base (it failed, refunded, twice
each for the Hornet and the Rot Dwarf), a pro `create_character` from a written description (3 credits) is the fallback. `tools/art/autosprite_import.py` brings every clip back to the
palette, scale and outline of 12.3 (`--loop` finds a clean cycle in a full-rate sheet); `tools/art/autosprite_fetch.py`
stores the source sheets compactly in `art/autosprite/`. One clip feeds several game animations (the jump clip gives
rise, apex and fall; the sword combo gives slash 2, slash 3 and the down strike).

**Swords:** attack-move clips draw the sword. Where the blade is visible in a frame it is kept (on the stone and sky
ramps) and the frame is marked `art_blade`, so the game does not draw a second one; where the clip blurs it out
the game draws its own blade from the code rig's angle. The hooded hero's clips show the sword in every picked swing
frame, so all five swings use the art blade; at 2x density a code-drawn blade would also look coarse beside the art.
Showing the equipped weapon (longer blades, recolors) is a later job.

**Heavy attacks from poses:** a custom clip of a big weapon can drift (the Iron Dwarf's slam clip grew a second
hammer head). Short attacks whose game animations are only a few key frames are made from `generate_pose` stills
(3 credits each) instead: `tools/art/autosprite_poses.py` keys out the white ground and any floor patch, keeps the
figure, and packs the stills on one spot (hips on the cell's middle) into a source sheet. A figure drawn too dark for
the game's rooms gets a manifest `"gain"` before its palette is cut; the Iron Dwarf also gets a lamp-orange rim.
A second look that changes one spot (Durgan's phase 3: hatch open, crystal harness) is one more pose of the
standing still, and `tools/art/autosprite_transplant.py` carries that change onto every other pose (turned and moved
to where the spot sits). A spinning drill is the still with its thread slid along the axis
(`tools/art/autosprite_screw.py`). Neither costs credits. Stills that each drew the floor at a different height (a
spider that crouches, rears or lies on her back) are packed with `rest=1`, so the lowest pixel of each rests on the
floor. Gloamweaver's sheet is made of poses alone (castgen writes empty placeholder frames for it); states without
their own still borrow the nearest one, and her lanterns, souls and web stay code-drawn on top.

Balance after the hooded hero's set, Gnarlmaw's parts, the Iron Dwarf, the Foreman, Durgan and Gloamweaver: 22 credits, about 7 poses. The redesign (concepts, the new base and its
leg fix, a trial clip and the full set) cost 145 and came out of the NPC and reserve lines.

| Use | Clips | Credits | Share |
|-----|-------|---------|-------|
| Tests (turbo idle, turbo vs pro run), spent | 3 | 20 | 4% |
| Player, first design (retired), spent | 13 | 113 | 23% |
| Player redesign, spent: concepts, base and leg fix, trial idle + run, full set of 10 pro clips and 2 sword moves | 14 | 145 | 30% |
| Enemies in the game now (Blinkhop, Hornet, Rot Dwarf): HD pose + move + attack each | 6 | 69 | 14% |
| NPCs: only if credits are topped up (they stay code-drawn) | 0 | 0 | 0% |
| Bosses, Gnarlmaw spent: three pro stills (head, plate, tail) | 3 | 9 | 2% |
| Durgan (boss) spent: base and six poses; open look and drill spin made by code | 0 | 21 | 4% |
| Gloamweaver (boss) spent: base and seven poses | 0 | 24 | 5% |
| Bosses still to come: stills or key clips for their rigged parts | 3 | 10 | 2% |
| Tower enemies, Iron Dwarf spent: base, walk clip, a rejected slam clip, five poses | 2 | 38 | 8% |
| Foreman (mini-boss) spent: base, walk clip, seven poses | 1 | 34 | 7% |
| Reserve for later enemies, fixes and regeneration | | 7 | 1% |
| **Total** | | **490** | |

If credits run short, cut NPC clips first (they can stay code-drawn), then enemies (more recolors, more code frames),
never the player. Every clip is judged against the code-drawn sheet it would replace; the better one ships (the
manifest in `art/autosprite/` says which animations are imported). Sean can buy more credits; the budget above is
what the first 500 cover.

**Rigged boss parts:** a part that turns or changes state rather than moving like a body is made from **one pro still**
(`create_character`, 3 credits) and its frames are built by code: `tools/art/autosprite_spin.py` turns a ring
around a still hub (Gnarlmaw's mandible wheel, 24 frames for a full turn), and `tools/art/autosprite_parts.py`
draws state frames over a still (cracked plates, the glowing tail). A clip is kept for parts that truly animate.

**Credit rules:** never regenerate a whole set to fix one frame (fix by hand or by code, or re-cut free); request at
the canonical size so no upscaling is needed; batch similar requests; log every call in `art/autosprite-ledger.csv`
with date, asset, cost and result.

### 13.3 Full asset list

**Legend:** AS = Autosprite, CODE = code-generated, BL = Blender then quantize, APP = art-app cleanup, SH = shader/particles, SEAN = Sean's library.

**Code rig and Autosprite:** every character also has a code-drawn version on a small 2D rig (`tools/art/spritegen.py`,
`castgen.py`), shaded and outlined to the rules in 12.3, in the same sheet format (PNG plus JSON with per-frame points).
Autosprite clips replace it animation by animation through `art/autosprite/manifest.json`. Since 2026-10-23 all 17 of
the player's animations are Autosprite (pro tier), including a new death collapse, and so are the three enemies in the
game (Blinkhop, Hornet, Rot Dwarf); the boss and NPCs are still code-drawn.

#### Player (AS, cleanup APP; scarf CODE)
idle (8 frames), run (10), run-start (3), turn (3), jump-rise (3), jump-apex (2), fall (3), land (3), double jump (5), wall slide (4), wall jump (4), dash (4 + afterimages SH), slash 1/2/3 (5 each), air slash (5), up-slash (5), down-strike/pogo (4), charged thrust (6), parry (4), cast spell (6), drink potion (8), hurt (3), death (10), meditate/save (6), item-get pose (4), door enter (4). Weapon swaps: blade drawn as a separate layer (CODE) so the 4 blades share animations.

#### NPCs (AS idle + talk; portraits CODE from the sprite, then APP)
Reeve Aldous, Ringreader (re-skinned per town by CODE), Hearthroot townsfolk (4), dwarf smith, Key master, Cartographer, Shopkeeper (2 variants), Kettleforge dwarf (3 variants by recolor), Princess/King's daughter (story).

#### Enemies (PixelLab or AS; variants CODE recolor)
About 30 families from section 7, each with idle/move, attack telegraph, attack, hurt, death. Mini-bosses: 4 (AS).
**The art loop (Sean, 2026-11-05):** foes and guardians are remade to one guide, `docs/art/enemy-style-guide.md`
(scale, palette, outline, the animation set with frame counts and rates, telegraph rules, the PixelLab recipe and a
review checklist), so foes made in parallel keep one look. `tools/art/lineup.tscn` draws every foe in every state for
review. The Shade Knight is the reference: a PixelLab base (`create-character-v3`, side view) and pixminimax clips
made from one guard pose, so every state shares the model.

#### Bosses
Each boss is built as a **rigged multi-part sprite** (separate head, limbs, segments) animated with Godot's Skeleton2D and AnimationPlayer. That keeps credit cost low and gives smooth, huge motion.
- Parts: AS for faces and key poses, BL for mechanical or rigid parts (Durgan's drill, Skyrend's cloud-wings via BL render, the Everbloom's core), CODE for glow, cracks, veins.
- 10 bosses: Gnarlmaw, Durgan, Gloamweaver, the Haar Wyrm, Snapcrown, Skyrend, Thane Vorrund, the Likeness (CODE: player sprite with a shadow shader, almost free), the Everbloom (3 forms), the Drowned Warden.

#### Tilesets (CODE, refined APP)
One per zone, 9 total (Hearthroot, Heartwood, Hollin Tower, Haar, Sylvarre, Carillon, Vorrhallow, the Evercrown, Sunken Spring). Each: 47-tile autotile set for ground, 47 for a second material, platforms (one-way), walls, slopes (22.5° and 45°), ladders, spikes/thorns, background wall tiles, and gate tiles (cracked stone, mist curtain, fire seal, conduit, illusory). Generated from a material spec (palette ramp + noise + edge rules) so new zones are cheap.

#### Backgrounds (BL + CODE)
4 to 6 parallax layers per zone (about 45 layers). Blender scenes of the Tree, branches, stillglass; rendered at low res, quantized, edges cleaned. Skies and fog gradients by CODE with dithering.

#### Props (CODE, some BL)
Doors (5 key doors + boss doors), chests (3 sizes), Ringreader shrine, meditation shrine, fountain (4 water states), lanterns, gears, minecarts, signs, banners, crystals, bounce pods, conduits, elevators, breakable pots, item pedestals, about 120 props total.

#### VFX (CODE sprite sheets + SH)
Hit sparks (3 sizes), slash smears (per blade), dust puffs (5 types), landing ring, wall-slide dust, double-jump feathers, dash afterimages, pogo star, coin sparkle, spell projectiles and impacts (Torrent, Stormcall, Kindle, Wisplight, Wither), heal glow, level-up burst, enemy dissolve, boss shatter, water splash, ambient particles (spores, mist, leaves, rain, embers, crystal motes).

#### UI (CODE)
HP masks, MP bar, flask icons, boss bar with phase ticks, gold counter, minimap, full map tiles and icons, pause menu, inventory grid, skill tree screen (node icons: 36), shop screen, dialogue box with portrait frame, title screen (BL key art + CODE logo), settings menus, controller glyphs (Xbox, PlayStation, Switch, keyboard), damage numbers (optional toggle). **The minimap (pass 373):** the list promised one and none was built, so a player had to open the full map to see where a door led. A window 96 by 54 px sits in the HUD's top right corner (`Minimap.SIZE`, 10 px from the corner) and shows the map around you at half the full map's scale (one screen of a room is 20 by 11): your room in the middle, pulsing in its zone's color, the rooms you have walked, the rooms a chart gives you dimmed, shrines and still-shut key doors as on the full map, and a blinking dot where you stand. It scrolls as you go from room to room. It hides while the full map, a menu or a dialog is open, and in the Inner Rings and the Boss Rush, whose clocks hold the top of the screen. `test_the_minimap_shows_the_map_around_you`. **Kept, not swept (pass 375):** the HUD draws it every frame, and it swept the whole catalog each time, about 0.17 ms a frame once every room is walked and charted, for a picture that changes only when you change rooms, walk a new one or buy a chart. The rooms in view are now worked out then and kept on the world (`Minimap.CACHE`); only the dot is worked out each frame, about 3 µs. `test_the_minimap_stays_cheap_on_a_full_map` holds the kept view to a fresh one, checks each of the three changes looks again, and keeps a full map's frame under 0.1 ms. Three other items on this list are built differently: the inventory is a list, not a grid (the Inventory page, pass 205); the dialogue box has no portrait frame, since the people are drawn in the world beside it; and the Controls page names pad buttons by the Xbox layout only (pass 365). Font: a custom 5x7 bitmap face made by CODE (`tools/art/fontgen.py`, every glyph drawn in the script; `PixelFont` builds it at runtime). It is used for everything, the title logo included, and it only scales by whole numbers (1x for body text, 2x for banners, 4x for the logo), so text stays on the pixel grid. Outlines are stamped one texel around, since bitmap fonts have no outline. (Changed in pass 40 from "a free licensed font such as m5x7": a font drawn in code needs no license check and matches the NES lettering more closely.)

#### Item icons (CODE, APP touch-up)
All weapons, armor, shields, rings, keys, spells, consumables, shards, abilities: about 60 icons at 24x24.

#### Audio
- **Music (SEAN, curated by Claude):** title, Hearthroot (and festival variant), Ringreader/shrine, each zone (7 + 2 optional), mini-boss, boss (a shared boss theme, plus a signature theme for each zone boss that has a strong idea: placeholder themes exist for Durgan's drill engine, Gloamweaver's hunting waltz and the Haar Wyrm's surge, three head-voices trading a motif across the stereo field; `BossArena.BOSSES` names each fight's track), Everbloom (3 phases), ending, credits. About 22 tracks. Each zone track gets a "restored" variant (filter/arrangement change) when its spring returns. As built (pass 150), the three springs' zones have one each (`Music.RESTORED`, keyed by the spring's flag): `heartwood_restored` (the plucks opened up bright, a shaker over the hand drum, the bell melody doubled by a warm lead an octave down), `haar_restored` (the waltz a touch quicker at 72 and no longer muffled, with a lantern-bell where the heartbeat thumped, and less reverb as the fog lifts by half) and `spires_restored` (the storm broken: 100 bpm, held chords and a walking bass, no thunder rolls, the gusting plucks falling soft like drops off the leaves). Carillon's storm bed becomes `amb_spires_restored`, a soft wind, a fine drizzle and drips, with no thunder. Each variant draws the same random notes in the same order as its zone track, so the melody is the same tune. Restoring a spring turns its room's music over 3 s (`Spring.RESTORE_FADE`), and a town keeps its own theme.
- **SFX (CODE synth + CC0 packs):** about 180 effects across movement, combat, UI, ambience, bosses.
  - **As built (pass 334):** **166 sound effects** (the plan said about 180), and **34 music tracks** with **14 ambience beds** (the plan said about 22 tracks). The 34 tracks are: a theme for each of the 10 areas (Hearthroot, the seven zones after it, the Sunken Spring and the Inner Rings); 6 for towns with their own (Kettleforge, Lampwick, Polesend, Sylvarre, Carillon's town, Old Hearthroot); 5 variants (the three restored springs, Hearthroot's festival `hearthroot_bloom` and the Evercrown's `evercrown_still` after the end); the shared `boss` and `miniboss` themes and 9 guardians' own; and the title and the ending, which also plays under the credits. There is no Ringreader or shrine track: a Ringreader's room keeps its zone's theme. `test_the_bible_counts_the_games_sounds` holds the three counts to `assets/` and fails on a sound file the code never plays.
  - **Voices (pass 254):** `Sfx` plays through a pool of 12 voices. It handed them out in turn, so the thirteenth sound always took the oldest voice, however long that sound had left. Footsteps alone come several a second when you run, and a fight adds cuts, hits and tells, so a 2.4 s victory (`boss_won`) or a 1.4 s phase break was cut off within about a second of starting. Now each voice keeps the time its sound ends (its length over its pitch, in real time, since the mixer ignores pause and slow-mo). A new sound takes a free voice, or else the one nearest its end, so a long stinger stands through a busy moment and a footstep gives way. A burst of one sound in a single frame takes one voice: a spray of eight coins, which used to sum eight copies and take two-thirds of the pool, rings once. `Sfx.recent` still lists every ask. Test: `test_a_long_sound_keeps_its_voice_through_a_busy_moment`.
- **Phase breaks (pass 96):** when a boss's health crosses one of its `PHASES` marks, `BossArena` plays the `phase_break` stinger (a deep impact, a reversed swell and a ringing fifth), ducks the music under it and the screen shakes. Then the boss theme rises a semitone, and its tempo with it (`Music.intensify`, `pitch_scale` 2^(1/12) per phase, eased over 0.8 s), the old key-change lift. A new track starts back as written.
  - **In real time (pass 476):** the music's fades, ducks and phase lifts ran on game time, so at 70% Game speed (14) a 1.2 s fade took 1.7 s, a boss's slow motion after its death blow (30%) stretched the fade under it toward four seconds, and hitstop stalled a fade or the phase break's duck mid-way. They ignore the time scale now (`Tween.set_ignore_time_scale`): the music is heard in real time, as the sounds always were. `test_the_music_moves_in_real_time_at_every_game_speed` fades a track in and lifts it at 70% and at 30%, and fades one in through hitstop, each in its own time.
  - **A phase reached another way (pass 252):** the break watched health alone. Gloamweaver comes down for good once all four lanterns are broken (8.3), and the four cost her only 72 of 260 HP, so she entered her third phase above the first mark with no stinger, no stagger and no lift in the music. Her health then crossed the two marks later and rang twice for a change that had already happened. Now the arena also counts the boss's own `phase`: a boss in phase n has passed n - 1 marks, whichever came first. The last lantern is one break, straight to the second level, and the health marks after it ring nothing. Every other guardian moves its `phase` on the same marks, so nothing else changes. Test: `test_her_last_lantern_breaks_the_phase_as_her_health_would`.
- **Attack sounds (pass 102):** every enemy and boss attack is heard as well as seen. Entering a telegraph state plays a rising cue at once, picked by what is coming: `tell_blade` (a scrape climbing in pitch: a blade drawn back), `tell_heavy` (a low groan swelling up: a slam, dive or plunge), `tell_charge` (a wobbling hum rising to a whine: a shot or spell), or the zone foe's own call (a buzz, screech, wail, swell). The strike has its own sound (a `whoosh` for swoops and lunges, `slash`, `heavy`, `zap` and so on), and every death plays `enemy_die`. `test_every_attack_tell_and_strike_has_a_sound` scans the enemy and boss scripts and fails on any attack state that starts in silence.
  - **Tells the scan could not read (pass 278):** most guardians pick a move and enter it as `set_state(pick + "_tell")`, or through a helper (`_open`, `_tell`), and the scan reads only a named state. Fifteen tells of seven guardians started in silence and were heard only as the blow came: the Foreman's pound and throw, Durgan's drill and slam, Snapcrown's bite, whip and spit, the Drowned Warden's thrust and undertow, Thane Vorrund's chop, sweep and burst, the Likeness's cut, heavy, cast and dash, and every tell of the Everbloom's avatar. Each now plays its cue as it starts, by what is coming: `tell_blade` for a cut, thrust, whip or lunge, `tell_heavy` for a chop, pound, slam, bite or heaved rock, `tell_charge` for a spell, spit, drill spinning up or gathering pull. Sounds the guardian already made at the start (the Foreman's charge roar, the Warden's wave swell, the brood's rustle) stay. `test_every_guardian_tell_is_heard_as_it_starts` fights every guardian in its arena for 25 s, pushing it past its phase marks halfway, and fails on any tell entered without a sound in that frame or the two after (35 tells across 14 guardians).
  - **Every tell heard, fought or driven (pass 284):** five tells never came up in 25 s of fighting (the Foreman's throw, the Drowned Warden's undertow, Thane Vorrund's burst, the Likeness's cast and dash), so the test could not hear them. It now drives each one directly after the fight (`DRIVEN_TELLS` in `test_boss.gd`), setting the state that picks it and calling the guardian's own chooser, and fails if it enters in silence. It also reads every `_tell` named in each guardian's script: each must be heard in the fight, driven, or entered only through a literal state change with its sound on the same or the next three lines. A tell added later with none of these fails the test.
- **Foe voices (pass 112):** the shared tells are for the older foes and the bosses. A foe built later gets its own tell and strike, made in its own material, so it can be told apart by ear even off screen. The Bark Knight has `bark_creak` (a stick-slip creak of wood under strain, climbing), `lance_thrust` (a woody rush ending on a thock), `root_grip` (a low crunch with splinters) and `bark_chip` (a dry crack over a hollow knock, louder on the last plate). The Drowned Dwarf has `anchor_heave` (a sodden groan with bubbles rising through it and water pouring) and `anchor_slam` (an iron thud into mud and a wet slap). The Leaf Mantis has `mantis_unfold` (a leaf rustle, then a chitter) and `scythe` (two thin cuts a hair apart). The Seed Bomber has `seed_rattle` (hard seeds rattling in a husk, faster and higher) and `seed_drop` (a pop and a short falling whistle); its seed has `seed_fuse` (a sputtering hiss that climbs) and `seed_burst` (a husk cracking and thorns spraying). The Marsh Hag has `hag_hex`, `mire_grasp` and `hag_fumble` (pass 119). The Spire Sentinel has `sentinel_grind` (stone dragged on stone over a low rumble, as it moves behind your back), `sentinel_raise` (stone creaking up over an electric hum that climbs) and `sentinel_slam` (a deep thud, a crack and grit) (pass 123). `test_the_newer_foes_have_voices_of_their_own` checks that each foe plays its own sounds and that no other script does.
- **Mini-boss voices (pass 173):** the Clockwork Alpha and the Bark Knight Captain were built from shared sounds: the Alpha's tells used the hound's ratchet and its jam the generic break, and the Captain's vault the generic charge tell. Each now has a voice of its own for the moves that are only its own:
  - `key_wind`: the Alpha's great key turned through four heavy detents, a deep clank each, with a spring groan climbing behind. It plays on its intro, its pounce and howl tells, and its turn to phase 2.
  - `spring_jam`: a hard clang as it meets the wall, then its mainspring buzzing loose with gear teeth skittering.
  - `bough_vault`: the Captain's lance planted and flexed, a deep woody groan, then the bough throwing him up.
  - `lance_plant`: his lance-first landing, a deep wooden boom with roots tearing the floor.

  The Captain still speaks with his knights' creak, thrust, root and chip for the moves they share, so the family reads by ear (pass 171). The voices test now holds both mini-bosses to their own sounds.
- **The Bloom's brood and the tide (pass 262):** passes 257 and 259 had built their sounds from others. The Mothlings screeched with the generic `buzz` and dove with `whoosh`, the Bloom's petal flare used the same buzz, and wading Vorrhallow's water sounded like the Haar's mud. Now each has its own:
  - `mothling_screech`: a thin shriek rising through the beat of its wings (the 0.4 s tell).
  - `mothling_dive`: a falling whistle and the air torn past folded wings.
  - `bloom_brood`: the Bloom's petals flaring, a dry rustle swelling under a low many-winged hum (the 0.7 s tell).
  - `brood_loose`: the petals snapping open with a soft pop and a flurry of wings as the three fly out.
  - `step_wade`: the shin pushing water aside, and two drips falling back off the boot. `Room.surface_at` gives `wade` in standing water.

  The voices test holds the Mothling and the Bloom to their own sounds, and the tide test checks that a step in the water sloshes and is not mud.
- **Locks (pass 122):** a locked door is heard as a door, not a spell. The card doors and the Sky Gate had borrowed `fizzle` (the spell-fail sound) when locked and the plain `door` creak when opened. Now they rattle their latch (`door_locked`) and the key turns with its own click, bolt and chime (`key_turn`); see 4.5, Key doors.
- **Refusals (pass 286):** a press that does nothing is heard when the player needs telling why. An empty Sapflask flask had been silent, though it is the press most often made in trouble, while a spell short of MP and an Stillstone still running back both `fizzle`. Now an empty flask has its own sound, `flask_empty`: a dry cork pop and two small glass rings, with no swallow after. It is a flask's sound, not a spell's, for the reason pass 122 gave the doors theirs. Each refusal sounds once per press and spends nothing. Two presses stay quiet on purpose: a drink at full health, and a cast inside its 0.35 s cooldown, where the cast you just made is still sounding. `test_a_refused_drink_cast_or_glass_is_heard_once`.
- **Tablets and wards (pass 358):** two more borrowed sounds. Reading any of the 142 lore tablets (12) played `door`, the creak of a door, so a sign sounded like a way opening. The bound fog (4.4) and the elven veil that turn you away without the Lantern Key or the Stride played `fizzle`, the spell-fail sound, the reason pass 122 took the locks off it. Now a tablet read brushes grit off the carving and the runes answer with two soft stone-struck notes a fourth apart over a low hum (`tablet_read`), and a ward pushes back with a low thrum that swells and is pressed down a step, with a breath of air (`ward_refuse`), once per approach, like its message. `test_tablets_and_wards_sound_like_themselves` reads a tablet and walks into the fog and the veil for real, and holds each cue to its own scripts.
- **A menu that says no (pass 366):** a menu's refusal still borrowed `fizzle`, the spell-fail sound pass 122 and pass 358 took off the locks and the wards. A row you cannot choose (a ware you cannot afford, an Barkbalm you do not have, a skill not yet in reach) buzzed like a spell short of MP, and so did a map with all 12 pins down and, since pass 365, a pad button the Controls page keeps. Now each plays `menu_refuse` (-4 dB): the cursor's square blip, lower and dull, twice, falling a fourth from 330 to 247 Hz in 0.17 s, so a menu says no in the voice it says yes in. `fizzle` is left to the spells and the Stillstone. `test_a_menu_says_no_in_its_own_voice`.
- **The fall you can hear (pass 374):** the Drowned Aqueduct's broken channel (3.3, pass 371) poured a white fall into the flood without a sound, the one moving water in the game with no voice. It now has its own: `falls_pour`, a seamless two-second loop of a white hiss of falling water over a low churning roar that gulps as the water lands, the last half second laid over the first so the loop has no hard seam. The spill plays it while you are within 320 px of where the water lands (`AqueductSpill.HEAR`), 4 dB under the sound volume beside it and 22 dB quieter at the edge, the way the forge's anvil carries, and silent beyond, so it marks the landmark without filling the room. `test_the_aqueducts_fall_is_heard_near_it`.
- **Every sound is heard (pass 390):** each cue above was held by its own test, but nothing held the other direction, that every one of the 166 files in `assets/sfx/` is played by some moment of the game. A whole run of the suite now keeps every name asked of `Sfx.play` (`Sfx.heard`), and the runner fails an unfiltered run that leaves a file unheard (`unheard_sounds`). The first run found four. Three were sounds no test had reached, though the game plays them: the creak of a door that fades (tests skip the fades), the Kettleforge anvil's ring (no test stood within `Forge.HEAR` of it), and the Broad Sword's heavy cut (pass 190's test only asked `swing_sound` for its name). `test_a_door_that_fades_is_heard`, `test_the_kettleforge_forge_rings_and_throws_sparks` and `test_the_dagger_the_broad_sword_and_the_equipment_page_have_voices` now play each for real, the anvil both inside its reach and past it. The fourth, `falls_pour`, loops on the Aqueduct spill's own player, not through `Sfx`, so the runner lists it with the test that hears it (`OWN_VOICE`). A sound added later with nothing to play it, or a cue that loses its caller, fails the run.
- **A mana orb (pass 294):** the orb a kill leaves 1 time in 10 (7) was taken with `blip`, the menu cursor's beep, the sound pass 128 took off the map for the same reason: a pickup in the world sounded like a menu. It now has its own, `mana_orb`: a soft breath of air under three glassy notes rising quickly up a major triad, each with a faint shimmer a fifth above. It is kept apart from bread's `heal`, so health and magic are told apart by ear. `test_kills_sometimes_drop_bread_or_mana`.
- **Blightcap an Barkbalm keeps off (pass 302):** every drop is heard as it is taken: bread and the loaf `heal`, the orb `mana_orb`, poison `hurt`, the War Glove and an Barkbalm `item`. Blightcap picked up under an Barkbalm only showed its line, "The Barkbalm keeps the poison off.", in silence. It now plays `ward` (-6 dB), the glassy ring of a ward turning harm. `test_every_drop_is_heard_as_it_is_taken` takes every kind of drop and requires a sound and a line for each.
- **A poison tick (pass 310):** since pass 306 a tick takes 2 to 4 health and since pass 309 it shows a green number, but it was the one loss of health with no sound. Each tick that bites now plays `poison_tick` (-4 dB): a small wet glug, a sine bubble sagging from 300 to 170 Hz with a smaller one behind it over murky noise. Its peak sits 3 dB under `hurt`'s, so a sickness never reads as a blow. A tick that takes nothing (the last point, under an Barkbalm) is silent. `test_every_poison_tick_is_heard`.
- **Rewards that sound (pass 165):** the newest rewards had no sound of their own. A blow that Executioner (9) drives into a boss's opening now cleaves with `execute`, played under the cut: a low body thud, a crack, and a short hard ring of the blade biting deep. A plain blow, or one on a boss on its guard, does not play it. A ring dye won (10) rings out over the trial bell with `dye_won`: cloth swept open, and the bell struck high, three quick notes falling into a held chord. A clear that earns no dye rings the bell alone.
- **Bloodletting is heard (pass 318):** since pass 314 every guardian opens without a parry, so Bloodletting (9) heals in every fight, and a dagger build that cannot parry leans on it. Its heal was silent, felt only as a number on the masks. It now plays `bloodlet` as the health comes back: a warm, quiet double pulse like a heartbeat taking, two soft swells rising 180 to 260 Hz (peak -10.5 dB, well under the `hit` it rides on). It plays only when health is actually given, never at full. Test: `test_bloodletting_heals_on_a_staggered_foe`.
- **Gear that sounds like itself (pass 190):** the newest gear had borrowed sounds or none. A Rune Dagger that lands now bites with `dagger_hit`, a high tick of the point going in over a small dry knock, lighter than a blade's `hit`; until then a throw that struck was silent. The Dwarvish Broad Sword, two-handed and 30% slower, cuts with `broad_slash` and `broad_heavy`, a longer, lower rush of air with weight under it, in place of the light blades' `slash` and `heavy` (`Player.swing_sound`). On the Equipment page a blade taken in hand slides from its sheath and seats with a click (`sheathe`), and armor, a shield or a helmet buckles on, leather and a clink (`gear_on`); only a dye keeps the cloth swish (`Menus.equipment_sound`).
- **A dagger on stone (pass 326):** a Rune Dagger that struck a wall still borrowed the shield's `guard` clank (at -8 dB), so a throw at stone sounded like a block, and since Sean's off hand (5.2) a dagger build never blocks at all. It now pings with `dagger_ping` (`RuneDagger.WALL_SOUND`): a glancing steel ping of two inharmonic partials (2.86 and 4.17 kHz) over a gritty tick of the point, 0.14 s, peak -8 dB played at -4. It is brighter and shorter than `dagger_hit`, so stone and flesh are told apart by ear. A throw that runs out of reach stays silent apart from its rune light. `test_a_dagger_on_stone_pings_and_never_clanks_like_a_shield`.
- **The title's flasks (pass 350):** the shelf of journeys (Sean's three flasks, 14) had borrowed three sounds that mean something else: taking a journey up played `save`, the rest's chime, though nothing was saved; beginning one played `title`, the two-second fanfare a Ringreader plays when he names your rank; and pouring one out played `splash`, a body going into water. Each now has its own. Taking a flask down, full or empty, is `flask_uncork`, a round cork pop with a little suck of air and the glow inside swelling up a fifth. Pouring a journey out is `flask_pour`, liquid glugging out of the neck, slowing, and the empty glass ringing once. Moving between the flasks keeps `blip`, the menu cursor's own. `test_the_flasks_sound_like_flasks` holds that every cue the shelf plays but `blip` is its own, played nowhere else.
- **Every press in a menu is heard (pass 398):** the sounds above were held by calling a menu's own functions, never through the buttons a player presses. Now each press of the real controller is heard as it should be. In a list menu, Up and Down blip, Left and Right blip on a row with a value, Jump or Attack on a row you cannot choose plays `menu_refuse`, and Left or Right on that row and backing out with Dash are quiet. On the shelf of flasks, Left and Right blip, Block on a full flask asks with a blip, Dash keeps it quietly, Jump then pours it out with `flask_pour`, Block on an empty flask is quiet, and Jump on an empty one plays `flask_uncork`. `test_every_press_in_a_menu_is_heard`.
- **Rests and pins, heard through the buttons (pass 406):** the shrine's save chime was heard only by the gate that every sound plays somewhere, and the map's pin sounds were never listened for. Now Up at a shrine, pressed on the real controller, rests and saves with one save chime; Jump on the open map drops a pin with `pin` and lifts it with `pin_lift`; and with the map holding all twelve pins, Jump plays `menu_refuse` and adds none. `test_rests_and_pins_are_heard_through_the_buttons`. **The Ringreader's pages, heard through the buttons (pass 454):** pass 398 heard the list menus, but no press in the Ringreader's own pages had been listened to. `test_the_ringreaders_pages_are_heard_through_the_buttons` presses for real: Down blips; Jump on a skill learns it with the `rank` chime and Jump on it again plays `menu_refuse`; Dash backs out to play quietly; Jump on Unlearn skills is heard as `heal`, and again with nothing learned as `menu_refuse`; Jump on a stop of the Grain Road plays the `save` chime once, and never a `jump`.
- **A turned blade is heard as a guard (pass 446):** since pass 442 a slash a guard turns pushes back as a clang, but every swing that met a foe still played the cut's `hit` over the shield's `guard`, so a turned blade sounded like one that landed, on a Rot Dwarf's shield and on the four guardians' guards (pass 444) alike. A swing that meets only guards is now heard by the guard's thud alone, and one that lands by the cut alone. A swing that does both, or breaks stone, still cuts. `test_a_turned_blade_is_heard_as_a_guard` slashes the dwarf's shield and then his back for real, and `test_a_guardians_guard_turns_the_blade_back` listens to each guardian's guard.
- **The ready chimes, on time (pass 414):** two sounds tell a player that a move is ready again without looking. `thrust_ready` plays when Attack held fills the Thrust's 0.4 s charge. `dagger_return` plays as the Rune Dagger reforms at your hip. No test had ever listened for either, so a chime early, late, twice or not at all would have passed. `test_the_ready_chimes_ring_when_the_move_is_ready` presses the real controller: Attack held chimes once, on the frame the charge fills. A dagger thrown at the wall chimes once, as it is back and ready. **The fight, heard through the buttons (pass 422):** pass 418 pressed Block for real against the Rot Dwarf and read each outcome, but the sounds of those outcomes were only heard with a scripted guard. `test_the_fight_is_heard_through_the_buttons` hears each once and alone: a parry rings (`parry`), a press a step too soon lets the blow land with the hurt cry (`hurt`), Block held thuds (`guard`) before the halved blow's cry, and Block held with too little MP cracks (`guard_break`) and the whole blow lands. **A menu closed by its button is not heard as the hero (pass 430):** pass 429 found the press that takes a journey up read a second time by the hero. The same thing happened with every menu closed by a button the hero also uses. The menu closes in the frame of the press. When no physics step fell between the press and the close, the hero's next step still saw it as just pressed. That is most frames on a display faster than 60 Hz, and every frame of the test runner. So Jump on Resume jumped, Attack swung and Dash dashed out of the pause menu, heard as `jump`, `slash` and `dash`. The hero now drops the presses of their first step after steps were missed while the game was paused, as on waking (`PlayerInput.drop_presses`), and held buttons stay held. `test_a_menu_closed_by_its_button_is_not_heard_as_the_hero` runs the whole game. It closes the pause menu with each of Jump, Attack, Dash, Block and Pause, held for a third of a second. Each time the menu goes and play goes on, the hero does not move, and no jump, swing or dash is heard. A fresh Jump afterwards jumps.
- **The map (pass 128):** the map is a sheet of parchment and sounds like one. It had opened, closed and taken pins with `blip`, the menu cursor's beep. Now opening it rustles as it unrolls and settles with a soft flap (`map_open`), closing folds it with a dull pat (`map_close`), a pin goes in with a tiny tick and a bright ring (`pin`), and lifting one rings falling with the tick of it coming free (`pin_lift`). A full map still fizzles.
- **Fields and the cloak (pass 137):** the wind currents of the High Boughs, Skyrend's updraft and the wrong-gravity pockets of the Evercrown had changed how you move without a sound. Now a current or updraft catching you blows past in a gust (`wind_catch`), a pocket lifting your weight rises in a hollow, glassy shimmer (`drift_in`), and leaving it drops into a low hum and a soft thud (`drift_out`). Each sounds once as you cross the edge, and waits `Player.FIELD_CUE_GAP` (0.3 s) before it may sound again, so hovering on an edge does not chatter. At Ilse's Dyeworks a dye goes on with a cloth swish (`cloth`). Wearing one you own again plays no coin, since nothing is paid.
- **After the end (pass 143):** on a cleared save the music follows the Tree's bloom (4.4) through `Music.after_the_end` (table `Music.AFTER_THE_END`):
  - **Hearthroot** plays its festival variant, `hearthroot_bloom`. It is the town theme a touch quicker (100), with a bell doubling the arpeggio an octave up, a frame drum and a bell answering the lead.
  - **The dead stillglass goes still.** the Evercrown drops its heartbeat track for `evercrown_still`: B minor at 60, the pads in tune where they had beaten against each other, no pulse, and once per pass a far bell climbing a B major chord. Its bed becomes `amb_evercrown_still`: the breath and heartbeat are gone, leaving a thin hollow wind, stone ticking as it cools and now and then a crystal cracking with a soft chime.
  - Other zones keep their themes, and a fight still plays its fight.
- **Big coins (pass 206):** since pass 199 gold comes in coins of 1, 5, 25 and 100, but every coin chimed the same two notes, so a 5,000 g chest sounded no richer than a single foe's few coins. The 25 and 100 g coins now ring `coin_big` (`Coin.sound`): the same rising chime a fourth lower, a third note above it, and a bell tail, 0.3 s against the small coin's 0.12 s, 2 dB louder in peak. The small coins keep `coin`. Test: `test_a_big_coin_rings_lower_and_longer`.
- **A guardian's fall (pass 222):** every boss win had played `item`, the same chime as a loaf of bread off the floor, while the boss theme faded out over 2.5 s on top of it, so the end of a fight sounded like a pickup. Now the win rings `boss_won` (`BossArena.WIN_SOUND`), the old 8-bit victory call: three quick brassy G's over a timpani roll, then a held C major chord with a low root, 2.4 s, ringing out as the boss theme drops away in 0.4 s (`BossArena.WIN_FADE`). It plays under the banner for every prize (the Severance included) and under the skill-point toast, and between fights of a boss rush. Tests: `test_a_guardians_fall_rings_a_fanfare_of_its_own`, and `test_every_guardians_win_says_what_it_gave` listens for it in every arena.
- **The glass runs out, and a ward rings (pass 230):** the Stillstone rang as it was turned and then went silent, so the only sign that stopped time was ending was the sand in the HUD, and frozen footholds melted and held shots flew on without warning. Now its last three quarter-seconds each tick (`glass_tick`, a dry clock tick), and time running again rushes (`glass_end`, air let go forward over a low thunk) (`Player._glass_cues`, `GLASS_TICKS`, `GLASS_TICK`). The shield's and helmet's ward (5.2, pass 225) turned part of a hex in silence; it now rings (`ward`, a bright glassy ring) under the hurt, and only for sorcery. Tests: `test_the_stillstone_ticks_before_time_runs_again`, and `test_a_shield_wards_a_foes_sorcery` now listens for the ring.
- **The glove on the wall (pass 238):** the wall slide was the one movement state with nothing to hear. It shed dust every 0.06 s, but every jump, landing, dash, glide, pogo and step had a sound, and the slide had none. Now the glove rasps (`wall_slide`: leather dragged down bark, a short gritty flutter at -21 dB loudness, under the glide's -21.6). It plays as you catch the wall, at -4 dB, and every 0.24 s after while you slide, at -8 dB (`Player.WALL_SLIDE_SOUND`, `WALL_SCRAPE`), so a long slide is a steady scrape under the dust. `test_a_wall_slide_rasps_down_the_wall` counts the rasps over a second's slide, and with no Glove there is none.
- **Mix (pass 96):** `python3 tools/audio/levels.py` measures the peak and the loudness of the audible part of every sound, track and bed. `--check` fails if any of them clips (peak at -0.1 dBFS or over) or strays more than 9 dB from its group's median. Generated music is normalized to -3 dBFS (it had been -1.4, and the Vorbis encoder's overshoot clipped 22 of 25 tracks past full scale), with `Music.MUSIC_DB` raised 1.7 dB to -8.3 to keep its place in the mix. The death sound was 10 dB over the effects' median and is now in line. **The engine's levels (pass 342):** a zone's ambience bed plays at `Music.AMBIENCE_DB` (-18 dB), under the music's -8.3, and a new track or bed crossfades in over `Music.FADE` (1.2 s). `test_the_audio_numbers_are_written_in_bible_13_3` holds these, the 12 voices, the music's semitone lift over 0.8 s, `Sfx.LIFT` (3.1 and 2.2 dB) and the mix check's own limits (`levels.py`: -0.1 dBFS, 9 dB, 1.5 dB of headroom) to the code. Until pass 342 no test read any of them from the bible.
- **Supplied music (pass 214):** a track Sean supplies takes the generated placeholder's name in `assets/music/`. The game had looked for `.ogg` first, and every placeholder is an `.ogg`, so a supplied `.mp3` or `.wav` was never heard. `Music.EXTENSIONS` now looks for `.mp3`, then `.wav`, then `.ogg` (`Music.path_for`). `musicgen.py` leaves alone any track with a supplied `.mp3` or `.wav`. A supplied `.ogg` replaces the placeholder's file, so don't regenerate that track afterwards. `levels.py` had measured only the `.ogg` files; now it measures whichever file each track plays from. A new CI job, `mix`, runs `levels.py --check` on every push, so a supplied track that clips, or sits more than 9 dB off the others, is caught before it merges. `test_a_supplied_track_replaces_its_placeholder` holds the order.
- **Headroom for the loudest cues (pass 270):** the hero's hurt sat 8.9 dB over the effects' median and the cut landing (`hit`) 8.0 over, so a single new quiet sound nudging the median tipped the mix check red (it did in pass 262). `levels.py` now asks every sound effect to keep `HEADROOM` (1.5 dB) inside the 9 dB spread. `sfxgen.py` saves `hurt` at gain 0.42 (was 0.6) and `hit` at 0.62 (was 0.8), about 12.5 dB RMS each, 6 over the median, and `Sfx.LIFT` plays them back 3.1 and 2.2 dB up on top of any caller's offset, so in game they are exactly as loud as before. A cue that must play louder than the mix allows is saved quieter and lifted the same way. Test: `test_the_loudest_cues_are_lifted_in_game_not_in_the_file`; the `mix` CI job holds the headroom.
- **Ambience:** one bed per zone (wind, drips, creaking wood, storm), and a room can name another (`ambience:`). In the Tower, the halls hum with machinery (amb_tower), the Old Flue and the Rafters howl with wind, a rattling shutter and creaking beams (amb_heights), and the Deep Workings drip, with carts rumbling through the rock and far-off picks (amb_mine). The Inner Rings' inner rings has its own theme and bed (`trials`, `amb_trials`, pass 116). A boss rush plays the same theme in the arenas between fights (pass 157). **No bed under the ending or the title (pass 468):** the ending changed the music but never the bed, so the Evercrown's heartbeat (`amb_evercrown`, the last room's) went on beating under the ending, the credits and the title it returns to. The ending now fades the bed out with its music, so the title after it plays its theme over no bed, as the first start always did. `test_the_game_plays_the_ending_then_returns_to_the_title` checks both.
- **Footsteps:** each step plays the sound of the ground underfoot (`Room.surface_at`): stone in Hearthroot, moss on the Heartwood's living wood, iron on the Tower's plates, hollow planks on every one-way platform, and a squelch in sap. A room can name another surface with `surface:`. A landing layers the surface step under the land thud, louder the harder the fall. Since pass 181 the Haar's marsh is mud (`step_mud`: a wet slap as the boot goes in and a small suction pop as it pulls free), where it had borrowed the Heartwood's moss; its towns' boardwalks stay plank, and the four rooms the dwarves built (the Scorched Hall, the Dry Cistern, the Sunken Chapel and the Haar Spring) are stone. The Matriarch's Mire is mud.
- **Every zone's sound, as built (pass 382):** the Ambience and Footsteps lines above name the Tower's beds and the ground of five zones, and never say what the rest play or stand on. Each zone plays its own theme (`Music.for_room`: the zone's id, or a room's `music:`), its own bed (`amb_` and the zone's id, or a room's `ambience:`) and its own ground (`Room.ZONE_SURFACES`, or a room's `surface:`):

| Zone | Theme | Bed | Underfoot |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Hearthroot | `hearthroot` | `amb_hearthroot` | stone |
| Heartwood | `heartwood` | `amb_heartwood` | moss |
| Hollin Tower | `tower` | `amb_tower` | iron |
| The Haar | `haar` | `amb_haar` | mud |
| High Boughs | `boughs` | `amb_boughs` | plank |
| Storm Spires | `spires` | `amb_spires` | stone |
| Vorrhallow | `vorrhallow` | `amb_vorrhallow` | stone |
| Sunken Spring | `sunken` | `amb_sunken` | stone |
| The Evercrown | `evercrown` | `amb_evercrown` | moss |
| Inner Rings | `trials` | `amb_trials` | stone |

  The High Boughs are walked on the elves' plank roads and boughs, and the Evercrown on the Everbloom's soft, grown stone, which takes the Heartwood's moss step. Twenty-two rooms name their own. Music: Kettleforge `kettleforge`, Lampwick `lampwick`, Polesend `polesend`, Sylvarre `sylvarre`, Carillon `spires_town`, Old Hearthroot `old_hearthroot`, and Amberlee `hearthroot`: since pass 05 the first town past Hearthroot has played Hearthroot's theme, the one town with no song of its own. Beds: the Old Flue, the Rafters, the Bell Loft and the Crown Lookout `heights`, the Deep Workings (Mine Mouth, Mine Shaft, Mine Vault, Rail Gallery, Sorting Floor, Smelter and Slag Heap) `mine`, and Old Hearthroot `none` (a sealed chamber, silent but for its theme). Ground: Lampwick and Polesend `plank`, the Haar's four dwarf-built rooms (the Scorched Hall, the Dry Cistern, the Sunken Chapel and the Haar Spring) `stone`, and Carillon `stone` (its zone's own). `test_every_zone_sounds_as_the_bible_says` holds each row to the code and `assets/music/`, and the list of rooms to every `music:`, `ambience:` and `surface:` line in `levels/`.

---

### 13.4 Hero body sets (paper doll)

The new hero is Sean's Autosprite character HeroStranger_Base (purple-haired elf, 2026-10-27), replacing the hooded
ranger clip by clip. The hero is drawn in layers so gear does not multiply animations:

- **Body + armor:** one full sheet per armor look, five in all: `hero_base` (no armor, green tunic), `hero_leather`,
  `hero_riveted`, `hero_plate`, `hero_battle` (`PlayerVisual.ARMOR_SHEETS` maps the worn armor to its sheet). Each is
  an Autosprite character of its own run through the same clip list. A clip a set lacks plays from the old `player`
  sheet until it exists.
- **Weapon, shield, helmet, cloak:** separate layers drawn from per-frame anchors the importer finds in each frame
  (`detect: "hero"` in the manifest): `hand` (the leading fist, as the code rig's front hand), `head` (the crown of the
  head, from `head_point`, for the helmet and the hood), `neck`, and `back` (the nape of the shoulders, where a cloak hangs). Anchors are per set and
  per frame, so the sets do not need to be frame-synced. Attacks are drawn empty-handed.
- **Elven Plate + Elven Helm:** worn together they swap to a body of their own, `hero_battle_helm` (Sean's HeroStranger_ElvenPlateArmor_ElvenBattleHelm, drawn with the helmet on; `PlayerVisual.HELMED_SHEETS`), and the helmet layer is not drawn over it. Its crown is the top of the figure (manifest `"head": "top"`). The bare-headed Elven Plate keeps its own Autosprite colors, which match this set; the pass-212 teal recolor toward the pixel helmet is dropped, since the pixel helmet now only goes over the other armors and the old clips. Now it is Sean's second take on this character with a hooded cloak drawn into the art (512 px cells, 56 frames: idle 6..50 step 2 at 7 fps, walk 28..53 at 34, run 2..16 at 40). The set carries the manifest flag `"cloak": true`, so `Cloak.shown()` hides the code cloak and hood over it (Sean found neither the code hood nor his pixel hood sprite matched). Its idle faces three-quarters toward the camera, unlike the other sets' profile idles. Clips it lacks (jump, attacks and the rest) still fall back to the ranger sheet with the pixel helmet and code cloak.
- **Scale:** all five share one scale, base idle = 46 world px (the ranger's height), so armor that stands taller or
  bulkier (plate, Elven Plate: about 1 px) shows it. Clips are 512 px cells at 14 fps; walk and run play every frame
  of one cycle, sped up until the planted foot keeps pace with the ground (run about 30 fps, walk up to 40).
- **Cloak** (`scripts/player/cloak.gd`, code-drawn, no credits): Sean's Elven cloak from his side views
  (`/mnt/project-files/armor/cloak-hood-up.jpg`, `cloak-hood-down.jpg`; its green #3f6a3f and gold #cba040 from his pixel cloak, `cloak-pixel-right.png`, which as a shrunk sprite hid the face at game scale): ankle length, flaring behind, gold trim
  down the front edge and the hem, a brooch at the shoulder. The cape is two verlet chains hung from the frame's
  `neck` and `back` points, behind the body; the hood is drawn over the head from `head`. **Hood down in a town, up
  everywhere else** (Sean, 2026-10-27; a town is a room where people stand and no foe or boss spawns,
  `Room.is_town()`), raised or lowered over 0.35 s when that changes, and always down under a helmet. It takes the
  worn dye's color (Warden Green is its own), so the 14 dyes need no extra art. The body sets themselves are drawn
  undyed, and while a clip falls back to the ranger's sheet (which draws its own cloak) the layer hides.

### 13.5 The PixelLab hero (2026-10-30)

Sean asked for the hero and his gear to be made in PixelLab, quality first. The base is **Sean's own PixelLab elf**
(character `b246489c`, "Right Facing relaxed": side view, 256 px canvas, the same purple-haired elf in the green tunic as
13.4's reference), so the look is his. Sources are in `art/pixellab/` (`hero/`, `delvers_pick/`), packed into strip sheets by
`tools/art/pixellab_sheets.py` (`art/pixellab/sheets/<sheet>/`) and imported by the same importer as Autosprite clips
(`art/autosprite/manifest.json`, whose `dir` points at the strips): same scale (46 world px), palette, outline and rim.

- **What works, by mode:** armor tiers as **character states** (`create-character-state`, 25 generations each: leather,
  studded mail, full plate and Elven Plate all match Sean's T-pose references). Clips in **pixminimax** mode (about 2
  generations for 12 frames at 256 px) stay in strict profile when the prompt says so, and draw props well (the jump,
  walk, the Delver's Pick pogo and swing). v3-mode attacks turned toward the camera on the recovery; skeleton-v3 jumps came
  out stiff. Outfit transfer works but costs 25 per 3 frames, too dear for whole clips.
- **Swords are drawn into the attack clips** (Sean, 2026-10-30): each armor's attacks are made holding each blade, not
  a separate sword layer. A state of the hero holding the weapon is made first, and the clip animates from it.
- **The Delver's Pick is drawn** (Sean, 2026-10-30: "build out the delvers_pick asset and swing animation"): a dwarven iron
  delvers_pick, adze blade and pick, on an iron-banded ash haft. The pogo (`strike_down`) is the hero driving it straight down
  beneath his tucked knees: two frames of the pixminimax pogo clip, moved 58 source px down (`"lower"`) so the knees
  sit at the feet and the iron head reaches about 12 px below them, into the strike's 24 px hitbox. Hero sheets are 84
  px tall (`spritegen.ARMOR_CELL`, 23 px under the origin) to hold it. The strike plays to its end over the bounce
  (`PlayerVisual.FINISH`), since a pogo ends the attack the frame it lands. An overhead swing that breaks stone is
  made (`delvers_pick/swing_mm`) for a later breaking pose; the item art is `delvers_pick/item/02.png`.
- **Imported so far (`hero_base`):** idle, walk, run (Sean's own run clip), rise, apex, fall, the Delver's Pick pogo, and
  (pixminimax, holding the Short Sword) the three-hit combo (a thrust, a rising cut and an overhead chop), the upward
  slash, dash, wall slide, hurt, death, drinking a potion and casting. Frames with artifacts are left out by `pick`
  (the dash's color smear, the hurt clip's red arrow, the cast's teal turn).
- **The armor tiers (`hero_leather`, `hero_riveted`, `hero_plate`, `hero_battle`):** the same seventeen clips, made
  from each armor's state (movement, hurt, death, drink, cast), its state holding the Short Sword (the four attacks) and
  holding the Delver's Pick (the pogo). Each clip starts from the state's own pose, so picks skip frame 0; some picks differ by
  armor where a take turned toward the camera or carries an effect (kept in the manifest). Pogos are made with "he stays
  high in the air the whole time and never touches the ground", since the first takes landed and struck the floor.
  They replace the Autosprite body sets. `hero_battle_helm`, the Elven Plate under the Elven Helm, is made the same way
  from a state of the battle set wearing a crested mithril helm (its head point is the crown of the helm, `"head": "top"`).
- **The other blades (`hero_<set>_<long_sword|broad_sword|severance>`):** every set's four attacks again, from its
