  state holding the Wayfarer's Sword, the Dwarvish Broad Sword (in both hands, as long as he is tall) or the Severance
  (silver, a faint blue edge glow, golden winged guard). Each blade sheet holds only the attacks, plus the set's own idle
  as the importer's reference for scale; `PlayerVisual.sheet_for` plays an attack from the worn blade's sheet
  (`BLADE_SHEETS`, `BLADE_CLIPS`), and everything else from the set. The Short Sword is each set's own attacks.
- **Shields, drawn into the block (`hero_<set>_<small|large|magic>_shield`):** each cloaked set from a state holding
  each shield up to guard (the Buckler a round wooden buckler with an iron boss, the Kite Shield a tall kite shield
  with a crimson face and silver rim, the Warding Shield a long leaf of pale sea-green mithril with gold trim), and from it
  a braced block clip that holds on its last frame. `sheet_for("block")` plays it for the worn shield (`SHIELD_SHEETS`);
  the code-drawn shield is held over the block only on a sheet without one (the old hero).
- **The Rune Dagger throw (`throw`, `throw_up`, Sean 2026-10-30):** a quick ninja-star flick of the LEFT hand from a
  low ready crouch, forward at chest height or up over the head, the sword kept in the right hand (so nothing is
  sheathed). Each set has it with the Short Sword, and each blade sheet with its blade (`BLADE_CLIPS`). The takes drew
  a dagger or a streak at the release, so the picks start just after it: the dagger is the game's projectile. A throw
  restarts the flick over the legs' poses and it plays out like a swing's recovery (`PlayerVisual.FINISH`); the old
  hero, with no flick, only squashes as before. The first right-hand, empty-handed flick (PR #343) is replaced.
- **Grey slabs:** PixelLab sometimes leaves a flat dull-grey patch behind a smear or a cloak (one exact grey with a
  faint warm, violet, green or blue cast, plus a few mottled neighbours); `pixellab_sheets.py` erases a connected patch
  of such greys when it is 400 px or more and mostly one colour, so the shaded armor steel is never touched.
- **Cloaks, drawn in (Sean, 2026-10-30):** every set, the bare elf included, is made again from a state wearing a long
  cloak in a chroma-key green with gold trim ("like a green screen"), with every clip and blade (`hero/<set>_cloak/`).
  `shaders/hero_dye.gdshader` recolors the key (a saturated pure green nothing else on the hero uses) to the worn dye,
  keeping its shading; Warden Green is the lit tone `PlayerVisual.WARDEN_KEY`. The sets carry the manifest flags
  `"cloak": true` (the code cloak and hood of 13.4 are not drawn over them) and `"key_cloak": true` (the shader). Where a
  take dropped the cloak to an empty outline it was retaken (the plate set's most often), picked around, or kept where
  the cloak only swings behind the body.
- **Hair (Sean, 2026-10-30):** the elf's violet hair is the only saturated purple on him, so the same shader recolors
  it to the hair color chosen on the Equipment page (a Hair section after the Cloak: Violet, the art's own, Copper,
  Raven, Silver, Gold and Ash; free, any time; `PlayerVisual.HAIR`, saved as `GameState.data.hair`). The armor's
  violet-tinted steel and the outline are too grey or too dark to count. The helmed Elven Plate (`"hair": false`) shows
  no hair, and its crest keeps its purple.
- **Helmet:** with any set but the Elven Plate, the Elven Helm stays a layer on the head point (Sean, 2026-10-30);
  only the Elven Plate has a helmed set. The layer is cut from that set's art (`tools/art/helmetgen.py`): the helm and
  the face and ear it frames, from the first frame of the helmed idle, at the sets' scale, so it matches the helmed
  Elven Plate (it replaces Sean's pixel helmet, made for the Autosprite hero). `PlayerVisual.HELMET_OFFSET` is what
  helmetgen measures on the bare Elven Plate, whose head lies where the helm's does.
- **Run speed:** each set's run plays at the frame rate its stride needs for the planted foot to keep pace with the
  ground at the run speed (165 px/s, section 6): 25 to 36 fps, from the foot's backward travel per cycle.

### 13.6 Sean's PixelLab scenery (2026-11-03)

Sean makes Hearthroot's tilesets and set pieces himself in the PixelLab app (palette Resurrect 64, 32 x 32 tiles, objects
at most 256 x 256), from the prompts in the project files (`hearthroot-prompts/`). Set pieces are drawn at the hero's 2x
density. `tools/art/pixellab_objects.py` packs an object's states into a `Sheet` (`assets/sprites/<name>`), one row per
state, cropped to one shared box with the origin at its bottom center, from sources in `art/pixellab/<dir>/<state>/`.
The first is **the Fountain of Hearthroot**: dry, then one state per restored spring (a trickle, a steady fall, full and
glowing), the last three animated. `Fountain` shows the state for the springs restored and keeps its code drawing only
as a fallback.
Market Row's four shops (Hearthroot Arms, Ilse's Dyeworks, Hearthroot Sundries and the Cartographer's house) are
`SetPiece`s, room props named in `SetPiece.NAMES`, each standing behind its keeper.
A set piece's building is one still frame: PixelLab redraws a whole object per animation frame, so its walls shift
(Sean tried it on the smithy). The smithy is Sean's own hand edit in Pixelorama (2026-11-04): 8 frames at 6 fps where only the chimney smoke, the
forge fire and the lantern move, so the walls hold still. Light is drawn in code over the sprite (`SetPiece.EFFECTS`):
the forge and lanterns cast a flickering glow, and a piece without drawn fire or smoke can have code embers and puffs.
**Hearthroot's ground** is Sean's town-ground tileset for the edges only (the flagstone lip, the rims), built by
`tools/art/pixellab_tileset.py` from its 16 corner tiles into the 47 blob tiles with their insides cleared; the inside
of every wall is one seamless 128 px PixelLab texture (stone carved into roots, moss in the cracks,
`assets/tiles/hearthroot_fill.png`) drawn by `GroundFill` behind the tiles by world position, so a wall no longer repeats
every tile (Sean, 2026-11-03: the tileset "looks very basic" next to the objects). Interior depth still darkens, as a
veil over the fill. One-way planks are Sean's ledge. `tilegen.PIXELLAB_SETS` names the zones built this way.

## 14. Technical Architecture (Godot)

- **Engine:** Godot 4.7.2 stable (pinned in CI and `tools/godot-version.txt`). GDScript with static typing.
- **Renderer:** the project's default, Forward+. The screenshot tool draws every room on the Compatibility renderer (`opengl3`, `tools/screenshot.sh`), so the fallback for low-end machines is exercised on every visual check. The plan's 2D lights and glow were never used: lamplight, glows and the fog are drawn by code and shaders.
- **Physics:** custom kinematic controller on CharacterBody2D with hand-written movement (no built-in floor snapping surprises), fixed 60 Hz.
- **Structure:**
  - **As built (pass 389):** the plan's structure (autoloads named SaveSystem, EventBus, AudioDirector, InputRouter and RoomManager; a `scenes/` folder per player, enemy family, boss, room and screen; .tres resources; a room_loader.gd, YAML headers and a world.yaml graph) was never built, and this list said so for none of it. Most nodes are built in code by `class_name` scripts, and `scenes/main.tscn` is the one scene (`docs/architecture.md` tells the scene flow).
  - `scripts/autoload/`: five autoloads, `Feel` (hit-stop, shake, rumble), `Controls` (the input map, built in code, and rebinding), `Sfx` (pooled sounds), `GameState` (everything that survives a room change or a save, and the saves) and `Music` (themes and ambience beds). Signals live on the nodes that raise them (`GameState.toast`, `banner`), and the World moves the player between rooms. Input is read through `PlayerInput` (buffering lives in the player's timers). Saving lives in GameState: one JSON file per slot in user://, written safely and read defensively (15.3, pass 109). **Three journeys, as three flasks (Sean, 2026-10-31):** pass 343 found that only one slot had been built, and Sean chose three, "graphical", with "details on hover", and no number or letter on any (no 1, 2, 3, no A, B, C): "like 3 colored orbs or flasks". The title's first row is **Journeys**. It opens a shelf of three flasks (`SlotPicker`, slots `Game.SAVE_SLOTS`, `user://save_1.json` to `save_3.json`), one in each spring's color: amber for the Heartwood, pale green for the Haar, blue for the Sky. A flask that holds a journey is corked and glows, filled as high as that hero's rank, with bubbles rising through it. An empty flask stands open and dark, its glass still tinted its spring's color. **Told apart by shape as well as color (pass 357):** the Accessibility line below promises colorblind-safe marks, and amber, pale green and pale blue are the colors a colorblind player confuses most. Each flask's glass now bears its spring's mark, full or empty: a leaf for the Heartwood, a ripple of mist for the Haar and a bolt for the Sky (`SlotPicker.MARKS`), a picture and never a number or a letter. `test_the_flasks_are_told_apart_by_shape`. Screenshot: `/mnt/project-files/save-slots/flasks-marked.png`. Focus a flask (Left/Right, or the mouse over it) and the shelf below shows that journey: where you last rested and in which zone, your rank, your gold, your time on the road, and how many springs flow. An empty flask says it is empty. Jump (or a click) takes a journey up or begins a new one in an empty flask. Block on a full flask asks to pour it out, and Jump then deletes that save (`GameState.delete_save`). The shelf opens on the journey last rested in, and `--continue` loads that one. The flasks read a save without loading it (`GameState.read_slot`, which falls back to the `.bak`). Every rest saves to the journey's own flask, the autosave the plan asked for. Each write goes to a `.tmp`, is read back whole, and only then takes the file's place, with the last good copy kept as `.bak`, which a damaged save falls back to (`GameState.write_safely`, `load_game`). `test_three_flasks_hold_three_journeys` holds the shelf, and `test_the_bibles_saves_and_options_are_as_built` holds the slots, the paths and the options below.
  - `scripts/player/`, `scripts/enemies/`, `scripts/bosses/`, `scripts/world/`, `scripts/ui/`, `scripts/combat/`, `scripts/pickups/` and `scripts/fx/` hold those classes.
  - `data/` holds JSON: `data/items.json`, `data/shops.json`, `data/skills.json`, `data/npcs.json` (people and their words) and `data/rename_map.json` (the retired names and ids, 1). A foe's numbers live in its script and are held to 7's table by tests, and the player's in `scripts/player/combat_tuning.gd` and `scripts/player/movement_tuning.gd`.
- **Level authoring for an AI team:** rooms are described in a text format (`levels/<zone>/<room>.room`: an ASCII tile grid under a header of `key: value` lines for doors, props, signs, map placement and camera, `levels/README.md`). The room script (`scripts/world/room.gd`) builds TileMapLayer nodes and entities from them, so no generated scenes need to be kept in sync. Text levels diff cleanly in pull requests and are easy for agents to write and review. There is no world graph file: each room's `door:` lines and `map:` placement are the graph, which the World reads into its catalog (`World.build_catalog`) for the map screen, and the reachability checker (`scripts/world/reach.gd`) reads the rooms themselves.
- **Reachability checker:** a tool that simulates the player's movement envelope (jump heights, dash distance) per ability set and proves every required item is reachable in order and that no soft-lock exists. It runs in CI. Its pickup sweeps cover the finds `prop:` lines lay as well as the grid's pickups (pass 203: until then a Glass Key, Elven Helm, Acorn Charm or Briar Wand moved out of reach would have passed).
- **Testing:** the project's own runner (`tests/test_runner.gd`, run by `tools/run_tests.sh`; not GUT) with a suite per area in `tests/`; scripted input replays for the movement controller with asserted metrics (jump height, dash distance, coyote window); headless boot of every room; screenshot capture per room under a virtual display (Xvfb) for visual review. **The menus, by the real controller (pass 397):** passes 384 to 394 drove every move and combat button through `Input` itself, but the menus read their buttons in `_process` (`Input.is_action_just_pressed`), and every menu test called `move`, `adjust`, `confirm` and `back` directly. `test_the_menus_answer_the_real_controller` presses the buttons for real. On a `ListMenu`, Down and Up move the cursor (wrapping), Right and Left change a value, Jump and Attack choose, and Dash, Block and Pause back out and let the game run again. On the shelf of flasks, Right and Left walk it, Jump uncorks the focused flask, and Dash puts it away. **The map, by its buttons (pass 405):** the map reads its buttons as input events (`_unhandled_input`), not by polling, so pass 397's presses never reached it, and every map test called `toggle` and `pin` directly. `test_the_map_answers_its_buttons` sends each button through the engine's input as an action event. Map opens it, pausing the game, with its rustle. Jump drops a pin where you stand, and Jump again lifts it. Map closes it and the game runs on. With the map shut, Jump drops no pin. Pause closes it too, and a frozen hero cannot open it. **The pause menu by the buttons (pass 421):** no test had pressed Pause in play, so the game's own check for it and the pause menu's rows were only reached by calling `Menus.open_pause`. `test_the_pause_menu_answers_the_real_controller` runs the whole game: Pause opens "Paused" over a frozen world on Resume, Down and Jump rub on an Barkbalm (none left, 10 s of safety, its shimmer, play goes on), a second Pause finds that row refused while one is on, and Dash backs out to play. **Options by the buttons, kept through a restart (pass 467):** the Options and Controls pages had only been reached with `confirm()` and `adjust()`, and no test changed an option with the buttons, quit, and started again. The first one to do it found a bug. Backing out of a page put the cursor back on the menu's first row, so in play the next Jump on what had been Options resumed the game, and on the title it opened the flasks. Backing out of a page now lands on the row that opened it, on the pause menu and on the title. `test_options_are_changed_with_the_buttons_and_kept_through_a_restart` runs the whole game: Pause, Down to Options, Jump; Right turns the shake up a step, Jump turns mercy on, Left three times lowers the sound to 5; Dash lands on Options, Down and Jump open Controls, and Jump on Map then a real N key rebinds it. The game is quit, the options in memory go back to their defaults, and a new game reads the file alone: the shake, mercy, the sound bus and the key are all back. On its title, Options backs out onto Options. **Ended and begun again, by the buttons (pass 469):** the ending had only been driven with `skip`, `tick` and `advance`, and no test took a new journey up after it in the same run. `test_the_game_is_ended_and_begun_again_with_the_buttons` runs the whole game from the Everbloom's death: Jump and Attack turn the epilogue's cards, Pause skips to the thanks card and opens no pause menu, Jump inside the card's one-second grace does nothing and after it returns to the title. The Jump that closed the ending opens no flasks; the title plays its theme over no bed. Jump on Journeys and Jump on an empty flask begin a fresh journey at the West Gate, with the HUD on the new hero, the ending gone, Hearthroot's theme and bed, and the hero walking on a real Right. All held: no bug. **A journey taken up by the buttons (pass 429):** pass 359 walked the title's real path through the flasks, but with `confirm()`, so no test pressed the buttons from the title into a saved journey. The first one to do it found a bug. The Jump that takes a flask up is read by the shelf while the game is paused. The hero's first step then read the same press again and buffered it, so every journey taken up with Jump began with a jump on the first landing. A press already under way when the hero wakes is no longer the hero's: the first step drops it (`PlayerInput.drop_presses`), and held buttons stay held. `test_a_journey_is_taken_up_with_the_buttons` runs the whole game. Jump on Journeys opens the shelf on the full flask. Jump, held, takes it up: the middle flask's journey, with its gold, in the room where it rested. The hero wakes standing and does not jump until Jump is let go and pressed again. **A sign put away with the buttons (pass 431):** pass 430 covered the menus, which pause the game. The dialog box doesn't pause the game. It freezes the hero instead, so no steps are missed. The Jump, Attack or Up that puts a dialog away is read by the box in the frame it is pressed, and the hero's next step read it too. Jump jumped, Attack swung, and Up read the sign straight back open, so a one-page sign could not be put away with Up at all. The box now asks the hero to drop the presses of their next step (`drop_next_presses`; at first any freeze or death did, which pass 432 found swallowed a jump pressed as a door or a respawn let the hero go), and held buttons stay held. `test_a_sign_is_read_and_put_away_with_the_buttons` reads a tablet with a real Up and puts the box away with each of Jump, Attack and Up, held. Each time the box goes and stays away, and the hero does not move. A fresh Up reads it again. **A sign read safely beside a foe (passes 435 and 436):** the dialog box froze the hero but not the world, so a foe or a hazard beside a tablet went on striking a reader who could not move: a Rot Dwarf took 20 HP from a hero reading one sign. Pass 435 kept a frozen hero from harm (`Player.invulnerable`), which still holds for a door's scroll, a fade and a respawn. Pass 436 found that made a tablet a dodge: read as a blow came down, any blow passed through the reader, a guardian's too, and thirteen guardian arenas hold tablets. The box now pauses the world as a menu does (13.3, pass 430), and lets it go as it closes, so a foe holds still while you read and a blow you read under still lands once the box is away. `test_a_sign_is_read_safely_beside_a_foe` reads a tablet with a real Up five steps before a Rot Dwarf's blade lands: the dwarf holds still for five seconds and nothing lands. Put away with Attack, the same blow lands. **A sign and the other screens take turns (pass 439):** a sign, the pause menu and the map now hold the same pause, and each lets it go as it closes, so two open at once would leave the game paused with nothing up or running under a screen. `test_a_sign_and_the_pause_menu_take_turns` runs the whole game at the East Gate's tablet with the real buttons: while the sign is up, Map and Pause open nothing over it, and put away the game runs. Up and Pause pressed in one frame, landing either side of a physics step, open one screen, and closing it leaves none up and the game running. With the pause menu up, Up at the tablet reads nothing. All held: no bug.
- **Performance budget:** 60 fps locked on a mid-2018 laptop GPU; under 1.5 GB RAM; rooms load in under 100 ms (preload neighbors). **As measured (pass 335), headless:** every room loads in 9 to 89 ms (median 19; the generated gym is the slowest) and a settled room takes about 1 ms a frame; `test_every_room_loads_and_settles_inside_the_budget` fails at 500 ms and 8 ms, the margin a busy CI runner needs. Neighbors are not preloaded, since no room comes near 100 ms. Memory had no test. `test_walking_the_world_holds_its_memory` enters every room three times: the engine's own memory peaks at about 285 MB, against the 1.5 GB budget, and the third lap must need no more than the second (under 4 MB) and leave as many live objects (within 50), so a room change that leaves anything behind fails. Headless, that memory is scripts, resources and nodes; textures on the GPU are not in it.
- **Full-screen effects** that snap to world pixels (the Haar's fog) render at one texel per world pixel in their own small viewport and are scaled up: a quarter of the fragments of the 2x scene, and the same look. Their noise comes from a small tileable texture, not per-fragment hashing.
- **Accessibility:** full remapping, screen-shake and flash toggles, game speed slider (70 to 100%; steps of 10), assist options (extra invulnerability, infinite double jump in Inner Rings only off by default), colorblind-safe gate icons, subtitles for all voiced grunts. *As built (pass 357):* the gates are told apart by shape: each key door bears its mark (acorn, leaf, crown, thorn, stillglass shard; pass 471, in place of card suits), the Sky gate a bolt, and every other gate has a look of its own (fire, mist, bramble, amber, cracked stone, a veil); the title's flasks bear their springs' marks (14). No line in the game is voiced (people speak in text, and the hero's grunts are sounds, not words), so there is nothing to subtitle. *The Options page, as built (pass 381):* eight rows, in this order: Screen shake (full, half, off), Screen flashes (full, reduced, off), Rumble (on to start), Damage numbers (off to start), Assist: mercy (off to start), Music volume and Sound volume (a bar of 10, at 8 to start) and Game speed (100% to start). Left and Right change a row, and each change is saved at once. Until pass 381 this line was the pass 09 interim's: it named shake (full, half, off), flashes (full, reduced, off), rumble, the volumes and game speed, and said gamepad remapping would come with controller glyphs, which pass 365 built without them. The game's own words had drifted the same way. The pause menu's Options row left out the mercy assist, the title's left out the mercy assist and damage numbers, and the title's Controls row still said "Rebind the keyboard." Both menus now read one line for each (`Menus.OPTIONS_DETAIL`, `Menus.CONTROLS_DETAIL`). `test_the_options_page_is_the_one_the_bible_lists` holds the rows, their order and their starting values to `Menus.OPTIONS` and `Settings`, and both menus' lines to every row. **The pad, rebound (pass 365):** "full remapping" stood above since the plan, and until pass 365 only the keyboard could be rebound: the Controls page said the pad kept its layout, so a player on a gamepad who could not reach a shoulder button had no way to move the dagger off it. Every action the page lists now takes a pad button as well as a key. Choose a row and press either; the row shows both ("Space / A"). A button taken from another action swaps with it, as a key does. The potion sits on the left trigger and has no button of its own, so it may take only a free button, never one another action would lose. Start (pause), LS (the Stillstone) and RS (next spell) are not on the page and are never taken from it, and Start cancels a rebind as Esc does. The buttons are saved under `[buttons]` beside the keys (`Settings.buttons`, `Settings.bind_button`, `Controls.rebind_button`), and Reset puts both back. The page names buttons by the Xbox layout (A, B, X, Y, LB, RB); a pad's own glyphs would come with the art. `test_the_pad_can_be_rebound` presses the real buttons through the input map. *The mercy assist (pass 325):* the line above and the open questions ("one tuned difficulty plus assist options") promised assists, and none were built, so a player who found a fight too fast had only Game speed. The Options row "Assist: mercy", off by default and saved with the rest (`Settings.mercy`, under `[assist]`), doubles the invulnerability a hit leaves (0.8 s to 1.6 s, `Settings.MERCY_MULT`, read through `Player.hurt_invuln`), and the blink lasts as long, so a flurry or a boss's follow-up can't take two masks in a breath. The hurt pose still lets go after 0.25 s, so control comes back as soon as ever, and the Amber Draught's rise keeps twice the hit's grace. It changes no number in 5.3 or 6, which stay the one tuned game. The Inner Rings' infinite double jump is not built: a Trial's miss already costs time, never health. `test_the_mercy_assist_doubles_a_hits_grace`. *Damage numbers (pass 154):* the UI list (12) names "damage numbers (optional toggle)"; they are built as the Options row "Damage numbers", off by default and saved with the rest (`Settings.damage_numbers`). On, every blow that lands shows the damage it actually dealt (after a foe's guard, bark or shell) over what it struck: white for a cut, gold and twice the size for a heavy blow (the finisher, a thrust, a Riposte), and red over the player for the HP a hit took after armor. A number pops up 18 px with an ease-out and a little sideways drift (alternating, so a flurry doesn't stack), holds through the hit-stop and fades over its last 0.25 s of 0.7 s (`DamageNumber`). A blow a shield turns shows none. Foes, bosses (segment bosses through `BossSegment`) and the gym's dummy all show them. **Blightcap ticks (pass 309):** a poison tick was the one loss of health that showed no number, and since pass 306 it varies with the zone (2 in the Tower, up to 4 deep). Each tick now shows what it took over the hero, in the poisoned masks' green (`GameHud.POISONED`), so the number reads as the poison's and not a blow's. A tick that takes nothing (at the last point, or under an Barkbalm) shows none (`test_damage_numbers_show_each_poison_tick`).
  - **Game speed slows the game, not its screens (pass 483):** the slider and a boss's slow motion slow the play; words and menus are read in real time, as the music, the door fades and the HUD's banners are (passes 476, 477, 482). Until pass 483 every screen ran on the frame's game time: at 70% a sign or a person's line typed out at 70% of its 60 letters a second, the ending's cards turned every 5.7 s instead of 4 and its credits climbed slower, and the title's spores, the flask shelf and the map's blink drifted slowly. The dialog box, the menus, the map, the flask shelf, the title and the ending now take each frame in real time (`Feel.unscaled`). `test_the_screens_run_in_real_time_at_every_game_speed` types a line, waits out a menu's input grace and turns the ending's first card at 100%, at 70% and in a 30% slow motion, each in its real time.

---

## 15. Milestones and the Build Loop

### 15.1 Milestones

| # | Milestone | Done when |
|---|-----------|-----------|
| M0 | **Repo and pipeline** | Godot project, folder layout, CI (headless tests, export builds for Windows and Linux, room screenshot job), art tool scripts, Autosprite ledger, this bible committed |
| M1 | **Movement prototype** | Graybox gym with every movement ability toggleable; all section 6 values implemented; juice checklist complete; replay tests passing |
| M2 | **Combat core** | Slash combos, pogo, parry, spells framework, hit-stop and shake, 3 test enemies, damage and death loop |
| M3 | **Vertical slice** | Hearthroot + Heartwood in final art; shop, Ringreader save and skill tree (first 6 nodes), map, gold drop, Gnarlmaw, one secret of each type available, music and SFX in |
| M4 | **Act I complete** | Hollin Tower, Durgan, smith, fast travel, world-state change 1 |
| M5 | **Act II complete** | Haar, High Boughs (both), 4 bosses, all springs, all spells |
| M6 | **Act III complete** | Vorrhallow, the Evercrown, Thane Vorrund, the Likeness, Everbloom, ending |
| M7 | **Content complete** | Optional areas, Drowned Warden, all secrets, Inner Rings, boss rush, completion screen |
| M8 | **Polish loop** | Iterate until the confidence target in 15.3 is met |

### 15.2 The iteration loop

Every milestone runs the same loop, and M8 runs it until done:

1. **Build:** implement the next slice of work as pull requests.
2. **Verify automatically:** CI runs unit tests, movement replays, the reachability checker, a headless boot of every room, a performance capture, and screenshots of every room.
3. **Review:** a reviewer pass compares screenshots and metrics against this bible (palette compliance, contrast check, telegraph timings, economy curve, secret counts) and files issues.
4. **Score:** fill in the vision rubric (15.3). Write the score and the open issues to `docs/loop/pass-NN.md`.
5. **Fix:** the next pass takes the lowest-scoring categories first.
6. **Stop** when the confidence target is met; otherwise go to step 1.

The loop runs as a scheduled routine once the repo exists, and Sean gets one short message per completed milestone with a downloadable build. Sean's playtests are welcome but never required to keep the loop moving.

### 15.3 Vision rubric (confidence score)

| Category | Weight | Measured by |
|----------|--------|-------------|
| Movement feel | 20 | Section 6 values met in replays; juice checklist; frame-perfect input response; zero stuck states in fuzzed input tests |
| Combat and enemies | 15 | Telegraph times (every wind-up swept since pass 234), hit-stop, every enemy has full animation set, damage balance sheet within targets |
| World and level design | 15 | Room count, landmarks, save spacing, reachability proof, sequence breaks safe, secrets per zone |
| Art cohesion | 15 | Palette compliance 100%, contrast check pass, no missing animations, parallax per zone, screenshots reviewed |
| Bosses | 10 | Each has 2 to 3 phases, tests its ability, cinematic kill, balanced HP (target fight time 2.5 to 5 min; the health sheet in 5.5, held by `test_the_guardians_last_as_long_as_their_place` since pass 204) |
| Systems | 10 | Save/load round-trip, shop, skill tree, map, fast travel, settings all tested |
| Audio | 5 | Every action has SFX, every zone has music and ambience, mix levels in range |
| Stability and performance | 5 | 60 fps budget, no crashes in a 2-hour soak bot run, load times |
| Story and world | 5 | Every town has a Ringreader and named people; every guardian has lore before and after its fight; each beat of section 2 shows in the game (the Reeve's doubt, the Ringreader's reading of the glass leaf, the miner's confession in Amberlee, Carillon's record of the Withering, Vorrund's surrender of Severance, the Leaffall ending); no retired name is left (`test_no_retired_name_is_left`). Replaced the Faithfulness row in pass 461 |

**Stability measures (pass 99):** the journey soak (`test_soak.gd`) walks the world twice, door to door with mashing play between. It rests and Continues into a fresh world every 20 rooms and opens the pause and skills screens on the way. The second lap must leave no live objects, orphan nodes or tree nodes behind. No frame of play (room loads aside) may pass 50 ms, save one lone frame a lap under 100 ms (pass 182, below). Anything a room will need mid-play is loaded while the room loads: a boss arena loads its boss's sprite sheet and music (`BossArena.warm`), because a fight starts mid-play. Every sound effect loads with the game (`Sfx.load_all`, about 20 ms once, 2.2 MB), so no first slash or phase break waits on the disk (pass 103). Every boss fight is timed for 15 s of mashing with a phase knocked off every 5 s (`test_every_boss_fight_holds_the_frame_budget`): the average frame must stay under 8 ms and the 99th percentile under 16.6 ms, and nothing may load from disk once the fight has begun.

**Every room, and the trials live (pass 166):** `test_every_room_loads_and_settles_inside_the_budget` enters all of the game's rooms in one world, the trials and the gym included. Each must load behind the fade in under 500 ms, and once it has settled its frames must average under 8 ms. The first room of a zone pays for its tileset, and a boss arena for its warm-up; the worst is about 150 ms here. The trials soak runs each Ringreader Trial as a live run, with the Stillstone turned whenever it is ready: hazards set you back instead of hurting, Stillness's water freezes and runs on, and no run is left open after.

**Guardians from the rooms, and beaten for good (pass 174):** the boss frame audit reads its arenas from the rooms' `boss:` headers instead of a hand-kept list, which had missed the Clockwork Alpha and the Bark Captain; every guardian `BossArena` knows must have exactly one arena. `test_every_beaten_guardian_stays_beaten_through_a_save` wins each fight (the Everbloom's starts the ending, so it is left to the ending's tests), saves, continues into a fresh world in that room and walks in: the doors stay open, no fight starts, and the prize (rings, keys, health, magic, points, abilities, armor) is neither lost nor given twice. A save's key counts come back as whole numbers, as they went (JSON had turned them to floats).

**After the end (pass 144):** every soak had run on a fresh save, so the post-game world (4.4: the blossom layer, `Music.after_the_end`, every boss arena idle) and Wanderer's Grace (`Player.on_kill` on every death) had never been walked. `test_a_journey_after_the_end_stays_lean` runs the journey on a cleared save: every boss beaten, every spring running, the Wanderer branch learned. Each lap walks 100 doors out of Hearthroot and 50 out of the dead stillglass's Threshold, resting and continuing every 20 rooms (each Continue must still be cleared). The checks:
- the second lap leaves no orphan nodes, no growth in the tree and fewer than 50 live objects behind;
- no two frames of play pass 50 ms, and no lone frame 100 ms (pass 182);
- no sheet or sound is first loaded from disk during play, once a room's first three frames have built it (pass 303);
- no engine or script error is logged (`ErrorCatcher`);
- blossom drifted, the Evercrown played still and Hearthroot its festival.

**Dying during a boss win (pass 239):** the edge case of a trade at the killing blow is closed: nothing can hurt the hero while a guardian falls (8, "A won fight stays won").

**One lone frame forgiven (pass 182):** the journeys' 50 ms check failed three times in full runs (58.6 ms in pass 174, 51.2 ms in `evercrown/heart_gate` in pass 178, 51.5 ms in `sunken/source_hall` in pass 179), each time a single frame in a room no change had touched, and each passed when run again alone. A frame-by-frame trace of the post-game lap (about 4,000 play frames) found nothing in the game behind it: the entry frame itself costs a median 5 ms (up to 66 ms, a load, not judged), and every judged frame after it a median 0.6 ms and at most 5 ms. A real hitch (a lazy load, a slow room, a leak) comes back every time its room is entered, so it shows as several slow frames; the host pausing the process shows as one. So each lap tracks its worst and second-worst play frames: the second must stay under 50 ms and the worst under 100 ms, and a lone frame over 50 ms is printed. `test_the_journey_frame_budget_forgives_one_lone_frame` checks the tally keeps both across the two halves of a post-game lap.

**A lone frame is not a load (pass 303):** pass 301's full run had one 103.8 ms frame in the Gnawed Stair, play frame 9, on the first journey lap. It passed when run again alone (worst 5.7 ms), and the same seed walks the same path. A probe of both journeys counted the character sheets (`Sheet`) and sounds (`Sfx.loads`) first loaded during play frames, and found none. So the frame was the host, as in pass 182, and the 100 ms limit stays. The probe is now a check on every journey lap, so a future lazy load (a new sheet first fetched mid-room) fails at once instead of waiting to show up as a rare spike.

**The host's pause, told from a hitch (pass 367):** the journey soaks allow one lone frame a lap between 50 and 100 ms, because full runs had shown such frames that a lap run alone never did (pass 182). Passes 359 and 363 then failed on bursts of two or three frames of 50 to 101 ms, in rooms that ran under 9 ms alone. Pass 365's lead was a full run holding about 900 more live objects. A trace of every test (`--trace` on the test runner prints what is alive after each) showed those objects are the zones' sheets, sounds and tiles cached for good. The tree stays at 22 nodes and no orphan is ever left, so no leak was slowing the game. The slow frames had the main thread off the CPU instead: a frame of 26 ms showed none of it on the main thread's own clock (`/proc/self/task/<id>/stat`). An empty loop with no game in it, run for two minutes in the same VM, met a 54.5 ms frame the same way, with the VM's steal counter (`/proc/stat`, the CPU time the host takes back) running through it; this machine loses about 1% of its CPU to the host, in bursts. So a frame over the 50 ms budget is now set aside only when both hold: the main thread was on the CPU for under half of it, and the host took time while it ran (`_host_paused` in `test_soak.gd`). Each one is counted and printed ("host paused"). A frame the game spent on the CPU is judged as before, steal or not. So is a frame off the CPU with nothing stolen, a wait the game may own (a lock or a disk), and so is every frame where there is no `/proc`. `test_a_host_pause_is_told_from_a_hitch` holds the rule and checks that both clocks read. **The boss fights too (pass 384):** the boss fights' budget (`test_every_boss_fight_holds_the_frame_budget`: 8 ms on average, 16.6 ms at the 99th percentile, over 900 mashed frames) had no such rule. A full run failed it in `heartwood/root_chamber` at 49.4 ms at the 99th percentile with 1.68 ms on average, a burst of the host's frames; the same fight alone ran 0.64 ms and 1.26 ms. A fight's frame over 16.6 ms is now set aside by the same `_host_paused` and counted, and a fight that loses more than a tenth of its frames to the host fails, since too little of it was judged.

**How long the soaks play (pass 383, deviation from the rubric's 2-hour run):** the Stability row asks for "no crashes in a 2-hour soak bot run", and no one had counted how much play the soaks cover. The test runner now counts the physics frames each soak plays and prints the total at the end of a run. A full run's 44 soaks (`test_soak.gd`) play about 50 minutes of the game, not two hours. That includes the two journeys, every room's mashing soak, every guardian's fight, the doors, the shrines and the boss rush. The remake keeps it at that and does not add a two-hour bot, because every push runs the whole suite in CI, about 35 minutes of wall time. Fifty minutes of play on every change catches more than a two-hour run now and then. Instead the run holds a floor: an unfiltered run whose soaks play less than `SOAK_FLOOR_MINUTES` (45) fails, so a soak cut short or dropped can't shrink the count without anyone noticing. `test_the_soak_floor_is_the_one_the_bible_gives` holds the floor to this line.

**A resting drop does its checks once (pass 311):** pass 308's spike check scans every child of the room, and it ran every frame a drop lay still: nine resting drops made 764 scans in 2 s where nine would do. Each scan is cheap (about 2 µs in the Flooded Hall), so no frame showed it, but the cost grew with every drop and every child. A drop now checks once as it settles, and again only after it leaves the floor (a torn leaf drops it, say). Pass 307's door clamp now asks the tiles only for a drop or coin within 40 px of a side edge, so a mid-room coin spray asks none. `test_a_resting_drop_checks_for_spikes_once`.

**A thousand coins (pass 199):** gold came out in coins of 1 and 5, each a small body that falls, bounces and flies to you. The biggest purses had grown (the Vorrhallow vault's 5,000 g chest, and since pass 197 boss purses of up to 1,000 g), so opening that chest threw 1,004 coins at once: 0.87 s to spawn them and a 27 ms frame, measured headless. Each coin also chimes when it is picked up. Coins now come in 1, 5, 25 and 100 (`Coin.DENOMINATIONS`), each drawn a little larger than the last. A spray uses the largest coin that leaves at least as much again for smaller ones, so small purses still scatter singles. The same chest is now 61 coins, spawned in 54 ms, with a worst frame of 1.5 ms. `test_a_big_purse_sprays_few_coins` holds every purse up to 5,000 g to at most 64 coins, adding up to the purse exactly, and keeps a purse under 10 g as single coins.

**No silent sounds (pass 207):** `test_every_sound_the_code_plays_exists` reads the scripts for literal names, but some sounds are named at runtime: a helper's choice (`Coin.sound`, `Menus.equipment_sound`, `Player.swing_sound`) or a name built from parts (`"step_" + surface`). A typo there played silence and failed nothing. `Sfx.play` now keeps every name it is asked for that has no file in `Sfx.missing`, and the test runner fails any test that adds to it, so the whole suite, the soaks' journeys included, checks the sounds that actually play. `test_a_sound_with_no_file_is_caught_at_runtime` checks that the list notices one, and that every choice the helpers and the rooms' footsteps can make exists. A misspelled `coin_big` fails the coin test through it.

**The mashing hand holds Attack (pass 151):** the soaks' mashing player pressed Attack but never held it, and none had learned a Blade skill, so Thrust (pass 149), Riposte and Rising Cut had never been soaked. The mashing now holds Attack for spans of 4 to 60 frames, most of them long enough to charge a Thrust, and every zone soak and both journeys learn the whole Blade branch. Each zone soak must see the player thrust (all but the Sunken Spring, which is all water). The first run found a bug that had nothing to do with the Thrust: the Polesend soak climbed into the Scorched Hall, fell back down its floor shaft and met Polesend's spikes before it first landed. Since M3 a floor or ceiling door had left the safe footing as the last room's until the first landing, so the hazard set it back at the Scorched Hall's coordinates, inside Polesend's ground. Every door's arrival is now the first safe spot (3.3, `test_spikes_before_the_first_landing_set_you_back_in_the_new_room`). The journeys now mash on dice of their own, so the doors they pick don't hang on the mashing, and the after-the-end leak check retraces its second lap, since a lap over new ground caches the music, sheets and tiles it meets for the first time.

**The mashing hand throws the dagger (pass 191):** the Rune Dagger (pass 184) had never been thrown in a soak, so every room's walls, doors, gates, planks and water, and every boss fight's `Enemy.take_throw`, had been met by it only in its own tests. The mashing hand now throws it every 25 frames. The timing comes from a counter, not the soak's random stream, so each soak's mashing stays the run it was before. Each zone soak checks that it threw at least once a room (`Player.throws`). The first full soak threw it 778 times across the rooms and in every boss fight with no problem.

**The mashing hand wears a shield too (pass 319):** the soak's hero starts a new game in every room, and a new game has no shield, so the guard, the parry, the Warding Shield's reflect and the ward had never been mashed in a real room. Since Sean's off hand (5.2) a shield and the dagger exclude each other, so one hero can't do both. Every other room of each zone's soak now wears the Warding Shield, and the rest keep the hand free. The soak checks the dagger was thrown at least once per free-handed room and the guard held at least 10 frames per shielded room. The first run guarded 2,085 frames across 62 shielded rooms with no problems, and frame times stayed between 0.57 and 1.11 ms.

**The title's path, walked as the game drives it (pass 359):** the shelf of flasks (14) had tests of its own (`SlotPicker`), but the game's side of it (`Game._journeys`, `_take_up`, and `_end_of_ending` back to the title) had never run in a test. Every soak starts its world directly, so no test had taken a journey up from the title, gone back to the title and chosen again on the same shelf. `test_the_title_takes_journeys_up_and_back_in_their_own_flasks` drives the real game: the title's Journeys row, the shelf, a full flask taken up where it rested, the ending back to the title (saving on the way), then an empty flask on the same shelf. On every trip it checks that one world plays and none is left after, the tree is unpaused, the shelf has one listener on each signal, and the journey belongs to the chosen flask. At the end, the first journey's save is in its own flask and the other two are empty. Nothing was broken. Mutations show the test holds: a new journey left in the last flask's slot fails it, and so does a world left behind at the title.

**Saves and settings (pass 109):** no file the game reads can crash it or leave it half-loaded.
- **Writing.** Saves and `settings.cfg` are written to a `.tmp` file, read back whole, and only then swapped in, with the previous file kept as `.bak` (`GameState.write_safely`). A write that fails leaves the last good file untouched.
  - **A rest that can't save says so (pass 255).** `write_safely` reported a failed write, but nothing listened: `World.save_at` dropped the result, and the shrine always said "Rested. Progress saved." On a full or read-only disk a player could quit trusting a save that never happened. Now `save_at` returns whether the file was written. A shrine or a Ringreader that couldn't write still rests you and sets your respawn point, but says "Rested, but the journey could not be saved." (`SaveShrine.NOT_SAVED`), and the Ringreader names no ring. Test: `test_a_rest_that_cannot_save_says_so`.
- **Loading.** A damaged file (empty, cut off, not JSON, not a save) falls back to its `.bak`. If neither reads, the load is refused and the game in play is left as it was. The title's Continue then starts a new journey with a note, rather than failing.
- **Sanitizing.** Whatever loads is rebuilt on a new game's data (`GameState.sanitize`):
  - every key is present and has its own type, and a wrong type falls back to the default;
  - lists keep only well-formed entries (skills, rings, pins, Ringreader stops), and the save point and purse must be whole;
  - maps read as yes or no (`GameState.BOOL_MAPS`: abilities, flags, charts, pickups taken, rooms visited) keep only true and false (pass 351). A hand-edited flag of "yes" had stopped the title's flask with a script error, so a full flask read as empty and taking it up began a new journey over the old one. `test_a_hand_edited_save_still_fills_its_flask`;
  - numbers are finite and never negative, and health, MP and flasks never fall below a new game's;
  - keys from a newer build are kept.
- **Missing rooms.** A rest in a room this build lacks wakes you at the new-game spot.
- **Settings.** Each option must be the right type and in range, or it takes its default; only a real keycode is bound.
- **Older files (pass 129).** A settings file from an earlier build has no line for the actions added since. It loads with those actions on their default keys and logs nothing. It had logged an engine error for each missing action.
- **Saves naming what a build lacks (pass 138).** A save can name a skill node, blade, ring or dye that this build doesn't have (renamed, or from a newer build). Unlearning skills used to stop at such a node with a script error, after taking the gold and before refunding any points. Now an unknown node refunds nothing and the respec goes through. The save keeps each skill and dye once, as strings, so a skill listed twice can't be refunded twice. An unknown blade falls back to the Short Sword untempered: it had taken the missing blade's temper. An unknown dye leaves the cloak as it is. `test_a_save_naming_things_this_build_lacks_plays_on` runs such a save through loading, dressing, the skill list, the dyer and a respec with `ErrorCatcher` listening. **A slot holds its own kind (pass 191):** a save can now wear a known item in the wrong slot, such as a sword as a helmet or plate as a blade, since the helmet slot and kept gear came in. Such an item comes off when the save is loaded, and an empty blade slot falls back to the Short Sword. The kept gear list is strings, once each, and always holds the Short Sword; the Cloak section lists only dyes the save owns. `test_a_save_with_gear_in_the_wrong_slots_plays_on` runs such a save through the Equipment page (every row chosen), the shops and a dagger throw with `ErrorCatcher` listening.
- **Moved pickups (pass 213).** A pickup's id is its room and cell (`vorrhallow/drowned_nave:31,6`), so moving one in a room file would hand a save that had already taken it a second copy at the new cell. `GameState.MOVED_PICKUPS` lists every move (old id to new id) and `sanitize` carries each taken one over. A scan of the level history found two: the Drowned Nave's Grain Shard (pass 67) and the North Bough's Life Fruit (pass 211, and again in pass 216). Since then: the Sky Steps' purse (pass 219). Moving a pickup means adding a line there. `test_a_moved_pickup_stays_taken` checks the carry-over and that each old cell is empty and each new one holds a pickup.
- **Saves from before the off hand (pass 317).** Sean's off-hand rule (5.2, 2026-10-31) means a worn shield holds the throwing hand. A save from an earlier build that had found the Rune Dagger and wore a shield would load with Throw doing nothing, with nothing to say why: the pickup's banner that explains it had played under the old rule. Such a save is now marked as it loads (`GameState.VERSION` 2; `OFFHAND_VERSION`; the flag `notice:offhand`), and the next Continue shows a banner, "The Off Hand: Your shield now holds the throwing hand. To throw, choose the Rune Dagger under Off Hand (Equipment)." It shows once (`GameState.take_load_notice` clears it). The shield stays on, because which hand to keep is the player's choice. A save with the hand already free, or one written under the rule, is not told. Test: `test_an_old_save_with_shield_and_dagger_is_told_of_the_off_hand`.
- **The Severance in the save (pass 221).** It is kept twice: as an ability (`abilities.severance`, which the reach checker gates the heart's flesh on and the pass 217 migration reads) and as gear (the Equipment page; the blade in hand is what cuts the flesh). It was the one ability missing from a new game's list (`GameState.fresh`), so `test_every_save_field_survives_a_round_trip` never carried it, a damaged value kept whatever type it had, and a save could hold one half without the other. It is listed now, and `sanitize` makes a save hold both or neither: the sword in the gear grants the ability, and the ability puts the sword in the gear (not in hand). `test_the_severance_is_held_as_ability_and_gear_together`.
- **No engine errors (pass 215).** The runner holds an `ErrorCatcher` over every test, and any engine error the test logs fails it, as a missing sound does (pass 207). Warnings are not counted. A test that sets out to provoke one, such as a damaged settings file that Godot's own parser reports, says so with `expect_engine_errors()`. Only the two settings tests do. The first run caught a real one: a test's wait captured a fireball that had already been freed, which Godot logs as an error. Waits now hold such nodes by weak reference. Loading a damaged save had also logged Godot's "Parse JSON failed" error before the game's own plain warning. It now parses with `JSON.new().parse`, which reports by return value, so a player's log shows only "user://save_1.json is not a save".
- **A clean slate for every test (pass 231).** The game keeps some state in statics (the options, stopped time, a trial or rush under way, a forced pose, the drop odds, the feel switches), and a static outlives the test that set it. A probe found the secret-ending test starting the real game, which loads and applies the options; it never put them back, so the six save tests after it ran with rumble on and the options file pointed at a test path. Worse, the suite's own `_restore` pointed the options back at `user://settings.cfg`, the player's file. Now the runner starts every test from one slate (`_clean_slate`: default options pointed at `TestSuite.SETTINGS_PATH`, never the player's file; no hitstop, rumble, sound or random drops; no stopped time, trial, rush or pose), fails any test that leaves one of them changed (`_leaks`), and wipes the slate either way. Test: `test_every_test_starts_from_a_clean_slate`. **Buttons held (pass 391):** since passes 384 and 386, tests press the real controller (`Input.action_press`, the suite's `press_for_real`) to time moves and combat buttons on their frame. A pressed action is engine state that outlives the test, so a test that forgot a release, or stopped short of it, would hand the next test a hero already holding Left or Attack, and nothing caught it. `_leaks` now names every game action still held down (`held_actions`, the engine's `ui_` actions aside), and `_clean_slate` lets them all go. The same test presses Jump and Attack and checks both count as a leak and are released. **Saves and pauses left behind (pass 399):** the suite shares `user://` with the game, so a test that saved to or poured out slot 1, 2 or 3 would take one of the player's own three flasks, and nothing stopped it; the tests keep to slots 7 to 9 and 96 to 99 only by habit. And since passes 397 and 398 drive open menus, which pause the game, a test that stopped short of closing one would hand the next test a world standing still. Now `_leaks` hashes every `save_*` file in `user://` when a test begins and names any the test wrote, changed or removed (`changed_saves`), and names a game left paused; `_clean_slate` unpauses it. A save file the test made and left is then removed, so it cannot leak into the next test or the next run; a file that was there before the test began is never touched. The same test writes and pours out a flask of its own, and pauses and unpauses the game, to show each is caught. **A save slot left behind (pass 407):** the tests that rest or save choose a test slot (`GameState.slot`) and put it back by hand, and nothing checked that they did. A test that left slot 9 chosen would send every later test's saves there, so a test that saved without choosing a slot would write to slot 9 instead of the player's first flask, and the save check above would no longer catch it. Now every test begins on the first flask (`_clean_slate` sets it), and `_leaks` names a slot left changed. Its first run found 18 tests that left a test slot chosen, five of them by loading a test save; each now puts the slot back. **A journey left behind (pass 415):** the runner never reset `GameState.data` itself. A test built with `make_world` starts a new game, but a bare hero (`make_player` in a room of its own) reads his gear, gold and keys from whatever journey the last test left, so a shield one test obtained (pass 412 equips one to parry Thane Vorrund) would ride into the next test's hero, and the gym's no-record switch (`GameState.active`) left off would stop every later kill from counting. Now `_clean_slate` starts a fresh journey (`GameState.new_game()`) and turns recording back on, `_leaks` names the no-record switch left off, and the run's summary counts how many tests left the journey changed (`Journeys wiped`). The same test obtains a shield, sets gold and turns recording off, and checks the slate puts all three back. **Music left playing (pass 423):** the music player is an autoload, so the track, the ambience bed and a boss phase's lift a test left playing carried into the next test. `Music.play` does nothing when asked for the track already playing, so 41 checks that a fight, a zone or the ending plays its music could have passed with no play at all, whenever an earlier test had left that track on. Now `_clean_slate` hushes the music (`Music.play("")`, `Music.ambience("")`, no lift), the run's summary counts how many tests left music playing (`Music hushed`), and the same test leaves a boss theme, an ambience and a lift playing and checks the slate silences all three.
- **Every option in the leak check (pass 327).** `_leaks` named each option by hand, so pass 325's mercy assist had to be added to the list, and an option left out would have leaked between tests unseen. Now `Settings.snapshot()` holds every option, and the runner compares it with the snapshot a fresh reset gives. `test_every_option_is_in_the_snapshot` reads the static vars in `settings.gd`, so an option missing from the snapshot fails it. It also flips each option to check it counts as a leak, and loads a file from before `[assist]` and one with a damaged mercy, both of which give mercy off without an engine error.
- **A soak that pushes a phase lands on it (pass 223).** The Hydra soak failed now and then ("the soak reached the flood"). The cause was in the test, not the fight: it pushes the boss's health onto each phase mark, and 99,999 x 0.3 rounds to 30,000, a hair above the mark, so the flood came only if a mashed blow happened to land after the push. Across 30 random seeds, 13 never got there. The soaks now push with `_push_to_mark`, which rounds down, and the Hydra soak checks the flood comes at the push itself. 30 of 30 seeds pass. The game's own marks were fine: a boss turns at or below its mark, and the phase-1 clamp stops above the next one.
- **The Bloom soak tears and broods (pass 263).** The Bloom soak claimed to tear the leaf in phase 2, but its player was put into biting range half a reach from the root, where a bite never tears. So the leaf was never torn in it (0 gaps, and the "never past 3 gaps" check passed on nothing), and pass 257's brood was soaked without a check. In phase 2, while the jaws choose and aim, the soak now holds the player 0.9 of a reach out over the wider side of the leaf, and it runs up to 20 s past its 30 if the leaf is still whole. It must tear the leaf (1 to 3 gaps) and loose the brood (never more than 3 at once). Then it fells the Bloom with a brood in the air, and no Mothling may outlive it.
- **The Thane Vorrund soak calls his guard (pass 271).** Pass 268 gave the King a household guard of Shade Knights called from the throne, but his soak only checked duels, the ward and shard waves, so a guard that piled up, came more than `GUARDS` times, or outlived him under mashing would pass. The soak now counts every knight in the hall each frame. The guard must come at least once (every knight seen is one he called), never more than one stands at a time, and he never calls more than `GUARDS`. Then he falls with a knight still standing (the soak checks one does), and none may be left 4 s later, when the hall must hold no more nodes than it began with, dust and his gold aside. Dust is left out because the fall's slow motion runs on the wall clock, which a headless run outpaces, so its dust can still be in the air (the soak also ends it with `Feel.reset()`). Without `_dismiss_guard()` in his `die()`, the soak fails.
- **Every guardian is soaked (pass 279).** Fight soaks covered the Widow, the Hydra, the Foreman, the Bloom, the Roc, Thane Vorrund, the Likeness and the Everbloom's three forms. Six guardians had none: Gnarlmaw, the Clockwork Alpha, the Bark Knight Captain, the Marsh Hag Matriarch, Durgan and the Drowned Warden. A lunge, web, hex, drill or wave that left an effect behind would have passed. `test_every_other_guardian_fight_soak_stays_lean` fights each of them in its arena for 20 s against a mashing hero who can't die. Halfway through it drops the guardian to 15% health, so its later phase is soaked too, and it holds the guardian's health there so the fight never ends. Then it lets go of the controls for 4 s. It checks that the room never grows by 60 nodes or more and settles back within 6 of where it started (leaving out dust and coins). It also checks that a frame averages under the 60 fps budget (0.5 to 0.6 ms on the test machine). Gnarlmaw's torn floor is meant to stay, so its spikes are counted apart and must never pass one per torn column (26). With the floor torn twice, the test fails. All six held, so nothing needed fixing.
- **Every foe's spoils stay in its room (pass 287).** Passes 283 and 285 kept drops and coins inside the room (`Drop.keep_in_room`), but only the two Silt Adder rooms were tested. `test_every_foes_spoils_rest_in_its_room` now kills every foe in every room at once: 82 rooms, each foe leaving a loaf and three coins, 1,012 pickups in all. Every one must rest on a floor inside its room within 5 s, and each room must keep its 8 ms frame budget while it all falls. All hold. With the coins' guard removed, 14 coins are lost across twelve rooms, not just the Silt Adder rooms: six in five rooms of the Heartwood (the Sapwell Deeps twice, the Sap Tunnel, the Sap Cistern, the Grub Warren, the Mossy Overhang), five in four rooms of the Tower (the Winch Hall twice, the Mine Mouth, the Mine Shaft, the Smelter), two in the Haar (Fog Cut, the Scorched Hall) and one in the Sunken Spring's Source Hall. So pass 285 kept more gold than it measured.
- **Tests.** `test_systems.gd` covers each case. `ErrorCatcher` (in `tests/`) lets a test check that a moment logs no engine errors at all.

**Exit rule:** weighted score of at least 95, no category below 85, zero open blocker bugs, and a full bot playthrough from new game to credits succeeds.

**Honest limit:** an automated score cannot fully measure "feel". The rubric uses measurable proxies benchmarked against the reference games. A short playtest by Sean at M1 (movement) and M3 (slice) would be the single most valuable input, but the loop does not wait for it.

---

## 16. Open Questions (decided by default, revisit any time)

| Question | Default decision |
|----------|------------------|
| Title | "Yldenfall" (Sean, 2026-11-04) |
| Voice acting | None; grunts and efforts only |
| Difficulty modes | One tuned difficulty plus assist options |
| Platforms | Windows and Linux first; macOS next; Steam Deck stretch |
| Localization | English only at launch; all text in a string table |
| Original music remixes | No direct remixes of NES tracks (IP); new themes inspired by their mood |

---

*End of design bible v1.0.*
